# Security Write Gates — every remote-reachable store mutation

**What this is:** the complete enumeration performed for 0.8.5, of every write
to the local database that an attacker can reach by sending us a frame, and the
gate each one sits behind. It exists so "we have no known holes" is a checked
claim rather than an assertion, and so any NEW ingest path can be checked
against a list instead of a memory.

**How to use it:** when you add a handler that writes to `MessageStore`, find
its row here. If it has no row, it has no gate — add both.

Rule of thumb for the whole surface: **the transport tells you WHO sent a
frame; it never tells you who AUTHORED the content inside it.** Every hole in
this file's history came from conflating the two.

---

## 1. Gated by the message signature (v2, REQUIRED)

`crypto_handler::check_backfill_signature` → `BackfillSig::is_acceptable()`, or
a direct `verify_message_signature_v2` on the live paths. As of 0.8.5 `Valid` is
the only acceptable verdict — see `REQUIRE_SIGNED_BACKFILL`.

| Write | Sites | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| `insert` (DM row) | swarm live DM arm; DmSyncBatch; DmSiblingSyncBatch; `fetch.rs` push DM; FileHeader sentinel (swarm + fetch) | v2 signature over `dm` + recipient master |
| `insert_channel_message` | `message_ops::handle_envelope_channel_message` (the ONE live ingest: MLS, plaintext public, Olm fallback); ChannelSyncBatch; `sync_handler` MLS batch; `fetch.rs` push channel | v2 signature over `ch` + `{sid}:{cid}`. LIVE (HOL-SEC-009/010, 2026-09-27): `live_channel_post_refusal` against OUR state (current member who can see AND post in the channel, not muted, media-only needs a file; slow mode after), on every transport and on push (`fetch_post_refused` over the stored state); a plaintext public frame only for a channel public in OUR state (`public_frame_accepted`, guest preview excepted); every MLS-decrypted envelope first passes `crypto_handler::mls_envelope_fits_group` (names the decrypting group's server, a subgroup carries only its channel, restricted-channel CONTENT only via its subgroup, no DM shapes). Sync backfill skips the live gate by design. Unit: `authz_live_post_needs_a_member_who_can_see_and_post`, `authz_public_frame_only_for_a_channel_public_here`, `authz_live_channel_post_is_judged_by_our_state`, `authz_mls_envelope_must_fit_the_group_that_decrypted_it`; wiring: `channel_ingest_gates_stay_wired` |
| `edit_channel_message` / `edit_dm_message` / `set_*_message_edited_at` | swarm live edit arms; both sync batches; `fetch.rs` | edit signature over `edited_at` + new text + the row's FULL extras |
| `hide_channel_message` / `hide_dm_message` | `message_ops::handle_envelope_delete_message` (every channel transport) / `handle_envelope_dm_delete` (Olm) | `ch-delete` / `dm-delete` signature over the row's current text + extras, signer from the ROW (row guard below first) |
| `set_*_message_hidden_verified` | `apply_verified_channel_deletion` / `apply_verified_dm_deletion`, 4 sync apply sites | author's deletion proof, REJECT-ABSENT (0.8.4). Author comes from the ROW, never the item |
| `repair_channel_message_sender` | `sync_handler::repair_wedged_sender` (via `ingest_synced_channel_item`) | only when `sig_verified` AND the row-ownership guard below passed: the row's stored key must already name the item's signer |
| EVERY remote change that touches an EXISTING row: sync items (edit, sender repair, card, deletion, file card) AND live edits, deletions, cards and push caption promotion | `sync_handler::ingest_synced_channel_item` (the ONE channel item path, Olm + MLS); DmSyncBatch; DmSiblingSyncBatch; `message_ops::handle_envelope_{edit_message,delete_message,link_preview_set}` (every channel transport, Olm arms included); `handle_envelope_dm_edit` / `handle_envelope_dm_delete`; `fetch.rs` edit, card, `persist_direct_message` caption | HOL-SEC-004 (sync, 2026-09-26) + HOL-SEC-008 (live and push, 2026-09-27): `message_ops::change_may_touch_row` first. A signature proves who wrote the CHANGE, not whose ROW its `mid` names: channel = row in the change's `{sid}:{cid}` AND author (sender collapsed to master, or the key its stored signature names) == signer, so a live channel change naming another server, or none, is refused (the mute gate reads that server); DM = row in the change's conversation AND same direction. Live DM changes get signer + context from the ROW via `live_dm_change` (friend = its own row in its conversation with us; our sibling = our row). A refused item is dropped whole. Channel batches additionally come only from a current member who can see the channel (`crypto_handler::channel_backfill_allowed_from`). Unit: `authz_synced_channel_item_cannot_rewrite_another_authors_row`, `authz_synced_dm_item_touches_only_its_own_conversation_and_direction`, `authz_channel_backfill_only_from_a_member_who_can_read_it`, `authz_live_dm_change_touches_only_the_senders_own_rows`, `authz_live_channel_change_must_name_the_rows_own_channel`, `authz_push_dm_change_touches_only_the_senders_own_rows` |
| `update_link_preview` / `update_channel_link_preview` | swarm live arms; `message_ops`; `fetch.rs` | runs only on `is_new` after a verified insert; the preview itself is bound by `lp_digest` inside the v2 payload |
| `update_link_preview_and_sig` / `update_channel_link_preview_and_sig` | `message_ops::handle_envelope_link_preview_set` (all 3 ingest paths: Olm DM/channel, MLS channel, plaintext `pub_lp_set`); the local-author `handle_attach_*_link_preview` | issue #45 late cards. Author comes from the ROW (channel: the row guard above with the sender resolved to master; DM: `live_dm_change`), never from the frame. Then a full `verify_message_signature_v2` over the row's text/extras with the NEW `lp_digest` — REJECT on failure. `kind`/`author`/`video_url` are inside that digest, so a relay cannot repaint a plaintext public-channel card HOL-SEC-068 (2026-09-29): a LIVE card applies only when its frame's seal time is later than the row's `lp_at` and its last edit (every arm and the push fetch node); signed sync backfill stays unordered. Unit: `authz_an_older_card_never_replaces_a_newer_one` |
| `add_reaction` | live envelope + Olm arms; **3 sync batches + `sync_handler`** (0.8.5); guest preview (0.8.5) | `reaction:{mid}:{emoji}:{ts}` signed by the reactor's MASTER — `message_ops::sync_reaction_accepted` for sync/guest. LIVE (HOL-SEC-008): the row must sit where the reaction says, `channel_reaction_target_ok` (row in the named `{sid}:{cid}`) or `dm_reaction_target_ok` (row in the reactor's conversation with us; any DM row for our sibling); a reaction that outruns its row drops and rides sync; MLS drops DM-shaped reactions and cards. Unit: `authz_dm_reaction_lands_only_in_the_reactors_conversation` |
| `remove_reaction` | live envelope + Olm arms (public, MLS, Olm channel, Olm DM all go through `MessageStore::remove_reaction`) | `unreaction:{mid}:{emoji}:{ts}`, same rule. HOL-SEC-068 (2026-09-29): deletes only a reaction added no later than the removal (ties to the removal, as the add rule), and our own stamps beat any recorded add or removal (`next_reaction_stamp`). Unit: `authz_an_old_unreaction_never_removes_a_later_re_add`; harness: `authz_a_replayed_public_unreaction_or_card_never_undoes_a_later_one` |
| Guest public-channel preview (RAM, no DB) | `swarm.rs` PublicChannelSyncResponse; the live public arms (post, edit, link preview, delete, reactions) | 0.8.5: `guest_item_accepted` drops unverified items; hidden flag via `verified_guest_hidden_at`; reactions per-item. HOL-SEC-095 (D6): with no server state, every public arm writes to `guest_view::GuestView`'s store, a shared in-memory SQLCipher DB held only while a room is browsed (`store_for`); leaving a room prunes its rows, the last leave drops the store; NOTHING reaches the guest's database. Author names and avatars only from the author's own master-signed card (`profile_card::guest_sender`: card master == sender, `card_holds`, avatar bytes hash to the card's); members keep co-members' cards from `ProfileUpdate.card` (`keep_card`, card master == resolved sender) and answer with `cards_for_guest` (1 MB avatar budget, 256 KB each); never a nickname or a name the responder picks. Server name/avatar/banner/channel list stay unauthenticated (AR-17). Unit `authz_a_guest_shows_only_what_the_senders_card_signs`, `a_member_hands_a_guest_only_signed_cards`, `a_guest_store_lives_in_memory_only_while_held`; harness `authz_a_guest_keeps_public_posts_in_memory_only`, `authz_a_guest_shows_an_author_only_by_its_own_card` |
| A channel's public flag (`ChannelPublicChanged`, visibility tier, visibility labels) | `op_allowed`, apply, `ChannelInfo::effective_public` | HOL-SEC-094 (D6): a RESTRICTED channel (tier above everyone or a label gate, `ChannelInfo::restricted`) is never public: `effective_public` requires unrestricted, restricting clears `is_public` at apply (lifting it later never reopens), `op_allowed` refuses flagging a restricted channel public, the author's `handle_set_channel_public` refuses with a message, and closing a public channel tells the room's guests (`tell_guests_if_closed`). Subgroups = restricted channels (`channel_uses_subgroup`, Dart `usesSubgroup` mirrors). Unit `authz_a_restricted_channel_is_never_public`, harness `authz_a_hidden_channel_never_shows_to_a_guest` |
| Live channel signals (UI only, no DB): unread/mention hints, typing | `swarm.rs` plaintext `ChannelNotificationHint` + `TypingIndicator` arms, MLS `Typing` arm | HOL-SEC-015 (2026-09-27): `message_ops::channel_signal_accepted`, the sender passes `live_channel_post_refusal` for that channel AND we can see it; plaintext typing from a blocked identity drops; meeting typing passes on group membership. The hint is still plaintext to the room (J9). Unit: `authz_channel_signals_only_from_a_poster_about_a_channel_we_see`; wiring: `channel_ingest_gates_stay_wired` |
| Voice seat (RAM participant set, and the dial it triggers) | `swarm.rs` plaintext `VoiceChannelJoin`; `voice_handler::handle_envelope_voice_channel_join` (MLS) | HOL-SEC-016 (2026-09-27): `voice_handler::voice_join_refusal`, a member who can SEE the channel and it is a voice channel; meetings by group membership. Unit: `authz_voice_seat_only_for_a_member_who_can_see_the_channel`; wiring: `channel_ingest_gates_stay_wired` |

**Message size (HOL-SEC-011, 2026-09-27):** a body over `MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES` (64 KiB, Dart `kMaxMessageBytes`) fails `verify_message_signature_v2` and gets its own `BackfillSig::Oversized` verdict, so every signed row above (live, push, sync, edits) drops it WHOLE; nothing clips any more (a clipped row fails its signature on re-serve). Meeting chat, which is unsigned, checks `message_body_fits` itself; our own FFI send, edit, caption and meeting paths refuse it (`refuse_oversized_message`). Tests: `message_over_the_size_limit_never_verifies`, `oversized_message_is_dropped_whole_on_every_path`, `push_dm_is_stored_whole_or_dropped_never_clipped`.

**Message date (HOL-SEC-018, 2026-09-27):** a message dated more than `MAX_FUTURE_SKEW_MS` (10 min) past our clock fails the same verification (`message_ts_fits`, `BackfillSig::FutureDated`), so nobody pins a post below later ones; relay auth holds honest clocks to 60 s. Slow mode judges a FRESH post by our receive clock (`message_ops::SlowModeClock`, per node, threaded through `handle_incoming_request`), a replay by its stamp. Tests: `message_dated_past_our_clock_never_verifies`, `slow_mode_judges_fresh_posts_by_our_clock`.

**No unsigned DM reaches the UI (HOL-SEC-013, 2026-09-27):** a decrypted Olm payload that does not parse as an envelope is logged and DROPPED (it used to surface as an unsigned "legacy" DM with no block check); swarm.rs emits `MessageReceived` from the verified DM arm only (`a_dm_reaches_the_ui_only_after_its_signature_verifies`).

**`album_id` on `messages` / `channel_messages` (Part C albums, 2026-09-17):** no new write path; the wire `album` rides the `insert` / `insert_channel_message` rows above and is bound by the same signature, which switches to the v3 payload (`hollow-msg3`, album before text) when an album is present. A present album that is not a hyphenated UUID (`is_album_id_shape`) fails verification, so the whole message is REJECTED, never stored with the field dropped; edit, delete and `lp_set` signatures bind the row's `album_id` like every other extra.

## 2. Gated by content addressing

Bytes are named by their own SHA-256, so tampering is self-detecting and the
sender needs no signature.

| Write | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| `save_asset_blob` (all asset kinds: emote/banner/sticker/gif/avatar/frame/profile; `save_emote_blob` = the emote-kind shorthand) | `emotes::handle_emote_assets` | REQUESTED-ONLY: the hash must be in swarm's `pending_asset_asks` table (a `PendingAsk` recorded when WE sent the request; the table outlives the socket, only its per-connection `asked` sets clear on WS Disconnected, see wiki `emotes` > Asset pull retry) — unsolicited blobs are dropped, so no peer can stuff our DB. The per-blob size cap comes from the RECORDED kind (`AssetKind::recv_cap`), never from anything the sender supplies. Then `decode_asset_bundle` drops any (hash, bytes) pair whose hash ≠ SHA-256(bytes), plus the WebP container check. A requested hash answered with invalid bytes KEEPS its ask and is re-asked from the next holder, but only when the answer came from the device we asked (since 2026-09-05; before, any room member could delete a pending ask by sending garbage for it) |
| Showcase assets | same codec | same |
| Server banner blob (`kind='banner'`, asset-rail Phase 2) | same `handle_emote_assets` path | same requested-only gate (1 MB cap from the recorded kind). The CRDT carries ONLY the hash — `settings["server_banner"]` rides `ServerSettingChanged`, MANAGE_SERVER-gated at author AND ingest (`op_allowed`). The pre-join `server_banner_thumb_b64` on `PublicChannelListResponse` is RAM-only (never a store write), decode-refused over 80 KB |
| Animated server icon blob (`kind='avatar'`, asset-rail follow-up) | same `handle_emote_assets` path | same requested-only gate (512 KB cap from the recorded kind). The CRDT carries ONLY the hash — `settings["server_avatar_anim"]` rides `ServerSettingChanged`, MANAGE_SERVER-gated at author AND ingest. The still icon stays base64 in `settings["server_avatar"]` (unchanged pre-existing path); nothing animated rides pre-join wire paths |
| Server sticker blob (`kind='sticker'`, asset-rail Phase 5) | same `handle_emote_assets` path | same requested-only gate (512 KB cap from the recorded kind). The CRDT carries ONLY the hash — `CrdtPayload::StickerAdded/StickerRemoved`, `MANAGE_EMOTES`-gated at author AND ingest (`op_allowed` validates `op.author`, the 64-hex hash, both ≤32-char control-free labels, and 1..=4096 dims). `MAX_SERVER_STICKERS = 50` enforced at authoring AND apply so replicas converge on the same refusal. No pre-join wire path carries stickers |
| Avatar frame blob (`kind='frame'`, issue #54) | same `handle_emote_assets` path | same requested-only gate (256 KB cap from the recorded kind — the tight EMOTE ceiling, not the rail's 512 KB, because a frame is decoration on every avatar you have ever seen). The PROFILE carries only the ID: `UserProfile.avatar_frame` is `""` / `b:<hue>` / 64-hex, and `social::sanitize_incoming_frame` is the sole validator on ingest. That validator matters more than it looks — the field is plaintext on the `HavenMessage::ProfileUpdate` fallback AND it keys a network PULL, so an unvalidated string would be a request-anything primitive. Anything unrecognised is treated as ABSENT (preserve what we stored), never as a clear, so a malformed field from a future client cannot wipe somebody's frame |
| Animated avatar/banner blob (`kind='profile'`, asset-rail follow-up) | same `handle_emote_assets` path | same requested-only gate (1 MB cap from the recorded kind, which is `image_convert::MAX_PROFILE_ANIM_BYTES` by construction — a test pins the equality so the wire cap and the authoring limit cannot drift). ONE kind covers avatar and banner: they share a replication profile (one of each per person you have ever met) so they share a budget. The PROFILE carries only the hash: `UserProfile.avatar_anim` / `banner_anim` are `""` or 64-hex, and `social::sanitize_incoming_anim` is the sole validator on ingest — same reasoning as `sanitize_incoming_frame`, because the field keys a network PULL. Anything unrecognised is ABSENT (preserve), never a clear. Signed like every profile field since A-D1 phase D (`hollow-profile2`, `crypto_handler.rs` signs the anim refs and the frame), and `ProfileUpdate` rides Olm/MLS only (`Lane::Carried`), so no relay rewrites them in flight |
| Vault / Share chunks | `vault`, `share_handler` | manifest root hash; a vault file rebuilt from shards must hash to its content id before it is decrypted (`pipeline::reconstruct_file`, HOL-SEC-024), and a share takes its manifest once (HOL-SEC-027). See §14 |
| `.hollowpack` import (`api::import_hollowpack`, LOCAL file picker / drag-drop; writes `save_asset_blob` kind `frame`/`profile` + the `owned_art` row) | `hollowpack::verify_pack_file` = the ONE trust boundary, shared with the `hollowpack inspect` CLI | A pack is remote-AUTHORED bytes even though the user picks it locally. Caps before any decode (8 files, 4 MB each, 16 MB total, 20 MB zip, 64 KB manifest); every file's sha256 RECOMPUTED and the whole pack refused on any mismatch; WebP decode with per-role ceilings (frame square and ≤512, avatar/still ≤512, banner/still ≤1200x480 at 2.5:1 (the 2026-09-01 ceilings; older packs at the old exact sizes stay valid because every bound is a ceiling)), animated roles must animate and still roles must not, frames re-pass `validate_frame_centre`; a manifest that disagrees with the bytes about size/dims/animation is REFUSED, never corrected; nothing is written by a manifest-supplied name (files are keyed by hash); bytes are stored AS-IS, never re-encoded (identity is the hash of the processed bytes; the phase-2 credential binds it). Import does not touch the profile |

## 3. Gated by owner identity

| Write | Sites | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| `insert_file_metadata` | 3 sync batches, `sync_handler`, Olm FileHeader, MLS FileHeader, `fetch.rs` | 0.8.5: `file_handler::file_meta_write_allowed` — the UPSERT may only be performed by the identity that owns the card (device→master collapsed). Sync sites go through `file_handler::synced_file_meta` (HOL-SEC-004): the blob's `fid` must equal the `file_id` the item SIGNED, the owner check takes the item's VERIFIED author (never the blob's own `sender`), and the stored sender + message id come from the item. See below. 0.9.4 added the `thumb_b64` column (blurred-placeholder thumbnail): remote-controlled bytes, so EVERY ingest site filters it to `img && len <= FILE_THUMB_MAX_B64_LEN` (32 KB b64) before the write, and the SQL COALESCEs so a thumb-less re-header can't blank a stored one |
| `merge_personal_emote_entry` (personal "Mine" emote rows, sibling convergence, issue #76) | `handle_incoming_request`'s `PersonalEmoteSync` arm | VERIFIED-SELF: `resolver::same_identity(sender, us)` or the whole frame is dropped, so only a user's own devices can write their personal emote set. Then per-row grammar before the merge (`valid_emote_name`, hash empty or 64-hex lowercase, `source` at most 64 chars, `added_at` not negative) and a 512-row cap on the batch; a row failing any of it is skipped, never corrected. The merge is per-name last-write-wins on `added_at` (an empty hash is the removal tombstone), so a replayed older frame changes nothing and the write is idempotent. NO BYTES ride this message: a name whose blob we lack is pulled over the asset rail, which keeps its own requested-only receipt gate (§2). Harness: `personal_emote_sync_from_non_sibling_is_dropped`, `personal_emotes_converge_across_siblings`. |
| `apply_remote_read_marker` (`seen:`/`seen_ts:` app_settings rows, read state across an identity's devices, issue #80) | `handle_incoming_request`'s `ReadMarkers` arm → `NetworkEvent::ReadMarkersReceived` → Dart → FFI `apply_remote_read_markers` | VERIFIED-SELF: `resolver::same_identity(sender, us)` or the frame is dropped and logged (`[HOLLOW-UNREAD] Dropped ReadMarkers from non-self peer`). The write itself is monotonic: a marker whose `ts` is not later than `read_marker_floor(key)` changes nothing, the key must start with `dm:` or `ch:` (anything else is an error, skipped), and the pointer can only land on a row THIS store already holds. A replay is a no-op; the worst a compromised sibling can do is mark our own conversations read. No bytes, no rows inserted. Harness: `read_markers_reach_siblings_on_verify_and_live`; storage: `remote_read_marker_advances_by_time_and_never_regresses`. |
| `peer_auto_dl` (RAM, not the store) | `HavenMessage::AutoDownloadPref` arm | advertised value clamped to 0..=2048 MB; worst case a peer lies about its own preference and we push/skip bytes to THAT peer — no cross-peer effect, cleared on disconnect |
| Media-forwarder stream state: register / allowlist / unregister (RAM, forwarder engine) | `forwarder::dispatch::admit_register` + `admit_owner_op` | `admit_register` pins `origin.peer == Olm-authenticated sender` (with a shared SFrame group key, a spoofed registration would attribute the registrant's pixels to a victim); `admit_owner_op` restricts allowlist changes and unregister to that same owner |
| Media-forwarder INGEST supply (`fwd_ingest_offer`) | `forwarder::dispatch::admit_ingest_offer` | The one place the owner≡ingest binding loosens (feeder election, §9.6): the owner, OR the single peer the owner delegated as `feeder` in its own owner-authored register. Grant is SUPPLY ONLY — auth/unregister stay on `admit_owner_op`, so a feeder can never change who may watch or tear the stream down. Empty `feeder` (every pre-feeder client) reduces to the original owner-only rule exactly. A malicious feeder can only degrade availability: SFrame auth tags make tampered frames undecodable rather than attacker-controlled, and it never holds group keys |
| Embedded (peer) forwarder: accepting a stream at all | `node::embedded_forwarder` expectation gate | A register is admitted ONLY for an `(originator, kind)` this client advertised `fwd_capable` for on a LIVE watch — a peer forwarder only ever forwards a stream its own user is watching |
| Friend DECLINE ingest: `remove_friend` on the requester (`HavenMessage::FriendReject`, async decline 2026-08-29) | `handle_incoming_request`'s `FriendReject` arm | **Attribution, then freshness.** ATTRIBUTION: the reject carries the decliner's OWN master-signed device list (same field, same shape and the same single verification path as `FriendRequest`'s carried list). When present it is REQUIRED to pass `verify_device_list` (signature plus the pubkey to peer_id binding, so the signer IS the master it claims) AND to name the relay-authenticated sender device in `devices` and not in `revoked`; any failure DROPS the frame, with no fallback to the resolver (`if list.is_some()` must not be the bypass). It is then ingested through `ingest_device_list`, so the resolver, the device store and the DM room key agree afterwards, and the master it names becomes the row key. A frame with NO list is a pre-carried-list client and falls back to `resolver::resolve`, which only works once the two have actually met. The list exists because they usually have not: an async decline answers a stranger who was never online with us, so a cold resolver returned the raw DEVICE id while the friend row is MASTER-keyed, and the reject was dropped with `row None` (field-verified on two fresh installs, 2026-08-29). FRESHNESS: the delete then runs only when the reject NAMES the request that row is made of, either `("pending","outgoing",stored)` with `requested_at` `0` or `>= stored`, or `("accepted",_,stored)` with `requested_at != 0` and `>= stored` (the mutual race: both sides auto-converged and the user then declined that same request, so honouring it keeps both sides symmetric instead of leaving one friended and one declined, re-sending `FriendAccept` forever; the mutual converge stamps both rows `MAX(ours, theirs)` so either side can name it). `save_friend` FREEZES `requested_at` on a non-pending row, so a reject replayed after a cancel and re-add carries the OLDER stamp and stays refused, and legacy `requested_at == 0` never reaches the accepted arm. A refused reject logs and returns WITHOUT emitting `FriendRequestRejected`. Companion gates on the same invariant, both in the `FriendAccept` arm: a row that is `declined` refuses the accept (`pending_friend_accepts` is re-seeded at every startup and would otherwise resurrect a tombstone), and since 0.11.1 an accept carries the `requested_at` of the request it answers (`FriendAccept { requested_at: Option<i64> }`, `#[serde(default)]`, omitted when unknown), and a stamp OLDER than the stored row is refused with a log and no store write: that copy is one the relay parked for a room the requester had left (the accept used to route first-match into `inbox:B` after the requester left it, and the relay replays a parked direct frame on the next join of that room, which is the re-add after a removal) or one a mailbox replayed, and after a remove and re-add it is not consent (CI 2026-09-06, `readd_while_online_requires_fresh_consent`). A bare accept is a pre-0.11.1 sender and stays honoured while a row exists; equal or newer stamps pass, so the idempotent redelivery of the SAME accept still converges. With NO row the accept answers nothing: every removal (`handle_remove_friend` and the `FriendRemove` arm alike) writes the KV tombstone `friend_removed:{master}` (`social::removed_key`) before deleting the row, and an accept for a master with no row and a tombstone is refused stamped or not (the direct copy plus the drain resend are two deliveries, and a removal that lands between them used to recreate the friendship on the remover alone; seen 9 of 48 under load 2026-09-06). The tombstone is never cleared: a re-add writes a row and the row rule takes over. Sender side, the accept now routes into the deterministic `dm_room_code` room, and the queue drain re-sends only for a master whose row is still `accepted` (`holds_accepted_friend`), so a sibling-side removal cannot leave a live queue entry. Harness: `stale_friend_accept_replayed_after_readd_is_dropped`, wire KAT in `friend_request_wire_stays_backward_compatible`. The reject stays plaintext on the relay exactly as before: the mailbox holds it for the TTL, which is retention of what the relay already sees, not new exposure. See `project_pending_joins_async_friending`. |

**Why this one needed a gate.** `insert_file_metadata` is an UPSERT keyed on
`file_id` that deliberately overwrites name/ext/mime/size/dimensions, so the FCM
background-fetch node's minimal placeholder gets filled in later. Without an
owner check, the same overwrite was reachable by any authenticated peer sending
a FileHeader carrying someone else's `file_id` — a display-spoofing primitive
(relabel an attachment). It was also reachable through sync: the item's v2
signature binds `file_id`, **not** the `file_meta` blob hanging off the item, so
a batch that passes the backfill check can still carry a forged file name, size
and sender.

## 4. Gated by the CRDT author (not the transport sender)

**The one gate: `ServerState::ingest_remote` (design E, 2026-09-27).** Per op it
runs the author's signature (`CrdtOp::verify_author`) and the clock bound
(`hlc::MAX_DRIFT_MS` ahead of the wall clock), then the FOLD: the state is a pure
function of every retained signed op, folded in HLC order, each judged by the
permission matrix (`op_allowed`, against `op.author`) at its own point. An op newer
than the log's tail applies at the tail; an older one rebuilds from the anchor
(section 17). Every remote ingest site calls it:

1. `swarm::apply_remote_crdt_op`, the plaintext `CrdtOpBroadcast`, which the
   gossip re-flood and the `ServerJoinResolved` arm also funnel through.
2. `sync_handler::handle_envelope_crdt_op`, the MLS `MessageEnvelope::CrdtOp`.
3. `crdt::sync::merge_ops_with`, which covers every `SyncResponse` path, and only
   ops that entered the log reach `insert_crdt_op` (`persist_admitted_ops`).

(The Olm-fallback CRDT arms are ignored since HOL-SEC-019.)

Until 2026-09-03 this section claimed `op_allowed` ran at every ingest. It did
not: `merge_ops` applied every payload except `ServerDeleted` with no check at
all, and the Olm-fallback single-op path had none either. On top of that an op
carried no signature, so `author` was a string anyone could write, and the
check that did run was asking whether the person the attacker NAMED would have
been allowed. That is the 2026-09 audit's CRDT-1.

**Signature rule.** `CrdtOp.auth` is `{sig, pk}` over
`hollow-crdt1:{server_id}:{physical_ms}:{counter}:{actor}:{author}:{payload_json}`,
signed with the MASTER key (author and the `members`/`roles` maps are
master-keyed). `verify_author` requires that `pk` DERIVES `author`
(`peer_id_from_pubkey_protobuf`) and that the signature verifies. An op with no
`auth` is REJECTED, with no tolerance branch and no legacy phase: a permissive
window on a public repo is a published hole.

| Write | Gate |
|---|---|
| `insert_crdt_op`, server state mutations | `ServerState::ingest_remote` at every ingest site. The signature binds `op.author` to a key; `op_allowed` validates that author, never the peer that delivered it, at the op's own point in the fold |
| Any op stamped in the far future | `ingest_remote` refuses `physical_ms > now + MAX_DRIFT_MS`. `AdminLwwReg::merge` is pure HLC LWW, so `u64::MAX` on a register would win every later comparison and lock the field permanently (audit CRDT-2). `Hlc::witness`'s drift check only ever protected our own clock |
| `ServerCreated` on a server we already hold | allowed ONLY when the state has NO Owner (a join skeleton replaying an op log) AND `op.author == owner_peer_id`. The real owner's own re-send is admitted and `apply_op` makes it a no-op. It used to return `true` unconditionally, which let any member mint itself Owner of anyone's server |
| `save_server_state` after a `ServerStateSnapshot` | `clamp_future_hlcs` pulls every LWW register back to `now + MAX_DRIFT_MS` on adoption. A snapshot is taken wholesale from ONE responder, so its registers are only as honest as that peer; clamping bounds a poisoned one instead of letting it lock the field. Whether to trust a snapshot's CONTENT at all is a separate open design question |
| `delete_server_state` | `ServerDeleted` tombstone, owner-author validated at EVERY ingest, now via `op_allowed`'s own `sender_role == Owner` arm inside `admit_remote_op`. The two ad hoc tombstone filters in the sync handlers were removed rather than duplicated |
| `ChannelVisibilityLabelsChanged` / `ChannelPostingLabelsChanged` / `ChannelGrantSet` / `ChannelGrantRevoked` (issue #32) | `MANAGE_CHANNELS` on `op.author` at author AND ingest — same arm as the other channel-property ops |
| `insert_crdt_op` + `MemberAdded` carried by `ServerJoinResolved` (parked joins, rung 1, 2026-08-29) | NOT a second ingest path: the arm parses ONE `CrdtOp` and hands it to `apply_remote_crdt_op`, the very function extracted from the plaintext `CrdtOpBroadcast` arm, so it inherits `op_allowed` on `op.author` (never the publisher), `insert_crdt_op`, the single re-flood and the payload's own event. Two gates run BEFORE it: we must already hold `server_states[server_id]` (a resolution for a server we are not in is nothing to us), and `resolver::resolve(sender)` must be a CURRENT CRDT member — a non-member cannot resolve anything, and anyone in the room can publish on a topic. `join_resolutions` is max-wins on `requested_at`, so an older copy replayed out of the three-day ring can never undo a newer answer |
| `LabelAssigned` / `LabelUnassigned` | self-toggle allowed ONLY for an EXISTING label with `access == false` (`ServerState::can_self_toggle_label`, shared by `handle_label_op` and `op_allowed` so the gates cannot drift); ACCESS labels gate channels, so self-assign would be privilege escalation — they require `MANAGE_ROLES`, and an UNKNOWN label_id fails closed. `LabelUpdated.access` is `Option<bool>` on the wire: `None` (a client that predates the field) PRESERVES the stored flag, so an old client's recolor can never silently demote an access label back to self-service |

## 5. Gated by a master signature

| Write | Gate |
|---|---|
| Device-list ingest | `verify_device_list` (master-signed, monotonic version, revocation tombstones) **AND `device_list_binds_sender`** — the delivering device must be named in the signed `devices` (or BE `master_peer_id`, the legacy keystone) and not tombstoned, or the whole list is DROPPED. Applies to the friend path and the sibling merge alike, and to every call site through the one function. CRYPTO-1 (2026-09-03): a signed list is public the moment it is announced, so `verify_device_list` alone says who WROTE it and nothing about who handed it over. The ingest used to fold the delivering device into the resolver and the persisted set on the theory that delivery proves membership — replaying a victim's own announce from any other socket then bound that socket to the victim's master, which routes the target's next DM fan-out to the attacker, satisfies `key_exchange_device_unauthorized` on the same poisoned set, and resolves CRDT role checks through it. Nothing is folded in beyond the signed array now. The sibling path is the worst case and gets the same rule: our OWN list is the one strangers are most likely to hold, and that path hands the sender our accepted-friend list and asks it to backfill our DMs |
| Olm key exchange | `REQUIRE_SIGNED_KEY_EXCHANGE` — device-signed bundle/request, recipient + freshness bound |
| Async friend request — carried bundle + device list (`FriendRequest` optional fields, 2026-08-28) | The carried `SignedDeviceList` rides the SAME `verify_device_list` + `device_list_binds_sender` gate as any device-list ingest (new SITE, unchanged master-signature/monotonic-version/revocation checks), stated at the CALL SITE too since 2026-09-03: this inbox is stranger-reachable, so a list that does not name its deliverer is a DROPPED request, matching the `ServerJoinRequest` and `FriendReject` arms. The carried Olm bundle is gated by `verify_carried_bundle` — device signature over the DOMAIN-SEPARATED `hollow-carried-keybundle:` payload (distinct prefix + `to_master` third segment, so it can never verify as a live bundle or the reverse), sender device present and un-revoked in that master-signed list, `to_master == our master`, freshness by `MAX_CARRIED_BUNDLE_AGE_SECS` (7d) NOT the live 300 s rule; REJECT-on-fail, an absent signature is never a bypass. Only store write it gates is the `app_settings` KV record `friendreq_in:{master}` (no friends-table schema change). The outbound-session bootstrap re-runs `verify_carried_bundle` at USE time (the row sat on disk since receipt) and is additionally glare-gated: it builds a session only when the requester is offline AND session-less, else it defers to the live key exchange. The relay mailbox that carries the request holds ciphertext only and is read-gated by the roster fold (`inbox_owner_by_roster`: the joining device must be a member of the roster the relay holds for that master, section 20); availability, never authority. See `project_pending_joins_async_friending`. |
| Parked server join — carried device list (`ServerJoinRequest.device_list`, rung 1, 2026-08-29) | Same `verify_device_list` gate as any device-list ingest (a new SITE, unchanged master-signature / monotonic-version / revocation checks), plus the sender binding: the relay-stamped sender device must be IN `devices` and NOT in `revoked`. Only then does `member_master` come from `list.master_peer_id` and the list get ingested through `ingest_device_list`. PRESENT-but-BAD is a DROP (no reject, no event, no member row) — `if list.is_some()` must never be the bypass; ABSENT falls back to `resolver::resolve`, which is byte-for-byte the pre-2026-08-29 path. This is load-bearing rather than cosmetic because a request read out of the `~join` ring comes from somebody the member has NEVER been online with, so the resolver returns the raw DEVICE id: without the list the ban check, the private/cap gates and the `MemberAdded` key would all be computed against a device id that matches nothing. Ring exposure: the parked copy stripped `twitch_proof_json` until 2026-09-03, because the old proof named a Twitch ACCOUNT and any socket in the room can `topic_catchup` the ring. A `T_TWITCH_FOLLOW` credential names a channel id, an age bucket and a subscription tier and nothing else, so it rides the ring copy and the same offline gate runs on it (see the Twitch row below). A parked request that carries NO credential still takes no action rather than writing a sticky `twitch_required` refusal into the ring |
| Parked server join, carried KeyPackage (`ServerJoinRequest.key_package`, rung 2, 2026-09-05) | `swarm.rs` `ServerJoinRequest` parked branch: the package is queued into `pending_mls_key_packages` only AFTER the parked admission has passed every gate in the row above (signed-device-list attribution, ban, Twitch credential, owner-verify, private, NSFW consent, member cap) and only when `MlsManager::key_package_identity(kp) == peer_str`, the relay-authenticated sender device the list attributed; a mismatch is DROPPED with `[HOLLOW-SECURITY] Dropping carried KeyPackage from {peer}: credential identity {id} is not the attributed sender device`. A package names the leaf it wants seated, so one naming another id is asking the admitter to seat somebody who never passed a gate. The credential is a CLAIM until `add_members_batch` validates the package, and the leaf identity IS the credential, so a member can only ever seat its OWN device. The Welcome for an absent device is addressed into the server room by NAME (`send_message_to_peer_in_room`), the relay buffers it under the device id, FIFO behind the snapshot and the SyncResponse. Ring exposure unchanged: a KeyPackage is public material by construction. Harness: `parked_join_key_package_survives_a_restart_before_the_welcome`, `parked_join_completes_with_zero_overlap`. |
| `save_profile` | 0.8.5: `profile_signing_payload` / `verify_profile_signature`. See below |
| MLS leaf seating: live `MlsKeyPackage`, meeting knock (HOL-SEC-017, 2026-09-27) | The package is refused unless `MlsManager::key_package_identity` equals the device the frame came from, BEFORE anything is queued (the parked-join branch above already did this), so a member or guest cannot be seated under another name. The device is the relay's word: credential binding, who may commit and Welcome validation are class D design. Guard: `authz_key_package_must_name_its_sending_device` |
| MLS commit catch-up ingest (`MlsCommitCatchup`, epoch-race fix 2026-08-07) | sender must be a server member; every frame revalidated by OpenMLS through the SAME `handle_mls_commit_frame` path as a live `MlsCommit` (epoch guard, group-member leaf signature, eviction check); additionally each frame must be exactly `own_epoch + 1` — a gapped/garbage frame is refused BEFORE it can reach the drop-group recovery, making catch-up strictly safer than the broadcast path it supplements; ≤16 frames per message |
| MLS epoch hints (`SyncRequest.mls_epoch` / `MlsEpochProbe`) | member-gated (`handle_epoch_hint`); a hint can NEVER drop a group (that would be a remote group-reset primitive) — a low hint only triggers authority-gated commit-replay/re-add service (10 s per-peer cooldown), a high hint only a throttled self-probe to the authority (under standing, when our own election names nobody, the first other member in `heal_order`, #135: a probe only asks). Of two forks the authority's copy stays, or, where our tree names no other authority, the HEAL LEADER's (`heal_leader`, only where our election names no responder: first in `heal_order` = CRDT standing among unbanned members that may see the channel, online with a bound unrevoked leaf in our tree or the online owner, us always counted); the leader computes none, so it takes no repair. Repair acceptance: `may_repair_held_leaf` (swarm `MlsKeyPackageRequest` arm) = `may_repair_our_leaf` OR the requester is our heal leader AND we asked it within 120 s (`LeadBook::heal_asked`, stamped only by a probe past our election or a fork-stay deferral): unasked, a tally-less higher-ranked member would repair a healthy group's stand-in (review F1). The stamp alone is not the defence (a forged digest in a probe earns a fork-stay deferral); the empty-election condition is: with any responder named, the heal order stands in for nobody. The cap filter in `heal_order` only keeps a capped master from being the probe target (a capped master declares, so the tree election names it first and `heal_leader` is None before the filter). Units `both_sides_of_a_fork_name_one_heal_leader`, `review_a_tally_less_admin_never_repairs_the_stand_in_without_a_fork`, `the_heal_ask_is_stamped_by_a_probe_past_the_election_and_by_a_fork_stay`, `an_epoch_probe_asks_the_owner_the_election_leaves_out`; harness `t16_*` |

**Why profiles needed signing.** `ProfileUpdate` has no sender field, so
attribution came from the transport — sound. But `ProfileRelay` exists so a peer
can hand us a cached copy of a THIRD party's profile, and it carries its own
`source_peer_id`. That field was attacker-chosen, the frame is plaintext, and
the only gate was an `updated_at` comparison the same attacker controls: one
`ProfileRelay { source_peer_id: <victim>, display_name: "Admin", updated_at:
i64::MAX }` overwrote any identity's name and avatar permanently, and no genuine
update could ever beat that timestamp again.

Now the owner signs, relayers forward the signature, receivers verify:
- The payload binds exactly the fields `ProfileRelay` carries (subject peer_id,
  updated_at, display name, status, about me, twitch, avatar HASH). Binding
  anything a relayer does not forward (banner, showcase) would make every relay
  fail to verify.
- Fields are **length-prefixed into a digest**, not `:`-joined — display name /
  status / about-me are free text and would otherwise be able to impersonate the
  next field's boundary.
- The signed **avatar hash is stored** (`profile_avatar_hash`), not re-derived:
  announces are light (hash only, no bytes), so a relayer's cached blob can lag
  the owner's. The receiver verifies the signature over the carried hash, then
  separately checks the bytes against it and drops **only the avatar** on a
  mismatch — a stale relayer still relays valid text, a swapping relayer is
  caught.
- `ProfileRelay` ingest: REQUIRED signature, and over-long fields are REJECTED
  rather than truncated (verifying a clamped copy would check a string the
  signer never signed, and truncation would make our stored copy diverge from
  the signature we forward on the next hop).
- `ProfileUpdate` ingest: the signature is **REQUIRED there too**, and the
  profile fields are not stored without it. The tempting argument is that the
  sender IS the subject on that path (no `source_peer_id` to lie about), so an
  absent signature cannot spoof anyone. That covers a malicious PEER and misses
  a malicious RELAY: the plaintext `ProfileUpdate` fallback used for DM peers
  and pre-MLS servers is an unencrypted JSON body the relay can rewrite in
  flight. The gate covers the profile FIELDS only — the sender's device list is
  ingested first and independently of this result, because it stands on its own
  two gates (`verify_device_list` + `device_list_binds_sender`) and needs
  nothing from the profile, while the profile signature needs IT: the signature
  verifies against `resolve(sender)` and the ingest is what teaches the resolver
  that mapping, so checking the profile first would refuse every first-contact
  announce from a device whose id differs from its master. A profile-less node
  also signs nothing at all (`own_profile_proof` returns `None` with no stored
  row) while still announcing a device list, and that announce is what collapses
  its devices into one online identity. `saved` also gates the member-list
  display-name write, or the spoof just lands one layer up.
- Announces sign on the fly when the stored row predates 0.8.5
  (`own_profile_proof`) — without that, requiring the signature would blank
  every upgraded user at their peers until they happened to edit their profile.
- **Carried profile on a friend request** (`FriendRequest.carried_profile`,
  2026-08-28) is a NEW SITE on this SAME gate, not a new gate: a stranger's
  request now carries the sender's own signed profile so the incoming card
  renders a real name instead of a raw peer id (a stranger has never sent us a
  `ProfileUpdate`, and in the offline case is gone before it can push one).
  `social::store_carried_profile` runs the identical rule as `ProfileRelay`
  ingest — REQUIRED `verify_profile_signature`, over-long fields REJECTED (not
  truncated), persisted via `save_profile` behind the verify — and ADDS a
  sender-binding check (`resolve(source_peer_id) == request sender's master`) so
  a sender can carry only its OWN identity's profile, never a captured third
  party's. LIGHT: the avatar HASH rides (in the proof), never the bytes. A bad
  or absent signature drops ONLY the profile; the friend request itself still
  lands (the sig is verified, never logged-and-stored — `if sig.is_some()` would
  be the bypass). Old senders omit the field; old receivers ignore it.

## 6. Local-only — not reachable from a remote frame

Listed so a future sweep does not have to re-derive that they are safe:
`set_peer_verified` / `remove_peer_verified` (`api/verification.rs`, user
action), `add_personal_emote` / `remove_personal_emote` (`api/emotes.rs`),
`add_personal_sticker` / `remove_personal_sticker` /
`remove_personal_sticker_pack` / `rename_personal_sticker_pack`
(`api/stickers.rs`, user action on a local-only vault),
`upsert_conference` / `delete_conference` (`api/conference.rs`),
`set_olm_key_pin` (`security_alerts`, local bookkeeping), `mark_file_complete` /
`mark_chunk_received` (local download path), `prune_*` (local retention),
`upsert_pending_join` / `delete_pending_join` (parked joins, rung 1 — the row
records OUR OWN outgoing request and is written only by our own join /
timeout / discard paths and by `handle_join_refused`, which acts solely on the
in-memory entry we minted and is nonce-gated: a refusal naming an ask other
than the one we currently have pending is ignored, which matters because the
refusal is now BUFFERED by the relay and replays on our next join of that room
(normally the user asking again). A remote frame can flip an existing row to
`rejected`, or DELETE it when the reason is interactive (`nsfw_confirm:` /
`twitch_required:` are questions, and a persisted question would re-open its
dialog at every boot), but it can never CREATE one — so it cannot make us
join, rejoin a room, or store anything for a server we never asked about).

**`keep_redeem_code` (`api/shop.rs`, the `hollow://redeem/<code>` deep link,
2026-09-02)** is the one local write a REMOTE AUTHOR can trigger without a relay
frame: a link on a web page (the shop's thank-you page, or anybody's) opens the
app and hands it a string. It is a keep-only list for the phase-2 support
credential, so the gate is shape plus budget, nothing else: the code must match
`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,128}$`, the table holds at most 64 rows (the 65th is refused
with a visible error, never silently dropped or rotated), a repeat is a no-op
(`INSERT OR IGNORE`), and nothing is fetched, sent or announced. Store builds
never reach it: the Dart handler treats the link as unrecognised when
`ShopAvailability.available` is false. The shop client's fetches
(`fetch_shop_catalog`, `fetch_shop_art`) are READS against a pinned origin and
write nothing: art bytes are refused unless their SHA-256 matches the requested
hash and they are never put on the asset rail (owned art enters ONLY through
`import_hollowpack` above).

**`save_channel_message` (`api/storage.rs`) is dead code** — a Dart-callable FFI
with a wrapper in `storage_service.dart` and no UI call site. It inserts a
channel row with no signature. Harmless today (Dart can only write to its own
DB), but it is the one path that could reintroduce unsigned rows, so it should
be deleted rather than left as a loaded gun.

## 7. File SERVING gate + guest file ingest (0.9.1)

Serving is a READ, but it belongs in this enumeration: it was the one
remote-reachable disclosure path with **no gate at all** — any authenticated
peer that learned a `file_id` (guests see them in plaintext public messages)
could pull ANY file we hold, DM attachments included.

| Path | Gate |
|---|---|
| `HavenMessage::FileRequest` serving (`swarm.rs`, before the disk read) | Blocklist refused. Then by the stored row's context: `dm` → requester must be the conversation counterparty or our own sibling (`same_identity`); `channel` → `is_member(resolved master)` OR `is_channel_public(cid)` (effective — voice never public); unknown context / missing server state fails CLOSED. `requester_is_member` also picks the header transport: Olm `FileHeader` for members/DM parties, plaintext `HavenMessage::PublicFileHeader` for non-members on public channels (they may hold no Olm session; the content is public — the relay already sees it). **Negative answer (2026-09-05):** when the gate has PASSED and we still cannot serve (`expired_at` set, no `disk_path`, or the read fails) the responder answers `HavenMessage::FileUnavailable { file_id, reason }` (`gone` / `expired`) instead of going silent, so the asker can rotate to another holder and the card can say why. It is sent ONLY after the same gate the positive answer passes, and only when a row exists: a blocked requester, an unknown `file_id` and a non-entitled requester all keep getting SILENCE, because answering "unknown" to strangers would let anyone tell "this device holds a row for X" (silence) from "it does not" (an answer), which is a membership leak. The requester's retry sweep covers silence. Harness: `file_unavailable_never_answers_a_non_entitled_requester` (the stranger and the entitled DM party ask for the SAME file in one run), `dm_file_request_gets_honest_gone_answer_when_holder_lost_bytes`. |
| `PublicFileHeader` ingest (registers the stream decrypt key → bytes land on disk → `insert_file_metadata`) | REQUESTED-ONLY, mirroring the asset rail: accepted only when `pending_public_file_requests` holds the file_id with a matching server, < 120 s old, and the server is in `guest_rooms` (entry removed on receipt; map cleared on WS disconnect). An unsolicited plaintext header would otherwise register a decrypt key and let a stranger stream arbitrary bytes onto our disk. Armed exclusively by `NodeCommand::RequestPublicFile` |
| Stored channel content serving + replication: `ChannelSyncRequest` (carried over Olm, `swarm.rs`; it has no MLS or Olm twin any more, and the channel probes and their responses are GONE since 2026-09-27, HOL-SEC-012: no sender since 0.10, and a response from anyone made a member send it the channel's per-author watermarks in the clear; guard `retired_channel_probes_are_refused_at_parse`), the `FileRequest` responder's channel arm (`swarm.rs`), `replicate_channel_file_full` and `channel_holder_candidates` (`file_handler.rs`, the holders `file_asks` walks AFTER the poster, whose devices it asks first without this check; the poster's responder gates) (2026-09-05) | `ChannelSyncRequest` gates on `crypto_handler::sync_partner` (not revoked, `is_member(master)`, `can_see_channel`): its page carries deleted rows' text, so a public channel's non-member is refused too and reads the guest page (HOL-SEC-194, section 60). The others gate on `crypto_handler::channel_readable_by`, which is `(is_member(master) OR is_channel_public(cid)) AND can_see_channel(master, cid)` on the requester's MASTER, asked FIRST, and a refusal RETURNS. Membership is the first rung because an unknown peer's role resolves to plain `Member` and would pass the visibility ladder for every `Everyone` channel (a stranger in the relay room, whose code is the invite, could pull any non-public channel). Unit: `authz_channel_served_only_to_a_member_who_can_read_it`. The guest page (`PublicChannelSyncRequest`) comes from `get_visible_channel_messages_before`, which leaves deleted rows out (HOL-SEC-014: their text is evidence for members, not for strangers; `guest_pages_leave_deleted_messages_out`). The public carve-out keeps guests reading public channels exactly as before (ring + `PublicChannelMessage`; the `FileRequest` arm already pairs membership with `public_ok`, and gives a non-member nothing for a file whose post is deleted, HOL-SEC-194). Why it matters: the per-channel MLS subgroup protects LIVE traffic only, so backfill was the way round it: a plain Member could pull an Admin-only channel's whole history, its file headers with their AES keys, and (full replication streamed to every member device) its bytes. Membership is not entitlement; the channel ladder is. Harness: `restricted_channel_history_and_files_never_reach_a_non_qualifier` (both rungs honesty-proved). |
| `FileUnavailable` ingest, 2026-09-05 (`node/file_asks.rs::handle_file_unavailable`, dispatched from the `HavenMessage::FileUnavailable` arm in `swarm.rs`) | The ONLY store write this frame can cause is `mark_file_expired` on OUR OWN row, and it takes BOTH gates. (a) The ASKED-SET rule: the frame changes nothing at all unless `pending_file_asks[file_id].asked` contains the sender's DEVICE id, i.e. a device WE chose through our own targeting (a DM counterparty's device, one of our own siblings, or a server member that passes `channel_readable_by`). An unasked device can neither steer the walk nor delete the ask, exactly as an unasked peer's `missing` list cannot move an asset-rail ask. A hard drop, not a log line. (b) `reason: "expired"` is VERIFIED LOCALLY before it is believed: the row must be a CHANNEL row, our own CRDT `retention_files` for that server must parse to a day count, and the row's own `created_at` must be past that window. Only then is the row marked, and only what the retention sweep would have marked at its next tick. Anything else is downgraded to `gone`, which touches no row at all: a member cannot expire someone else's file by lying, and a DM row or a permanent policy has no window to be past. Harness: `file_unavailable_from_unasked_device_changes_nothing`, `expired_answer_is_verified_locally_before_marking_our_row` (case A fresh row stays unmarked, case B genuinely past the window is marked). |

Guest-side sync/live `file_meta` blobs are display-only and never DB writes; the
v2 item signature binds `file_id`, not the blob, so receivers require
`fm.fid == item.file_id` (same reasoning as the §3 owner guard). Harness
coverage: `file_request_gate_refuses_stranger_and_serves_guest_public`.


## 8. Transport temp files + the update channel (0.10.2)

| Write | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Stream reassembly temp file (`.ws_recv_{id}.tmp`, `.webrtc_recv_{id}.tmp`) | `ws_stream_transfer::parse_id` (Rust, WS relay lane), `wire_transfer_id.dart::parseWireTransferId` (Dart, WebRTC lane) | The 64-byte wire id NAMES the file, so the parser is the gate: `[A-Za-z0-9:_-]` only (own ids are 32-hex, `hex:index`, `link_<code>`), anything else drops the frame before a path exists. File and shard first frames open only under a 64-hex stream id (`ws_stream_transfer::is_stream_id`, Dart `rtcStreamIdFits`); link snapshots and share chunks keep their own shapes. Before 0.10.2 the raw id reached `format!` unchecked; on Windows a `/../` id walked out of the files dir (Win32 collapses `..` lexically), POSIX was safe only because `.ws_recv_` is not a directory. Precondition was an established peer, impact create/truncate/delete of any `*.tmp` path. Same family as `safe_file_name()` (section 6 of `feedback_sender_controlled_filename_sanitization`) |
| Update manifest (`fetch_version_manifest`) | `api/updater.rs::verify_manifest_signature` | `manifest.json.sig` (base64 Ed25519 over the manifest's EXACT bytes) must `verify_strict` against a key in `MANIFEST_SIGNING_PUBKEYS`; the sidecar rides the same URL + cache-buster. The download host can serve bytes, it cannot mint a signature. Dart then treats only a strictly NEWER `latest` as an update (`version_compare.dart`), so a replayed old signed manifest is not a downgrade lever. Signing: `rust/hollow_manifest` + `scripts/sign_manifest.ps1`, key outside the repo |
| Update zip on disk, then extracted into the app dir (`download_update` + `apply_update`) | `api/updater.rs::download_inner` | https only; SHA-256 accumulated while streaming and compared to the `sha256_<platform>` field of the SIGNED manifest before the file is kept (mismatch, cancel or any error deletes it and ends the stream with `DownloadProgress.error`). An entry without a checksum for the platform is refused in Dart before the first byte. `extract_zip_to` keeps its own path-traversal rejection |

## 9. Artist shop: packs and support credentials (2026-09-02)

| Write | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| `.hollowpack` import: asset-rail blobs (`save_asset_blob`) + `owned_art` rows | `api/network.rs::import_hollowpack` / `import_hollowpack_bytes` → `hollowpack::verify_pack` | LOCAL-ONLY trigger (a file the person picked, dropped, or the redeem path fetched from the pinned shop origin), never a remote frame. The pack is verified WHOLE before a byte lands: caps (8 files, 4 MB each, 16 MB total, 64 KB manifest), every SHA-256 RECOMPUTED from the bytes (the manifest's claim is never trusted), decoded dimensions against the role's ceiling, animated/still against the role, the see-through-centre gate re-applied to frames. Bytes are stored AS-IS under the recomputed hash (content addressing, §2). Importing never touches the profile. Wiki `hollowpack.md`. |
| `user_profiles.support_creds` (a peer's support credentials) | `social::save_incoming_profile` → `support_creds::sanitize_incoming_support_creds(raw, master)` (both ingest paths: `handle_envelope_profile_update` and the plaintext `HavenMessage::ProfileUpdate` arm pass the RAW field) | The ONE validator. Raw field over 16 KB or not a JSON array = ABSENT (preserve). Each entry: shape (`t` known, `item` 64-hex, `parts` sorted/unique/1..32 and `item == sha256(parts)`), `issuer_sig` under the PINNED root (`verify_strict`), `key_sig` under the issuer over `t||item||period||key`, RSA-3072 key parses, then the blind signature verifies over the credential message built from THIS profile's RESOLVED master peer id. Invalid entries dropped in silence, deduped by item, capped (3 item + 1 supporter), survivors RE-SERIALIZED so nothing a sender appended reaches the row. A transplant (minted for another identity) fails the last link. NOT part of the profile signature by design (the entry binds the identity itself). Harness: `support_credential_replicates_and_transplant_is_dropped`. Wiki `support_credentials.md`. |
| `user_profiles.support_creds` — the FIELD SIGNATURE, REQUIRED (2026-09-03) | `social::gated_support_creds`, called from `save_incoming_profile` ahead of the entry validator above; signed at every announce site by `crypto_handler::sign_support_creds` (`handle_update_profile`'s two frames, `send_own_profile_inner`, the swarm backfill announce) | The field sits OUTSIDE `profile_signing_payload` because each entry binds the identity by itself — which covers forgery and misses DENIAL: on the plaintext `HavenMessage::ProfileUpdate` fallback a relay rewrites the field to `""` in flight, the profile signature still verifies, and `Some("")` is the holder's explicit clear on every receiver. So the field carries its own master Ed25519 signature over `"hollow-support-creds-sig/v1" \|\| len(peer):u16 BE \|\| master \|\| updated_at:i64 BE \|\| field` (`support_creds::support_creds_sig_message`), verified with the profile's own `profile_pk` (re-derived to the master, so a real signature by somebody else is not one by this identity). TWO outcomes and no third: a VALID signature applies the field through the entry validator; anything else — no signature, a signature over another field or another timestamp — is a REJECT of the FIELD (treated as ABSENT, preserve what we stored, logged once per master, never a clear; `if sig.is_some()` is not the bypass). `Some("")` is covered by the same rule, because the explicit clear is the single most useful thing for a relay to forge. Additionally the field is ignored whenever the announce's `updated_at` is STRICTLY OLDER than the stored row's, signature or not: `save_profile`'s freshness guard tolerated 24 hours of backdating until session 45 (§36), so replaying a genuine older announce from before a purchase would otherwise clear the marks. SHOP-1 (2026-09-03): this used to be a per-master PIN instead — an unsigned field applied until that master had been seen signing once, `user_profiles.support_creds_signed`. A relay that stripped the field AND its signature from the first announce it ever carried for a master kept the pin unset forever, held that master on the unsigned branch, and could then write the field itself; a baseline learned from the network is worth nothing against an attacker present for the baseline. The pin column stays (no schema churn) and NOTHING consults it; both accessors are gone. Unit: `unsigned_support_creds_is_refused_and_preserved`, `signed_empty_support_creds_clears`, `unsigned_empty_support_creds_does_not_clear`. Harness: `stripped_support_creds_never_clears_a_pinned_mark`. |
| Twitch-gated server admission — `MemberAdded` + the whole join payload (2026-09-03) | `swarm.rs` join gate → `twitch::validate_follow_credential(entry_json, member_master, settings)`, on the LIVE request and on the parked ring copy alike | Replaces `twitch::validate_proof`, which parsed the JOINER's own unsigned JSON (`twitch_user_id`, `followed_at`, `is_subscribed`) and never contacted anybody: a modified client wrote whatever it liked and walked in. The joiner now presents a blind-signed `T_TWITCH_FOLLOW` credential and the owner verifies it OFFLINE: the chain to the PINNED root, `t == 4`, `parts == [broadcaster_id, age_bucket, tier]` under the follow recipe, `period` inside its 90-day window (current or previous), and the RSA blind signature over the credential message built from the JOINER's resolved MASTER — so a credential lifted off another identity or another channel fails. Then the policy: `parts[0] == settings.channel_id`, `bucket >= min_follow_days` (the bucket grid is 0/1/3/7/14/30/60/90/180/365, so a setting rounds UP to the next step), and `tier != "0"` when `require_sub`. An OLD-shape JSON proof is refused outright with `twitch::OLD_PROOF_SENTENCE`, never half-trusted. Because the credential names a channel, a bucket and a tier and nothing that identifies the joiner's Twitch account, the `~join` ring copy now CARRIES it (`sync_handler::deposit_parked_join`) and the rung-1 strip is lifted — a Twitch-gated server parks and resolves like every other one. The owner no longer mints `TwitchUsernameChanged` from a join: there is no handle in a follow credential, and no new client renders one. Harness: `twitch_follow_gate_accepts_bucket_and_refuses_the_rest`, `parked_twitch_gated_join_carries_the_credential_and_leaks_no_identity`. |
| `support_creds_own` rows + our own `support_creds` (redeem) | `api/shop.rs::redeem_code` | LOCAL-ONLY trigger (a code the person typed or a `hollow://redeem/<code>` link they clicked, behind `shopAvailableProvider`; the link only KEEPS a shape-checked code, 64-row cap). The shop's chain is verified against the pinned root BEFORE the code is spent; the finished entry is verified again exactly as a viewer will; only then stored and republished. The pinned `SHOP_ORIGIN`, no redirects, bounded bodies (256 KB JSON, 40 MB pack). |
| `redeem_codes` (kept codes) | `api/shop.rs::keep_redeem_code` from the deep link | Shape check (`valid_redeem_code`: 8..128 of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`) + 64-row cap; nothing reads the code until the person presses Redeem. |

Shop side (Node, `anonlisten-sites/shop/src/lib/server/redeem.js`, the audit's M4 gate): `isKeyRefused` then `isKeyBurned` BEFORE `activateKey`; the blinded message is signed in memory first; `burnKey` + `incrementRedeemed` are the authority; the shop stores only the key's SHA-256 and the pack token's SHA-256. Rate buckets per code hash and shop-wide, never per IP.

## 10. Call signaling transport (2026-09-03)

| Write | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| `NetworkEvent::CallSignal` (the ringing UI, the peer connection it negotiates, and the SFrame media key it installs) | `voice_handler::handle_call_signal_message`, reached ONLY from the `MessageEnvelope::CallSignal` arm of the post-decrypt match in `swarm.rs::handle_incoming_request` | The Olm ratchet IS the gate. 1:1 call signaling used to ride the wire as a bare `HavenMessage::Call*` frame, so the relay read the AES-128-GCM SFrame key out of every CallInvite and CallAccept, read every SDP and ICE candidate, and could inject a forged CallAccept carrying a key it chose (TRANSPORT-1, Critical). The signal now travels as `MessageEnvelope::CallSignal { signal: Box<HavenMessage> }` inside the ciphertext, so `peer_str` is the device the session authenticated. The 17 PLAINTEXT `Call*` arms collapse to one that logs `[HOLLOW-SECURITY] REJECTED plaintext call signal from {peer}` and does nothing; the same envelope arriving over MLS is rejected as well, because over a group it would be readable and forgeable by every other member. The receiver whitelists the 17 variants a second time on the way out, so nothing else can be smuggled inside the envelope. Sender side: no Olm session means the signal is DROPPED and a throttled signed KeyRequest goes out, never a plaintext fallback; delivery targets `pick_online_device` inside the deterministic `dm_room_code(local_master, resolve(target))` (TRANSPORT-2), falling back to the first-match room with a log only when the device is not in the DM room. Harness: `call_invite_never_exposes_sframe_key_to_the_relay`, `plaintext_call_signal_is_rejected`, `call_signal_routes_to_friend_device_and_drops_unknown`. Wiki `rust_voice_handler.md`. Still open: the `Rtc*` / Share data-channel lane (`handle_webrtc_send_signal`) remains plaintext, a documented follow-up. |

## 11. Inline file bytes, the voice exemption, and vault shards (2026-09-03)

| Write | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Inline-image FileHeader write (`{fid}.{ext}` in the files dir, plus the `mark_file_complete` that follows) | `swarm.rs::handle_incoming_request` `MessageEnvelope::FileHeader` arm, and `fetch.rs::handle_file_header` (the FCM background node's twin) | THREE gates in order. (1) SHAPE: `file_transfer::is_wire_file_id(fid)` (ascii-alphanumeric, 8..=64) and `is_wire_ext(ext)` (ascii-alphanumeric, 1..=8), else the header is dropped with `[HOLLOW-SECURITY] REJECTED inline FileHeader from {sender}: bad file id or extension`. (2) PATH: the name is built by `file_transfer::final_file_path`, which sanitises both components, never by joining the wire strings. (3) The auto-download gate below still decides whether any bytes land at all. FILE-1 (Critical, 2026-09-03): both sites did `files_dir.join(format!("{fid}.{ext}"))` on the raw wire strings while the sanitiser sat unused two functions away. `PathBuf::join` DISCARDS the base when the joined component is absolute, so a peer sending `fid: "C:\\Users\\<user>\\AppData\\Roaming\\Microsoft\\Windows\\Start Menu\\Programs\\Startup\\x"`, `ext: "exe"` wrote attacker-chosen decrypted bytes anywhere the process could write. Precondition was only an established Olm session (a friend, or anyone whose request was accepted). Same family as `feedback_sender_controlled_filename_sanitization`: a hash-verified blob is still an untrusted NAME. Unit: `final_file_path_stays_inside_files_dir`, `wire_file_id_and_ext_shapes` |
| The auto-download gate's voice exemption (what it lets past the user's "never for this conversation" and past the size threshold) | `file_handler::is_voice_note_exempt`, the ONE predicate, read by `auto_download_allows` (swarm DM arm, MLS channel arm, `fetch.rs`) and mirrored by the sender-side `receiver_pref_declines` | ALL FOUR must hold: the header's `voice` flag, `is_voice_message_name(file_name)`, `ext` equal to `ogg` case-insensitively, and `size <= VOICE_NOTE_MAX_BYTES` (8 MB, against ~90 KB per 30 s of 16 kHz mono Opus). FILE-2 (High, 2026-09-03): the predicate was `voice \|\| is_voice_message_name(name)`, and BOTH are sender-chosen, with no size bound and no look at `ext` — which is a separate wire field. A 34 MB header flagged `voice: true` with `ext: "exe"` therefore landed on disk in a conversation with auto-download OFF, and Dart then handed it to ffmpeg. The legacy name-only fallback for pre-0.9.4 senders is GONE with it (a name is not a claim anyone can check); genuine notes from those clients now follow the ordinary threshold. Unit: `voice_exemption_requires_flag_name_ext_and_size`. Harness: `forged_voice_flag_does_not_bypass_auto_download_gate`, and `dm_voice_message_bypasses_auto_download_gate` still proves a real note rides through a gated conversation |
| Vault shard receipt (`content_store::store_shard`, disk file + `vault_shards` row) | `file_handler::handle_shard_stream_complete` | An ERASURE shard (the pending stream's `k`/`m` are non-zero) must carry `ShardMetadata::shard_sha256` and match it, or it is DROPPED before the store with `[HOLLOW-SECURITY] DROPPED vault shard {i} for {cid} from {peer}: missing or wrong per-shard hash`. Replication-mode shards (k = m = 0) are the raw ciphertext with no erasure header and skip this, unchanged. FILE-3 (2026-09-03): `store_shard` hashes the bytes it is handed against THEMSELVES, so a holder returning someone else's bytes was indistinguishable from an honest one; the only symptom was an AES-GCM failure at the end of reconstruction, naming nothing. `erasure::encode` now stamps each shard with the SHA-256 of its own payload, `erasure::decode` treats a shard that fails its stamp as MISSING (erasure coding is exactly the tool for that, so a decode with spares still succeeds) and names the bad indices when too few are left, and `attempt_vault_reconstruction` reads through the CHECKED `read_shard`. `#[serde(default)]`: shards written before this field stay readable, an empty stamp meaning "cannot check" on decode but NOT on receipt. Unit: `encode_stamps_shard_hashes`, `decode_treats_shard_with_wrong_hash_as_missing`, `decode_accepts_shards_with_no_stamped_hash` |
| `save_asset_blob` decode ceiling (an addition to the §2 asset-rail row, not a new path) | `emotes::handle_emote_assets`, alongside the existing requested-only / size-cap / `is_webp` checks | The blob's canvas is read from the WebP HEADER (`image_convert::webp_header_dimensions`, VP8/VP8L/VP8X) and refused over `MAX_DECODE_DIM` (4096 per side) BEFORE the bytes are cached. A byte cap says nothing about decoded size: a 512 KB WebP may declare 16384x16384, about a gigabyte of RGBA, and every later reader of `load_emote_blob` decodes (the public-channel banner thumb, for one). A blob whose header does not parse keeps the pre-existing rule (container magic plus content hash, no decode, `asset_cap_enforced_per_kind` pins that contract) — a decoder refuses an unreadable header outright, so there is no allocation to bound. The same check guards `.hollowpack` import (`hollowpack::blob_shape`, SHOP-2), which used to decode each pack image in full and only then compare against the role's ceiling; there an unreadable header IS a refusal, because that path decodes unconditionally two lines later. Unit: `webp_header_dimensions_reads_vp8_vp8l_vp8x`, `blob_shape_rejects_oversized_canvas_before_decode` |

| `user_profiles.avatar` / `user_profiles.banner` (a PEER's profile stills) | `social::gated_profile_image`, called from `save_incoming_profile` (both ProfileUpdate ingest paths and the ProfileRequest response) and from the relayed-profile arm in `handle_profile_relay` | The ONE validator for incoming profile BYTES, and the mirror of `sanitize_incoming_support_creds` for the byte-shaped fields. THREE checks, in this order: a byte cap (`PROFILE_AVATAR_RECV_MAX_BYTES` 1 MiB, `PROFILE_BANNER_RECV_MAX_BYTES` 3 MiB — the sender-side encoders cap at 250 KB and 400 KB, so this is those bounds with slack for an older client's fatter encode); then `image_convert::validate_remote_image_header`, which reads the DECLARED dimensions without decoding a pixel, refuses anything over `MAX_DECODE_DIM` (4096/side) and anything that is not PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP. A refusal DROPS the blob and returns `None`, which is the same thing as "nothing was offered" and therefore PRESERVES what we already stored — refusing must never be a way to blank somebody's avatar. `Some(&[])` is the owner's explicit clear and is not an image, so it passes through untouched. PROFILE-1 (2026-09-03): these bytes were previously stored with NO format, size or canvas check whatsoever — the profile signature covers the avatar HASH, which proves the owner sent those bytes and says nothing about whether they are safe to decode — and `process_sync_avatar` decodes them later when a guest asks for a public-channel preview, so a peer could park arbitrary bytes in our database and choose the moment we decoded a bomb out of them. `handle_profile_relay` is the worse of the two paths: there the bytes come from a peer that is not even the subject. Unit: `incoming_profile_image_bomb_is_dropped`, `incoming_profile_image_over_byte_cap_is_dropped`. |

**Shard writes since HOL-SEC-024 (2026-09-27):** every remote `store_shard`
path (Olm ShardStore inline and streamed, ShardResponse, ShardMigrate, stream
completion) runs `vault_ops::shard_write_refused` (member of the server, a shard
we do not hold yet, inside our pledge), and reconstruction refuses a rebuilt
ciphertext that does not hash to the content id, so a lying holder can only fail
a download, never substitute it. Still no RETRY on a bad shard: reconstruction
waits for the next arrival (a retry needs a `cmd_tx` into
`attempt_vault_reconstruction` and a per-index attempted-holder set).

Image decoding is no longer on that list: PROFILE-1 (2026-09-03) put every
decode in the crate behind `image_convert::load_bounded` and every REMOTE entry
point behind `validate_remote_image_header` — see the profile row above and
wiki `emotes.md`. What remains is narrower: `link_preview`'s
`convert_to_webp_preview` fetches and decodes an `og:image` chosen by the page
we are previewing, which is sender-side and self-inflicted but still an
attacker-chosen URL, and it has the allocation bound only, not the dimension
one. `validate_frame_centre` and `process_avatar_frame` hand bytes to
`webp_animation::Decoder` (libwebp, where the image crate's limits do not
reach); on the remote path `blob_shape` has already checked the canvas before
either runs, and the local path is a file the user picked.

## 12. At-rest file protection and the loopback media server (issue 78)

Every content file under the data root is AES-256-GCM on disk (`node/at_rest.rs`,
format `HFE1`, one random key per file in the `file_keys` table of messages.db, so
the keys inherit whatever identity protection the user chose). Video and audio
players only take a URL, so `node/at_rest_server.rs` hands the decrypted bytes back
over HTTP on `127.0.0.1`.

**The server is listed here even though no frame can reach it.** It is the one
place at-rest bytes leave the process in the clear, and a reader auditing what can
reach our disk should find it here rather than discover it. It writes nothing, and
it can only ever hand out a file that already sits under the data root.

| Path | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Serving a decrypted file over HTTP | `at_rest_server::respond`, reachable only through `api::at_rest::at_rest_media_url` | FOUR gates. (1) BIND: `TcpListener::bind(("127.0.0.1", 0))`, never `0.0.0.0`, never the IPv6 wildcard, so nothing off the machine reaches it. (2) TOKEN: 32 random bytes minted once per process on first use and compared in constant time; a request whose first path segment is not the token gets a bare 404 that says nothing about whether the file exists. (3) CONFINEMENT: the rest of the path is percent-decoded, refused outright if any component is anything but a plain name (`..`, a root, a drive prefix), then joined onto the data root and CANONICALISED, and the resolved path must still start with the canonicalised data root, so a symlink planted inside the root cannot point out of it. (4) METHOD: GET and HEAD only; anything else closes the socket. Request line and headers are capped at 8 KiB, an idle socket is dropped after 30 s, and at most 64 connections are served at once, so a local program cannot hold the process open or grow it. Responses carry `Cache-Control: no-store`. One range per request (`bytes=a-b`, `bytes=a-`, `bytes=-n`); a multi-range request is 416 rather than a multipart body, and the body streams through `at_rest::read_range` a chunk at a time, so a 4 GB video never costs 4 GB of memory. Nothing about a path is logged. Unit: `at_rest_server_range_semantics` |
| `write_at_rest` / `remove_at_rest` / `at_rest_media_url` (the Dart surface) | `api::at_rest` | The path must resolve under the data root before anything happens. The file may not exist yet, so it is the PARENT that is canonicalised and checked. Outside the root the call is refused, so Dart cannot be talked into writing, unlinking or publishing a URL for anything else. `read_at_rest*` and `export_at_rest` deliberately DO accept a source outside the root and pass it through unchanged, which gives Dart one read primitive for both a user-picked file and a stored attachment |
| `file_keys` rows | `node::at_rest` only | No frame reaches this table. A row is written only by a local write path, at the moment we store a file, and deleted only with the file it belongs to, which is the cryptographic erase. A file carrying the `HFE1` header whose key row is gone is an ERROR, never a passthrough: serving raw ciphertext as content, or silently re-keying it, would both turn a lost key into a corrupt file nobody noticed. A file with no header is a legacy plaintext file and reads back unchanged, so the boot sweep can convert an existing install in the background while the app runs. Every change to a file's bytes (`write_all`'s swap, `Writer::create`'s truncate, `remove`, `rename`, the boot sweep's swap) runs under one swap lock with the read of the key it retires, so that key is always the replaced file's and no replaced file's row stays (a `rename` over a sealed file retires the replaced file's key); each write seals into a temp named `{name}.{first 8 key bytes in hex}.hfe.tmp`, so two writers never share one. The lock is PER PROCESS: the iOS push extension and a push fetch write under their own, and a race across processes can leave only an orphan row, never an unreadable file. A read judges the header in the handle it reads from; when the key it names was just retired it reads the newer file the path now names (bounded); a key missing from the ring is looked up once in the database (a row another process wrote after our ring loaded) before it counts as missing.
