The boot sweep re-checks under the lock that a legacy file is still the plaintext it sealed (same length and time, no header), else drops its copy and key; it takes as orphans only temps modified before this process first touched the layer (3 s slack), only `{name}.{16 hex}.hfe.tmp` (or the old fixed `{name}.hfe.tmp`) beside a loose file, forgets the key a swept temp's header names when the name tags it, and sweeps the cleaned send-copy folders (`shares/send_*/`, removed once empty). Unit `at_rest_concurrent_replaces_of_one_path_stay_readable`, `at_rest_concurrent_small_replaces_leave_one_key_row`, `at_rest_a_read_whose_key_a_swap_retired_reads_the_newer_file`, `at_rest_a_file_another_process_sealed_reads_back`, `at_rest_a_write_during_the_sweep_is_not_reverted`, `at_rest_a_write_racing_the_sweeps_swap_waits_for_it`, `at_rest_a_remove_beside_replaces_leaves_no_stray_row`, `at_rest_a_remove_create_or_move_beside_a_replace_leaves_no_stray_row`, `at_rest_a_move_over_a_sealed_file_retires_its_key`, `at_rest_the_loose_orphan_sweep_spares_another_file_s_temp`, `at_rest_the_sweep_spares_a_temp_this_process_is_writing`, `at_rest_a_folder_sweep_takes_orphan_temps_and_their_keys`, `at_rest_migration_resumes_from_every_crash_point`; harness `t14_deny_a_refused_server_s_file_is_served_to_nobody` (served on the first ask, red 2/5 without the fix) |

Residual, named so a sweep does not have to re-derive it: another process running
as the same user can read the port and, given the token, fetch a file. That process
could already read the SQLCipher key material and the ciphertext itself, so the
server does not widen the threat model it sits inside, which is the disk after the
app is closed, uninstalled, stolen or browsed, never a live unlocked session.
Archive export is the other deliberate exit: a `.hollow-archive` carries PLAINTEXT
attachments by design, as does a Save-as copy, both to a path the user chose.

---

## 13. Destruction orders and the relay kill list (Part 2, 2026-09-11)

A destruction order is the only remote frame in Hollow whose SUCCESS is a write of
nothing: it erases this install. It is master-signed and self-authenticating, so it
travels three lanes that are not trusted, and the whole decision lives in ONE place
(`node::destroy::judge_own_order`). A refusal is logged at warn and changes nothing.

| Path | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| `DestroyIdentity` over the sibling lane (Olm `MessageEnvelope::DestroyIdentityOrder` and its plaintext twin `HavenMessage::IdentityDestroyed`) | `node::destroy::handle_envelope_destroy_identity` / `handle_identity_destroyed`, dispatched from `swarm::handle_incoming_request` | FIVE clauses, all in `judge_own_order`. (1) SIGNATURE: `verify_destroy_identity` binds `master_pubkey_b64` to `master_peer_id` by derivation and checks the master's signature over the canonical `hollow-destroy:{master}:{issued_at_ms}:{sorted csv targets}:{notify_friends}`, with the targets re-sorted before verification so they cannot be reordered after signing. (2) OWN MASTER: an order for anybody else's identity is refused outright; a valid order signed by a stranger's own master says nothing about us. (3) TARGETING: a non-empty `targets` that does not name this device is refused. (4) LINK FRESHNESS: `issued_at_ms` older than the KV `device_linked_at_ms:{device}` stamp, written at the first `start_node` after `identity.device` was created, is refused. A re-linked device gets a NEW device id and the imported database carries no row for it, so it stamps fresh and a captured order cannot follow the user onto the machine they linked afterwards. (5) REPLAY: `issued_at_ms` at or below the newest order this device already acted on is refused, which is what makes the Olm and plaintext copies of one order idempotent against each other. That stamp is IN PROCESS, keyed by local device id, and deliberately NOT persisted: it is written BEFORE the wipe runs, so a disk copy would turn "the wipe never finished" into a permanent refusal that gets acked, and the device would survive forever. A fresh process acts on the same order again, which costs nothing, because a device whose wipe DID finish boots to Welcome with no identity and never authenticates as that id again. Only after all five does the node emit `DestroyReceived` and Dart run `api::wipe::destroy_local`. The order deliberately carries no authority over the SENDER: unlike a device list it never registers anybody in the resolver. Harness: `destroy_scope_c_online_sibling_wipes`, `destroy_refuses_signal_older_than_link_time`. Unit: `destroy_identity_signature_and_freshness_rules` |
| `IdentityDestroyed` about a FRIEND (the same frame, somebody else's master) | `node::destroy::apply_friend_order` | Signature as above, and then ONE extra rule that exists purely to bound what a stranger can make us write: the stamp is recorded only for a master we already know (an accepted or pending friend row, or a stored device list). Without it any authed peer could plant an `identity_destroyed:{master}` KV row for an identity we have never met. Writes are the KV stamp the banner reads and `remove_peer_verified(master)`; a stamp at or below the one we hold is a no-op. Nothing is deleted and no message row is written. Harness: `destroy_friend_announce_flips_verified_and_banner` |
| The banner clearing + `KIND_IDENTITY_REAPPEARED` | `node::destroy::note_identity_reappeared`, called from `ingest_device_list` BEFORE its no-change early return | Runs only behind that function's own gate (`verify_device_list` + `device_list_binds_sender`), so the list has already proved the master signed it and that the deliverer is named in it. ANY later list clears the banner, an unchanged one included: a mnemonic can rebuild a destroyed identity, the safety number is unchanged, and the alert is the whole deliverable. Deduped by the deterministic alert id like every other, so a dismissed warning stays dismissed across reconnects |
| `kill_signal` from the RELAY (`ServerMsg::KillSignal` -> `WsEvent::KillSignal`) | `node::destroy::handle_kill_signal`, and `fetch::handle_kill_frame` in the push isolate | The blob is base64 of the same signed payload and is judged by the SAME `judge_own_order`, so the relay is a courier with no say over the device: a blob it invented or somebody else deposited dies at clause (1) or (2). The relay does read it for ONE decision (D5, HOL-SEC-096, `relay-uws/src/kill_order.h`): an order the master signed, backed by the recovery key pinned in the roster the relay holds for that master (directly or by a phrase-signed delegation to a member), for a target that consented to that identity, waits in the target's PROVEN slot, which no opaque deposit can evict and only a newer proven order replaces; every other blob is opaque. `kill_order.h` mirrors `verify_destroy_identity` + `destroy_order_authorised`, pinned by `node/kill_vectors.rs` -> `relay-uws/test/kill_vectors.json`. ACK RULE: `WsCommand::KillAck` is sent after a wipe (from `api::wipe::destroy_local`, bare: clears everything) AND after a PERMANENT rejection (naming the signal's issuer AND stamp, `KillSignalId`; the relay ignores a stamp alone, which could not tell junk from an order sharing it), never after a transient one. Without the ack the relay re-sends on every auth for 365 days, and a junk deposit by any authed peer would ride along forever; acking a transient failure would instead drop an order that was never judged. The relay's own side caps a blob at 2 KB, 16 targets per deposit, 64 entries per issuer (its OWN oldest evicted, never a refusal), 8 opaque issuers per target and 10 000 globally (heaviest address share pays), 10 000 proven slots (their own fair-share ledger), and refuses guest and fetch sockets as issuers. Harness: `destroy_scope_c_offline_sibling_wipes_on_next_auth_via_kill_list`, `kill_signal_with_foreign_blob_is_dropped`, `authz_turning_away_junk_that_shares_an_orders_stamp_keeps_the_order`. Relay: `test_kill_list`, `test_kill_order` |
| Self-revocation delivery (destruction scope (b)) | `crypto_handler::is_minimal_self_revocation`, consulted by both device-list ingest paths | `device_list_binds_sender` refuses a list delivered by a device that list tombstones, and a device revoking ITSELF is the only one that can announce its own revocation, so the two rules would cancel out. The exception is a MINIMAL DIFF against what we already store and NOTHING else: there must BE a stored list for that master, `list.version` must exceed it, `list.devices` must equal the stored devices minus the deliverer and `list.revoked` the stored revoked plus the deliverer, both as sets. This is narrow on purpose. Every device holds the master key, so the sender rule is the ONE thing that makes a revocation final against a revoked device running a modified client; a loose "the deliverer appears in `revoked`" test would let that device sign a newer list tombstoning itself AND dropping its legitimate siblings, and have it applied everywhere. With no stored list there is no diff to be minimal against, so the baseline rule decides and a self-tombstoning deliverer is refused. The deliverer gains nothing even when admitted: both paths already skip the resolver binding and the friend-list hand-off for a revoked sender. Cost of the narrowness: a receiver holding a DIFFERENT device set from the issuer refuses the self-revocation rather than guessing, which is the safe direction and heals as lists converge. Unit: `self_revocation_carve_out_admits_only_the_minimal_diff` (both lanes, three refusal shapes). Harness: `destroy_scope_b_sibling_drops_the_device` |

The duress slot is not in this table because no frame reaches it: `identity.duress`
is read only by `unlock_identity` and `change_password`, from a secret typed on this
machine. `change_password` probes it with the NEW password and refuses a match,
because the identity slot is tried first and would silently disarm the duress code. It is worth
stating anyway that every password unlock derives Argon2id against BOTH slots and
combines the results only after both finish, so the cost of a wrong password and the
cost of a duress code are identical, and that the slot EXISTS for the life of
password protection (random bytes under a random throwaway key when no code is set)
so its presence reveals nothing. Unit: `duress_both_slots_always_derived`,
`duress_slot_dummy_when_unset_is_indistinguishable_in_size`.

Residual, named so a sweep does not have to re-derive it: a duress code typed at a
COLD LAUNCH can only destroy locally. The master and device keys are wrapped by the
real password, so nothing can be signed and no socket can be authenticated; scopes
(b) and (c) reach the network only when the node is already running, which is what a
mobile App Lock re-unlock looks like. The local erase always runs.

What the local erase takes (session 35, decisions A and B): everything under the root
(`sweep_root`), Hollow's own files beside it (`Outside`), and the call and screen
recordings THIS profile listed in its keyless `recordings.list` (`remember_recording`
before the recorder starts; `erase_recordings` inside `sweep_root` unlinks only regular
files named `Hollow_<stamp>.mp4`, plus the ffmpeg log, directly in a real `Hollow
Recordings` folder, then that folder if empty; a held one stays listed for the boot
wipe). The marker says `destroy`, so `perform_pending_wipe` tells a finished destroy
from a cancelled link; a finished destroy takes only that profile's entry and pin out
of profiles.json (`registryAfterWipe`, never the whole file) and restarts into another
profile that holds an identity, or Welcome (`relaunchAfterWipe`,
`settleProfileAfterBootWipe`). Another profile is erased only through
`erase_profile_at` (absolute, not the running one, empty or holding an identity; a
debug log alone proves nothing, it sits beside the Windows exe). Tests:
`a_wipe_takes_this_profiles_recordings_and_nothing_else`,
`the_boot_wipe_tells_a_destroy_from_a_cancelled_link`,
`erasing_another_profile_takes_its_recordings_and_refuses_the_running_one`,
`test/profile_wipe_test.dart`, `test/wipe_profile_guard_test.dart`.

The boot wipe has a third trigger, local and bound to one key (#123, t14-123):
"Link a device" makes a throwaway identity, marked the moment it exists
(`mark_link_throwaway`: `link_throwaway.marker` = SHA-256 of `hollow-link-throwaway1`
and the key file). At launch `settle_link_throwaway` queues the wipe only while the
key file still matches the mark (constant time), no link is stashed and no wipe is
already queued (a `destroy` marker is never overwritten); any other key just clears
the mark, and a landing clears it. Nothing remote reaches it. The boot wipe sweeps
nothing while this process holds an identity open (store, node, fetch node): an
Android screen that closed keeps its process, so the link-mode relay switch ends the
process instead. The first-run link has no exit but Back and a landed link
(`runFirstRunLink`, no system back on the placeholder, the link dialog or the
link-mode key prompt), so nobody uses the throwaway as an account. Tests:
`an_unfinished_link_wipes_its_throwaway_at_the_next_launch`,
`a_link_mark_never_wipes_another_identity_or_a_stashed_link`,
`a_landed_link_is_kept_and_a_refused_one_goes_back_to_welcome`,
`a_link_mark_never_settles_over_a_queued_destroy`,
`the_boot_wipe_never_sweeps_while_this_process_has_the_identity_open`,
`test/widget/first_run_link_test.dart`, `test/widget/review_link_back_exit_test.dart`.


## 14. Files, vault, recovery, share and relay gates (security audit session 6, 2026-09-27)

| Write | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A FileHeader registering a decrypt key or writing inline bytes (every arm: Olm, MLS, push `fetch.rs`, guest `PublicFileHeader`) | `file_handler::file_header_refused` + `file_bytes_on_disk`, run FIRST | For a file we hold a card for: the card's owner (device→master) or the holder we asked (the pull's asked set, or the decrypt-fail retry target); a channel header only from a current member who can read the channel; nothing for bytes already on disk; inline bytes only on DM headers; on the Olm arm the pull's receipt and ask end only after EVERY gate passed, the card gate included (`header_meta_judged`, the size, mute, channel and block checks), so a refused answer leaves the ask in flight and the retry sweep asks the next holder; the MLS arm likewise spends the receipt and clears the decline pin only after its cap, drop and card gates, so a refused push leaves the asked holder's answer its auto-download bypass (unit `a_refused_mls_push_keeps_the_pull_receipt`); Dart gets a `share_ref` only from the owner. HOL-SEC-022, tests `authz_file_header_delivers_only_for_its_owner`, `file_header_gate_stays_wired` (the gate order), harness `t14_a_wrong_proof_answer_is_refused_and_the_pull_goes_on` |
| `FileChunk` | gone | No sender existed; the type is refused at parse (`retired_file_chunk_is_refused_at_parse`) |
| Guest public-file receipt | `pending_public_file_requests` | Records the peer asked; only its answer counts |
| WS stream temp (`.ws_recv_`) | `ws_stream_transfer::ws_stream_receive` | Owned by the device that opened it (takeover only after 10 s idle), never past its declared size, 16 open per peer and 128 in all, swept at boot; resume offsets asked only of the same device. HOL-SEC-023 |
| Vault shard writes, serving, deletes, manifests | `vault_ops::{shard_write_refused, shard_serve_refused, handle_shard_delete, ingest_vault_manifest}` | Writes: member, shard not held, pledge. Serving: member who can read the file's channel when we hold its manifest. Deletes: member with Manage Server (override-aware), only that server's shards and placements, both transports. Manifests: from the member named as creator, never over another creator's, well-formed content id, relink only the creator's own cards. Placement confirmed only by its target. Vault envelopes other than delete/manifest are Olm-only. HOL-SEC-024 |
| Vault cache file | `pipeline::cache_path` | Alphanumerics only in both parts (content id and extension came from a peer's manifest) |
| Restricted-channel files | Dart `ChannelInfo.usesSubgroup`, `vault_ops::handle_vault_upload_file`, the channel send's `use_vault_only` | Never vaulted: the manifest carries the key to the whole server. HOL-SEC-025 |
| Recovery pool state (members, plan, stop) | `swarm.rs` recovery interception | Frames only from the pool's own room; plans only from the coordinator (lowest member id) and only to pool members; `RecoveryManifestSync` deleted. HOL-SEC-026 |
| Share manifest / Have | `share_handler` | Manifest taken once; a Have only against a known manifest. HOL-SEC-027 |
| `ServerSettingChanged` | `ServerState::setting_change_allowed` (authoring and `op_allowed`) | Retention policies and their `_since` stamps from the Owner only, a policy only with an app value (`vault::adaptive::RETENTION_CHOICES`); readers treat any other value as keep. HOL-SEC-021 |
| Any CRDT op | `CrdtOp::verify_author` | The op's clock must name its author (`OpReject::ActorMismatch`). HOL-SEC-020 |
| Relay pre-auth frame | `relay-uws/src/auth_frame.h::parse_auth_frame` | Type-checked, 16 KiB cap; auth and binary dispatch wrapped so no input unwinds into uSockets. HOL-SEC-028 |
| Relay kill list | `relay-uws/src/kill_list.h` | One slot per issuer per target (max 8), every slot delivered at auth, a stamp past the relay clock + 10 min refused, `kill_ack` with `issued_at_ms` removes one slot (bare ack all). HOL-SEC-029 |
| Relay topic rings; guest sockets | `ring_evict.h`, binary dispatch, `handle_direct`, `guest_broadcast_refused` | A full ring drops the oldest frame of whoever holds most; guests may not send 0x07 or JSON `direct`; a guest's broadcasts, binary (0x03, 0x0A) and JSON `msg` alike, share one count of ten a minute, refused before the breaker and the send budget (0.14, live `test_guest_text_msg_counts_against_the_minute`). HOL-SEC-030 |
| Relay device-list marks | `snapshot_codec.h` v3 | Survived a restart. HOL-SEC-031. Gone in 0.13 with the 0.11 inbox proof they guarded: the codec still reads an older relay's `marks` and drops them, and writes none |


## 15. Revocation, push parity, friends, calls, profiles, reach (security audit session 7, 2026-09-27)

| Write | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Resolver bind + sibling share after a sibling proof | `swarm::on_verified_sibling` | `crypto_handler::sibling_proof_refused` FIRST: a device in our own list's tombstones or recorded revoked is neither bound nor sent anything. HOL-SEC-032 |
| Olm session from a key exchange | `key_exchange_device_unauthorized` | Refuses a revoked device. Enforced revocations are recorded (`revoked_marks` table, per device and master since 0.13; 0.14 moved the last 0.12 `revoked_devices` rows in and dropped that table in one transaction) and `resolver::warm_from_store` loads them, with the block list and device links, in EVERY process that judges frames (main, push fetch, iOS extension). HOL-SEC-032, -035 |
| Attribution of a carried device list (join request, friend request, decline, accept) | `crypto_handler::carried_list_master` after `ingest_device_list` | The list's master only when ingest bound the sender; the ingest's `newly_revoked` is enforced on every arm. HOL-SEC-033 |
| Friend destroy notice; "identity reappeared" | `destroy::apply_friend_order`, `ingest_device_list` | A notice must beat a never-cleared floor stamp; only a list adding a never-seen device clears the banner. HOL-SEC-034 |
| Push fetch: DM rows, channel rows, banners | `fetch.rs` | Blocks warm; a PreKey pins the Olm key (`pin_olm_identity_key`); mentions judged from our own decryption, per-channel level applied in `run_fetch`; a wake for a server we are not in fetches nothing; fallback banners only for `push_sender_known`. HOL-SEC-035 |
| Friend row on `FriendAccept` | `swarm.rs` FriendAccept arm | Lands only on our pending outgoing row (or re-confirms accepted); siblings learn through `social::share_friend_with_siblings`, and `FriendListSync` upgrades only a pending row. HOL-SEC-036 |
| DM edit, card, delete, reaction from a blocked person | `message_ops::live_dm_change`, `dm_reaction_target_ok` | Dropped. The friend request arm re-checks the block after the carried list binds. HOL-SEC-036 |
| 1:1 call state | `voice_handler::call_invite_allowed`; Dart `CallNotifier.handleCallSignal` | Only an accepted friend or our own device rings us; every other signal must come from the live call's peer (identity-collapsed); secure call ids. An invite replaces a live call only from the DEVICE in that call (incoming: the device that rang; outgoing: `CallState.answeredBy`), under a new call id, while its media link is lapsing (`decideInviteAction` `replaceLost`, #146): the same device `call_book` lets end the call, never a sibling. A healthy call answers busy; an invite superseded during the teardown is told busy, and an `end` naming the waiting invite cancels it (`call_lost_by_peer_test`, `call_invite_decision_test`). HOL-SEC-037 |
| Share assignment / feed report | `voice_handler` screen_assign, screen_feed_state | Assignment only from the stream's originator; a feed report must name our own stream. HOL-SEC-037 |
| Profile row | `social::save_incoming_profile`, `MessageStore::save_profile` | Avatar bytes must hash to the signed hash; a stale profile writes nothing (clears included) and reports unsaved; text over `PROFILE_*_MAX_BYTES` refused whole on every path. HOL-SEC-038 |
| Message edit; reaction add | `edit_message_in`, `add_reaction` | An edit must be newer than the row's last edit; an add signed no later than a recorded removal is refused (every removal recorded). HOL-SEC-039 |
| General data channel; gossip overlay; voice re-announce | `voice_handler::data_channel_peer_allowed`, swarm gossip feeds | Dial and answer only own devices, friends, shared-server members; the overlay takes CRDT members only; voice presence only to a member who can see the channel. HOL-SEC-040 |


## 16. MLS group state: leaves, commits, Welcomes, failures (design D, security audit session 8, 2026-09-27)

Design: `reports/shipped/security/audit/design_D_mls_authority.md`. The rules live in
`node/mls_authority.rs`; the staging lives in `crypto/mls_manager.rs`.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A leaf's identity | `mls_manager::classify_leaf` | Bound only when the leaf's signature key is the device key its id encodes AND the credential `hl1:{device}:{master}:{sig}` carries the master's signature over `hollow-mls-leaf:{master}:{device}`. Everything else is unbound: its messages are ignored, it never enters a tree, and we never encrypt or commit from our own unbound leaf. HOL-SEC-041 |
| Seat a KeyPackage (live, parked join, meeting knock) | swarm `MlsKeyPackage` arm, parked-join arm, `conference::handle_join_request` | Live: only inside Olm since 0.14 (Lane::Carried; the Olm session names the sending device, a plaintext copy is dropped). The package must be bound to the device the frame came from (parked: and to the joiner's master); its certified master must be a current, unbanned member who can see a subgroup's channel. HOL-SEC-041 |
| Merge a received commit (live or catch-up) | `crypto_handler::handle_mls_commit_frame` -> `MlsManager::process_commit_judged` -> `mls_authority::commit_verdict` | Staged, judged, then merged. Refused: non-member sender, foreign proposals, unbound or identity-changing leaves, any unbound committer (0.14: no rebind; a bound member repairs the leaf, `repair_unbound_leaves`), a revoked add, a meeting committer other than the pinned host. Held (retried each batch tick, 60 s): unknown committer or added member, removal of a current member unless the same commit re-adds that device. HOL-SEC-042 |
| Our own commits | swarm MLS batch timer -> `mls_authority::plan_membership` -> `MlsManager::commit_membership` | Same rules applied before committing; one commit per group per tick; a current member's leaf leaves only with its re-add. HOL-SEC-042 |
| Install a Welcome | swarm `MlsWelcome` arm, batch retry -> `join_from_welcome_judged` -> `welcome_verdict` | Before staging (which deletes the KeyPackage it names), `welcome_sender_refusal` judges the frame's sealing device: a meeting's only while we knock and from the host device its `conf_host` proof binds (`conference::welcome_host`), a server's never from a revoked or disowned device. Staged with `replace_old_group`; refused for another group id, any unbound, revoked or removed leaf or a master id (a device our roster does not place yet is HELD, §37), a leaf that is not ours, or replacing a held group unasked (`asked_for_leaf`: our push, eviction grace, own join, or an answered request FROM THAT SENDER); held for a non-member or banned sender or ANY leaf (subgroup: one that cannot see the channel), re-judged each tick as our view catches up. A KeyPackage the Welcome names is put back unless it installs, and an installed Welcome ends a held one. A meeting needs our pending knock and pins its sender as the only committer. HOL-SEC-043, C-MLS-03, C-MLS-05 (harness `authz_a_guest_cannot_spend_another_guests_key_package`, `a_welcome_that_spends_nothing_sends_no_new_knock`, `authz_a_welcome_never_seats_a_non_member`, `a_join_whose_view_lacks_a_newer_member_still_takes_its_seat`) |
| Mint a KeyPackage for a requester | swarm `MlsKeyPackageRequest` arm | Our server only (no meeting, no pending join), requester a current member, subgroup only if we qualify, once per group per 10 s, and while we hold a leaf only for `crypto_handler::may_repair_our_leaf` (owner, our catch-up responder, subgroup coordinator). HOL-SEC-043 |
| Drop a group | everywhere | Only on an authenticated event (an accepted commit that evicts us, an accepted Welcome) or our own CRDT state. Decrypt and commit failures never drop: garbage is ignored, anything else probes; probes carry `epoch_auth_digest`, and the answering member repairs a same-epoch fork. HOL-SEC-044 |
| A group's secrets on disk | `conference::forget_meeting_group` (`remove_group` + persist: leave, the host's end, its kick notice), startup `forget_unloaded_groups` | A participant out of a meeting keeps none of its group; a restart erases every stored group nothing loads (meetings, subgroups of channels no longer restricted), never one of a server whose state did not load, and nothing when the server rows were not read. C-MLS-06 (harness `a_meeting_left_leaves_no_group_secret_on_disk`, `a_restart_keeps_no_meeting_group_and_every_server_group`; unit `a_restore_forgets_the_groups_nothing_loads`) |
| A late frame's past epoch (0.13) | `MlsManager::decrypt_fresh` -> `mls_past::PastEpochs::open` | Only a `PrivateMessage` naming our group at an epoch below ours opens there, at most 3 epochs back, as an application message only (anything else clears the past store's proposals and is ignored); a past store never commits, merges, exports or holds an epoch key pair, and dies with its group (`remove_group`, a Welcome that replaces it, `forget_unloaded_groups`). The sender is classified against that epoch's tree. Unit `a_frame_from_a_merged_epoch_still_opens_up_to_three_epochs_back`, `a_past_epoch_keeps_no_secret_it_does_not_need`; harness `a_channel_frame_from_before_a_merge_still_opens_after_it` |
| Voice signaling decrypted from a group | swarm MLS arm, `VoiceChannel*` | Dropped unless the encrypting leaf is the relay-stamped device. HOL-SEC-045 |

## 17. CRDT state authority: anchors, the fold, admission, history (design E, security audit session 9, 2026-09-27)

Design: `reports/shipped/security/audit/design_E_crdt_authority.md`. The fold lives in
`crdt/fold.rs`, self-certifying ids in `crdt/anchor.rs`, the rules in
`ServerState::op_allowed`.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Any remote op | `ServerState::ingest_remote` | Stateless checks, dedup against the retained log and the held pool, then the fold: tail-apply when newer than the log's tail, else a rebuild from the anchor. Refused-for-authority ops wait in `held` (256, 10 min) and are re-judged at the next rebuild. HOL-SEC-047 |
| The anchor of a new server | `ServerState::found`, `op_allowed` founding rule | 40-hex id = `SHA-256("hollow-server1:{owner}:{nonce}")[..40]`; a founding op lands only on an ownerless state, from the key that hashes to the id. HOL-SEC-046 |
| The anchor of a pre-0.12 server | `ServerCheckpoint { state, covers }`, `checkpoint_allowed`, `sync_handler::author_due_checkpoints` | Only from the anchor owner (`owner_pin`, else the current owner; trust on first use for an unpinned 32-hex joiner), `covers` no later than its own clock and past the current checkpoint's, a state of this server whose one Owner is its author. It sits in the fold at `covers` (`fold_order`): it replaces what came before, ops after `covers` fold on top; ops up to `covers` are pruned (DB too), except a self-certifying id's founding op and the late leaves §54 keeps. The owner authors one per existing server, then per 2000 ops, at most hourly. HOL-SEC-046/047 |
| A joiner's starting state | swarm `ServerStateSnapshot` -> `ServerState::accept_join_snapshot`; SyncResponse skeleton | No snapshot for a 40-hex id, none that replaces an anchored state, and with an invite pin (`owner=`, `PendingJoin.owner_pin`) only one owned by the pin. An anchored join completes only when the fold admitted our own `MemberAdded`. An adopted snapshot is one member's word, so it keeps who belongs and what exists and decides nothing: only the owner's role stays, role permissions, bans, mutes, label assignments and channel grants are dropped and every channel is not public, until the owner's own ops or checkpoint say otherwise. HOL-SEC-046, HOL-SEC-093 (unit `authz_a_join_snapshot_carries_no_authority`, harness `authz_a_members_snapshot_of_an_older_server_decides_nothing`) |
| `MemberAdded` | `op_allowed` -> `admission_allowed` | Author a current member; the carried `ask` (`JoinAsk`, `hollow-join-ask1`) signed by the TARGET's own key for THIS server, and for a non-member newer than every ask that admitted it before (`MemberSpan.asked_at`), so nobody is listed who did not ask and an old ask never brings back someone who left; target not banned; not private; under the cap; owner-verify admits only through the owner; Twitch gate needs the carried `follow` credential, verified for the target at the op's own time. The joiner signs the ask over each request's `requested_at` (`PendingJoin::ask_again`), the admitter copies it from `ServerJoinRequest.ask`. HOL-SEC-049, HOL-SEC-092 (unit `authz_member_added_names_only_someone_who_asked`, harness `authz_a_member_lists_only_someone_who_asked_to_join`) |
| Any op's author | `op_allowed` -> `author_role` | Read by its own id (never the resolver) and only while a current member. HOL-SEC-050 |
| Device-keyed registers | `canonicalize_members` (legacy servers only) | Adopt-only: never onto a master with its own register or the Owner, never Owner, no ban or mute onto a Moderator+. HOL-SEC-051 |
| Moderation edges and ownership | `op_allowed`, `role_change_allowed`, `kick_allowed`, `lifts_register`; authoring via `author_checked` | Unban/unmute need the setter's rank; nickname-class edits of others only over lower ranks; role permissions only admin/moderator/member and only bits held; nothing makes anyone Owner or touches the Owner; our own ops go through the same predicate. HOL-SEC-052 |
| Backfilled channel posts | both `ChannelSyncBatch` arms -> `crypto_handler::backfill_author_allowed` | The author (master) must have been a member at the post's time per `member_record` (10 min slack); legacy-anchored servers are not judged until their checkpoint. HOL-SEC-048 |

## 18. Every relay frame names its sender (design A, security audit session 10, 2026-09-27)

Design: `reports/shipped/security/audit/design_A_frame_authority.md`. The seal lives in
`node/frame_auth.rs`; the replay classes are `HavenMessage::live_only` and
`MessageEnvelope::live_only` in `node/types.rs` (exhaustive, a new variant must pick).

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Any frame on the relay (0x03, 0x07, 0x04, image directs, 0x09, 0x02) | `frame_auth::spawn_sealer` (send), swarm Message/DirectMessage and BinaryDirect arms, `fetch.rs`, `forwarder/signaling.rs` (receive) | Sealed by the sending DEVICE over room, route (`*` or the target device/master), ms stamp, nonce and the body's hash; opened against the key inlined in `from`; wrong room/route/delivery, our own device as `from`, or a stamp over 300 s ahead is refused; unsealed is refused, at the standalone forwarder too, which answers every peer sealed. HOL-SEC-053, A-MED-09 |
| Live-only frames | swarm arm after the rate limit; the standalone forwarder's `Sealing::admit` | Older than 300 s, or a nonce this sender already used inside the window (`ReplayGuard`), is dropped. Stream chunks: fresh only. HOL-SEC-054 |
| Signals inside Olm or MLS (calls, voice, forwarder, typing, sync requests) | Olm and MLS envelope dispatch; the standalone forwarder's `open_envelope` | Judged by the carrying frame's seal time. HOL-SEC-054 |
| A repeated Olm frame | `OlmManager::already_decrypted` before any teardown | The session's last 512 decrypted ciphertext digests; a repeat is dropped unread. HOL-SEC-054 |
| Late-deliverable frames | FriendRemove, FriendAccept, MemberKickBroadcast (+MLS), ServerJoinRequest, ServerJoinRejected, ServerJoinResolved arms | Bounded by the seal time: removal vs the friendship's stamp, accept vs our request, kick vs `member_since`, join vs `left_at` by the later of seal and `requested_at` (no slack; the joiner stamps a rejoin past its own leave, §55c), rejection = the exact pending ask, resolution never after its own seal. HOL-SEC-054 |
| The op log | SyncRequest arm | Only to a current member; a tombstone serves anyone only its `ServerDeleted` op. HOL-SEC-055 |
| A pending join from a sibling | SiblingServerAnnounce arm; senders `sync_handler::announce_server_to_own_devices` (create, join completion) and `swarm::on_verified_sibling` | Never for a held server: a quiet list refresh (`ServerCreated`), never `ServerJoined`; pinned to the announcing device's anchor owner (`owner` field). HOL-SEC-056. Durable (may arrive late from the inbox buffer), so the join it starts is `PendingJoin::from_sibling`: it never signs a `JoinAsk` (`ask_again`), keeps no row (`save_pending_join`, so boot never restores it as a parked, master-signed ask) and a refusal of it surfaces nothing (`handle_join_refused`); its completion announces nothing back. Sent only for a server we are a member of and not deleted, to roster devices not revoked; re-sent on every sibling return, leaf or not, since it heals a list that missed a server the group holds. Harness `t1_review_stale_announce_for_a_left_server_leaves_no_join_behind`, `t1_review_completed_sibling_join_does_not_bounce_server_joined`, `t1_review_phone_foreground_does_not_replay_server_joined`, `t1_sibling_holding_a_leaf_but_missing_the_server_gets_it_back`, `authz_a_sibling_announce_for_a_held_server_starts_no_join`; unit `t1_sibling_announced_join_never_signs_an_ask`, `t1_sibling_announced_join_keeps_no_row`, `t1_sibling_announce_names_only_a_server_we_are_in` |
| Our full profile | ProfileRequest / ProfileRequestFor arms, `social::profile_request_allowed` | Own devices, friends, co-members, people we asked; a relayed profile only about someone the asker shares a server with. HOL-SEC-057 |
| Share-audio output | Dart `onScreenAudioReceived` (call + voice providers) | Only the call's peer while we watch its share / a sharer we asked to watch. HOL-SEC-058 |
| Parked destroy orders | `fetch::handle_kill_frame` | A junk or refused order is acked by its own stamp; bare ack only after a wipe. HOL-SEC-059 |
| Anything `HavenMessage::lane()` marks Carried (sibling lane, server control, DM sync, file and emote asks, the guest file header, the auto-download advert) | swarm plaintext dispatch (lane check), Olm `Carried` arm | A plaintext copy is dropped before any handler; a carried one counts only as sent by the device whose Olm session decrypted it. Claim C-24, A-D1 |
| Share control (manifest, haves, chunk requests) | swarm dispatch, `share_handler::open_control` | Only inside `ShareSealed`, opened with the link key, in that share's own room, naming that same share. A-D1 phase F |
| Anything `lane()` marks Join (join request, snapshot, refusal, resolution) | swarm plaintext dispatch (lane check), `join_lane::open_for_members` / `open_for_joiner` | Counts only inside `JoinSealed` in the server's own room: a join box holds only a request with its reply key or a members' resolution, a reply box only a sync answer, snapshot, refusal or resolution, each for this server; what a box holds is judged as a frame of its own. HOL-SEC-062 |
| A sync answer for a join still pending | Olm `Carried` arm (`handle_incoming_request`) | A `SyncResponse` naming a server in `pending_server_joins` is dropped over Olm; it counts only from our reply box. HOL-SEC-062 |
| Our profile to a peer | `social::profile_audience` in `send_own_profile_inner`, ProfileRequest arm | Full profile only to own devices, friends and co-members (`voice_handler::data_channel_peer_known`, then the friend row); a card (name, avatar) to either side of a pending friend request; nothing to anyone else or to a revoked device. HOL-SEC-062 |
| A received profile's fields | ProfileUpdate / ProfileRelay arms, MLS profile arm | `hollow-profile2` over the peer, `updated_at` and all eleven fields is required on every path (absent is refused); the fields of a sender with no relationship (`profile_audience` None) are ignored, its device list still ingested. HOL-SEC-062 |
| A card | `profile_card::store_card`, `profile_card::open_from`, `conference::handle_inbound_chat` | `hollow-card1` must verify and name the sender's own master (the resolver after the carried device list; the leaf's certified master in a meeting); a friend request's sealed card opens only under the two masters' pair key for that `requested_at` and must name the requester. Written only when newer, and it clears any stored full proof, so a card is never relayed. HOL-SEC-062 |

Decided for session 11 (plan items 13-16): all of C-24 into Olm/MLS in 0.12 (structural
leaks included), a signed file content commitment, meeting host pinning, the relay and
smaller items (design section 5).


## 19. The join lock (design A-D1, security audit session 16, 2026-09-28)

Design: `design_A_frame_authority.md` "BUILT (session 16, the join lock)". Code: `node/join_lock.rs`, `crdt/lock_state.rs`, `node/lock_keeper.rs`, `relay-uws/src/join_lock.h`. Finding HOL-SEC-062.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A lock op (`CrdtPayload::JoinLock`) | `ServerState::join_lock_allowed` (fold, every ingest) | Author owner, admin or mod; an owner-signed link only from the owner; any other link only a successor a held link's change key signed; a door only its own secret; grants only to owners, admins and mods. |
| The relay's chain | `join_lock.h` `JoinLocks::put` / `relay_put` (Rust mirror), `ws_handler.cpp` `handle_lock_put` (`LockCounted` = `RosterBook::counted_authority`, server authority's removal walk over the held roster since 0.14 track R2 (§61), cached in the book until that roster changes, each walk charged to the breaker by its statements; each signature verified once per put) | First valid extension of the newest lock wins; an owner-signed link past the newest resets a fork; a shorter chain ending in the same lock compacts; a legacy id's record is keyed by its owner, an owner-signed link must name it. 0.14 (track R, C4/M5/N11/N12a): every link carries `dev` (`hollow-lock-dev1`, the device's own signature naming its master, which must be the owner on a base); a proof that does not verify is a forgery. An owner-signed link the relay does not hold needs an owner device the relay's authority COUNTS (no roster held = nobody); a reset jumps at most `RESET_STEP` (2^20) past the newest; a new link after one with a proof, or with one, needs a counted device; a DEAD link (its proof names a device authority no longer counts; a link without a proof is never dead) gives way: a counted device extending an older held lock replaces every dead link after it only when it is the owner's or every one of them is its own identity's (t14-lock review H1: an ex-mod's older change key forks nothing); a newest lock past `FROZEN_FROM` gives way to a counted owner device's new start. Unit `join_lock::tests::*` + C++ `test_join_lock.cpp` (pinned `PINNED_DEVICE_CHAIN` both sides), live `test_lock_needs_a_counted_device`, harness `t14l_a_removed_owner_device_cannot_reset_the_relay_lock`, `t14l_a_dark_link_of_a_removed_mod_device_gives_way`, `t14l_a_dead_link_gives_way_only_to_its_own_identity`, unit `a_dead_link_gives_way_only_to_the_owner_or_its_own_identity` / C++ `test_dead_link_identity`. 0.13 clients: the first link carrying `dev` makes the lock op UNREAD there, so every 0.13 member (not only mods) loses the newer door, the D1 room view and join answering on that server; 0.13 links extend only until that first `dev` link (accepted, AR-38). |
| What a joiner seals to | `sync_handler::handle_join_lock_chain` | Only a chain it verified back to the owner the id or the invite names. |
| An answer to our join | `hold_join_answer` -> `judge_join_answer` | Held until a lock read asked after it arrived; opened only from that read's newest door (the box is sealed FROM the door, `seal_from`); an older door is never read, only a reason to ask again. |
| A post-completion sync answer | `recent_join_answer` | Only a `SyncResponse`, only from the door verified at completion, within 5 min. |
| The joiner's card | ServerJoinRequest arm | Stored only when it names the requester's own master and verifies. |
| A join request | `join_lane::open_for_members` | Sealed to a held door AND the invite key together; an ask naming its invite key (`JoinAsk.key`) names the key it was sealed to. |
| The invite key (`JoinKeySet`, Reset invite link, 0.14 t14-reset) | `op_allowed` (fold, every ingest); `sync_handler::handle_reset_invite_link` (`OpGate::Owner`) | The OWNER only, in the fold (an admin's reset counts for nothing). A reset retires the old key (`join_secrets_retired`, newest 8 by clock, carried by checkpoints, capped to the newest 8 on every load, rebase and legacy join snapshot, which keeps them minus the key in force so a legacy joiner refuses the keyless admissions its members refuse; read only to refuse) and moves the join lock at once, as a kick does (`note_removal` in the fold, the owner's `lock_keeper.nudge`): a member that missed the reset cannot prove the newest door, so under D1 it sees no ring and opens no box sealed to the new door until a member hands it the door with the reset. Harness `t14_reset_only_the_owner_resets_and_an_old_link_admission_counts_for_nothing`, `t14_reset_a_parked_old_link_ask_is_refused_and_the_new_link_joins` (the lock moves), `t14_reset_a_member_back_alone_admits_no_old_link_ask`; unit `op_allowed_ingest_matrix`, `a_join_lock_op_is_judged_by_who_may_move_the_lock` (a reset moves the lock, a first key does not), `a_join_snapshot_brings_the_newest_eight_retired_keys`, `a_legacy_joiner_refuses_a_keyless_admission_after_a_reset_as_its_members_do`. Since a second key IS a reset, the missing-key sweep (`sync_handler::author_missing_join_keys`, every batch tick) sets a key only through `sets_missing_join_key`: no pending join and no whole-log fetch due for the server, no lower online device of ours in its room, and this connection's catch-up there finished (`JoinHold::caught_up`: the room shown and a member's nonce-echoed final sync, own siblings included, or no other member device visible). Harness `t14_reset_two_owner_devices_sweep_one_join_key`, `t14_reset_an_owner_sibling_mid_sync_mints_no_join_key`. Residual: the fold still reads two concurrent first keys as a reset; only the sweep gate keeps them from happening, and a lone owner of a keyless server with a whole-log fetch due sets no key until a member answers it. |
| An ask made with a reset link | `join_lane::open_as_member` (a retired key opens only a request, and says so), `join_verdict::link_refusal` (live, first gate, before anything names the server; the box fact rides `join_verdict::OLD_LINK`, through `join_hold` in `HeldAsk.old_link`), `admission_allowed` (fold), `JoinAsk::verifies` | Live: a newcomer's request a retired key opened is refused `invite_reset` on that fact alone, as is an ask whose signed key is not the current one; one naming no key, or carrying no ask, `invite_outdated`; parked or live, after a member back from away has synced (`join_hold`). Fold: an admission opening or extending a span on an ask naming a key other than the one in force at its point is refused, and a keyless one wherever a reset is held at its point, whichever order the reset and the admission arrive in (no key in force yet = nothing compares; `JoinKeySet` enables held ops); a keyless ask before any reset still folds. The ask's key is signed (`hollow-join-ask2`), so nobody moves it or strips it, and only in its one text form (signer refuses anything else, never downgrading to keyless; verifier requires `key_to_text(key_from_text(k)) == k`, HOL-SEC-143/154). Harness `t14_reset_a_parked_old_link_ask_is_refused_and_the_new_link_joins`, `t14_reset_a_member_back_from_away_refuses_an_old_link_ask`, unit `an_ask_with_a_reset_link_opens_no_span_after_the_reset`, `an_old_link_admission_after_an_invite_reset_is_void_in_any_order`, `a_keyless_admission_counts_before_a_reset_and_not_after`, `a_join_ask_binds_the_invite_key_it_names`, `a_join_ask_key_is_a_key_in_its_one_text_form`, `open_as_member_reads_an_old_link_only_to_refuse_it`, `the_link_gate_comes_first_whatever_the_box_held`, `an_askless_request_meets_the_link_gate_first`. |
| A refusal of our join | `sync_handler::handle_join_refused` | Never ends the join: the tile shows the reason, the row, room and ring copy stay, and a real admission still completes it; a question (NSFW, Twitch) goes to the user once per ask. HOL-SEC-062 |
| A member's parked ask read back | ServerJoinRequest arm (parked) | A copy sealed before that member's admission (`member_since`) is history and ignored; one sealed since gets the state again, never a second admission. HOL-SEC-062 |
| Our next lock | `node/lock_keeper.rs` | Moved by an online owner, admin or mod when a removal, ban, leave, demotion from mod+ or the owner's invite reset marked it due, or when a roster fold removed a device of a member identity (`note_removed_devices` from `enforce_device_revocations`, task-local per node loop; at most one such move per identity and server per `REMOVAL_GAP`, 10 min, N12b); a mod+ remover moves first, on the same socket ahead of its own removal, every removal inside the gap too (`move_before_removal`, N12b closed in t14-r2; that move guards the relay tip only, and one the relay refuses for a stale view acts at once only when the relay's newest link was made by a device its own identity's roster removed, else waits `RETRY_LOCK` for the newer holder's grant; the mark dies with its offer at `PUT_TIMEOUT`, unit `a_refused_move_before_a_removal_overrides_only_a_removed_device`, `a_timed_out_move_before_a_removal_marks_no_later_refusal`); a removal is settled only by a door newer than the one held once no MLS group of the server (main and subgroups, `server_leaves`) lists the removed leaf, per device, devices past 256 judged together by any leaf a due identity removed (t14-lock review M1/L3); the op is written only after the relay took the link. Grants go to each device the mover's view counts (`grant_key(master, device)`, at most 8 per identity, never a removed device or the bare master); until the fold takes device keys (t14-lock REQUESTS R1) they go to masters. A mod+ device that cannot open the newest lock's change key extends the newest lock it holds after `RESET_GRACE` (the relay takes it only past dead links). HOL-SEC-062, harness `t14l_a_removed_device_loses_the_door` (every MLS send of the newest door went under a tree without the leaf), `t14l_device_removals_move_the_lock_at_most_once_per_gap`, unit `lock_keeper::tests::a_removal_settles_only_on_a_door_newer_than_its_leaf` |

## 20. The relay and its sibling-lane stamps (design A-D4 and A-D5, security audit session 17, 2026-09-29)

Relay state is not the store, but a relay write the wrong client can make is the same
bug class, so the rows live here. HOL-SEC-063..068.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| An authenticated socket | `relay-uws/src/ws_handler.cpp` :: handle_auth | v2: a signature over the relay's own domain, the nonce handed to THIS socket (one attempt) and every flag (`auth_v2_message`); a v1 frame (no `v`) does not parse (`parse_auth_frame`) |
| A room slot for a fetch socket | handle_join (fetch branch) | Never over a slot its device's full socket holds; tracked in the socket's own `fetch_rooms`; no roster, presence or listing |
| Inbox owner status | `inbox_owner_by_roster` -> `RosterBook::show` | The shown roster is folded into the one the relay holds for that master (same rules as the apps, section 22) and the socket owns the inbox only while its device is a member; a shown roster decides on its own; a change drops every owner it no longer counts. A 0.11 master-signed list (`inbox_proof`) is not read (live `test_inbox_proof`). Kept through plain re-joins of the same socket |
| Who hears whom in an inbox | `receives_in_room` (every fan-out, roster, presence, discovery, check_peers) | Owners only; a deposit for the master reaches the owners live and the mailbox |
| A ring's creation, retention or stop | `handle_set_topic_buffer` + `ring_auth::authorized` | Signed by the change key of the relay's newest join-lock link for the server, within ten minutes of the relay's clock, as `hollow-ring2` naming THIS relay's `auth_domain` (`"v":2`; the client stamps relay time, so an unnamed control could be stamped ahead by one relay and replayed at another holding the same chain); a relay with no domain takes none; `hollow-ring1` is gone (0.14): a signed control without `"v":2` does not parse, so it is no control and refreshes nothing (`ring_auth::parse`), and a ring1 signature never verifies as ring2; in a legacy (32-hex) room every channel must lie in the signing owner's topics (`{owner}.{channel}`, `in_own_topics`) and a stop reaches only those (HOL-SEC-071); unsigned = refresh only (live `test_rings`: an unsigned control opens no ring). Harness: `authz_only_the_servers_authority_changes_its_rings`, `authz_a_legacy_servers_rings_follow_its_owner_not_the_first_signer`; unit `a_ring_control_counts_only_at_the_relay_it_names`, C++ `test_ring_auth` (pinned ring2 vector; "a signed control without its form (ring1) is no control at all", "a ring1 signature is no control at this relay"), live `test_rings` (a ring1 control opens nothing, nor does a ring1 signature sent as ring2, nor another relay's ring2) |
| A ring frame | the 0x07 tee | At most 256 KB; eviction takes the oldest frame of the address share holding the most bytes; a frame keeps the shorter of its arrival retention and the ring's current one |
| Anything a stranger can make the relay hold: waiting frames, rings, join lock chains, destroy orders, push registrations, failed link-code guess records (`link_guess.h`, 65,536, `test_link_guess_flood_keeps_others`) | `fair_share.h` via `socket_share` (every writer) | Charged to the writer's address share (v4 address or v6 /48, BLAKE2b under an hourly key); a full table evicts from the share holding the most, least recently used first; byte budgets for frames (with 1 KB overhead each), lock chains and registrations. Nothing refuses. HOL-SEC-069/070; C++ `test_fair_share` and the table tests |
| Idle connection-rate windows (an address that closed its last socket inside the minute) | `conn_rate.h` IdleBook via `session_bounds.h` (`admit_socket`, `free_ip_slot`, `sweep_rate_windows`) | `ip_idle` holds at most 65,536 addresses; full evicts the least recently idled entry of the exact block (v4 address or v6 /48) holding the most, which only lets that address in sooner and never refuses anyone. Idle addresses get 30 new sockets a minute, addresses holding a socket 10. RAM only, never snapshotted; C++ `test_conn_rate`, `test_session_bounds` `test_rate_limit_idle_address` |
| A socket's topic subscriptions | `handle_subscribe` | Room code valid; at most 1,024 rooms and 16,384 topics per socket, topics up to 128 bytes; a set past a cap is dropped (that room unfiltered). HOL-SEC-069 |
| A nickname binding | `handle_claim_nickname` | A claim the named master signed for this socket's device within 300 s (`nickname_claim_message`); an unsigned claim is refused (`invalid_claim`, live `test_nicknames`). The resolver checks it again (`nick_claim::verified_master`) and a lookup sends nothing. Harness: `authz_a_nickname_names_only_a_master_that_signed_for_it` |
| TURN URIs, the relay's forwarder, a license refusal (client) | `ws_client::turn_uris_on_relay`, swarm `MediaForwarderInfo` arm + Dart `forwarderPinDecision`, `ConnectError::License` | Relay host only; never a known identity, pinned per relay; exact refusal codes, the key never erased |
| Meeting knocks, lobby info, denials, ends, kicks and the meeting Welcome | swarm relay-frame opener + `conference::open_meeting` (`MeetingSealed`, `Lane::Meeting`) | Open only under the meeting link's key (`key=`), in that meeting's room, for the sealing device, and only holding a message for that same meeting; a plaintext copy falls to the lane check. Harness: `c24_a_meeting_shows_the_relay_no_name_and_no_host`, `authz_a_meeting_frame_counts_only_under_its_link_key`. HOL-SEC-072 |
| Recovery pool traffic | `recovery_pool.rs` (`RecoverySealed`, `Lane::Recovery`) | Opens only under the pool token in its own room for its sender; the room is named by a hash of the token. Harness: `authz_a_recovery_frame_counts_only_with_the_pool_token` |
| Sibling-lane stamps (emote rows, read markers, friendship stamps) | swarm arms via `frame_auth::stamp_ceiling` | No more than 300 s past the frame's time: emote rows refused, markers and friendship stamps clamped. Harness: `authz_a_sibling_lane_stamp_never_runs_ahead_of_its_frame` |

## 21. Identity authority: the roster and the recovery phrase (design ID-1, security audit sessions 20-22, 2026-09-30/10-01)

BUILT, tested and driven on throwaway fleet peers (session 22); findings HOL-SEC-002,
076, 077. Design: `reports/shipped/security/audit/design_ID1_identity_authority.md`
(section 7a: the rules the build forced). The master-signed device list of sections 5 and
13 is REPLACED by the roster; those rows describe the pre-0.12 model. Every rule below
survived a mutation pass (22/22 roster rules and 9/9 link rules fail a named test when put
back; each session-22 rule fails its own test alone).

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Which devices are an identity's (every carrier: profile, card, friend request/accept/reject, join request, `RosterNotice`) | `node/roster_book.rs::ingest` over `identity/roster.rs` | Every statement verifies alone under its own `hollow-id1-*` tag; a device counts only with its own key's consent to that master; membership comes from the fold (recovery base, vouches, 7-day pending joins on THIS observer's clock, legacy claims only until the first recovery, removals as a union, counted only from a rooted signer); the first recovery key seen is pinned; a roster for a master we hold nothing about is kept only from one of its members. The resolver holds members only; "added" is judged against the SAVED links, so a pending join that matured is saved and alerted. Unit: `identity::roster::tests`, `roster_book::tests::authz_*`. Harness: `authz_a_stolen_backup_is_never_a_member_until_approved`, `authz_the_phrase_takes_the_identity_back_from_a_stolen_device`, `a_restored_device_matures_at_a_contact_after_seven_quiet_days`, `friend_reject_with_bad_carried_list_is_dropped`, `replayed_device_list_from_an_unlisted_device_does_not_bind` |
| A sender's attribution from a carried roster | `roster_book::carried_master` after ingest, every carried arm in swarm; `carried_member` for carried key bundles | The sender must be a member of what we now hold for that master; a carried roster is judged merged with ours, so a forged recovery key admits nobody once we hold the real one. Unit: `verify_carried_bundle_accepts_valid_and_rejects_tampered` |
| What a device that contacts refuse must still tell them (a pending join, a phrase change) | `roster_book::{announce_pending, announce_phrase_change}` -> `fan_out` | In the clear (`RosterNotice`, Relay lane, not gated on a revoked sender): own inbox mailbox, each friend's whole DM room (never `devices_for`, empty for a single-device friend), each server room. Harness: `authz_the_phrase_takes_the_identity_back_from_a_stolen_device` |
| Our own roster | `roster_book::{ensure_own, vouch, remove, remove_all_others, remove_self, recover, admit_by_phrase}` | Only a current member signs vouches and removals; the phrase (FFI `api/roster.rs`, never handed to the node) signs recoveries and phrase admissions; a pending or removed device never seeds itself |
| Sibling state (friends, servers, DM backfill) | `swarm::on_verified_sibling` | Roster members only; the master-key sibling proof is deleted |
| An MLS leaf's seat and its messages | `mls_authority::refused`, swarm MLS arm, `fetch.rs` | Revoked, or disowned (`resolver::disowns`: we hold that master's devices and the roster does not admit this one). Unit: `disowned_devices_are_never_added_or_welcomed_and_always_removable`, `disowns_judges_only_a_known_master`; source scan `channel_ingest_gates_stay_wired` |
| A destroy order (own and friend) | `destroy::{judge_own_order, apply_friend_order}` via `crypto_handler::destroy_order_authorised` | The pinned recovery key's signature, or a member device holding a phrase-signed permission for ITSELF (duress scope); the master alone only for an identity with no recovery key yet. Unit: `authz_a_destroy_order_needs_the_phrase_once_protected`; harness `authz_a_destroy_order_needs_the_phrase` (Olm lane, kill list, friend); api `duress_everywhere_*` |
| What leaves the device in a backup or link snapshot; what an import keeps | `api/storage.rs::scrubbed_db_copy` (export) and `land_snapshot` -> `clean_staged_database` (import, on the STAGED database before anything moves) via `MessageStore::scrub_device_secrets` | No Olm account or sessions, no MLS identity, no stored phrase; every restored or linked device mints its own (a link installs the key its presenter vouched for). A scrub that fails on import FAILS the landing (the old identity stays): the device would otherwise speak as the source. Unit: `snapshots_leave_device_secrets_behind_both_ways`, `a_snapshot_whose_scrub_fails_never_lands` |
| A device link (HOL-SEC-002) | `node/link_pake.rs` + `node/link_handler.rs` (LinkState in the event loop) | The relay sees only the code's 6-char rendezvous part; SPAKE2 on the 4-char secret part, bound to the rendezvous; the presenter confirms with an HMAC the joiner checks before sealing anything; the code answers ONE handshake, only from its room, and a hello that does not open burns it; everything after is AES-256-GCM per direction with the rendezvous and direction in the AAD; the snapshot key travels inside the channel and the presenter vouches for the device key the joiner minted. Harness: `link_the_relay_cannot_open_the_snapshot`, `authz_a_relay_that_answers_the_code_gets_one_guess`, `authz_link_frames_from_a_stranger_are_refused`; unit `link_pake::tests` (incl. `every_layer_binds_on_its_own`), `a_pending_link_installs_the_device_key_it_was_made_for` |
| What the roster FFI may create | `api/roster.rs::ctx` | An EXISTING identity only (`load_existing_identity`): Home asks for the roster behind Welcome, and a minted key skipped Welcome on a fresh install. Unit: `a_roster_read_never_mints_an_identity` |
| This device's lock (removed, or waiting to join) | `hollow_shell._syncRosterLock` over `rosterGateProvider`; start-up `DeviceListUpdated` for our master | The gate re-reads whenever our own roster moves, so a restored backup locks without a restart; the lock, the join ask and the phrase upgrade (which shows the stored phrase) go up only once the app lock's cover lifts (`_untilUnlocked`). Harness `a_restored_device_tells_its_ui_once_it_waits`; the cover order seen on screen (session 22) |
| An answer to a join ask (Approve vouches, Refuse removes) | `roster_lock.dart::showPendingDeviceDialog` | Asked only while the roster still holds the device as pending; an open ask closes when the roster settles it elsewhere, since its Refuse would remove a member. Widget `pending_device_dialog_test` (each layer alone) |
| Which devices stay refused after a removal (memory and disk) | `roster_book::{point_resolver, revoked_rows}` -> `resolver::{mark_revoked_by, unmark_revoked_by}`, `MessageStore::{record_revoked_devices, clear_revoked_devices}` (`revoked_marks` table, keyed by device AND master since 0.13) | A roster's removal marks the device for THAT master; only that master's own recovery lifts its mark, so a removed key that consents into another identity stays refused, whichever roster saves last. `is_revoked` = any held roster removed it (an install mints a fresh key per identity). 0.14 (clean break) ends the 0.12 one-row-per-device `revoked_devices`: at the first open its rows are copied into `revoked_marks` and the table dropped in ONE transaction (`move_old_revocations`); records write the marks only. Fail closed: while a failed move leaves the old table, `revoked_marks()` reads both and a lift takes the old row only when it names the lifting master, so the next start's move never revives a lift and no removal is lost. Units (memory and disk) `g13c_a_removed_device_joining_another_identity_stays_refused_for_its_old_one`, `g13c_a_lifting_by_one_master_leaves_anothers_removal`, `w2c_old_revocations_move_into_the_marks_and_the_old_table_goes`, `w2c_a_failed_move_keeps_the_old_table_and_refuses`, `w2c_a_lift_before_the_move_takes_only_its_own_old_row`; harness `g13w3_a_removed_device_joining_another_identity_stays_refused_for_its_old_one` covers the path end to end in memory only (the harness shares ONE resolver, so X's own node decides the in-memory mark before the friend's store is consulted) |
| A removed own device told its removal again (0.13) | `roster_book::tell_removed_device_in_room`, from the `PeerJoined` arm (arrived here) and from a `RoomMembers` refresh that lists the device | Only a device our held roster removed (`is_revoked` prefilter), only in a room we see, never in a `fwd:` room and never to a bare master (G1); it hears the roster as a `RosterNotice`, so a device removed while it was connecting learns it the moment it appears, without waiting for a later live change. Harness `g13w3_a_device_removed_while_connecting_learns_it_when_it_appears`, `g13w3_a_removed_device_listed_in_a_room_refresh_learns_it`, `g13w3_only_our_removed_device_hears_the_roster_when_it_appears`, `g13w3_the_arrival_notice_alone_skips_a_bare_master` |

## 22. ID-1R: the relay learns rosters, and what mirroring the fold fixed (session 25, 2026-10-02)

Design: `reports/shipped/security/audit/design_ID1R_relay_rosters.md`; findings
HOL-SEC-078..081. Relay DEPLOYED 2026-10-02.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Which statements a roster keeps (every ingest, merge and the relay's book) | `identity/roster.rs::compacted` (mirrored by `relay-uws/src/roster.h`) | A vouch or removal stays only when its SIGNER has standing (an admission path in the current base: the phrase's roots, or a pending join, then vouches), whatever it names; what stays is ordered by the signer's rank (tier, depth, id) before the ceilings cut (128 vouches, 96 removals, 16 kept per removal), each standing device's best vouch first, so compaction is stable and a flood pushes out nothing shallower than its signer. HOL-SEC-079. Vouches (HOL-SEC-189, 0.14, embargoed): the removal walk runs over every held statement first; kept first are each standing device's vouch from its nearest VALID voucher, the vouches that made each walk-alive signer valid at its turn (RC32-10), and each device's best-ranked vouch; the rest give way by validity, then signer rank. The cap's walk counts only tier-0 signers' removals (a master-key pending join never demotes a vouch), and standing is ranked over the consents the cap keeps (the best 96, ranked again). Units `hol_sec_189_*`, `review189_*`, `authz_pending_joins_removing_the_phrases_devices_never_displace_them`, `compaction_is_idempotent_under_a_vouch_flood`; C++ `test_roster` (vectors `hol-sec-189-*`, each compacts to itself). Unit `authz_a_strangers_flood_pushes_no_statement_out`, `authz_a_members_flood_never_displaces_a_signer_closer_to_the_phrase`, `authz_pending_joins_never_displace_the_phrases_devices`, `compaction_is_stable`, `the_largest_roster_fits_on_the_wire` |
| A removed device's vouchees | `Roster::fold` (`kept_by`) | Kept only when EVERY counting removal of it keeps them. HOL-SEC-080. Unit `authz_a_removed_device_cannot_bring_a_device_back_through_its_own_removal`, `a_removed_device_keeps_only_what_every_removal_keeps` |
| Whether waiting admits a device | `Recovery.no_wait` (inside the phrase's signature, `:nowait`) | Only the phrase sets or clears it (`api/roster.rs::set_backup_wait`, a recovery keeping every current member); with it no pending join matures, at contacts or the relay. Unit `the_phrase_can_turn_off_joining_by_waiting` |
| Our own self-removal (destroy scope (b)) | `roster_book::remove_self` | Keeps every current member it vouched for. HOL-SEC-081. Unit `removing_itself_keeps_the_devices_it_linked` |
| What the relay holds for an identity | `RosterBook::show`, `restore` (snapshot v7) | Folded from every roster shown on its inbox (anyone's: each statement verifies alone); refused whole past a ceiling, over 256 KiB or for another master; first sights on the relay's clock; fair-share 128 MB, charged to the member who last showed it. C++ vectors `test/roster_vectors.json` (130 cases, regenerate with `HOLLOW_WRITE_ROSTER_VECTORS=1`); harness `authz_the_master_key_alone_never_owns_a_protected_inbox`, `authz_a_removed_device_loses_the_inbox_at_once`, `mailbox_requires_a_roster_that_counts_the_device` |
| When the relay hears our roster | `roster_book::show_relay` | On every connect, after every ingest that changes our roster, and after every change this device makes (`announce_roster_change`), so a removal locks the removed device out at once even when it hears nothing |

## 27. Phase B's leftovers closed (session 33, 2026-10-04)

| Path | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Join asks reaching a member that may have missed a removal | the join lane arm in swarm.rs -> `join_hold::JoinHold::hold`; release at the loop head through the same `handle_incoming_request` | A parked ask read during our own `~join` replay waits for the relay's end mark (`topic_catchup` with `end`, answered by `topic_catchup_done`); on a server held while no socket was up, ANY ask waits until a member besides the asker answered a sync ask of this connection (the `nonce` it echoes, so a buffered old answer never counts), at most 10 s after the room shows; while the relay hides us in that room (back after a lock move, or the 60 s grace ended), every ask is dropped. Back from away (a new socket or a gapped resume, which re-reads every chain, `lock_keeper::reread`), asks also wait for this connection's read of the relay's lock, and while its newest door is not one our STATE holds they are held, never judged, until it is (a member handed us the door before the sync that brings the move; the 10 s wait forces nothing here) and dropped unjudged after 60 s, the relay's grace after a move, which can still show a resumed session the room and its ring (0.14 t14-reset; unit `a_member_back_judges_nothing_behind_the_relays_lock`, harness `t14_reset_a_member_resumed_in_the_doors_grace_judges_no_ask`, `authz_a_member_back_hidden_answers_no_direct_ask` for the honest parked ask judged once the door's sync lands); no read inside the 10 s judges as before. Residual (INFO): the relay can answer the lock read with an older chain it once held (a replayed `lock_chain`), and an empty chain reads as no lock, so judging goes on as before the read; both need a lying relay that already sees the room. HOL-SEC-121 incl. the live-ask and direct-ask variants. Harness `authz_a_member_back_from_away_gives_a_kicked_joiners_old_ask_nothing`, `authz_a_member_back_from_away_admits_no_banned_identitys_live_ask`, `authz_a_member_back_hidden_answers_no_direct_ask`, `authz_a_member_that_loses_the_room_answers_no_direct_ask` |
| Forwarder rooms (relay) | `relay-uws/src/fwd_room.h` `paired`, `Audience::shares`, the direct/0x02/0x04/0x09 handlers | In `fwd:{X}` each member knows of and reaches X alone; X everyone; no ring. HOL-SEC-126. `test_fwd_room.cpp`, live "forwarder rooms", harness `authz_a_stranger_in_a_forwarder_room_meets_only_the_forwarder` (MockRelay mirrors it) |
| Channel copies and pushes (0x09) | `handle_binary_channel_direct` | Only from a socket that sees the room (a door prover in a locked room). HOL-SEC-127. Live "a hidden socket leaves no channel copy", harness `authz_a_socket_the_room_hides_leaves_no_channel_copy` |
| Olm decrypt failures | both swarm Olm arms (PreKey and normal) -> `OlmManager::read_past`; the mark moves in `persist_olm_read` (node) and `fetch::handle_binary_frame` (push fetch, iOS extension) | A failure on a frame sealed at or before the newest seal we decrypted from that device is dropped quietly; a newer one re-keys as before. The mark lives in `olm_read_marks` (MAX on write, after the session pickle). AR-19's Olm half. Harness `olm_a_frame_replayed_after_a_restart_leaves_the_session_alone`, `olm_a_sender_back_on_an_older_copy_of_its_session_is_re_keyed` |
| Meeting seats | `conference::unseat_refused` (host, loop head on a resolver move + MLS batch tick), `drop_leafless_from_call` (host and every member merging a meeting commit), the MLS voice join (`conference::seated`) | The host commits out every refused or unbound leaf in one commit; a device with no leaf leaves the call everywhere; a past epoch that still decrypts no longer seats a voice join. HOL-SEC-123 residual. Harness `authz_the_host_unseats_a_device_its_roster_drops_mid_meeting`, `authz_a_device_out_of_the_meeting_group_loses_its_call_for_good` |
| Join timers (reliability) | `CheckPendingJoinTimeout` / `RetryPendingJoin` carry `opened_at`; `join_lane::went_out` | A timer acts only on the ask that armed it; the re-ask window counts from the first copy that went out, and a joiner the room hides re-asks the whole room. Harness `a_join_timer_acts_only_on_the_ask_that_armed_it`, `a_join_whose_lock_read_lands_late_is_still_asked_again`, `join_survives_a_coordinator_that_vanished_silently` |

Residuals: a member alone judges a parked ask with what the ring gave it; after 10 s an ask is judged on what we hold; a stranger host never unseats (AR-15); the media plane of an unseated device is not harness-covered; a sender whose clock steps back waits for a real re-key (AR-19).

## 32. Session 42 (2026-10-08/09): the identity landing (team wave t1)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Identity landing (link import, backup restore) | `api/storage.rs::land_snapshot` via `import_snapshot_bytes` / `import_backup_bytes` / `import_pending_link`; `recover_interrupted_landing` at `has_pending_link` and before every landing | A remote snapshot reaches it only through the SPAKE2 link pipeline, after `check_snapshot`'s allowlist. The identity set (messages.db, -wal, -shm, identity.device, identity.key) swaps as ONE: staged in `.landing/`, the old set moved to `.replaced/` (3 s wait for a held file, else refused with the old pair kept) and marked `moved`, then committed; the fsynced ROOT mark `.landed` is the commit point; cleanup places content, drops `moved`, deletes `.replaced/`, removes `.landed` last. Recovery: with `.landed` only finish the cleanup, never undo; without it undo (with `moved`: root set out, old set back); a put-back never overwrites a root file. Nothing lands or is put back while a wipe is pending; `load_or_create_identity` mints nothing while a landing runs. A stash that cannot land gets 3 launches (`pending_link.tries`, reset by every new stash); a refused one is dropped at once. Field incident: a program outside Hollow held messages.db and the old `let _ = remove_file` landed a new identity beside the throwaway DB ("file is not a database"). `landing_tests::*` incl. `a_link_never_lands_beside_a_database_another_program_holds`, `a_finished_landing_survives_a_crash_at_every_cleanup_step`, `t1_review_a_held_replaced_file_never_undoes_a_finished_landing` |
| Boot wipe marker | `api/storage.rs::perform_pending_wipe` | The marker stays while any identity-set file, `.landing/`, `.replaced/` or `.landed` survives (3 s wait); while it stays, `load_or_create_identity` and `land_snapshot` refuse with `IDENTITY_NOT_READY`, the SAME words a landing uses, so nothing on screen names a wipe (duress); landing recovery is skipped; still logs nothing. `a_boot_wipe_keeps_its_marker_while_the_database_survives`, `a_boot_wipe_keeps_its_marker_while_a_landings_leftover_is_held`, `a_pending_wipe_refuses_in_the_words_a_landing_uses`, `t1_review_a_held_boot_wipe_never_names_a_wipe` |
| A sibling's server announce | see §18 "A pending join from a sibling" (durable, `PendingJoin::from_sibling`, quiet `ServerCreated`) | t1-sibling, reviewed. |

The three items once open here (tmp8.txt 2a-2c) are closed in §34.

## 33. Session 43 (2026-10-09): fleet speed wave fs

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A synced DM landing on a stored row that has no mid | `MessageStore::reconcile_dm_by_timestamp` from DmSyncBatch and DmSiblingSyncBatch (`swarm.rs`), after the backfill signature and `change_may_touch_row` on the item's own mid | fs-olm: only a row with NO mid, in the item's conversation, at its timestamp AND in its direction; a row with any mid is another message and is never touched. Before, any same-millisecond row with another mid and other text qualified, so a burst merged two DMs into one row (one lost for good, the survivor keeping the other's `file_id`, `reply_to`, `order_us` and album under the new text and signature), a write `change_may_touch_row` never judged; an own item could also land on a received row. The direction keeps the row's author the item's signer (the DM rule of `change_may_touch_row`). The row becomes exactly the signed item (mid, text, signature, reply_to, file_id, order_us, album, edited_at), so it re-serves under that signature. Unit `a_same_millisecond_dm_is_never_reconciled_onto_another_message`, `a_row_without_a_mid_takes_its_synced_copy_whole`, `a_synced_dm_never_reconciles_onto_a_row_of_the_other_direction`; harness `a_burst_past_the_bucket_from_an_online_friend_is_asked_for_again`, `a_dm_stored_without_a_mid_takes_its_synced_copy_once` (both batches, both directions) |
| An Olm session built from a KeyBundle | `swarm.rs` KeyBundle arm | Unchanged gates first (device signature, recipient, freshness, device in its master's signed list). The arm no longer defers for glare: the `key_bundle_sent_to` record went stale (a bundle that never left, never got read, or got used long ago) and made the higher device wait for a PreKey nobody sent. `OlmManager::open_prekey` is the one glare rule (the lower device keeps its session, the higher switches, replaced sessions retire). `olm_an_answered_bundle_is_no_glare_later`, `olm_a_bundle_the_peer_never_read_is_no_glare`, `olm_a_key_bundle_that_never_left_is_no_glare`, `olm_true_glare_converges_on_the_lower_devices_session` |

## 34. Session 45 (2026-10-10): identity integrity (wave md, tmp8 item 2)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A key written by a phrase restore or a new identity | `identity/keys.rs::restore_identity_from_mnemonic` / `generate_new_identity` (also the no-key branch of `load_or_create_identity`), both through `land_identity_key` | Local only. (1) `api/storage.rs::refuse_while_not_ready`: no key while a wipe is pending, a landing or a relink (§35) runs, or THIS process destroyed the root (below) (`IDENTITY_NOT_READY`, the landing's words). (2) The key is staged first (`identity.key.tmp`, flushed and read back), then `keep_aside_a_database_this_identity_cannot_open` opens the database under the NEW identity's key; one it does not open (another identity's, or damaged; SQLite NOTADB) moves aside KEPT as `messages.db.unreadable-<UTC stamp>` (+`-wal`, `-shm`, `-journal`, all or none, `set_database_aside`, the name family md-recovery's repair shares); one that cannot be read at all (held) refuses. A DB the phrase's own identity opens stays. (3) Only then (a restore also moves an encrypted key to its `.locked` name) is the staged key renamed in; a key that does not land (held, read-only on Windows, a full disk) puts the database back (`put_database_back`, never over a file in place) and the `.locked` key back, and zeroes the staged copy, so the identity still in the root keeps its database (review 1 finding 3). Kept aside, never refused: a different phrase is a person choosing another identity on this device, and the kept file still opens with the old phrase: before the rename, where the root then holds no database, the newest kept `messages.db.unreadable-*` the NEW key opens is put back (`put_back_a_kept_database_this_identity_opens`; a kept file it cannot read refuses the landing, `IDENTITY_NOT_READY`), and a key that does not land moves it aside again under its own name (`move_database_to`). `md_review_merge_the_right_phrase_after_a_wrong_one_gets_its_database_back`, `md_merge_a_right_phrase_that_does_not_land_leaves_both_databases_where_they_were`; fleet A4/A5. `a_phrase_for_another_identity_never_lands_beside_its_database`, `a_new_identity_never_lands_beside_a_database_it_cannot_open`, `a_phrase_restore_never_lands_beside_a_database_it_cannot_read`, `a_phrase_restore_never_lands_while_a_wipe_is_pending`, `a_key_that_does_not_land_puts_everything_back`, `md_review_a_restore_that_cannot_write_its_key_leaves_the_database_in_place` (Windows), `a_kept_database_takes_its_rollback_journal_along` |
| Welcome's choices after a held boot wipe | FFI `load_or_create_identity`, `generate_new_identity`, `restore_identity_from_mnemonic`, `import_backup` via `finish_wipe_before_a_new_identity`; Dart `_finishPendingWipe` before the Welcome path's load (settles a finished destroy's profile as the boot does) | Where the marker is, the wipe runs again first and the choice goes on once it finishes, so the refusal clears when the holder lets go, not at a relaunch (the stuck refusal was the duress tell). The re-run is gated on what THIS process has open, never on a key file (review 1 finding 4: a held KEY kept Restore a backup refused all session): no store singleton, no full node, no fetch node (`api/network.rs::a_node_runs`); with any of them open it refuses instead (after an in-session destroy the node still runs until Dart's relaunch). The old file copy after an ignored `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` left behind every row a reader kept in the WAL. Under WAL it is ONE read, which never blocks a writer. Under a rollback journal (iOS, TRUNCATE) a reader blocks every commit, so it copies 256 pages a step with a 2 ms pause: a waiting writer holds its PENDING lock, the next step answers busy until the writer has committed, and no writer's 4 s busy wait runs out behind a large snapshot. |---|---|---|
