A fresh database is stamped `device_linked_at_ms:{device}` from the earliest time the identity files show (an older destroy order still counts); that time is written to `repair_link_time` (flushed) BEFORE anything moves and removed after the stamp, so a start after a crash in between stamps it, not its own time (`node/destroy.rs::stamp_device_link`). A kept copy with a `-wal` OR a `-journal` beside it is read `mode=ro`, never immutable (an immutable read skips a hot journal). Refuses while not ready (as Repair). Order: the origin mark `relink_origin.json` (naming THIS root) and the root journal `relink.json` FIRST, then a keychain-locked identity's key is stored in the kept folder's own slot AND read back (refused, nothing moved, when it does not keep), then the database set, the rest, `identity.*` with `identity.key` last, into the sibling `<root>-old-<UTC day>[-n]`; removing the journal is the commit point. | Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A block or unblock from a sibling | swarm FriendListSync arm -> `social::take_sibling_blocks` -> `MessageStore::block_peer_at` / `unblock_peer_at`, then `blocklist`, `handle_block_changed`, `NetworkEvent::BlockListChanged` | Only from our own roster-counted, unrevoked device (the arm gate it shares with removals); each stamp held to the frame (`stamp_ceiling`); per master only the newest of its block and its lifting in the list counts (a person blocked again appears as both), and it wins: a block lands only when none is held and it is newer than the last lifting (the `block_lifted:{master}` settings tombstone every unblock writes), a lifting ends only a block older than it; never any id of our own identity; newest 256 entries per list. Rides every sibling friend list (`FriendListSync.blocks`: both convergence paths and the state-sync answer) and goes live on every block and unblock (`share_block_with_siblings`). A block or unblock made HERE is stamped past the opposite word we hold (`block_peer` = `max(wall, block_lifted_at + 1)`, `unblock_peer` = `max(wall, blocked_at + 1)`), so a lifting a sibling stamped with a clock ahead never undoes a newer block (md-review). Upgrade edge: an unblock made before 0.12.1 left no `block_lifted:` tombstone, so a sibling still holding the older block re-blocks that person once after the upgrade (fails toward more blocking; unblock again). The store reads run in `spawn_blocking` (`own_block_entry`, `take_sibling_blocks`). | A sibling's friendship after our own removal | swarm `FriendListSync` arm -> `social::ended_by_removal` | A removal made on THIS device is stamped past the friendship it ends (`max(now, since + 1)`) and recorded as `friend_removed_here:{master}`; a sibling's listed friendship stamped before it is the ended one and is not re-added (strict). Removals a frame carried keep the `LIVE_SKEW_MS` slack (`older_than_removal`), since their stamp may sit that far ahead. Before, a friend removed within 5 minutes of the friendship came back from a sibling's older list, and the two devices then re-added and removed it from each other's lists for good (fleet gate R6, fwd-Background remove_friend). Harness `md_a_sibling_list_never_brings_back_a_friend_we_just_removed` |
| A server we leave (or a member we kick or ban) to our own devices | `sync_handler::broadcast_removal_op` -> `olm_lane::carry_to_every_own_device` | Not a new gate (the receiving fold judges the op as before): the op goes live to each own device in a room and through our own inbox to every other device our roster counts, so a phone the relay hides (put away) drops the server at once instead of ~20 s after it comes back, and an offline one gets it from the relay's buffer. One copy per device. Harness `md_a_hidden_sibling_drops_a_server_we_leave_at_once` |

## 37. Session 45 (2026-10-11/15): an MLS add our roster has not placed yet (wave md, F1)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A received commit that adds a device our copy of its master's roster does not list | `mls_authority::commit_verdict` (`may_be_placed_later`), re-judged by `MlsManager::retry_held_commit` on every batch tick (swarm Phase 3) | HELD, never merged until our roster places the device; then it merges on the next tick (2 s). Still REFUSED: an add of a removed or revoked device (`resolver::is_revoked`, which every roster removal sets), of a master id (G1, bare or not), any add in a meeting (meetings never hold), and a commit carrying such an add next to an unplaced one. Bounds: `HELD_MAX_AGE` (60 s), `HELD_COMMITS_CAP` (8 per group, one per committer; a commit that finds the cap full is reported REFUSED, never "Holding", and leaves a lapsed record like one past its bound). Before, the add was refused for good and the refusal spent the commit's key, so a presenter that heard its own new device's roster a second after a friend committed it stayed an epoch behind with every catch-up failing (`SecretReuseError`). Unit `an_add_our_roster_has_not_placed_yet_holds_until_it_is_placed`, `a_removed_disowned_or_bare_master_leaf_neither_commits_nor_is_added`, `an_mls_add_waits_for_a_genuine_roster_and_a_removal_refuses_it` (real ingests: a forged roster places nothing, a removal refuses); harness `a_commit_adding_a_device_our_roster_has_not_placed_yet_is_held_not_refused` |
| A commit we processed and never merged, and the repair it calls for | `MlsManager::process_commit_judged` (`CommitError::Spent` on `SecretReuseError`) -> `crypto_handler::handle_mls_commit_frame` (`CommitApplyOutcome::Spent`) -> swarm `MlsCommitCatchup` arm -> `crypto_handler::repair_after_spent_catchup` -> `ask_leaf_repair`; swarm Phase 3 `lapsed_commit_passes` -> `send_epoch_probe` | The heal needs nothing kept in RAM (md-mls-2, review findings 1-4): a catch-up frame at our next epoch failing with `SecretReuseError` is a commit we processed and never merged (held past 60 s, dropped at the cap, held when the app closed, or REFUSED: a refusal spends the key too), so ONE repair is asked: only when the frame came from the member our own election names as catch-up responder (`epoch_catchup_responder`, whom `may_repair_our_leaf` trusts and who may ask for our KeyPackage anyway), only while no held commit of ours may still merge, throttled by `mls_bootstrap_requested` (60 s, the stamp that makes the repair's Welcome asked-for); the Welcome is judged by `welcome_verdict`. A live commit failing the same way only probes. Lapsed facts (RAM, `LAPSED_MAX_AGE` 10 min, our epoch unchanged) only speed this up: once they would pass we send one epoch probe with our digest, never a repair, so a member that adds its own device and vouches it after the bound costs a repair only where the group really moved past us. `ask_leaf_repair` checks membership, ban, a subgroup's channel visibility, that the responder is reachable and that no Welcome of ours waits (`welcome_waits`, the repair already on its way) BEFORE it mints. Accepted (review, LOW 2): a commit we refused, re-served by our responder, asks a repair once a minute; that heals a group that only lagged our view, and where the refused leaf is still in the group the repair's Welcome is refused (bounded churn, one epoch a minute until the views meet). Follow-up: the other leafless askers (swarm `PeerJoined`/`RoomMembers`/`MlsChannelMessage`, `tend_own_subgroups`, `voice_handler`, `message_ops`) do not yet check `welcome_waits`; they want one shared may-ask-for-a-leaf predicate. Unit `a_commit_processed_before_says_its_key_is_spent_and_one_dropped_at_the_cap_is_refused`, `a_leaf_repair_mints_nothing_it_may_not_ask_or_cannot_send`, `a_commit_held_past_its_bound_lapses_unmerged_and_calls_for_a_repair_once_it_passes`, `a_lapsed_commit_is_forgotten_when_refused_or_passed_by_our_epoch`, review `a_commit_dropped_at_the_held_cap_still_leaves_a_lapsed_record`; harness `a_spent_commit_brings_one_repair_from_our_responder_once_nothing_is_held`, `an_add_vouched_after_the_bound_costs_no_repair_where_the_group_did_not_move`, `a_held_add_whose_roster_never_comes_lapses_unmerged_and_heals_only_once_placed`, review `md_review_mls_a_restart_inside_a_held_add_still_heals_once_placed` |
| A Welcome seating a device our copy of its master's roster does not list | `mls_authority::welcome_verdict` (`may_be_placed_later`), re-judged by `MlsManager::retry_held_welcome` (swarm Phase 3); `MlsManager::seat` | HELD (60 s from the first held Welcome, each batch tick; a Welcome replacing a held one keeps that wait), never installed until our roster places the device; its KeyPackage is put back while it waits and spent only if it installs. Still REFUSED: a removed or revoked device, a master id, any meeting Welcome, a Welcome carrying such a leaf next to an unplaced one, and every whole-Welcome refusal (another group, not our leaf, an unbound leaf, replacing our group unasked). While a Welcome waits (`Seat::WelcomeWaits`) neither the eviction grace nor the leafless re-ask mints another KeyPackage (no second repair). Before, the repair Welcome that carries us past a spent commit was refused when the group held another member's just-linked device. Unit `a_welcome_naming_a_device_our_roster_has_not_placed_yet_holds_until_it_is_placed`, `disowned_devices_are_never_added_or_welcomed_and_always_removable`, `a_held_welcome_is_a_seat_on_its_way_and_keeps_our_key_package`, `a_held_welcome_replaced_by_another_keeps_its_bound`, review `md_review_mls_2_no_second_leaf_repair_while_the_first_ones_welcome_waits`; harness `a_repair_welcome_naming_an_unplaced_device_is_held_not_refused` |

## 38. Stream overlay (#97, wave so, 2026-10-15)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| `user_profiles.stream_overlay` + `stream_overlay_at` (the `stream_overlay` field: `off` / `show` / `share:<look stamp>`) | `overlay::ingest_field`, the ONE gate, from `social::save_incoming_profile` (Olm `ProfileUpdate` and MLS `MessageEnvelope::ProfileUpdate`; once before the empty-profile guard and once more after a first profile made the row) and `profile_card::{store_card, keep_card}` (join request card, `ProfileCard`, `ProfileUpdate.card`, the sealed friend card, the meeting group line) | Its OWN master signature (`hollow-overlay1:` over sha256 of the length-prefixed stamp, master and value), outside `hollow-profile2` / `hollow-card1` so no shipped signature breaks; REQUIRED: unsigned, signed by a key that does not derive the master, changed after signing, or not in the closed grammar (`FieldValue::parse`, canonical decimal stamp) counts as ABSENT and what we stored is kept (absent reads as `show`). Stamped past the frame's `stamp_ceiling` (the meeting line: our own clock) is refused. Newest `at` wins in SQL (`stream_overlay_at < ?`), so a replayed older `share` never undoes an `off`. Written only onto an existing profile row for others (our own row is made by `publish_field`). The cards carry it because a pending friend and a meeting peer see only the card. Unit `a_field_verifies_only_under_its_own_masters_signature`, `ingest_keeps_only_a_signed_newer_field_and_off_removes_the_person`, `a_stream_overlay_field_is_newest_wins_and_needs_a_row_unless_ours`; harness `overlay_field_needs_its_own_signature_and_newest_wins`, `overlay_field_reaches_a_meeting_peer_through_the_card` |

Only a field that passed the gate above reacts. The removal reaches Dart as `OverlayPersonRemoved` through a notice queue keyed by the database file (`overlay::notice_key`), drained on the overlay tick, so ingest outside the event loop's task (push fetch, the blocking pool, the FFI) is never lost. The queue holds 256: past that a removal displaces a plain field change (any stranger can make one with a signed card, a removal needs a person we added) and the drop marks the queue LOST, which makes the loop send a catch-all `OverlayFieldChanged` (empty master: Dart re-reads everyone and publishes again) and pull our own look from a sibling. Every drained notice also re-projects the OBS server's last snapshot (`overlay_server::reproject`), so the page drops someone before Dart publishes; a block leaves a notice even for someone never added (they may stand in the room). | Our own look and field from a sibling (`app_settings.overlay_own_look`, our field) | `overlay::react_to_notices` (our own field changed, from the device that sent it) -> ask -> `overlay::apply_answer` | Our field arrives through the gate above like anyone's; the pull asks only devices the roster counts for our master; a sibling's look replaces ours only when its stamp is newer. Unit `a_sibling_look_replaces_ours_only_when_newer`; harness `overlay_look_reaches_own_sibling_on_stamp_change` |
| A look and its images from an answer (`overlay_people.their_look`, `held_stamp`; `overlay_blobs`, the overlay's OWN table, never `emote_blobs`) | swarm `HavenMessage::OverlayLookAnswer` arm -> `overlay::handle_envelope_overlay_look_answer` -> `apply_answer` (off the loop) | Lane `Carried`, `live_only`. Dropped WHOLE, before store and emit, when the sender's master is blocked (`blocklist::is_blocked`), or unless we hold a want for the sender's identity AND asked THAT device (`Want.asked`, per device, cleared on disconnect) AND the roster still counts it for that identity (`roster_counted`: not a bare master, not revoked). Nobody blocked is asked: `wanted_from_store` skips them and `dispatch` skips a want whose person was blocked since the last reload. Then only a person still on our overlay (or our own identity) takes one, and for anyone but us only while the field we hold for them says `share` NOW (consent is the current field, not the one we asked under: an answer still on its way after they turned sharing off stores nothing); a look that does not normalize refuses the answer; each image must be named in the answered look, hash to its name (bundle codec) and pass `image_convert::received_overlay_image_fits` (never anything the sender says): at most `MAX_OVERLAY_IMAGE_BYTES` (2 MB, the authoring limit), a RIFF/WEBP container, a canvas within `OVERLAY_DIM` (512), and for an animation its `ANMF` frames at the canvas size within `MAX_OVERLAY_DECODED_BYTES` (128 MiB, the authoring budget), read from the container before anything decodes it (a chunk running past the RIFF body refuses). Held = present in `overlay_blobs` (or stored by this answer): whether we re-ask never depends on what the rail holds, so a look cannot probe which emotes or stickers we hold, and an overlay write never claims or removes a rail row. Paging: an incomplete look re-asks the same device with the grown `held` set, at most `MAX_ROUNDS` before a rest. | Our field to the people who see our profile (a SEND) | `overlay::handle_command` (save off the loop, then `OverlayBook::want_announce`) -> the overlay tick -> `announce_own_field` | A toggle or look save never fans out on the event loop per change: changes inside `ANNOUNCE_WINDOW` (1 s) ride one announce; the message, our card, our roster and the friend rows the in-memory audience cannot place are read once on the blocking pool (`build_announce`), then each room peer gets what `social::profile_audience` allows (split into `profile_audience_known` + `audience_by_friend_status`, one ladder): the light profile, the card to a pending friend, our roster alone to a removed device of ours, nothing to anyone else; meetings get the card over MLS (`conference::broadcast_built_card`). Unit `field_announces_coalesce_per_window`, `an_announce_gives_each_peer_what_it_may_see`; harness `overlay_field_announces_coalesce` |
| Serving the OBS overlay (a SERVE; the only write is its own `overlay_server.json`) | `node::overlay_server`, reachable through `api::overlay_server` (`overlay_publish`, `overlay_publish_live`, `overlay_server_*`) | Bound to 127.0.0.1 only. The Host must be `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>` (DNS rebinding); an Origin must be absent or our own; the token (first path segment) compares in constant time; GET and HEAD only, 8 KiB head with a total deadline, 16 connections (a refused request does not keep its slot), 8 streams, 30 s idle; every refusal is a bare 404, no CORS, CSP `default-src 'none'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`. Starts LOCKED (Dart unlocks after App Lock and identity are open); locked = streams send `locked`, images 404; the wipe stops it. The snapshot Dart publishes is PROJECTED before any byte reaches a page: peer ids dropped, a non-self person dropped when their signed field says `off`, when we block them (`Backend::blocked`), or when the snapshot names no master (`Backend::field_off`, fail closed), names cleaned, `#rrggbb` only, looks run through `overlay_look::normalize` (a refused look draws the plain avatar; absent keys take the manifest's defaults), look images only when `overlay_blobs` holds them (`Backend::holds_overlay_image`) and, for anyone but us, only images they may be drawn with (`Backend::look_images` = `overlay::drawable_images`: our picks, and their own look only while their field says `share`), a non-self entry naming our own identity or a device of ours dropped (`Backend::local_master` + `resolver::resolve`; unknown while the node is down, then nobody counts as us), an animated avatar only when held on the rail as kind `profile`. An image is served only while the current projection names it, re-checked against its hash and kind and sniffed as an image. A field or block change re-projects the last snapshot (`reproject`, under the publish lock; streams hear only a projection that differs; a wipe in between brings nothing back). Nothing about paths or people is logged. ## 40. Getting back ops an older build skipped (B1, 2026-10-17)

An op this build cannot read whole (an unknown variant, or a known one with a field it drops, whose re-serialized payload fails the signature) is never sent again by a delta once a later op of its author arrived. It is KEPT as signed and read again by the next build; where that cannot cover it, the server is marked and fetched again whole.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Kept skipped ops (`server_blobs` key `crdt_resync_kept`: author, hlc, build, raw JSON) and the mark (`crdt_resync`: `due`, `lacks`, `lost`, `partial_from`) from a REMOTE frame | `CrdtResync::note_skipped`, from the plaintext/Olm `SyncResponse` arm, `crdt_resync::read_live_op` (in `apply_remote_crdt_op_inner`: carried broadcast, kick notice, join resolution; and `sync_handler::handle_envelope_crdt_op`, MLS) and `take_resync_page` | `operations::read_op` calls an op UNREAD only when the author's signature verifies over its EXACT payload bytes (serde_json `raw_value`) and either the payload does not parse or it re-serializes to other bytes (H2); a current member of the server it names must have signed it (`vouched`). Bounded per server (128 ops, 512 KiB, an op over 64 KiB not kept) and fair per author: over the bound, the author holding the most of what is over (ops or bytes) gives up its oldest first; anything not kept sets `lost`. `lacks = this build` asks nothing and refuses nothing: our pages say `partial`. Stored through the CRDT actor. Unit `only_a_members_signed_unknown_op_counts_as_skipped`, `review_a_known_variant_with_a_newer_field_counts_as_unread`, `kept_ops_are_bounded_and_a_flood_pushes_out_only_its_own`, `a_skip_by_this_build_outlives_a_whole_fetch`; harness `b1_a_signed_op_this_build_cannot_read_marks_the_server`, `b1_a_known_op_with_a_newer_field_is_kept_on_the_live_paths` |
| Kept ops folded in at start (a LOCAL re-ingest of remote bytes) | `CrdtResync::load`, before the event loop: kept rows of an older build go through `read_op` again; what reads now goes through `ServerState::ingest_remote` (the same fold, every op judged), persisted with `persist_admitted_ops` + the state in one transaction with the kept blob | The bytes are judged exactly as a live op would be, by this build. Still unreadable = kept as this build's skip; junk dropped. `due` only for what was not kept (`lost`, or an older `lacks` with nothing kept). Unit `a_newer_build_folds_in_what_an_older_one_kept`, `load_marks_every_server_once_and_turns_an_older_lost_skip_due`; harness `b1_a_kept_op_folds_in_at_start_with_nobody_online` |
| The first-start sweep (every held server `due` once per `SWEEP_GENERATION`, app setting `crdt_resync_sweep`) | `CrdtResync::load` | Local only. The setting is saved only after every mark landed, so a failed save sweeps again. Asks start after a random 0 to 120 s. Unit `load_marks_every_server_once_and_turns_an_older_lost_skip_due`; harness `b1_a_mark_with_nobody_to_answer_survives_restarts` |
| Ops from a page of a member's whole log (`SyncResponse.page`) | `swarm` `SyncResponse` arm -> `CrdtResync::claim` -> `take_resync_page` -> `crdt_resync::ingest_page` (spawn_blocking; a panic unwinds the loop, disk keeps the pre-page state) -> `ServerState::ingest_remote`; persisted via `CrdtStore::persist_admitted` + `save_state_snapshot` | A page counts only from the device we asked, with the nonce of the page we asked (fresh per page); any other page is DROPPED, never merged as a delta. We ask only while anchored (never legacy, AR-10) and a member. The mark clears after the LAST page of an ask folded in from a responder whose pages were not `partial`, or once completed fetches from TWO DISTINCT IDENTITIES (masters, never our own) were partial (H1, `partial_from` persisted). A partial responder's identity is not asked again; a device known partial is asked last. A refusal, a page-less answer (older client), a timeout (counted from when our paced queue to the device emptied), a page that does not advance or does not fold moves on (30 min skip); `busy` on the first page moves on with a 2 min skip, mid-log the same page is asked again (20 s, up to 6 times). Askers spread over a rank tier by a hash of (asker, responder); page asks paced 2.5 s apart. Unit `two_identities_marked_logs_clear_the_mark_and_one_never_does`, `review_a_whole_log_from_a_swept_responder_does_not_clear_the_mark`, `a_device_known_to_be_marked_is_asked_last`, `askers_spread_over_a_tier`, `a_busy_responder_is_skipped_briefly_and_waited_for_mid_log`, `an_ask_still_queued_does_not_time_out`, `only_the_asked_devices_answer_with_the_asks_nonce_clears_the_mark`, `a_page_less_or_declined_answer_moves_on_and_a_page_must_advance`, `a_timed_out_ask_frees_its_slot_and_keeps_the_mark`, `a_legacy_replica_never_asks`, `a_deleted_servers_mark_and_kept_ops_go`; harness `b1_an_op_skipped_before_a_later_one_comes_back_after_the_upgrade`, `b1_a_swept_owners_log_alone_clears_no_mark`, `b1_an_unasked_answer_never_clears_the_mark`, `b1_a_legacy_server_is_never_fetched_whole` |
| Our whole log to a member (a SERVE, `SyncRequest.full`) | `swarm` `SyncRequest` arm -> `CrdtResync::serve` -> `crdt_resync::page`, sent through `frame_budget::pace_answer` | Members only (a non-member's full ask falls through to the delta path). Declined while legacy or deleted. Flagged `partial` while we hold `due` or `lacks`; a sweep-only replica keeps serving (refusing would deadlock the release day). Budget: 6 pages then 1 per 2 s per asking device, 20 then 1.5/s in all (10 then 0.5/s on a phone); over it the page says `busy`. Pages follow the log's FOLD order, which `restore_op_log` now keeps after a restart (M1; before, a retained checkpoint sat at its own clock, pages skipped it and the fold's tail check judged a late op against the newest state). Unit `a_legacy_replica_declines_and_a_marked_one_serves_partial_pages`, `serving_is_budgeted_per_asker_and_in_all`, `review_one_members_junk_op_leaves_nobody_to_serve`, `pages_cover_the_log_in_order_and_stay_bounded`, `review_a_restarted_log_with_a_checkpoint_pages_every_op`, fold `a_restarted_log_with_a_checkpoint_folds_a_late_op_at_its_point`; harness `b1_a_former_member_gets_no_page` |
| The op log by range digest (a SERVE, `SyncRequest.digest`, 0.13) | `swarm` `SyncRequest` arm -> `CrdtResync::answer_digest` -> `crdt::digest::answer` | Members only. A non-member's digest is never compared against the log (no counts, no fingerprints): it gets the tombstone or `removal_notice(.., always)` (HOL-SEC-114) and nothing else; a stranger gets nothing. A legacy-anchored replica answers `vector: true` and the asker re-asks with the state vector, only for a legacy (non-self-certifying) id (`op_sync::reads_vector`; for a 40-hex id that answer is malformed). The state vector itself (a `SyncRequest` with no digest and no `full`) is read only for a legacy id: for a self-certifying one it is answered with nothing (0.14; it loses ops, HOL-SEC-175). Harness `t14a_a_vector_ask_for_a_self_certifying_server_is_answered_with_nothing`, `t14a_a_legacy_id_server_still_catches_up_by_the_vector`, `t14a_a_strangers_vector_ask_on_a_legacy_server_gets_no_op_log` (the vector path serves the op log only to a member), `t14a_catch_up_converges_by_digest_alone`; unit `only_a_legacy_anchored_replica_asks_with_the_vector`. An ask over 256 ranges, past round 6, or with a range whose ends cross or that overlaps or precedes the one before it, is left unanswered (an answer costs O(log size)). Kept unread ops go out exactly as their author signed them, re-kept by the asker's tolerant parse. Harness `g13_non_member_digest_gets_only_the_removal_notice`; unit `crdt::digest::tests::*` |
| A digest answer's split (`SyncResponse.digest.groups`) | `swarm` `SyncResponse` arm -> `CrdtResync::digest_answered` -> `op_sync::OpSync::answered` | Followed only for OUR open ask to that device for that server, same ask id and round; the book is purged on `SessionLost` and on a gap repair. The ops in any answer enter only through the unchanged merge (`ingest_remote`). A reply whose groups are unsorted, overlap, leave the spans that round asked, or hold more than 256 sub-ranges in all is MALFORMED: the whole reply is dropped and the ask closed (`Step::Malformed`), nothing of it is fingerprinted. `join_hold` counts only an answer that ends the ask. A silent ask is sent again unchanged (same id, round and nonce) up to twice with doubling waits (5/10/20 s), only to a device still a member and reachable, and only after it left our pacer and Olm queue; a copy names our MLS epoch NOW, and only if the round it copies named one (an old epoch reads as a stale member and starts a leaf repair), as does every vector re-ask. After the last copy no more copies go (0.14: no device is ever sent the vector instead), the ask staying open 60 s from its round for a late split. A `vector: true` reply re-asks with the vector only for a legacy id, else it is malformed. A duplicate answer to a copy is dropped as unasked. Unit `a_split_continues_only_its_own_open_ask`, `a_malformed_split_closes_the_ask`, `review_follow_up_bounds_the_sub_ranges_it_compares`, `only_the_final_digest_answer_counts`, `a_silent_ask_goes_again_then_closes_and_never_turns_into_the_vector`, `a_vector_reply_asks_again_with_the_vector_only_for_a_legacy_id`, `review_a_late_split_inside_the_old_ttl_is_still_followed`; harness `g13_join_hold_waits_for_the_final_round`, `g13w2_a_late_digest_answer_brings_no_vector`, `g13w2_a_lost_digest_answer_is_asked_again`, `g13w2_no_copy_goes_to_a_device_kicked_meanwhile`, `a_spent_commit_brings_one_repair_from_our_responder_once_nothing_is_held` |

**Accepted residual (L2):** an asked responder can WITHHOLD ops (hand a log without an op it holds) and its signature-checked pages still count. A non-partial responder alone clears the mark, so one malicious member that is asked can keep an op from us; the two-identity rule narrows it only while the responder is marked. Ops cannot be forged or altered (every op is author-signed and judged by the fold), and the next delta from any member still carries every author's newer ops. Not fixed: proving completeness needs a log digest the owner signs.

**Channel history (0.13, `node/channel_asks.rs`):** a joiner or a returning member asks ONE holder per channel at a time instead of every member device. Only pages we asked for keep the ask (an unsolicited or non-advancing page is ignored), a silent holder hands the ask to the next, and each new ask starts at another member in an order random per process and channel, so one holder decides for at most `MAX_TENURE` (10 minutes). The L2 residual applies per channel: a holder that leaves rows out keeps them from us until the next holder serves the channel; every row it does serve is still signature-checked. Harness `g13w3_opening_a_channel_while_joining_takes_one_copy_of_its_history`, `g13w3_a_silent_channel_holder_hands_the_ask_to_the_next`, `g13w3_pages_nobody_asked_for_never_keep_a_channel_ask`, `g13w3_a_page_nobody_asked_for_is_never_followed`.

Before the relay lists the DM room this session (a phone that opens and sends before its session exists) the DM is held with no ask and no clock; the listing (`on_room_listed`, RoomMembers) sends the asks and starts the 10 s, else the queued frame and the resend queue would reach a removed device the moment the socket opens. "Listed" is a per-session set (`roster_reach` sessions: filled by RoomMembers, emptied by `SessionLost` and a fresh `Connected`, one room out on our own `LeftRoom`), never `ws_room_peers`, which PeerLeft empties when a contact's last device leaves; a session up 10 s that never lists the room sends the asks and the DM anyway (backstop, re-armed by every session that comes up), so a held DM always goes out once there is a session. A carried part never stamps the sender's `roster_confirms` slot. Sender (`roster_reach::for_dm` -> `carried_for`): only removals that COUNT in our own fold (`Roster::counting_removals`, never a pending signer's junk), all of one device together, newest learned first (`roster_removal_carried`, keyed (master, base, device), dated and pruned in `save`; the carry itself only reads, dating once a removal stored before 0.13), none older than 90 days, none once EVERY device the contact's roster counts (as we hold it) showed in its newest DM our roster as we hold it, or held it after we learned the removal (`roster_carry_device_confirms`, per contact DEVICE: one device catching up says nothing of a sibling asleep), only where our profile audience is not `None` (asked only when something would ride). Unit `g13c_a_carried_removal_is_judged_as_any_removal`, `g13c_a_carried_removal_keeps_only_what_every_removal_keeps`, `g13c_a_dm_carries_only_removals_that_count`, `g13c_a_carry_stops_once_the_contact_showed_it_holds_it`, `g13c_the_carry_goes_on_until_every_contact_device_showed_it`, `g13c_no_carry_outside_our_profile_audience`, `g13c_a_dm_with_nothing_to_carry_asks_no_audience_and_writes_nothing`, `g13c_the_carry_bound_holds_at_its_edges`, `g13c_an_oversized_carry_is_not_news`, `g13c_a_fold_that_fails_to_commit_moves_no_resolver`, `g13c_a_0122_receiver_reads_an_rd_carrying_removals`; harness `b3_phone_a_reply_after_the_sender_left_skips_the_removed_device`, `g13c_a_carry_goes_on_until_every_contact_device_showed_it`, `g13c_a_carried_removal_lands_before_the_digest_is_judged`, `g13c_a_flooded_roster_still_carries_the_real_removal` |
| `roster_dirty(master)` (another process changed a contact roster in the store) | written by the push fetch / NSE store-only ingest when it changed a roster; followed by `roster_reach::send_due_asks` on EVERY session up (fresh and resumed, also sending the NSE's noted asks) and by `hold_for_stale_roster` before each DM to that identity (a DM whose targets the store moved is re-sent to the stored members) | Local. `roster_book::reconcile_dirty` takes the mark, points the resolver at the stored fold and returns EVERY device that fold removed (enforcement is idempotent: the resolver another path moved first must not hide one); the DM gate holds the DM and its release, like the session-up drain, drops those devices' Olm sessions and MLS leaves and emits `DeviceListUpdated` per identity (`follow_reconciled`). The push fetch also drops a DM from a device already revoked, before decrypting (as live). Defense in depth: a removed device's PreKey frame already fails the signed-device check and any other frame the master signature, so `g13c_the_push_fetch_drops_a_dm_from_a_device_a_carry_just_removed` proves the outcome (no session opened, nothing stored), not this layer alone (mutant F18 survives). Harness `g13c_a_removal_the_nse_stored_reaches_the_running_app`, `g13c_a_carried_removal_the_nse_stored_reaches_the_running_app`, `g13c_a_session_up_follows_what_the_nse_stored`; unit `g13c_the_push_fetch_drops_a_dm_from_a_device_a_carry_just_removed`, `g13c_the_push_fetch_keeps_a_carried_removal_and_marks_it_for_the_app` |

Residuals: two contacts holding different rosters for one identity is not detected (needs contact gossip); an active relay can drop every Olm frame between the owner's devices and a contact, blind (jitter reduces what size and timing tell it); a held DM's asked device that never answers costs that DM 10 s once per 35 days, or once per DM of that identity whose digest differs (only the identity's own devices can cause it, for DMs to themselves); a DM held before the DM room is listed waits until it is, or 10 s into a session (the app quits first: the row reaches the contact by DM sync); a reply after the remover went offline reaches the removed device only when the remover's DMs could not carry the removal (0.13 F2): a removal signed by a device the contact does not count yet, or in a base it has not seen (a recovery it missed; carrying the newest recovery is not done), is dropped by its fold until a trade; a removal signed by a device that stands only through a pending join is not in the digest, so a contact that missed it learns it only from a roster notice or trade; an app whose session never went down learns what its NSE stored at its next DM to that identity (the DM gate) or its next session.


## 42. Sealed push wakes (AR-26, track B6, 2026-10-18)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| What a push wake makes the phone do (fetch, nudge, banner, tap route); the wake comes from Google, Apple or anyone holding the token | `crypto::push_seal::open_wake(root, fields, Reader)`, called by the Android background handler (`Reader::App`) and tap router (`Reader::Tap`) through FFI `open_push_wake(fields, tap)` and the iOS NSE (`hollow_push_open_wake`, `Reader::Extension`) | Once `push_seal.key` exists only an RFC 8291 block sealed to it counts: plain fields, a tampered tag, a second record, a wrong keyid length, bad padding or a blob over 4096 chars drop the wake (iOS keeps the generic alert); a sealed block's fields are shape-checked and nothing outside them is read. A broken key file drops every wake; a blob with no key drops too. The block's `ts` (sidecar seconds) is REQUIRED; App and Extension drop it past 24 h + 10 min (`OFFLINE_BUFFER_TTL_SECS`) either side of the phone's clock (`WINDOW_SECS`, symmetric: a phone behind the sidecar is real, #107, and only a key holder can seal a future stamp), and drop a blob that reader already opened: `push_seen/{ts}-{salt}.{a|n}` made with `create_new` (two processes, one winner), kept across restarts, pruned past the window and to 2048 oldest-out, a root that cannot hold the record drops the wake. A tap only routes and records nothing. Unit `push_seal_refuses_a_stale_wake`, `push_seal_refuses_a_replayed_wake`, `push_seal_each_reader_opens_a_blob_once_and_a_tap_routes_at_any_age`, `push_seal_seen_records_are_bounded_oldest_first`, `push_seal_a_wake_it_cannot_record_is_dropped`. Unit `push_seal_drops_an_unsealed_wake_once_keys_exist`, `push_seal_drops_a_tampered_or_oversized_blob`, `push_seal_refuses_a_flipped_tag_or_body_bit`, `push_seal_refuses_bad_headers`, `push_seal_inner_wake_is_shape_checked`, `push_seal_a_broken_key_file_drops_every_wake`, `nse_open_wake_opens_sealed_and_refuses_plain`; Dart `test/push_wake_seal_test.dart` |
| `push_seal.key` (local, written at FCM/APNs registration) | `push_seal::load_or_create` under `identity::encryption::key_file_lock()` | Made once per data root, re-made only when the node's id changes; 0600, staged then renamed; in the wipe's `KEY_FILES`. Refused, and NOTHING registered (`register_push_token` errs), when the root was destroyed in this process, holds the wipe marker or has no `identity.key` (the destroy zeroes keys under the same lock, so a registration after it sees the wipe). A repair keeps it and `push_seen/` in place (same device id); a relink moves them aside with the old identity, and `kept_keys_gone` waits for it like any key. Unit `push_seal_token_keeps_its_key_and_rekeys_for_a_new_identity`, `push_wake_keys_are_wiped_like_any_key`, `a_registration_after_destroy_mints_no_push_key`, `push_seal_a_wiped_root_registers_nothing`, `a_repair_keeps_the_push_key`, `a_kept_folder_holding_only_the_push_key_still_holds_keys` |

Apple sees no grouping: a sealed APNs wake has no `apns-collapse-id`; the NSE threads by person (hints `master`, else the sender) or `server:channel` and removes older delivered banners of that thread, and Dart clears the same threads through `hollow/app_group` `removeDeliveredInThreads` before it posts (sidecar test `a keyed ios token keeps the generic alert and carries no collapse id`). Residuals: on iOS a replayed blob shows the generic alert and fetches nothing, and a tap on it still routes to the chat it names; a blob pushed out of `push_seen/` by more than 2048 wakes in its day can be acted on once more; `push_seen/` names show when wakes arrived (no ids) for a day, erased by the wipe; the relay, holding the token's keys, can seal a wake of its own; 0.12.1 phones keep the plain block until they update.

## 43. App lock per profile and tied to the phone (B4, B5 / AR-24, 2026-10-18)

Local only: no frame reaches any of these. Listed because each is a gate a typed secret passes.

| Write / read | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| App Lock entries (lock type, launch secret, biometric secret, longer-PIN flag, legacy-checked and duress-rebind markers) | Dart `AppLockService`, keys `<base>.<app_lock.id>` | Each profile reads and writes only its own id's entries; the pre-0.12.2 global entries move ONCE into the profile whose identity a stored secret opens (`app_lock_secret_opens_identity`: no duress probe, not paced), never into another; a phone (one profile) also takes the bare markers and drops stale secrets. A wipe clears its own id's entries (the id is held in memory, the wipe deletes `app_lock.id`); erasing another profile clears that profile's (`app_lock_profile_id_at` read first); on a phone `clearAll` also drops every scoped entry another id names (a wipe finished in another process). The one-time duress rebind note reads `DuressStatus.rebind_due` (slot header flags beside a bound identity, never a probe of the code). `test/app_lock_per_profile_test.dart`, `test/widget/duress_section_test.dart` |
| `identity.key` / `identity.device` / `identity.duress` wrap, flag `0x04` | `encryption::slot_key`, `api/identity.rs` `open_secret`, `tie_to_hardware`, `bound_key` | Device-bound = HKDF(argon2id, HW(argon2id)) with the profile's hardware key (`identity/hw_key.rs`: Android Keystore HMAC, StrongBox or TEE, software refused; iOS Secure Enclave ECDH against the RFC 9380 hash of the stretched secret). With a key present EVERY slot costs one hardware op whatever its flags (duress timing). No key for a bound file (the keystore says it is gone) = `hardware_key_missing`, nothing deleted, not a counted miss; a key that failed an operation or a keystore that could not say (`Presence::Unknown`: Android -1, iOS any status but `errSecItemNotFound`) = `hardware_key_busy`, refunded, retried from the busy screen, and never a reason to make a key: a PIN change on a bound file uses `existing()` only. An old wrap moves at its next unlock under the same salt (identity file, then device file; `repair_device_file` heals a cut between them); an old duress slot keeps its own wrap. The key is created fresh only by `enable_password_protection`, deleted by remove-protection and every wipe (a relink's kept folder too, in `zero_kept_aside`); `app_lock.id` stays in the root on a database repair (`stays_on_repair`). `b5_*`, `a_busy_keystore_is_not_a_lost_key`, `a_pin_change_never_disarms_the_duress_code`, `a_database_repair_keeps_the_app_lock_working`, `a_refused_keystore_logs_no_missing_key` in `api/identity.rs`, `identity/hw_key.rs` |
| A STORED secret on the duress slot (launch secret, biometric copy) | `api/identity.rs` `unlock_identity_with_stored_secret` -> `open_secret(.., Duress::Inert)` | Both slots still cost the same, but a duress match opens nothing and fires nothing: it answers `WRONG_SECRET`, as any stale copy does (Dart drops the copy, the typed prompt follows, no hint). Nobody typed it, and a stale launch secret (a PIN change it missed) can equal a duress code set later. Only a typed secret (`open_typed_secret`, `Duress::Fires`) wipes. `a_stale_stored_secret_never_fires_the_duress_code` |
| `identity.attempts` (pacing) | `identity/attempts.rs`, `charge` in `open_typed_secret` before any derivation | 4 free misses, then 30 s, 1 min, 5 min, 15 min, 1 h; per profile on disk, survives restarts; each try is CHARGED first (under one lock, so two prompts each pay; a count that cannot be written refuses the try) and refunded only for busy/missing; waits on the boot clock (`CLOCK_BOOTTIME`, `mach_continuous_time`, `GetTickCount64`), a boot clock behind the stamp (reboot) restarts the full wait; only a typed success or `person_present` (biometric) clears it, also a new lock and a phrase restore; the launch secret (`unlock_identity_with_stored_secret`) neither counts nor clears; never a wipe. `b5_misses_wait_and_only_a_person_clears_the_count`, `b5_settings_prompts_share_the_count`, `a_clock_set_forward_never_ends_a_wait`, `a_reboot_restarts_the_wait`, `a_new_lock_starts_a_fresh_count`, `a_try_that_cannot_be_counted_is_never_checked` |
| Password key cached in the OS keychain (desktop silent start) | `api/identity.rs` `password_keychain_allowed` (enable, toggle, change, unlock heal), `platform_keystore::store_key_at` | Only on the OS's own data root (`default_eligible_for`), never an override, portable or a pinned profile elsewhere; every keychain write runs without prompts. A key an older build cached off the OS root (flags `0x03`) leaves at the first unlock with the password in hand (`drop_cached_key_off_os_root`: file flag first, then the slot is forgotten, no keychain write), and Settings keeps "Ask before Hollow starts" switchable while it is cached. `a_typed_unlock_off_the_os_root_drops_the_cached_key_once`, `a_password_on_an_override_root_never_reaches_the_keychain`, `a_keychain_store_never_asks_the_person` |
| App Lock launch secret on a computer (Dart `storeLaunchSecret` / `readLaunchSecret` / `clearLaunchSecret`) | `AppLockService.launchSecretAllowed` = FFI `app_lock_launch_secret_allowed` (`launch_secret_allowed_for`) | A phone always; a computer (Linux too) only on the OS's own data root (`is_os_default_root`): an override, portable or pinned root never writes or reads it in the OS secure storage, so an ssh session gets no keychain dialog, and "Ask before Hollow starts" shows always on and disabled there (switchable while an older build's password key is still cached, flags `0x03`). There a Settings store, clear or PIN change (`followSecretChange`) DELETES the copy an older build left, so none goes stale; never at launch. On such a computer the whole App Lock store is off (`_storeOff`: lock type and biometric are a phone's): every read answers its default (longer-PIN and rebind notes "asked"), no write, no keychain class migration and no legacy read; a deliberate erase (`clearAll`, `clearProfile`) still deletes. Where none is allowed the legacy migration never adopts the global launch secret (it still proves the profile and goes with the global entries). Rust `a_launch_secret_is_kept_only_on_the_os_root_or_a_phone`, `an_override_root_keeps_no_launch_secret`; Dart `a root outside the OS default keeps no launch secret`, `a secret stored before the gate never outlives a change or a clear`, `the legacy migration writes no launch secret where none is allowed`, `a locked launch on a root that keeps nothing asks the secure storage nothing` (`test/app_lock_per_profile_test.dart`), the cold-start row in `test/widget/keychain_protection_test.dart` |
| Hardware keys of gone profiles (delete) | `hw_key::sweep_orphans`, run after a new lock (`enable_password_protection`) and after every successful unlock (`unlock_logged`); `Oracle::list` = `HollowHwKey.list` (Android aliases) / `hwList` (iOS tags `hollow.applock.hw.*`) | Only where one profile lives (`one_profile`: Android, iOS); only with a keystore that could list (asked FIRST: a key is made after its id is written), then a readable live id (`app_lock.id`); only aliases of exactly `hollow_applock_hw.<16 hex>`; keeps the live id's key and that of EVERY `<root>-old-*` folder at any depth, whatever its origin mark says (`recovery::kept_named`), and deletes nothing when such a folder, its mark or its id cannot be read; at most 16 a sweep. `a_lock_on_a_phone_sweeps_orphaned_hardware_keys`, `an_unlock_on_a_phone_sweeps_orphaned_hardware_keys`, `a_sweep_takes_only_gone_profiles_keys`, `a_sweep_keeps_the_key_of_old_data_kept_aside`, `a_computer_never_sweeps_hardware_keys`, `a_sweep_that_cannot_tell_deletes_nothing`, `a_keystore_that_cannot_list_loses_nothing`, `a_sweep_is_bounded`, `a_sweep_keeps_the_kept_folders_key_after_the_root_path_moved`, `a_kept_folder_with_an_unreadable_id_keeps_its_key` |
| `identity.duress` on a landing (link import, backup restore) | `storage::IDENTITY_SET`, `land_snapshot` swap under `key_file_lock`; `duress::write_slot` / `remove` under the same lock | The slot leaves with the identity it was made for (a snapshot never carries one): moved aside, zeroed when the aside folder goes, put back by a rollback; slot writes wait for a key operation in flight, a removal zeroes first. `a_landing_drops_the_old_duress_slot`, `a_landing_waits_for_a_key_write_in_flight`, `a_slot_write_waits_for_the_key_file_lock`, `a_removed_duress_slot_is_zeroed_first` |

Residuals: no hardware attempt counter exists for an app's own PIN on Android or iOS, so code running on the unlocked phone guesses at hardware speed (and can reset the count); with the silent start (default) or biometric unlock on, the PIN itself sits in OS secure storage readable by code running as the app (a byte copy cannot read it); a wipe run by the push fetch or the NSE (no oracle registered there) leaves the profile's hardware key behind until the app's next lock or unlock sweeps it (it opens nothing without the wiped files meanwhile); on a computer's OS root the App Lock markers (longer-PIN and duress-rebind notes) still use the OS secure storage, read only once a password exists or in Settings, and off it only a deliberate erase or a Settings change of the lock touches it (a delete). The App Lock entries of such a wipe are cleared by the next `clearAll` on a phone, which drops every `hollow_app_lock_*.<id>` entry whose id is not the live one.

## 43b. Where Apple keychain secrets live (HOL-SEC-187, track sec-keychain, 0.14)

Local only: no frame reaches any of these; a same-user process (an old signed Hollow with
a planted library) is the attacker. Design `reports/shipped/security/HOL_SEC_187_KEYCHAIN_DESIGN.md`.

| Write / read | Site | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| macOS protected-store write | `platform_keystore::store_key_at` -> `put` | Only the data-protection keychain, the store `protected_writable()` answered yes for (keys group, else the app-ID group); `AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`, never synchronizable; update in place, never delete first; never the file-based keychain (`add`/`put` refuse `Store::Legacy`). `a_protected_write_never_deletes_first`, `no_flow_writes_the_legacy_keychain`, `no_source_writes_the_legacy_keychain` |
| Every macOS keychain call | `platform_keystore::gate` | Refused (`Failure::Unchecked`) until `hollow_bundle_checked` ran with exactly our groups (keys group fixed, app-ID group `<TEAM>.com.anonlisten.hollow`). `the_protected_store_reads_nothing_before_the_bundle_check`, `only_our_groups_open_the_keychain` |
| The bundle self-check | `macos/Runner/BundleSeal.swift` (`enforceAtStart`, first line of `MainFlutterWindow.awakeFromNib`) | The list comes only from `Info.plist` bytes that hash to the Info.plist slot of the executable's code directory whose hash is the kernel's cdhash (`csops`); the executable is dyld's `MH_EXECUTE` image, never `Bundle.main`. Every loaded image inside the bundle matches the list by its in-memory code directory and 32 sampled `__TEXT` pages; none outside carries our team. ANY start mismatch, or keychain entitlements without a bound list and a team, ends the process before the engine (alert, exit 78; never the key-missing screen); a later mismatch aborts; ad-hoc dev builds (no list, no keychain entitlement) start shut. `scripts/macos_bundle_seal_test.sh` (incl. "an Info.plist edited after signing keeps the process from starting"), signed `scripts/macos_keychain_check.sh`, gate `signed` refuses an inexact seal |
| macOS App Lock launch secret | `AppLockService.readLaunchSecret` | Read only after `hollow/bundle_seal` says the start check verified this process (no answer = no read, nothing deleted). `macOS reads the launch secret only after the bundle check passed` |
| Writability (store rule 4) | `protected_writable()` | A probe add in the keys group, else the app-ID group: yes, no (`errSecMissingEntitlement` on both) or unknown (anything else, never cached). Unknown offers, writes and deletes nothing (`keychain_takes_new_keys`); the cached-key drop path reads ONLY the root rule (`password_keychain_allowed`). A password change keeps the cached key only where the keychain takes the new one (no = the flag goes, nothing deleted; unknown = nothing changes). Reads try every protected group, so a stale keys-group key never hides the app-ID one. `a_failed_probe_never_drops_the_cached_key_on_the_os_root`, `an_unknown_probe_is_not_cached`, `a_missing_entitlement_keeps_the_legacy_key_and_deletes_nothing`, `a_password_change_keeps_the_cached_key_only_where_the_keychain_takes_it`, `a_stale_keys_group_key_never_hides_the_one_that_opens` |
| Mover a: login keychain -> protected | `settle_move` via FFI `settle_keychain_move`, ONE launch point after the pending wipe and `settle_relink`, before any unlock | Only OS-root or keychain-flagged roots, never an override; only on a definite yes. Only `wrapping_key` / `wrapping_key.<16 hex>` with 32-byte values, 32 a start, own slots first; quiet read returning the item (file-based: the item OBJECT, `kSecReturnRef`; a persistent ref there resolves by service+account, so it would delete a key 0.13 rewrote after the read; protected: the persistent ref, a row id), add (a different protected value is never overwritten, and the login item is then LEFT, not copied, not deleted: its profile re-keys off it; no conflict copies from the login keychain), read back byte for byte, delete exactly that item (`kSecValueRef`; the file keychain then IGNORES the service+account beside it, so the object alone picks the item, pinned by `a_file_keychain_delete_takes_exactly_the_item_object`). `errSecInvalidOwnerEdit` (-25244, field report 2026-11-14: only the app that MADE a login item may delete it, so 0.14 cannot delete 0.13's) = HELD, logged once with its copy; an item whose copy a past start made is tried again only while its key still opens one of this profile's files (`settle_move_judged`), so an inert one is never retried. MOVED only when the delete answered 0 AND a NEW read by name finds nothing: a delete answering "not found" = kept (another process rewrote it, by delete+add or in place, and this process's own re-read can miss the new key while a fresh process reads it); never a check by the delete's own reference (a shared lookup that misses agreed on "gone" while the login item stayed: every start said "1 moved"); still there or a newer key under the name = kept, retried next start; every kept or skipped item (would ask, gone by its read, not 32 bytes) logs one line, status only, never bytes. Every file-based query sets `kSecUseDataProtectionKeychain` FALSE, so it never answers with or deletes the protected copy. PROOF in two halves: the SIGNED half (what an entitled process's queries reach, the real login item) is BY HAND on a notarized build, re-run for EVERY release that changes keychain code (Vitalik 2026-11-15: no CI job runs it, the release-apple keychain job is removed; the Rust tests cover the logic, mutation-tested): `scripts/macos_keychain_check.sh` plus check A (0.13 new keychain identity, then 0.14 logs the re-key, 0.14 again silent, 0.13 `keychain_key_missing`) and the `0x03` password case. Script stages: stage 1 = the field case (the seeded item is made by a helper of the script with `SecItemAdd` and an access list trusting both apps, so 0.14 may read it and may not delete it: it MUST stay, the re-key MUST be logged and its key MUST open neither file; an item 0.14 could delete = "field case not reproduced" = FAIL), 1b = a downgrade's new login key (re-keyed off too, same requirements), 2 = the next start logs no "Keychain move:" and no "Keychain re-key" line. The 0.14 proof is the hand run of 2026-11-15 (notarized b887e706 on the mini, macOS 26; macOS 12/13 never run signed): 0x02 re-key at the first start, 0x03 re-salt at the typed App Lock password, each next start silent, 0.13 locked out of both; PASS (design section 6). The UNSIGNED tests prove the file-keychain calls and the move's logic only (an unsigned binary gets -34018 from the protected keychain): `a_delete_that_does_not_take_is_never_counted_as_moved` (both movers), `a_delete_that_found_nothing_is_never_counted_as_moved`, `a_legacy_key_written_after_the_delete_is_never_counted_as_moved`, `a_login_item_the_delete_missed_is_never_counted_as_moved`, `a_copy_that_cannot_be_made_says_why`, real login keychain in the test service (FAIL where no keychain is usable; `HOLLOW_SKIP_LOGIN_KEYCHAIN=1` skips knowingly): `the_real_file_keychain_item_a_move_read_is_really_deleted`, `the_real_file_keychain_item_rewritten_after_the_read_stays`, `a_login_item_another_process_rewrote_after_the_read_stays` (child process of the test binary), `a_file_keychain_delete_takes_exactly_the_item_object`, `a_login_item_another_process_made_opens_nothing_after_the_rekey` (the item's maker is a COPY of the test binary at another path: the login keychain lets the test read it but refuses the delete with -25244, the field rule), `the_login_keychain_tests_leave_no_item_behind` (the suite again in its own process under a run tag; a FRESH process lists, since a process's view goes stale after another process writes; each process deletes what it made, a fresh process cleans an item another one rewrote). Fake twins: `a_login_item_only_its_maker_may_delete_is_held_and_tried_again_only_while_it_opens_ours`, `a_login_key_a_profile_moved_off_is_never_copied_back`. Wipes, forgets, unlocks and typed secrets never move. `a_legacy_key_moves_*`, `a_move_cut_short_*`, `a_protected_copy_that_does_not_read_back_*`, `a_legacy_key_rewritten_during_the_move_is_never_deleted`, `two_movers_at_once_keep_every_key`, `planted_legacy_items_are_not_moved`, `a_flood_of_planted_slots_never_holds_back_our_own`, `a_portable_or_override_session_moves_nothing`, `an_unlock_never_moves_a_key`, `a_duress_wipe_moves_nothing`, Dart guard `the keychain move runs once at launch` |
| Re-key off the login keychain (macOS) | `keychain_rekey::settle` from `settle_keychain_move_at`, right after the mover, for this root and every folder a relink kept aside from it (`recovery::kept_aside`, each under that folder's instance lock `recovery::take_instance_lock`, skipped while another Hollow holds it); `keychain_rekey::resalt` at every typed unlock (launch, App Lock, a Settings unlock) and from FFI `finish_keychain_rekey` (the one ask after start); only on a definite yes | The files: `identity.key`, `identity.device` AND the set-aside `identity.key.locked`/`identity.device.locked` (same master secret; re-sealed, NEVER deleted). Keychain-only (`identity.key` flags EXACTLY 0x02): at start onto a fresh CSPRNG key. Password + cache (EXACTLY 0x03): only with the password, which derives the new key under a FRESH salt (`identity.key` takes it; the device file keeps its own; the duress slot is untouched); the start only reports `PasswordKey` and the app asks ONCE per start (`keychain_rekey_wants_password`, "Not now" asks again later; `finish_keychain_rekey` passes `open_typed_secret` first: paced, a duress code wipes, never above the App Lock cover or a roster lock). A set-aside password master moves only when a login key opens it AND the typed password opens it (it takes the live master's new salt). Exposure = a 32-byte key under our names in the login keychain opens a movable file (AES-GCM to a keypair, never a name match), or a half-done re-key. The start reads the login keychain ONLY with prompts off (`settle` inside `without_prompts`, nesting-safe), names read own slot first, machine slot next, then sorted, 32 max (planted items never hide ours, in the reads and in the delete). Plan first (a live file no stored key opens stops the run before anything is staged); key staged as `<slot>.rekey` (add, never overwrite, NEW read must answer it; of several staged values the one that opens a file) BEFORE any file changes; each file re-sealed, proven, `write_key_file` (staged, fsync, read back, rename), read again; key file, device file, set-aside pair (a failure puts back every file the run wrote); the profile slot takes the key only once every file opens with it; then each staged value no file opens is deleted, per store (never one a file needs); retire: own profile slot in a group holding a key no file opens, conflict copies, a machine-slot copy of a replaced key no file opens and no login item mirrors, one quiet delete per login item holding a replaced key (refused = inert, logged), also on a finishing start with nothing left to write. Unlock tries the staged key right after the profile slot and NEVER heals it into the profile slot (`Source::Rekey`); a keychain-flagged unlock (with or without a password, every platform) re-wraps a device file a cut left on another stored key (`heal_device_file`, read back), which may ask only when the unlock's own search read no login item (login items are read one at a time). A wipe forgets `<slot>.rekey` too. Constant-time compares, `Zeroizing` keys (also `key_that_opens`), status-only logs. Drill (2026-11-01, iOS 18.7.8 real iPhone, macOS 15.7.4 old iMac): U1 PASS on macOS
15.7.4 only (12, 13, 14, 26 not run); U2 PASS; U3 PASS on iOS 18.7.8 (same ECDH before
and after the move, and from the new team after the group moved); U4 PASS on iOS and
macOS 15; U5 PASS; U6 PASS at 0 h only (restart and 48 h not run); the new team could
register the deleted group name at once. The re-bind fallback stays for other iOS
versions.

Residuals: until the old team's certificate is revoked, old builds keep the privacy grants
and can phish; an item a quiet read cannot open stays in the login keychain; a pre-0.14
Time Machine copy of the login keychain holds the key; the keys group registration is the
signing team's power; a library linked at launch runs its initialisers before the
self-check (dyld order), so the check keeps a swap away from the keychain, the engine and
the person, not from running its constructors (macOS 14 library constraints, U14).

| The `blocked` report a block sends | `BlockedUsersNotifier.block` (Dart), after `block_peer` saved | Only a block the person makes here; a sibling's block arriving through `load()` sends nothing. Fire-and-forget, never waits for `report_ack` (an older relay drops the category silently). `report_message_test.dart` "blocking" |
| A message report | `reportMessageAction` -> `showReportUserDialog(aboutMessage: true)` -> `report_user(target, category)` | Target = the author's MASTER through the resolver at every surface (desktop DM + channel + guest view, phone sheet); null on our own messages and any device of ours. The FFI carries only target + category, so no text, id or file can ride it. `report_message_test.dart` |
| `app_settings['terms_agreed_version']` (local only) | Welcome result -> `_afterIdentity`; `showTermsAgreementDialog` | Not remote-reachable. Welcome offers no create/link/restore until "Agree and continue"; the one-time question waits for the app lock and the roster lock and closes only on a saved agreement. `terms_agreement_test.dart` |

## 47. Presence audits and the member cap (scale wave g13-s1b, 0.13, 2026-10-22)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A re-join of a room (relay room state, the node's `ws_room_peers` through the answer) | `WsEvent::PresenceDrift` arm (swarm), raised by ws_client's `presence_audit::Audit::on_answer` | Relay-triggerable and writes no store: one `JoinRoom`, only for an audited room (a server of ours or the active room); what comes back passes the RoomMembers gates (bare presence, door status). A digest proves nothing and admits nothing: a match only skips a re-join. A difference counts only after a recheck 6 to 10 s later, an answer older than a `members` for the room is void, nothing is judged while the app is in the background, and a room that still differs right after its re-join pauses 5, 10, 20, then 30 minutes. Unit `presence_audit::tests::*`, ws_client `presence_frames_feed_the_audit_and_a_difference_drifts`, harness `g13_drift_audit_*`, `g13_matching_audit_sends_nothing_more` |
| What `discovered_peers` tells (relay) | `ws_handler.cpp` `discover_peers`, through `presence_view.h` (`visible_peers` is the ONE filter of `members_of`, `roster_for`, the join answer and the digest) | Members only; never an id (0.14): every ask, with or without the `digest` flag, gets the digest and count of what the socket may see, or `joined:false` only to the socket itself when it does not hold the slot. Unit `test_presence_view`, live "presence digest" cases ("an ask without the digest flag names no ids, only the digest") and every `discover()` audience check |
| A relay socket that dropped a write | `write_raw` marks `write_dead`, `sweep_sessions` closes it (never inside a room loop) | Only a socket carrying a session; it writes nothing more, closes abnormally (grace, not 1008) and the client resumes from its own count. `close()` discards what uWS still buffered for the socket: frames the session ring no longer holds (past its 8 MB / 4096 frames) come back as a `gap` and the node's catch-up, never as a silent shift. The close also costs one presence flap (`peer_left`, then `peer_joined` on resume); watched in the G1 and fleet reruns. Live `hs_dropped_write_resumes_cleanly` |
| A `MemberAdded` the node authors | `ServerJoinRequest` arm: ban, private, full (`member_cap::is_full`), Twitch, owner-verify, NSFW | The cap counts `members` (master-keyed = identities) against min(owner `max_members`, `MEMBER_CAP` 512); returning members and our own devices pass. ADMISSION ONLY, never `op_allowed`: a fold rule one version applies splits membership and forks MLS, so the cap is advisory load shaping, not a security boundary. Harness `g13_member_cap_*`, `g13_full_server_refuses_before_nsfw_consent` |

## 48. MLS self-update turns and the epoch-rate rule (AR-25, AR-27, wave g13 s3, 2026-10-22)

Design: `reports/shipped/security/audit/design_G_mls_turns.md`. Code: `node/mls_turns.rs`, `crypto/mls_tally.rs`, `crypto/mls_rate.rs`.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A member's self-update merged by the committer and broadcast | `mls_turns::handle_turn_commit` -> `MlsManager::process_commit_judged_then` | Only for the turn we hold open (same device, nonce, epoch, lease not run out: `TurnBook::close_turn`), while we still commit for the group, hold no commit of it back (`holds_commit`) and `MlsTurn::live()`; judged by `judge_commit` with a Hold read as a refusal and only a path-only commit of the turn holder's own leaf passes; broadcast BEFORE the merge. Harness `g13s3_a_late_turn_commit_is_dropped_and_the_committers_add_waits_for_the_turn`, `g13s3_turns_and_declines_count_only_from_their_own_party`; unit `a_turn_commit_counts_only_for_its_own_lease_before_it_runs_out` |
| Our own staged self-update merged | `MlsManager::process_commit_judged_then` (`own_staged`) | Only on the exact bytes we staged, at the epoch we built them; anything else at that epoch clears it. Unit `a_self_update_is_what_a_0_12_2_judge_accepts_and_merges_on_its_echo` |
| A turn acted on / declined | `mls_turns::handle_turn`, `act_on_turn`, `handle_turn_declined` | A turn counts only from the device our own election names as committer (our own identity's committing device too, when it is a sibling and not us); acted on only on an `MlsTurn` tick, at that epoch, within 4 s, under the cap; a decline ends a turn only from its holder. Harness `g13s3_a_backgrounded_device_takes_no_turn`, `g13s3_the_committers_own_sibling_refreshes_on_its_turn` |
| Any received commit (epoch-rate rule) | `mls_authority::commit_verdict` -> `mls_rate::rate_refusal` (first, before every CRDT hold) | Judged on the pre-commit tree and the commit only. Skipped for meetings and the server's ANCHORED owner (`is_anchor_owner`, never a role); an unbound committer is refused outright (0.14). Applies where every other master's leaves that declare the tally carry one that decodes (G); a leaf without the capability (0.12.2) no longer turns it off, so a squatted old leaf spares nobody the cap (0.14, unit `a_pre_0_13_leaf_of_another_master_never_turns_the_rule_off`). A commit charging its committer (own leaves or no proposals) or a repaired master must carry the exact tally `mls_rate::expected_tally` derives; at most 3 counted commits per master in 12 epochs. The committer's earlier carried entries count only from a leaf a commit wrote (never a KeyPackage's), never one naming its own master or out of order; at most 1024 carried masters (`MAX_CARRIED`), and no tally that fails to decode is ever derived (`Tally::checked`; layout version 2, carried count a u16). Units in `crypto/mls_rate.rs` (incl. `a_key_package_cannot_plant_a_history_for_another_master`, `an_honest_batch_repair_of_many_masters_stays_parseable`); harness `g13s3_a_flooding_member_is_capped_while_honest_members_converge`, `g13s3_a_stand_in_coordinators_repairs_carry_their_charge_and_keep_one_group` |
| Who may answer, repair or commit for a group | `crypto_handler::unfit_responders` / `unfit_committers` into `epoch_catchup_responder`, `group_authority`, `may_repair_our_leaf`, the `MlsKeyPackage` election, `mls_turns::committer_identity` | A master its OWN commit-written history shows at its cap (`mls_tally::own_tallies`; what other leaves carry for it caps only repairs to it, never its fitness), or, for responders, keeping a leaf without a tally (`declares` but unreadable keeps G off, as above), is never our responder or repairer, the subgroup coordinator clause included (unit `an_unfit_subgroup_coordinator_may_not_repair_our_leaf`); as committer it is skipped while anyone else is fit. The fork winner (`is_authoritys_acting_device`) stays presence-only. Residual until option B: a carried list written while G was false may name epochs still ahead and, once the group reaches them, cap repairs of the master it names (up to 1024 masters); it never touches fitness. Harness `g13s3_a_capped_stand_in_authority_cannot_repair_honest_members_onto_its_fork`; units `a_dormant_future_carried_list_never_makes_its_victim_unfit`, `unfit_masters_follow_each_masters_own_history` |
| A forwarded KeyPackage | swarm frame dispatch -> `mls_turns::unwrap_forwarded_key_package` | Inside Olm only (0.14, Lane::Carried); from a member's device only, bound to a member's device, then the ordinary `MlsKeyPackage` arm as if that device sent it; each package forwarded once per local device. Residual: a member can forward a captured, still unused KeyPackage of another device and so cause a repair of it; `RepairBudget` (and, where receivers judge, the cap) charges that device's master, never the forwarder |
| Leaf changes we commit for a master / meeting seats | Phase 2 `RepairBudget`, `over_cap_devices`; `conference::KnockPacing` | 3 KeyPackage-driven changes per master per 10 min, then a doubling backoff; nothing a receiver would refuse for the cap; repairs past the carried bound or of a master whose history no tally can restate wait, the owner's too, and `commit_membership` commits nothing where receivers judge a tally it cannot derive; the anchored owner and a meeting host, whose tally no receiver judges, state a fresh one instead (`commit_membership_as`, `rate_exempt`; unit `an_owner_with_an_unreadable_tally_can_still_remove_a_member`). Meetings without a waiting room: knocks queue (one place per knocker, 64 per meeting, 2 min), every due knock is seated in ONE commit per batch tick (`commit_admissions`: a KeyPackage the batch refuses as a whole, e.g. capabilities or a reused key, is left out and the rest seated; unit `one_poisoned_knock_never_blocks_the_others`), at most 6 such commits a minute, re-seats back off per device, a knocker blocked meanwhile is dropped. Harness `g13s3_key_packages_in_a_loop_get_bounded_repairs`, `g13s3_knocks_on_an_open_meeting_are_seated_together`; units `knock_pacing_tests`, `a_committer_at_the_carried_bound_leaves_further_repairs_waiting`, `a_repair_no_tally_can_state_waits_even_for_the_owner` |

## 49. AR-35 protocol hygiene (track g13w3-ar35, 0.13, 2026-10-23)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A live frame's nonce kept past a restart (`live_frame_nonces`) | `frame_auth::ReplayGuard::first_sight` -> `LiveNonceSink::note` -> CryptoStore `NoteLiveNonce` -> `NonceBook::note` -> `MessageStore::insert_live_frame_nonce` | Only after the frame's seal verified and it was a first sight of a live-only frame; the row lives while the frame is not stale (expires = seal time + 300 s, kept while expires >= now, pruned on the 30 s tick, rows expiring past now + 600 s dropped and never loaded). Bounded and fair, never refused: a sender the held roster places under our identity, an accepted friend or a co-member (`ReplayGuard::placement`; a stranger's self-delivered roster places nothing) is charged to its master in a pool of 32,768 rows no stranger touches; everyone else shares another 32,768; each share holds at most 1,024 rows (a full share drops its own oldest row, a full pool the oldest row of its heaviest share). Counts live in the actor (no COUNT per note); notes past a backlog of 1,024 queued (stranger) or 4,096 (placed) are not queued, so a flood never holds the Olm session saves that frames wait on (AR-30). Loaded before the event loop reads a frame; when the rows do not come in 5 s or cannot be read, `[HOLLOW-SECURITY]` and every live frame stamped before the start is refused until they arrive (late rows merge). Scrubbed from backups and link snapshots with `olm_read_marks` (both describe this device's own Olm traffic). Residual: a frame acted on just before the process died, whose note had not committed, can be replayed once after the restart (the note is fire-and-forget on this hot path); a stranger that floods past the actor's write rate, or evicts the strangers' pool, costs only other strangers their stored rows (the RAM guard still refuses repeats until restart); when our clock jumps back, the stored row of a frame now more than 300 s ahead of it is dropped, so after a restart that frame can return once the clock catches up (the RAM guard keeps it while the process runs). The forwarder keeps no rows (no disk on the relay box; its fwd signals need `FwdStreamAuth` and a restart drops its streams). Harness `ar35_a_live_frame_replayed_after_a_restart_is_refused`, `ar35_a_friends_live_frame_is_kept_where_strangers_cannot_evict_it`; units `the_replay_guard_outlives_a_restart_until_its_frames_turn_stale`, `a_stranger_flood_never_switches_off_persistence_for_honest_senders`, `a_full_stored_guard_evicts_from_the_heaviest_stranger_never_from_a_known_sender`, `a_stranger_flood_queues_no_more_than_its_backlog`, `a_session_save_behind_a_nonce_flood_settles_fast`, `a_stored_nonce_lives_while_its_frame_is_fresh_and_one_from_far_ahead_goes`, `a_stored_guard_that_cannot_be_read_fails_closed`, `a_guard_whose_stored_rows_come_late_refuses_frames_from_before_its_start`, `a_nonce_is_kept_through_the_last_millisecond_its_frame_is_fresh`, `only_our_friends_and_co_members_devices_are_placed`, `a_staged_database_loses_its_olm_read_marks_and_live_frame_nonces` |
| A share manifest taken (bitmap, then the partial file at ShareStart) / a stored seed rebuilt at start | `share_handler::handle_envelope_share_manifest_response`, `rebuild_seed_state` | Root hash first, then `ShareManifest::well_formed`: version 1, chunk size 262,144, size above 0, chunk count = ceil(size / chunk size), at most 2^20 chunks (`MAX_SHARE_CHUNKS`, above what a 64 MB frame can carry), one hash per chunk (C-FILES-06). `build_manifest_from_file` refuses past the same ceiling. Units `a_share_manifest_that_does_not_add_up_is_refused`, `a_stored_seed_whose_manifest_does_not_add_up_is_skipped` |
| An archive opened (not a store write: what it shows as verified) | `archive::loader::load_archive` -> `verify_archive_level_signature` | Formats 1 and 2 open; the manifest (inside the content hash) picks the signed form via `archive_signed_bytes`: 1 = the bare hash, 2 = `hollow-archive1\0` + hash (C-OLM-07). Exports stay format 1 (`ARCHIVE_WRITE_FORMAT`) until 0.14. Unit `an_archive_signature_verifies_only_in_its_own_format` |

## 50. Friend re-add vs the seal clock (track g13w3-readd, 0.13, 2026-10-23)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A friend's removal (`FriendRemove { at }`) | swarm `FriendRemove` arm -> `social::removal_predates_friendship`, `social::removal_mark`, `note_removal` | `at` is when the remover ended the friendship (`social::removal_stamp`: past that friendship's stamp, our accept mark included, on every device of theirs, whatever its clock; queued drains send `queued_friend_remove`). A removal naming an end at or before our row's stamp ended an earlier friendship: a late copy (relay-held, mailbox) is ignored, so it never ends a re-add. The end `at` names is held to `stamp_ceiling(min(seal, now))` (`social::named_end`), so a seal ahead of our clock never stretches it; the tombstone is the seal, or that end where later. Siblings are told both (`FriendRemoval.theirs` carries the end): the friend's own removal ends every friendship of ours stamped before its end, whatever our accept of it (`ended_by_them`: `take_sibling_removals`, `ended_by_removal`, `older_than_removal`; kept as `friend_removed_by_them:{master}`, never moving back). A bare removal (pre-0.13 sender) keeps the seal rules. Harness `a_removal_of_a_friendship_our_accept_stamped_ends_it_everywhere`, `a_removal_of_a_friendship_our_accept_stamped_reaches_a_sibling_through_our_share`, `a_late_copy_of_the_friends_removal_never_ends_our_readd`, `a_late_copy_of_a_removal_of_a_friendship_stamped_ahead_never_ends_our_readd`, `a_removal_names_the_friendship_it_ends_whatever_the_seal_clock`; units `a_friends_own_removal_ends_what_it_names_whatever_our_accept`, `a_removal_sealed_ahead_names_no_end_past_our_ceiling`; wire KATs `friend_request_wire_stays_backward_compatible`, `a_sibling_list_without_the_new_stamps_reads_as_before` |
| Removals in a sibling list | `social::friend_removals` (sender), `take_sibling_removals` (receiver, §36 row) | A tombstone a friendship or pending OUTGOING request of ours outlived is told clamped to just below that row's stamp, never dropped: an away device still ends the friendship it holds (pending rows are in no shared list) and never the re-add. Harness `a_removal_reaches_an_away_sibling_while_we_ask_again`; unit `a_readd_outlives_the_tombstone_it_supersedes` |
| Our accept of a request inside the window before a removal (`friend_accepted_here:{master}` = `{row stamp}:{accept stamp}`) | `social::handle_accept_friend_request` -> `note_accept`; swarm `FriendListSync` arm -> `note_sibling_accept` (own device only, the arm's gate) | Written only when the request's stamp sits at or before the removal we hold; weighs only the row it names. Our devices tell each other the request's stamp and the accept apart (`FriendListEntry.accepted_at`, from `accepted_friend_entries` and `share_friend_with_siblings`); a receiving sibling stores the request's stamp and writes the same mark (`note_sibling_accept`). `friendship_since` = max(row, mark) weighs only OUR removals (`take_sibling_removals` without `theirs`, `friend_removals`, `ended_by_removal`, our own removal's stamp); the friend's removals weigh the request alone (the `FriendRemove` arm, `ended_by_them`). The wire accept still names the request. Harness `an_accept_inside_the_skew_of_a_sibling_removal_stands`, `an_accept_of_a_request_both_our_devices_hold_stands_on_both`; unit `an_accept_past_a_removal_stamps_the_friendship_for_our_devices` |

## 51. Who leads an MLS group while the owner is away (standing, g13 wave 3, 0.13, 2026-10-23)

Design: `reports/shipped/security/audit/design_G_standing.md` (v2.1, three review rounds). Code: `node/standing.rs`. Before 0.13 every stand-in was the lowest master id by string sort, which anyone can grind.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Who commits, answers a join, serves an epoch catch-up, repairs, wins a fork, coordinates a subgroup or the vault | `standing::{rule, rank_masters, positions}`, used by every election in `crypto_handler`, `mls_turns::committer_identity`, `mls_authority::founds_on_key_package`, the `voice_handler` heal | 0.14: every ANCHORED server leads by standing, whatever its trees hold (`standing::rule_from`; no gate). Candidates are masters with an ONLINE device holding a bound leaf that DECLARES the tally (every bound leaf while no ONLINE leaf declares; `holders`, `Rule::admits`; with no copy of the group, the CRDT's members). Online = in a presence list, or THIS device (`Holders.us`), never our other devices, so replicas with one tree and one presence view admit the same masters (unit `w2b_replicas_with_one_tree_and_one_presence_admit_the_same_masters`), ordered by role tier, then fold position (the least fixed point over `MemberSpan.admitted`, never before the admitter's current position; a departed admitter counts as last), then own opening clock, then id. A LEGACY server (no checkpoint, no provable fold) keeps the 0.12.2 rule byte for byte (golden unit over 3,000 tables) while its tree holds a leaf without the tally. Abstain (a Legacy view of an all-declaring tree) leads nothing and takes a leaf repair from the owner alone until the checkpoint rebases it. A silent responder is skipped after 15 s, for 10 minutes, one per group; a served catch-up counts as an answer. The owner is preferred as committer only. The ONE exception to reading the tree's declares view: the fork HEAL leader (`crypto_handler::heal_leader`, #135, 2026-11-18), used only where our tree's presence-only authority is us or nobody, ranks by standing every online member holding a bound leaf (declaring or not), only where our election names no responder; its fork stays, and it may repair our leaf (`may_repair_held_leaf`) only within 120 s after we asked it to lead the heal (see the epoch-hint row). The cap filter there only steers the probe target.
