Units `the_rule_matrix`, `a_removed_members_backdated_admission_buys_no_standing`, `positions_are_the_same_for_every_insertion_order`, `three_successive_silent_responders_never_move_ours_past_the_second_in_standing`; harness `g13w2f_a_ground_low_id_never_becomes_the_stand_in_committer`, `g13w2f_the_stand_in_is_the_highest_role_then_the_earliest_join`, `g13w2f_a_ground_id_is_neither_catch_up_responder_nor_repairer`, `g13w2f_a_fork_made_by_a_ground_id_heals_to_the_standing_authority`, `g13w2f_a_squatted_old_leaf_keeps_no_server_on_the_old_rule`, `g13w2f_the_owner_back_online_leads_under_both_rules` |
| A groupless joiner's Welcome whose leaves no held roster places | `mls_authority::welcome_verdict` (`view_refusal`) | For a leaf whose master the resolver holds no roster of (`resolver::holds_roster_of`), the server's authority view judges: a device it removed is refused, one it holds statements for and does not count waits like an unplaced add (the hold), a master it has no statements for stays unjudged; a roster we hold always judges its own devices. Unit `a_welcome_is_judged_by_the_servers_view_where_no_held_roster_places_its_leaves` |
| A commit writing a tally-less leaf (the ratchet) | `mls_authority::commit_verdict`, after the rate rule and before every hold (`standing::ratchet_on`); the committer's `plan_membership` drops such KeyPackages; OpenMLS itself in every group we found since 0.14 (`RequiredCapabilities` naming the tally, `mls_tally::tally_required`, in `MlsManager::create_group`) | 0.14: in EVERY anchored server, the owner's leaf included (no exemption; an old owner device gets its seat back once it updates); in a Legacy one once its tree declares, there sparing only the owner (`ratchet_exempts`). Meetings exempt (and their groups require nothing: a 0.13 guest's knock lists no tally). Groups founded before 0.14 get no context change (the judge covers them) and no sweep of old leaves (a sweep forks every 0.13 replica). Units `the_ratchet_holds_in_every_anchored_server`, `a_group_we_found_refuses_a_leaf_without_the_tally`; harness `g13w2f_an_anchored_group_refuses_an_incapable_leaf_by_every_layer` (planner and judge each alone, beside an old leaf; the OpenMLS layer by the unit) |
| A leafless device's KeyPackage request and where it goes (the walk) | `walk_leaf_ask`, `leafless_may_answer_request` (H1b), `MlsKeyPackageAck` (Lane::Carried since 0.14, `live_only`) | One target at a time by standing, owner first (0.14: no old-rule last resort); an owner device with no sibling online walks too. A member is online for the walk when the resolver places one of its devices in a room, or when a device in the server's OWN room is placed only by the server's authority view (`standing::view_master`: the one master whose counted roster lists it; never a device the resolver places, a revoked device, a device any view entry removed, one two rosters count, or any id that is a view entry's master, a member, the owner or an op author of the server, or a master the resolver links; nothing while the view is unset), and the ask goes to those devices (`standing::WalkView`, built once per decision): a joiner holds co-members' rosters only in that view until its Welcome, so the resolver alone saw no leaf holder and two leafless joiners asked each other forever (t14-mlsjoin). Only the walk (and H1b's walk target) reads the view: every other leader search (`standing::walk_order`, the stale-leaf repair) places by the resolver alone, since its sends reach only what the resolver places, and the stale-leaf repair checks its target is reachable before it mints (`key_package_devices`). A walk serves one membership (`standing::membership_span`, `LeadBook::settle_walk`): a leave, kick or rejoin starts it afresh. An owner device that asks its siblings ends its walk, so a Cannot heard while none was online never mints on every tick. A walk that asked everyone starts a new pass, with a fresh package, only after a gap that doubles per pass from 6 s to 60 s (`LeadBook::walk_may_restart`, `standing::pass_gap`), never on each Cannot: a target that said Cannot may learn of the admission a moment later (`a_groupless_owner_device_admits_a_parked_joiner_without_founding`), while two leafless members answering each other settle at one package a minute. Harness `t14_two_leafless_joiners_get_their_welcome_from_the_leaf_holder`, `t14_two_leafless_joiners_are_seated_by_the_stand_in_while_the_owner_is_away`, `t14_leafless_joiners_with_nobody_to_seat_them_mint_no_key_package_storm` (per-node blind spots, `resolver::blind_for_test`); harness `t14_a_lone_stranger_joiner_is_seated_by_the_only_member`; units `a_device_only_the_servers_view_places_joins_the_walk`, `a_walk_starts_a_new_pass_once_per_window`, `a_removed_device_or_a_named_master_id_is_never_a_walk_device`, `review_a_master_id_only_the_view_knows_is_never_a_walk_device`, `review_every_master_the_walk_order_offers_is_one_the_repair_send_reaches`, `a_walk_never_outlives_the_membership_it_served`, `an_owner_device_that_asks_its_siblings_ends_its_walk`, `a_leafless_device_judges_a_request_by_its_current_walk_only`. Since 0.14 (t14-rostercheck, §70) the relay's fold of the target identity's rosters is a second source: on a relay that answers `roster_check`, no KeyPackage reaches a foreign device the merged copies do not count, and the same check holds every server Welcome and stages a first-sight roster from the room; the residual is AR-15's relay-memory one. A leafless device answers a KeyPackage request only at or above its walk target. An ack counts only from a device we sent our package to, for the tag we sent; Cannot steps at once, none after 6 s, a Commit without a Welcome asks a fresh package; a package is forwarded only to a tally-declaring coordinator. Harness `g13w2f_the_walk_reaches_the_committer_past_a_leafless_and_a_silent_target`, `g13w2f_review_a_ground_id_never_receives_a_leafless_askers_key_package`; units `the_walk_order_its_cursor_and_the_h1b_gate`, `an_ack_counts_only_from_a_device_we_asked_for_our_package` |
| A repair's Welcome taken through a named forward | `LeadBook::note_repair_delegate` + `standing::may_commit_named_repair` | Repairs are committed by the coordinator alone (one committer per epoch); the member that asked for the repair KeyPackage forwards it unless it is the coordinator, and its ack names the committer (`MlsKeyPackageAck.via`, serde default). The prober takes that committer's Welcome only when the ack comes from a device of the master whose repair request it answered, names the package it answered with, and `via` is the anchored owner or a current unbanned CRDT member holding a bound leaf in the prober's tree; 120 s, one per group. Otherwise an asked-for Welcome stays requester-scoped (`authz_a_welcome_never_replaces_a_group_unasked`). Unit `an_ack_naming_a_non_member_or_another_package_sets_no_delegate` |
| The parked `~join` copy's KeyPackage | the join request builder | No KeyPackage for a genesis id or an owner-pinned invite (checkpoint anchor), and a row's stored package is dropped on such a re-ask; still sent for a 32-hex invite without a pin (may be Legacy). Not asked-for in anchored servers. Harness `g13w2f_review_a_parked_joiners_key_package_reaches_only_its_walk` |
| Who answers a door ask | `door_room::first_to_answer` | One slot per master, ranked by SHA-256(asker, per-ask nonce, master), never by id |
| `MemberSpan` opened or extended by a `MemberAdded` | the fold | A duplicate `MemberAdded` adds its author to an OPEN span only and never opens one (`was_member_at` as in 0.12.2 for old rows); the one-time standing refold marks itself only after refolding a non-empty anchored log. Units `a_duplicate_backdated_admission_cannot_worsen_a_members_position`, `a_departed_admitter_demotes_only_what_it_alone_admitted` |
| `MlsEpochProbe` standing / answer flags | the probe arm | Only OFF yields to ON; `answer:true` is never itself answered; fake higher-epoch hints never move our responder (harness `g13w2f_review_fake_epoch_hints_never_move_our_responder`). The relay learns which askers run 0.13 with the gate on (accepted). Tally layout frozen at v2 until 0.14 |

Residuals: a backdated role grant from an ex-admin buys stand-in precedence (AR-11, widened). Every live join ask arrives twice, so every member serves it (cost only; F1 follow-up: key `repeat_ask` on (server, peer, nonce)). R6 CLOSED in 0.14: `mls_kp`, `mls_kp_forward` and `mls_kp_ack` ride Lane::Carried (`crypto_handler::carry_key_package*`, the one send path; a plaintext copy drops at the lane gate), so the relay reads no KeyPackage and cannot hand one to a colluding member (harness `w2b_c24_a_joiners_key_package_never_rides_in_the_clear`, `w2b_a_key_package_in_the_clear_seats_nobody`; unit `key_packages_their_forwards_and_acks_ride_olm_only`). `mls_kp_req` stays Lane::Relay (it names no package). KeyPackages no longer cross 0.13 and 0.14 in either direction (decided). Mixed 0.13/0.14 (decided, clean break): in an anchored server whose tree holds a leaf that does not declare the tally, or an unbound one, 0.13 replicas elect by the lowest id while 0.14 ones go by standing, the ratchet and rule G judge such commits differently, and a fork between the two sides has no heal path (each drops the other's KeyPackages), so live MLS messages and voice between 0.13 and 0.14 members of such a server split until every member updates; the 0.13 status notice says so. A group whose every leaf is unbound (0.11 straight to 0.14) has no bound member to repair it and stays mute (accepted, no re-found: a re-found would drop a group on our own judgement and race a second founder). A 0.14 device whose own leaf predates the tally rides a path that declares it on every commit it makes (`commit_membership`), refreshes it at once off the cadence when it commits for the group (`mls_turns::tick`), and otherwise asks for its repair (`ask_repair_of_stale_leaves`): the group's authority first, then, with no Welcome within the bootstrap timeout (60 s), the next member holding a leaf by standing, each once per run, one pass (unit `a_stale_leaf_ask_walks_past_an_authority_that_never_answers`, harness `w2b_a_stale_leaf_whose_authority_never_answers_asks_the_next_member`); until it declares it stands for leadership only while no online leaf declares (`Rule::admits`), and as responder a master counts unfit only when none of its leaves declares (`unfit_masters`). Review finding 1: harness `w2b_the_first_declaring_leaf_never_makes_a_stranger_the_only_leader`, `w2b_an_absent_only_declaring_leaf_never_leaves_the_group_without_a_leader`, `w2b_leaves_from_before_the_tally_refresh_without_a_join`, `w2b_a_leaf_from_before_the_tally_refreshes_by_itself_or_by_its_repair`; units `the_rule_matrix`, `a_commit_by_a_leaf_without_the_tally_refreshes_it`, `unfit_masters_follow_each_masters_own_history`. Residual: while no online leaf declares, a 0.12.2 device can be the elected leader (it outranks a 0.14 device whose leaf predates the tally) and leads nothing, since it hears no carried KeyPackage; the group waits for a declaring device to come online (clean break). While leaves from before the tally remain, a declaring device going on or offline moves every such master in or out of the candidates at once, so replicas whose presence views differ for a moment can elect two committers and fork until the probe rule heals it; the window closes as those leaves refresh. A KeyPackage handled inside a forward gets no ack (its asker counts acks only from where it sent it); the mark is the dispatch's own (`mls_turns::VIA_FORWARD`), so a later direct copy of the same package is acked (harness `g13w3m_a_forwarded_repair_seats_the_prober_through_the_named_committer`). 0.12.2 askers send an empty door nonce, so their answerer order is grindable, and a member that proves the door but never answers still takes an answerer slot (L5, accepted).

## 52. The relay's held bytes and the circuit breaker (OOM + G2, 0.13, 2026-10-23)

Design: `reports/shipped/security/audit/design_G2_breaker.md`. Code: `relay-uws/src/send_budget.h`, `breaker.h`, `msg_plan.h`, `door_room.h`, `fair_share.h`; one close helper `close_once` in `ws_handler.cpp`. Every table is RAM only, never logged, never in the restart snapshot.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Bytes the relay holds for a socket (the send-budget ledger) | `send_budget::Ledger`, admitted per frame (Pass / Trip / Refuse) before the write; `write_raw` checks the budget first, then the breaker's write hook | At most 64 MB per socket; a global budget of a quarter of RAM, at least 512 MiB (`HOLLOW_SEND_BUDGET_BYTES` overrides), charged per address share (`socket_share`). The share cap applies only under pressure (above half the budget). The victim is the least progressed reader of the heaviest share, ranked by real progress; a sender's own-share co-tenants go before the sender trips. A stalled reader is swept after 30 s. A trip closes 1013 with a plain reconnect hint through `close_once` (never 1008), never charges the breaker, and its data is resent on resume. Unit `test_send_budget`; live `oom_piled_receivers_never_trip_the_sender`, `oom_trips_are_no_breaker_trips`, `test_breaker_trips_are_no_budget_trips` |
| Breaker tables (cooldowns, start pools, clean devices) | `breaker.h` | Process-lifetime key; FairShare by `socket_share`, 65,536 entries; a full table evicts the heaviest share (`victim_except` spares the payer). Shadow by default: counts what enforce would trip, closes nothing; `HOLLOW_BREAKER_ENFORCE=1` builds enforce. `auth_ok` carries the credit and `enforced` only on an enforcing relay. Unit `test_breaker` (cooldown table, start pools, `tables_stay_bounded_when_the_newcomer_holds_the_top_share`) |
| Stranger deposit trackers | `breaker.h` `StrangerBook` | One 32 MiB byte budget, FairShare by `socket_share` evicting the heaviest share's least recently used tracker, at most about 11 KB per payer. Unit `test_breaker` (stranger tracker bytes, stranger book bound, `stranger_book_stays_bounded_when_the_newcomer_holds_the_top_share`) |
| Stored door proofs that open nothing | `door_room.h` | Free below 64 per room, then 8 per address group unless the socket saw the room; judged at lock moves at the relay's cost (a saturating DH count). Unit `test_door_room` (junk proofs, honest NAT); live `breaker_junk_proofs_do_not_cost_the_mover` |

Order per frame: a guest's refused frame (a binary direct, a topic frame, a binary or JSON `msg` broadcast past its ten a minute; `guest_refuses`, `guest_broadcast_refused`) is refused first and judged at 1 W, then the breaker judges (in shadow it only counts), then the send budget; both return at once after a close. Plan-level checks (w2-d, 0.14): a test build notes the W each frame was judged at (`/breaker-test` `plan_w`); live `breaker_a_move_pays_for_member_proofs` (C6) and `breaker_a_non_member_leave_costs_one` (C9), unit `test_plan_prices` (`breaker::lock_put_w`, `leave_w`), live `oom_guest_refusals_judged_first`. Follow-up before the enforce switch: the reconnect hint lost when a closing socket holds more than 64 KB. Residuals: AR-01, AR-36.

## 53. Tables a stranger can grow (track b14-bounds, 0.14)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Relay stream chunks a peer's ask makes the serving device queue (no data channel): a FileRequest, a ShardRequest, a recovery-pool plan | `swarm.rs` FileRequest and ShardRequest arms -> `ws_stream_transfer::relay_stream_admits` (before the read) -> `ws_stream_send` / `ws_stream_send_bytes` (via `stream_to_peer`, `stream_to_peer_bytes`, `vault_ops::stream_shard`, `apply_recovery_plan`); every relay stream, our own pushes and the link snapshot included, takes a registered place and its own staged `.stream_send_relay_{id}_{seq}.tmp` | One relay stream per (asker device, stream id) at a time: a repeat while it runs starts nothing and sends no header (each file ask re-encrypts under a fresh key). A device that joins a room we already list it in (a fresh session: its receive state is gone) ENDS its streams (`end_relay_streams_to`); one that leaves every room we share (PeerLeft, or a RoomMembers refresh no longer listing it) may only be HIDDEN and still receiving, so its streams go on, marked stale (`orphan_relay_streams_to`), and its next ask for one REPLACES it instead of meeting Running. Each stream has a `RelayRank`: Guest (public-channel non-member), Member, Own (our sends, our own devices, outside every cap). A displaced or ended stream sends nothing more: each chunk leaves only while its stream still holds its place, under the lock a successor of the same id takes it under (`send_while_placed`), so a chunk read meanwhile never lands behind the successor's first chunk (frames name only the id; the receiver would append it). Per asking identity 16 (`MAX_RECV_STREAMS_PER_SENDER`); per device 64 streams and 272 MiB staged (`RELAY_STAGED_BYTES`, one stream alone may exceed it); past either the lowest rank gives way, within it the asker holding the most (streams or bytes) loses its NEWEST stream, a tie refuses the newcomer (silence; the retry sweep asks again). Chunks wait for credit from one 4 MiB `RELAY_STREAM_QUEUE_BYTES` semaphore per device, carried in `WsCommand::SendBinaryDirect.credit` and given back when ws_client writes or prunes the chunk; a waiting stream ends only when the socket has taken NONE of the device's chunks for 120 s (waiting behind other streams is no stall). Unit `repeated_relay_streams_hold_a_fixed_byte_budget`, `vault_shard_relay_sends_hold_a_fixed_byte_budget`, `relay_streams_are_bounded_per_asker`, `a_full_device_makes_room_from_the_heaviest_lowest_asker`, `relay_streams_hold_a_fixed_staged_byte_budget`, `an_ended_stream_never_touches_its_successor`, `a_late_frame_from_a_displaced_stream_never_joins_the_new_one`, `a_task_displaced_mid_read_sends_nothing_more` (unix), `a_device_that_left_is_served_again_when_it_asks`, `a_stream_waiting_behind_others_is_not_stalled`, `a_socket_that_takes_nothing_ends_its_streams`; harness `repeated_file_requests_stream_a_big_file_once`, `review_b14_a_reasked_file_after_session_loss_is_served` |
| `reports.json` fingerprints and counts (relay RAM + disk) | `handle_report` -> `ReportsState::add(.., socket_share)` | Each report is charged to the reporter's address share in a `FairShare` under a 128 MiB byte budget at the constant `ENTRY_BYTES` (string length never picks a victim); past it the heaviest share loses its least recently used report AND its count, the new report spared (`victim_except`). Nothing is refused. Counts carried from a file the key cannot read live in `carried`, outside the ledger (no runtime path adds one), bounded at load by `MAX_CARRIED_BYTES`, smallest counts first. File v3 stores each report as (fingerprint, target, category, n), oldest first; shares ride only the restart snapshot (codec v11 `report_shares`), a report without one is a share of its own. Older files: same-salt fingerprints keep dedup alone (n 0), counts are carried. Unit `test_reports` "a flood from one address block", "a restart keeps each report's share", "a file past the budget", "a 0.13 file", "a 0.13 file past the budget", "every report weighs the same", "carried counts past their budget"; codec `test_snapshot_codec` v10/v11 |

## 54. A server leave that lands (track t14-lost-leave, 0.14)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Our own leave on its way, and the room and door it keeps (local, persisted in the `leave_book` setting inside the SQLCipher DB) | `sync_handler::handle_leave_server` -> `leave_book::LeaveBook::begin`; `drive` on the leave and every MLS batch tick; `swarm::release_leave_rooms`; `door_room::DoorRooms::keep_for_leave` | Only our own `LeaveServer` command writes it. The op, its clock (`left_hlc`), the owner, the other members (taken BEFORE the op removed us) and the door we proved are kept; the device keeps the server's room and proves its door (rejoined on every connect, restored on start, a reconnected socket proves it again) until the leave LANDS. A hand-off is no landing (a dead socket, or an owner refusing the op until it learns of our admission, both lose it): each other member is handed the op once, the owner's online devices get the op and then a sync ask (`ask_owner`: a digest ask with no ranges, so a member is answered with nothing and a former member with its removal notice, HOL-SEC-114) at once, again whenever an owner device comes online, and on a 20 s doubling to 1 h backoff. Bounded: 32 leaves (one already handed to a member goes first), given up after 30 days; the stored door goes with the entry, so it is erased as soon as the owner's answer lands the leave or at the give-up. Saves run on a blocking thread, the newest written wins (generation counter). Harness `a_leave_made_while_the_owner_is_away_reaches_it_on_return`, `a_leave_pending_at_shutdown_reaches_the_owner_after_restart`, `a_leave_pending_across_a_reconnect_reaches_the_owner`, `a_leave_reaches_an_owner_on_a_device_of_its_own`, `t14r_a_leave_handed_to_a_dead_owner_socket_still_lands`, `leave_racing_a_sibling_announce_join_stays_left`; unit `a_leave_lands_on_the_owners_word_alone` |
| A leave landing on the owner's answer (drops the pending leave, leaves the room) | swarm `SyncResponse` arm, for a server we hold no state of and join nowhere -> `LeaveBook::on_answer` | Only an answer echoing one of our last 4 ask nonces, from a device the resolver places as the recorded owner (not revoked; Olm proves the sender), and only when it holds the owner's signed deletion, or its last op is a signed removal or ban of our master at or after our leave's clock. A member's answer (the owner still counts us), an older removal (it may predate a rejoin), another member's answer or another server's op change nothing. Unit `only_the_owners_answer_to_our_ask_lands_a_leave` |
| A leave ended by the owner closing the server to us | swarm `CrdtOpBroadcast` arm, first, when we hold no state of the server -> `LeaveBook::owner_closed`; `LeaveRoom` unless a join is pending | Only an op signed by the recorded owner: its `ServerDeleted`, or its removal or ban of our master at or after our leave's clock, whoever carried it. Unit `only_the_owner_closes_a_pending_leave`; harness `a_pending_leave_ends_when_the_owner_deletes_the_server` |
| A member's own leave reaching a replica behind an owner checkpoint that never saw it | §59 (checkpoint coverage): an ordinary op the base does not decide, folded on top at its own clock. Option A's `late_leaves`, `close_late_leaves` and the store re-insert are DELETED (t14-cover). | §59 |
| A rejoin made while a leave is on its way | swarm `SyncResponse` arm, join completion -> `LeaveBook::drop_pending` | A member again: the old leave must never land and end the new membership. Harness `a_rejoin_drops_a_leave_still_on_its_way` |
| Our identity's leave of a server, held against a later join (the `left` marks) | swarm `CrdtOpBroadcast` arm -> `leave_book::own_leave` -> `LeaveBook::note_left`; the leaver's own `begin` | `own_leave`: the SENDER is one of our own roster-counted, unrevoked devices (`is_own_unrevoked_device`), the op parses whole, names this server, is authored by our master, removes our master, is at most `MAX_DRIFT_MS` ahead and `verify_author` passes. A signature proves who left, the sender's place in our roster that we may act on it: a stranger handing on our real leave, a leave under another key, another identity's leave, a removal of us by someone else, and our removal of someone else all change nothing. Bounded at 256 marks, one per server; a full book evicts from the own device that told us the most (fair share by teller, never age alone). Harness `an_announced_join_ends_only_on_its_own_identitys_leave`; unit `a_full_left_book_evicts_from_the_busiest_device` |
| Ending a join a sibling announced (pending join, adopted state, room, KeyPackage) | the same arm -> `sync_handler::end_join_we_left`, only for `PendingJoin.from_sibling` and only for a leave whose OWN clock is after the announce's seal (`PendingJoin.announced_at`) | Judged by the leave's HLC, never its carrier's seal: an old leave parked and sealed afresh may predate a rejoin it does not know of, and waits for the completion fold below. A join the user asked for is never ended by a leave: its completion judges it. Harness `t14r_a_stale_leave_sealed_late_does_not_end_a_rejoin` |
| A join completion that a membership from before our leave answers | swarm `SyncResponse` arm: the held leave is folded through `merge_ops_with` (the one ingest, `ingest_remote` + `op_allowed`) before the completion guard (`left_here`) | Completion needs us to be a member AFTER our own leave in HLC order: a sibling's announced join ends (`end_join_we_left`), a join the user asked for stays open until a fresh admission. A fold that keeps us a member makes the leave history (`forget_left`). Harness `a_sibling_refuses_a_join_its_identity_left_after_a_restart` |

Join request vs `left_at`: decided in track w2-d (§55c), still no slack. Residuals: a lock moved while our leave waits (we are then hidden in the room and reach members only through other shared rooms); a leave reaching the owner behind an owner checkpoint is handled by the late-leave row above, with its same-admitter residual. The harness resolver is process-global, so a leaver that cannot place a multi-device owner's devices (no roster held) is not covered there: it would hand nothing to that owner until it can.

## 55. Cleared server history and the channel_messages key (track w2-c, 0.14)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A channel message row on ANY path (live, MLS/Olm, public, sync, backfill, push fetch) for a channel this device cleared | `MessageStore::insert_channel_message[_judged]` (the one insert into `channel_messages`) | One statement: `INSERT ... SELECT ... WHERE NOT floor_refuses_sql(...)`. Refused: a row at or before the channel's `exact_from` (floor minus `CLEAR_SKEW_MS` = `MAX_DRIFT_MS`, or higher where the id set overflowed), a row without an id at or before the floor, or a row whose id hash (`cleared_id_hash`, SHA-256 of a tag and the id, never the id) is in `channel_cleared_ids`. So a slow clock's post made after the clear, stamped within the skew below the floor, lands. A local choice, not a trust verdict; live and backfill alike. Floors are set only by our own clear (`clear_history_in`): per channel its newest held row capped at our clock, or our clock for a channel holding nothing yet (a server still backfilling); never raised by a peer. A held row stamped past the floor (a clock ahead of ours) goes with the clear by its id. Units `w2c_cleared_history_is_refused_when_it_comes_back`, `w2c_a_floor_never_reaches_past_now`, `w2c_a_fast_clocks_post_goes_with_the_clear`, `w2c_a_slow_clocks_post_after_a_clear_lands`, `w2c_a_server_clear_takes_its_empty_channels_and_nothing_else`; harness `w2c_cleared_history_stays_cleared_and_stays_on_other_members` |
| `channel_cleared_ids` (hashes of ids a clear took within the skew of the floor, or stamped above it) | `remember_cleared_ids` inside the clear's marking transaction | Written only by our own clear; at most `MAX_CLEARED_IDS` (4096) per channel at or below the floor: past it the oldest go and `exact_from` moves up over them, so a forgotten id is still refused; a later clear never moves the cut back. Above the floor at most another 4096, the nearest to it kept; those never move `exact_from`, which never passes the floor (a flood of future stamps cannot refuse real posts). The start-up finish finds held rows by the remembered time (`idx_cleared_ids_ts`), never by scanning the history. Units `w2c_cleared_ids_are_bounded`, `w2c_fast_posts_never_move_the_cut_past_the_floor` |
| What a cleared row would show: the live `ChannelMessageReceived` (MLS/Olm/public arm), the push banner, a synced item's extras (file header + `FileHeaderReceived`, reactions, hidden flag, link card), a live FileHeader (MLS and Olm arms) | `message_ops::persist_incoming_channel_message` (outcome `ChannelInsert::Cleared`, nothing emitted, not even a duplicate), `fetch::insert_channel_row` (no banner), `sync_handler::ingest_synced_channel_item` (`refuse_below_floor` before the upsert), `file_handler::handle_envelope_file_header` + the swarm Olm FileHeader arm (`channel_floor_refuses`) | The relay ring replays the half hour before a reconnect: without these, cleared posts reappeared in an open pane after a restart. Live reactions already need the row (`channel_reaction_target_ok`). Units `w2c_cleared_history_reaches_no_pane` (incl. a row a cut-short clear still holds), `w2c_a_push_never_brings_back_cleared_history`, `w2c_a_cleared_items_reactions_are_not_stored_again`, `w2c_a_cleared_messages_header_registers_nothing`; source scan `w2c_cleared_history_gates_stay_wired` (Olm arm, both push arms, start-up finish) |
| What a cleared channel's sync asks name (no write of its own) | `channel_sync_request`, `get_per_sender_timestamps`, `channel_gap_anchor`, `channel_next_page_request` | Since t14-ar39 a cleared channel asks exactly as it would have without the clear, counting what it cleared as held (section 65, `channel_cleared_*`): no ask starts at or names the floor, and `channel_floor_senders`/`channel_ask_start` are gone (dropped at open). The slow clock's post the floor accepts still arrives by sync, inside its sender's 30-minute lookback or by the gap digest. Local only: `scrub_device_secrets` drops floors, cleared ids and counts, and pending file names from a backup or link snapshot. Units `w2c_a_cleared_channel_asks_as_before_its_clear`, `w2c_sync_asks_reach_a_slow_clocks_post_the_floor_accepts`, `w2c_a_next_page_after_a_clear_never_asks_below_the_floor`, `w2c_a_device_copy_forgets_the_floors` |
| Serving, not a write: a channel sync answer, or a guest's public page, from a device that cleared the channel | `sync_handler::build_channel_sync_batch` (the swarm responder, its MLS/Olm twins and the pending flush all build here) asks `MessageStore::channel_held_from` and `channel_ask_reaches_below`; the guest responder (`PublicChannelSyncRequest`) asks `sync_handler::guest_page_more` | Channel sync asks ONE holder at a time and a last page ends the ask, so a device holding nothing at or below its floor never answers an ask reaching there (a plain `since` below it, a sender named at or below it, a sender it cleared that the ask leaves out, served in full, or a gap digest starting at or below it) as the channel: it answers no rows and `has_more` (`Page::Stuck`), and the asker's sweep moves to another holder within a second. No rows at all, since any row would move the asker's watermarks past the history we no longer hold. A guest page reaching the floor says more follows, and an empty one is not sent (every member answers a guest). Since t14-ar39 this is the answer to an asker that does not set `partial_ok` only, judged against the uniform held-from value; a 0.14 asker gets the rows past it (section 65). Units `w2c_a_cleared_holder_never_ends_an_ask_below_its_floor`, `w2c_a_gap_below_the_floor_is_never_answered_whole`, `w2c_guest_pages_from_a_cleared_device`; harness `w2c_a_cleared_holder_never_ends_a_joiners_catch_up`, `w2c_a_cleared_holder_never_ends_a_returning_members_catch_up`, `w2c_a_cleared_member_never_shows_a_guest_the_channels_start` |
| One row per message id in `channel_messages` (the content key is gone) | `idx_channel_msgs_msg_id`, now `UNIQUE ... WHERE message_id IS NOT NULL`; the 0.13 `UNIQUE(server, channel, sender, timestamp, text)` table is rebuilt once at start (`rebuild_channel_messages`, the single-connection window in `migrate_auto_vacuum_once`) | Dedup is by id at the insert sites and now in the schema: a second row with the same id (two writers past `channel_message_exists`) is ignored; two distinct posts alike in sender, millisecond and text both land. The rebuild is one transaction (same ids, the oldest row of an id kept, the sequence carried, indexes and search triggers recreated, search rebuilt only if a copy went), refuses a column shape our DDL never wrote (a foreign database's schema is never run as SQL), and on any failure leaves the old table whole for the next start. iOS holds a background task across the node start and the clear (`holdingStorageTask`, AppDelegate `hollow/storage_background`), so neither is frozen holding the container's lock (0xdead10cc). Units `w2c_two_posts_alike_in_one_millisecond_both_land`, `w2c_one_message_id_is_stored_once`, `w2c_an_old_database_loses_its_content_key_and_keeps_its_history`, `w2c_a_failed_rebuild_leaves_the_old_table_whole`, `w2c_a_rebuild_refuses_a_column_it_did_not_write`, `w2c_only_plain_columns_are_rebuilt`, `w2c_sender_watermarks_seek_the_sender_index` |

Not a remote write, noted for completeness: the clear itself (Settings > Files & storage > Clean up for every server, a server's own Files & storage page for one: `api::storage::clear_server_messages(server_id)`) deletes every row the floors refuse with its edits, deletions, reactions, reaction removals (each kept where a DM row shares the id) and file rows, a batch per transaction, then unlinks our own download of each file at the name it was written under (`file_transfer::final_file_path`, the alphanumerics of id and extension, kept only when directly inside `files/`; via `at_rest::remove`; a stored path may name a former iOS container). The names ride `channel_cleared_files`, written in each batch's own transaction and forgotten only once the name is gone from disk (a download held open keeps it), so a crash between them leaves them for the next start and, once a voice note went, the Windows `audio_cache` (keyed by a path and time nothing names any more); DMs untouched, nothing sent; the file then gives its pages back (`release_free_pages` steps `PRAGMA incremental_vacuum` to the end). A clear cut short finishes at the next start (`api::storage::finish_interrupted_clear` in `start_node`: the rows, then the pending names). iOS counts the holders of `hollow/storage_background` (a clear overlapping a node start) and ends the task only at zero; a hold an expiry ended spends its later end on itself, never on a task taken since (`TaskHolds`). An empty channel this device cleared says so (`channel_history_cleared`), never that the channel starts there. Catch-up while every online holder cleared the channel = AR-39, closed by section 65 (held from X). Units `w2c_clear_takes_what_hangs_off_the_messages_and_leaves_dms`, `w2c_a_clear_keeps_a_dms_evidence_under_a_shared_id`, `w2c_an_interrupted_clear_finishes_later`, `w2c_a_cut_short_clear_still_names_its_files`, `w2c_the_next_start_unlinks_a_cut_short_clears_files`, `w2c_a_clear_unlinks_our_downloads_by_name`, `w2c_a_clear_unlinks_the_download_where_it_was_written`, `w2c_a_name_whose_unlink_failed_is_kept`, `w2c_a_long_history_is_cleared_whole`; widget `clear_server_messages_test.dart`. Residuals: a reaction stamped after the floor to a cleared message older than the skew window is refused only by its missing row on the live path, but a synced one riding a re-served row is refused with it; floors survive leaving and rejoining a server, and a restored own backup loses them (`scrub_device_secrets`) and syncs everything again.

## 55b. Resumed streams, fallback rank, catalog previews, SFrame on camera and gossip (track w2-e, 0.14)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A relay stream's receive state when its sender opens it again | `ws_stream_transfer::ws_stream_receive` | Every first chunk is a new encryption of the whole file: one from the stream's own sender drops the partial temp and starts over, never extends it; another sender's first chunk is still refused while the stream is live (H8). Asks never claim an offset (`file_asks::dispatch_one`, the rowless arm of `handle_request_file`) and a holder always streams the whole file under the ask's new key (swarm `FileRequest` arm; the field stays on the wire, ignored). Completion still decrypts and hashes against the committed id first (`try_decrypt_file_stream`). A continuation carries nothing tying it to its first chunk (each seal has its own random nonce), so a late chunk of a replaced stream is kept out at the SENDER (`send_while_placed`, row above); one from a 0.14.0 sender fails that decrypt. Unit `a_new_first_chunk_from_its_sender_starts_the_stream_over`, `a_late_frame_from_a_displaced_stream_never_joins_the_new_one`, harness `a_stream_asked_again_after_a_cut_delivers_the_file_whole` |
| A file ask made for a cut stream (no Download press) | `file_handler::reask_dead_file_streams` <- the swarm retry tick: a sender back from a gap in its session (`PeerJoined` after it left every room, or of a peer already listed = a fresh session; `file_senders_back`) whose stream sent no frame for `file_stream_return_window` after that return, and any file stream quiet `dead_file_stream_idle` (past the sender's own stall cutoff) | Presence never ends a stream: the relay announces a sender gone while its session only waits in grace or hides, and a resumed session carries its streams on. Only a stream we were taking or a header whose push already passed the header-time auto-download gate (`pending_file_streams`, so the ask's receipt bypasses nothing the push did not pass), and of those only a file still wanted: a live ask, a guest pull, or a header that came as a push (`PendingFileStream.pushed`), so "Stop waiting" and a declined push stay so. A returned sender's headers whose bytes have not begun are asked for only when no live data channel to it could be carrying them (the answer's new key would fail that copy). No row or a row already complete asks nothing. The ask is the ordinary `handle_request_file` one: one holder at a time, a whole new stream under a new key, hashed against the id. Harness `a_push_cut_by_a_killed_sender_completes_when_it_returns`, `a_push_whose_sender_died_before_its_bytes_completes_when_it_returns`, `a_push_cut_by_a_sender_that_left_completes_when_it_returns`, `a_push_cut_by_a_sender_the_snapshots_purge_completes_when_it_returns`, `a_push_that_stalls_is_asked_for_again`, `a_relay_stream_survives_its_senders_session_blip`, `a_file_on_a_live_data_channel_is_not_asked_again_after_a_session_blip`, `a_cancelled_ask_is_not_asked_again_when_its_stream_dies` |
| The relay stream a failed data-channel send falls back to | `file_handler::handle_webrtc_transfer_failed` from `WebrtcSend.rank` (set by `stream_to_peer`, `stream_to_peer_bytes`) | The fallback keeps the rank the send was admitted at (guest < member < own, §53), never a flat Member. Unit `a_data_channel_send_falls_back_to_the_relay_at_its_own_rank` |
| Catalog preview bytes to Dart (GIF and sticker grids, FFZ emotes, IGDB covers) | `api/catalog::fetch_catalog_preview` <- `GifThumbCache`, `CatalogPreviewImage` | Only an address our own searches hand out (the FFZ and IGDB CDN prefixes, the GIF proxy base, direct-mode media hosts), judged as reqwest will fetch it (`catalog::https_url`/`url_under`, also behind `gifs::url_host`, the FFZ import and the IGDB fetchers: https, no userinfo, the exact host and port, the path below the prefix after dot segments and backslashes resolve, no encoded separator), no redirects, 6 MB cap, then the image check (`catalog::shown_or_held`: bytes the check was too busy for are kept, bounded, and the re-ask judges them again instead of downloading; Dart waits without a download slot). Every such URL comes from our own catalog answers or the person's own saved library, never from a peer (checked on 0.13 main). No `Image.network`/`NetworkImage` in lib (guard). Units `only_catalog_addresses_are_fetched`, `catalog_preview_refuses_a_backslash_authority`, `url_host_parses_and_refuses_tricks`, `a_preview_reaches_dart_only_after_the_image_check`, `a_preview_still_being_checked_is_never_fetched_twice`; `gif_thumb_cache_test`, `image_file_decode_guard_test` |
| Shop preview art to Dart | `api/shop::fetch_shop_art` | Hash and container check, then the image check (`catalog::shown_or_held`, kept as above): the hash proves the shop sent it, never that a decoder may take it. Unit `shop_art_reaches_dart_only_after_the_image_check` |
| A 1:1 call's camera frames | `voice_service._bindSframeVideoSender` / `_bindSframeVideoReceiver` on every camera add, device switch, onTrack, key apply, heal and transport rebuild; `FrameCryptorService.rebindSender/rebindReceiver` | The camera sender and receiver carry the call key's cryptor, created only once a key exists and moved to each new transceiver (same sender = no rebind, no clear gap). Unconditional (0.14) while the call has a key: a 0.13 peer gets black video both ways and its failing video decryptor fires the 5 s heal, which also re-creates the audio decryptor (clean break, covered by the RED 0.13 notice). `sframe_camera_gossip_test` |
| A voice a gossip neighbour forwards | `voice_channel_service._syncForwards` (sender), `_receiveForwarded` (receiver) | SHIPS OFF (`gossip_forward.gossipForwardingEnabled`, tests `forwarding ships off`, `with forwarding off no heard voice goes onto another leg...`, `turning forwarding off takes a live forward back off its leg`): a re-sent remote track on a sender races the audio device's capture thread and libwebrtc aborts (RTC_CHECK_RUNS_SERIALIZED, fleet w2e5). When a transport that owns its send stream turns it on: Forwarded only to a leg that asked for that origin (`fwd_want`) and only while the cryptor holds a key, each forwarded sender bound to its own `fwd:{origin}` cryptor before the one batched renegotiation that carries it (`ForwardRenegBatcher`, per leg per burst), and kept only once that cryptor is bound (`FrameCryptorService.encrypts`), one hop. The far side learns forwarded tracks from the SDP's `hfwd-{origin}` stream id (`gossip_forward.dart`, written against the sender id the SDP names, which differs from the track id when a mic-less forwarder sends on its leg's own audio section) before applying it, so a forwarded voice never takes the forwarder's mic decryptor, volume or speaking light, and it is kept silent while its origin is heard another way. A marked stream counts as a forward only with forwarding on, from the leg we asked for that origin, for a member we hold no live leg to (`_acceptsForward`); anything else is that member's own voice, under its volume and speaking light, and silence is kept per forwarder, never by a track id another leg can copy. Until forwarding exists a channel stays a full mesh up to 16 people (`VOICE_GOSSIP_THRESHOLD_UP` = `DOWN` = 17, `maxVoicePcs` 15), since gossip mode hears only overlay neighbours. Every re-offer narrows the leg's video sections to VP8 first (`_offerVideoAsVp8Only`): a leg we answered keeps the offerer's codec list and an iOS forwarder's re-offer failed to apply. The origin a forwarder names is its own claim (insider, AR-03). `sframe_camera_gossip_test`, `gossip_forward_service_test` (a mic-less forwarder still names the voice; the re-offer carries VP8 alone; a forward never asked, or of a member heard on its own leg, is the forwarder's own voice; a copy kept silent never silences another leg's voice; an unbound forward comes back off), unit `a_voice_channel_stays_a_full_mesh_up_to_sixteen_people` |
| A leg's ask for forwarded voices (`vc_fwd_want`, targeted, Olm) | `voice_handler::handle_envelope_voice_channel_fwd_want` <- both swarm VC arms; Dart `_onForwardWant` | Counts only from a participant of that call, names only other participants (never the asker, never a stranger), at most 64 peer-id-shaped origins; Dart keeps it per leg (dies with the leg) and forwards only voices it hears straight. A listener asks only for members it neither dials nor holds a leg to, so a room where everyone is a neighbour asks for nothing and pays no renegotiation; it asks again each time a leg connects, since the far side drops a want that beat its own leg. `gossip_forward_service_test` drives a want through to the renegotiation for a forwarder that sat in the channel before the switch and one that joined after. Units `a_forward_want_names_only_other_participants`, `authz_a_vc_signal_counts_only_from_a_participant_of_that_call`; harness `authz_a_vc_signal_from_outside_the_call_is_refused` |
| A received voice's playback volume (deafen, member slider, 1:1 call slider, the meter loopback's silent copy) (HOL-SEC-190, track t14-vol) | Dart `setRemoteReceiverVolume` (`remote_track_volume.dart`) <- `voice_channel_service.setDeafened`/`_applyRemoteVolume`/`_onPeerTrack`/`_receiveForwarded`, `voice_service.setRemoteAudioVolume`/`_onRemoteTrack`; `local_meter_loopback`; fork `Helper.setReceiverVolume` -> native `setVolume` (Android `receiverSetVolume` + `PeerConnectionObserver.receivedAudioTracks`, Apple `receiverSetVolume:`, Windows/Linux `FlutterWebRTC::ReceiverSetVolume`) | Named by the local connection id and the receiver id, never a track id: the sender writes that id, and the old lookup (local tracks first, then every connection) let a participant name its voice after our microphone or another member and escape deafen or move someone else's slider. Native looks only among THAT connection's receivers (Android: the transceivers `onTrack` handed over, by mid, since a `getReceivers()` list is disposed by the next call), never a local track, never another connection; nothing matched = an error and no change. Every audio receiver of a leg but accepted forwards takes the slider and deafen (the peer decides how many it opens), one opened later starts at the wanted volume, and a slider moved while deafened is only recorded. `remote_receiver_volume_test` (DM receivers and late tracks, a new call starts at full volume, source guards on all three native lookups and on lib/ never setting a volume by track), `gossip_forward_service_test` group `receiver-keyed volume` |
| The Windows entire-screen share's voice redirect (which received voices play from the excluded child process, and at what volume) (HOL-SEC-191, track t14-redir) | Dart `VoiceService.startVoiceRedirect`/`stopVoiceRedirect`/`_onRemoteTrack`, `VoiceChannelService.startVoiceRedirect`/`stopVoiceRedirect`/`_redirectNewVoice` <- `call_provider` / `voice_channel_provider` share start and `_disarmVoiceRedirect`; fork `Helper.voiceRedirectStart(receivers)` -> native `voiceRedirectStart` -> `FlutterVoiceRedirect::Start`; `FlutterWebRTC::ReceiverSetVolume` -> `FlutterVoiceRedirect::SetWantedVolume` | Each voice is named by (local connection id, receiver id) with its wanted volume, never a track id: the sender writes that id, and the old global lookup (local tracks first, then every connection) let a participant get our microphone redirected and its own voice captured into the share. Native looks only among THAT connection's receivers; unknown = skipped. The child-bound PCM takes the wanted volume (0 = nothing sent), a volume set while redirected moves only the redirect and keeps hollow.exe's playout at 0, Stop restores each voice at its wanted volume and the services re-apply it, a voice arriving mid-share or WHILE the redirect arms joins at its wanted volume (`VoiceRedirectArm` holds it), every track op rides the audio op queue. At most 16 taps per connection and 256 in all: a voice over a cap stays silent in hollow.exe (fails closed for its own connection), closed connections' voices are dropped on each Start. A render child that died is never replaced before Stop (the capturer excludes only its pid; voices stay silent in-process), the writer is joined before any restart; a VC share stopped during the arm disarms when the arm returns, an arm returning pid 0 stops native. `remote_receiver_volume_test` (1:1 redirect, restore, late voice, arm race, call ending mid-arm, pid 0, guards `voice_redirect_resolves_only_named_receivers`, `a redirected voice keeps the volume the listener chose`, `a render child lost mid-share is never replaced or restarted over`, `one connection cannot take every redirect slot`), `gossip_forward_service_test` group `share voice redirect`, `vc_leave_teardown_test` (stop during the arm) |
| Catalog fetches (GIF search and picks, FFZ search and import, IGDB search, details and covers, previews) | `api/catalog::catalog_client` in `gifs.rs`, `emotes.rs`, `showcase.rs`, `catalog.rs` | No redirects: a 3xx from an allowed address is an answer, never a hop to a host no allowlist names. Unit `catalog_fetchers_refuse_redirects` (a local 302 is not followed, and no catalog file builds another client) |

## 55c. What a sender a locked room hides may reach (track w2-d, 0.14)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A hidden sender's broadcast (0x03, 0x0A, text `msg`) into a door-locked server room, its fan-out, held copies and its breaker and send-budget walks | `ws_handler.cpp` `cast_of` -> `Cast::reaches` / `writes` / `reaches_held` (the one predicate the delivery loops, `walk_cast` and `fanout_bytes` share); `door_room::ReachPicks` | A sender the room hides (no door proof: a joiner, a member whose door is stale, anyone holding the server id) reaches at most `HIDDEN_REACH` = 3 live provers, the lowest by `ReachOrder` over the sender and the frame's first 12 bytes (a sealed frame's magic and seal time, fresh per frame, so a retry picks afresh and nothing is ground in advance); on-screen provers first, away ones (`PerSocketData::hidden`) only for the slots left (they answer no ask but hear a door-less device's `RosterNotice`); never a guest, fetch or dead socket, never a session in grace. The picks are built once per frame (`CastSeen::picks`, reused by the send budget and the delivery) and charged for the three deliveries plus the room walk (room size / `IDS_PER_W`). Live `test_hidden_sender_reach`, `test_hidden_sender_reaches_away_provers`, `breaker_hidden_sender_pays_for_three` (W and one build), `oom_hidden_sender_counts_three_copies`; unit `test_reach_order_is_pinned`, `test_reach_picks_the_lowest_three`, `test_reach_picks_on_screen_first` |
| A hidden sender's topic frame (0x07): the ring tee and the fan-out | `handle_binary_topic_msg` | Only the join ring (`~join`): any other topic writes no ring and reaches nobody; a `~join` frame is teed and reaches the same three provers. Live `test_hidden_sender_reach` ("a hidden topic frame reaches nobody", "kept in the join ring") |
| Who answers a door ask or a hidden joiner's live request (client) | `door_room::reached` from `DoorRooms::answer_ask` and the swarm `ServerJoinRequest` arm (a joiner our room view does not list) | A member answers only if the frame's order puts it among the first three of the room devices it sees, the relay's pick mirrored (Rust `reach_rank` pinned with the C++ order, `reach_rank_matches_the_relays_pinned_vector`); for a hidden joiner the join coordinator is elected from those three alone (`door_room::reached_view`), so one of the members the frame reached serves it, never one the relay did not hand it to; a re-ask (`repeat_ask`) is served by any member it reaches, as before. On a relay that does not pick, still at most three answer a door ask and one serves a join. Only an on-screen member answers either (`ws_client::backgrounded()` returns early in `answer_ask`, door grants and removal notices alike, and in the hidden-joiner branch; a re-ask is still served): an away device's own view is frozen, so whom it thinks a frame picked may not be whom the relay picked (w2-d re-check F3). An on-screen view lists only on-screen peers, so the relay's away fill never breaks the agreement (unit `away_provers_fill_only_what_the_on_screen_leave`); a phone in a call, VC or meeting never reports away (`relayRealtimeProvider`), so it keeps answering. `RosterNotice` and delivery do not read the flag. MockRelay mirrors the relay (`hidden_reach`). Harness `w2d_a_hidden_joiner_is_served_by_the_members_its_frame_reached`, `w2d_a_hidden_ask_its_frame_did_not_pick_us_for_is_left_to_others`, `w2d_an_away_pick_serves_no_hidden_join_ask`; unit `door_answerers_are_the_three_the_asks_frame_picks`, `an_away_pick_answers_no_door_ask` |
| Join request vs the joiner's last leave | swarm `ServerJoinRequest` arm (both legs, and the `MemberAdded` it authors); the joiner's `sync_handler::ask_stamp` from `LeaveBook::ask_floor` (`AskFloor`, the `left` marks), `PendingJoin::ask_again` never back | Refused when its seal is no later than the joiner's `left_at`, no slack; its device-sealed `requested_at` counts too, but only when the ask names (`follows_leave` = `AskFloor::follows`) the leave that closed the joiner's last span (`MemberSpan::until_hlc`, `ServerState::left_by`), so an earlier rejoin's ask handed back from the ring never outruns a later leave stamped at the same clock lead. The joiner names the leave the fold closes the span with: the EARLIEST own-identity leave at or past the ask that opened that membership (`LeftMark::closer`, `opened`), whichever of our devices made it, kept beside the newest (`op_json`, which a join folds in); a leave stamped before that ask ended an earlier membership, and one after a sibling's announced rejoin (`from_sibling` join it ends) starts a new one. The ask is stamped past the NEWEST leave of ours held (`AskFloor::past_ms`), and the member that admits a REJOIN (a target with a closed span) witnesses the ask's stamp before authoring `MemberAdded` (a first-time joiner moves no clock, `w2d_a_first_time_joiners_ask_moves_no_admitters_clock`; a refused admission restores the clock), so the admission sorts after every leave of ours the joiner knew of and a sibling's later leave handed on afterwards ends nothing (w2-d re-check F2). A book saved before 0.14 names its newest leave until it hears another. A leave is stamped past the ask that admitted its membership (`ServerState::witness_own_ask`), so the next ask never repeats one the fold already spent. A leave stamped by an HLC a co-member's clock ran ahead of our wall used to refuse an immediate rejoin for minutes (parked copies too, so the tile waited for "Request again"); the joiner now stamps the ask just past its own leaves (capped at `MAX_DRIFT_MS`). Since HOL-SEC-207 (t15, after 0.14.0) a rejoin ask stamped PAST the joiner's own wall clock (`at > made`, the lead case the old rule cannot judge) SIGNS what it follows (`JoinAsk.follows`: the named leave and `made`, the joiner's wall clock at signing; `hollow-join-ask3`), so the FOLD judges it too: `admission_allowed` refuses an admission whose ask names a leave older than the leave or removal that closed the joiner's last span before the admission (`members::closer_before`) unless the ask was signed after it (`made` past it). A member that authors an admission on such a spare from before a later leave or kick re-admits nobody on any replica, whatever the ask's stamp. Every other ask (first joins, rejoins with no lead) signs the exact 0.14.0 ask2/ask1 bytes (`JoinAsk::sign_rejoin`): a 0.14.0 `read_op` keeps an ask3 admission UNREAD and never counts the joiner, so its MLS leaf add is Held and lapses (60 s) and a rejoin among 0.14.0-only members never completes; an ask stamped no later than it was signed is judged by its stamp (`opens`), since a later leave of ours is stamped past it. Never carry ask2 and ask3 signatures together (still unread on 0.14.0, and a strip to ask2 re-opens 207). The joiner signs at the first ask, every re-ask (`PendingJoin::ask_again`) and a restored parked row (the book's `ask_floor`); the live check above still reads the sealed frame's `follows_leave`. Units `a_spare_reask_folds_out_after_a_second_leave` (RED with the fold check off; covers a kick for an ask3), `a_spare_reask_folds_out_whichever_arrives_first`, `a_spare_reask_folds_out_on_a_checkpoint_base`, `a_spare_reask_folds_out_after_a_leave_in_the_admissions_millisecond`, `a_rejoin_ask_binds_the_leave_it_follows`, `a_rejoin_ask_payload_is_pinned`, `a_rejoin_ask_names_a_leave_by_a_peer_id`, `a_rejoin_without_a_clock_lead_signs_the_0140_ask`; harness `t15_a_member_readmits_no_leaver_on_a_spare_ask`, `t15_a_rejoin_without_a_clock_lead_signs_the_0140_ask` (0.14.0 modelled by the bytes, `JoinAsk::reads_as_0140`). Mixed with 0.14.0 members: only a lead rejoin is refused live and kept unread by them, and only while the lead lasts (a re-ask past it signs ask2). Harness `w2d_a_rejoin_inside_our_own_leaves_clock_lead_is_served` (RED before), `w2d_an_ask_from_before_the_leave_handed_back_readmits_nobody` (RED under any slack), `w2d_a_parked_reask_handed_back_after_a_second_leave_readmits_nobody` (RED before the binding), `w2d_a_rejoin_after_both_devices_left_inside_a_lead_is_served`, `w2d_a_rejoin_after_the_sibling_left_first_inside_a_lead_is_served` (both RED before the named closer); unit `a_new_ask_names_the_leave_that_ended_our_membership` |
| A hidden member's door ask and the grant that answers it (client, t14-hidden) | `DoorRooms::on_status` -> `lock_keeper::watch` (a fresh read of the relay's chain); the ask goes from `DoorRooms::chain_read` in the `LockChain` arm, or after `CHAIN_WAIT` (15 s) with no read; then `on_grant` | A grant counts only for the newest door of the relay's chain as verified back to the owner (`on_grant`, unchanged). The ask waits for that read: a grant that beat it was dropped, and answerers hand one device a door once per 20 s, so a stranger joiner whose fold holds no door (a join answer that left the lock op out) stayed hidden for good; a member back from a lock move stayed hidden until a later sync brought the move. Harness `t14_hidden_a_door_ask_waits_for_a_slow_lock_read` (RED with the ask sent at once), unit `a_door_ask_waits_for_the_chain_read` |

Residuals (accepted_risks AR-37): one identity with many online proving devices takes answer slots in proportion to its devices (design G §4.7 gave each identity one slot; the relay picks devices, as it cannot tell their masters apart outside inboxes), redrawn on every frame and every retry (Vitalik 2026-11-01). 0.13 clients on a 0.14 relay answer the picked door asks and hidden joins unreliably (their own answerer rules rarely meet the relay's picks: a 0.13 door ask is answered about 71% of the time at 10 provers, 17% at 50); accepted under the clean break and the RED 0.13 notice, no relay switch, self-hosters update clients with the relay (Vitalik 2026-11-01). An ask a joiner's OTHER device sealed before the leave on a clock ahead of the leaving device's HLC passes, as before (bounded by the clock skew a seal may carry). A joiner that lost its `left` mark (a reset, or the mark evicted from a full book) stamps from the wall clock and waits out the lead as before. A resolution naming an ask stamped past its own seal by more than `LIVE_SKEW_MS` is ignored (only with a lead near the full drift bound), which costs the dedup of that join, never the join. The named leave follows only the leaves our devices heard: a kick that raced our leave, a leave a sibling made that never reached the joining device, or one a sibling's rejoin this device never heard of came between, leaves the name unmatched and the ask counts by its seal (fails closed, waits out the lead). An admitting member's clock moves to a REJOINER's ask stamp (at most `MAX_DRIFT_MS` ahead, the bound every witnessed op has) once its verdict passed; when the fold refuses the admission the clock is put back. HOL-SEC-207 residuals: the fold judges `follows` only on lead asks an updated client signed; ask2 spares 0.14.0 joiners signed, and asks without a lead, stay under the 0.14.0 rule (for a no-lead rejoin the gap is clock skew between two devices of one identity, as live). Kicks are covered only for asks carrying `follows`: `members::derive` sets no last leave on a Remove, so a member can re-admit a kicked member on a spare ask without `follows` (a first join's, or a rejoin's with no lead) made before the kick (reviewer test `review207_a_first_join_spare_after_a_kick`); counting a removal in `opens` forks from 0.14.0 replicas, so it waits for a coordinated clean break. The restored parked row's signer has no test (mutation survivor; it re-sends only on a live peer or after 12 h).

## 56. Device proof, identity side (track t14-ident, 0.14, embargoed)

DEVICE_PROOF_DESIGN sections 4, 8.1, 8.5, 8.6. Part 1 (the vouch cap, HOL-SEC-189) is the §22 row.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Who writes for an identity in server authority (read by the checkpoint fold, track C, and the relay lock, track R2) | `identity/roster.rs::authority()` (`Authority`: counted, removed, counted_removals), mirrored by `relay-uws/src/roster.h` `authority()` | A pure verdict over statements that each verify alone: no clock, no maturity, no first sight. Signers are the devices the current base's roots vouch (pending joins seed nothing), and the removal walk decides which removals count: a signer stays alive only while no earlier alive signer removed it and it is still in the valid closure at its turn, so a mutual removal goes to the device closer to the phrase, never to both, and a removed thief's puppets uncount nobody. A removal of a pending join counts. Units `authority_ignores_first_sight_and_maturity`, `a_removed_device_cannot_uncount_the_device_that_removed_it`, `an_equal_rank_mutual_removal_goes_to_the_lower_id`, `a_removed_thiefs_puppet_cannot_uncount_the_device_that_removed_the_thief`, `a_puppet_two_levels_under_a_removed_thief_cannot_remove_the_root`, `a_deep_thief_that_empties_the_walk_is_undone_by_the_root`, `a_minted_key_and_a_recovered_away_thief_never_count`, `the_bare_master_counts_only_where_its_statements_do`; C++ `test_roster` compares `auth_base`/`auth_counted`/`auth_removed` on every vector |
| Which removals a roster keeps (every ingest, merge and the relay's book) | `compacted`, reserved slots | Per standing target the removal the walk counted first takes one of `MAX_RESERVED` (32) slots outside the 96 general ones, the shallowest targets first; `within_caps` allows 128. So no flood, not even the target's own, pushes out the removal that holds it. Units `a_device_never_floods_out_a_removal_of_itself`, `a_thief_flood_never_pushes_out_the_roots_removal_of_it`, `a_voided_best_signer_does_not_hold_the_reserved_slot`, `the_reserved_slots_go_to_the_shallowest_targets`, `compaction_is_idempotent_and_keeps_the_walk`, `the_largest_roster_with_reserved_slots_fits_on_the_wire`, `the_compaction_walk_ignores_a_removed_thiefs_puppet`. The slots come from a WIDE walk (every standing signer, the pending tier included, so a waiting device's removal is held for the view where it counts) and the vouches that keep its signers alive are kept with the walk's support; only tier-0 signers' removals void a vouch (§22). Units `a_pending_joins_removal_keeps_its_reserved_slot`, `the_wide_walks_support_keeps_a_waiting_signers_path` |
| When THIS node first saw each removal and vouch of our own roster (`roster_statement_seen`, local, never synced) | `roster_book::save_rows` (own master only) -> `note_statements` -> `MessageStore::note_roster_statements`; keyed by `roster::removal_seen_key` / `vouch_seen_key` (SHA-256 of kind and signature) | Written only by our own saves of our own roster, whatever path brought it (live, carried, push fetch, our own change): a new statement is dated now, one that left the roster is marked dropped and goes 30 days later, at most 4096 rows per identity, the longest dropped first. A database from before the table carries one mark row (`'*','*'`, migration, only when `device_lists` held a roster); the first own save after the upgrade takes it and dates at 0 only what the roster held BEFORE that save (`date_held_before_upgrade`), never what the save brings, so a push fetch saving first cannot pre-date a thief's new statement. Not `roster_seen` (that name is dropped on every open). A link snapshot carries the presenter's rows (its own sights, all in the past). Our own members are noted the same way (`roster::device_seen_key`), dated at 0 by the upgrade mark. Units `a_roster_seen_row_outlives_its_statement_for_30_days`, `first_sights_stay_under_the_cap_longest_dropped_first`, `an_upgrade_dates_at_0_only_what_our_roster_held_before_it`, `a_fresh_install_dates_its_first_roster_now` |
| A phrase recovery's keep list and its T (our roster; the settling reads T, track C) | FFI `recovery_kept_by_default(lost_since_ms)` -> `roster_book::recovery_kept_by_default`; FFI `recover_with_phrase(phrase, keep, lost_since_ms)` -> `note_recovery_lost_since` (setting `recovery_lost_since:{base}`) | Only our own command after the phrase verifies. The dialog asks when the person last had the other devices before it shows the keep list; only devices this node counted strictly before T start ticked (a fresh install ticks none, "not sure" = 0 ticks none), never the device recovering; T is stored per new base, clamped at 0, and with no other device it is the moment of the recovery. Units `a_recovery_keeps_by_default_only_devices_counted_before_t`, `a_recovery_remembers_when_the_dropped_devices_were_last_had`; widget `recovery_phrase_dialogs_test` |
| Removing one of our devices: retire or lost or stolen (our roster; then the relay, contacts, Olm and MLS as any removal) | FFI `revoke_device(device, lost_since_ms, with_dependents)` and `remove_device_plan` (`RemoveMode::from_lost_since`) -> `NodeCommand::RevokeDevice` -> `sync_handler::handle_revoke_device` -> `roster_book::remove(mode, with_dependents)` | Only our own command. Every removal the walk counted from a removed device is re-signed by this device with the same keep (8.5): retiring re-signs all, lost or stolen only those this node first saw strictly before T (seen at T counts as after). Then, until nothing changes, every device the walk removed before and no longer does gets a counted removal of it re-signed (a removal moves the death of an earlier remover before its turn, and that remover's removals stop counting); only the lost-or-stolen lapses come back. ONE helper, `roster_book::add_removals`, for `remove` and `remove_all_others`. Devices only the target places (`Roster::dependents_of`, outside the valid closure without it; a device another device also vouches is never one, nor the device removing) go with it: those whose placing vouch was not here before T always (named, no toggle), the older ones when "Remove them too" stays on (on for lost or stolen, off for retiring). Each removal keeps the target's vouched members minus the devices removed with it. `handle_revoke_device` returns every removed id, so Olm sessions and MLS leaves go for all of them. `takes_effect_for_servers` is false when a device closer to the phrase removed ours first. Units `lost_or_stolen_re_signs_only_removals_first_seen_before_t`, `lost_or_stolen_drops_devices_the_thief_linked_after_t`, `lost_or_stolen_never_removes_a_device_the_thief_vouched_after_t_that_another_device_holds`, `retiring_re_signs_every_removal_of_the_retired_device`, `a_removal_plan_never_takes_the_device_making_it`, `a_removal_by_a_removed_device_does_not_take_effect_for_servers`, `the_lost_since_answer_picks_the_mode`; F1 `review_ab_retiring_a_device_never_brings_back_what_an_earlier_device_removed`, `review_ab_lost_or_stolen_never_brings_back_what_an_earlier_device_removed_before_t`, `review_ab_lost_or_stolen_keeps_out_what_another_device_removed_after_t`, `review_ab_removing_all_other_devices_never_brings_back_a_device_a_sibling_removed`, `lost_or_stolen_keeps_out_what_a_kept_device_also_removed`, `retiring_re_signs_a_removal_an_earlier_device_also_made`, `removing_a_device_never_re_signs_its_removal_of_us`, property `removing_a_device_never_brings_back_a_device_the_walk_kept_out` |
| A roster first seen through a `RosterNotice` or `RosterSync` (the global book, R31-8) | `swarm.rs` notice arm -> `roster_book::ingest_notice` -> `notice_may_be_kept`; push fetch `roster_reach::fetch_roster` (both fetch arms) -> the same check with no co-members | Kept only for our own master, a master we already hold a roster for (no new row), one with a friend row of any state (by master, or by the delivering device: a nickname friend's row, which the first roster naming that device re-keys to its master, so it admits one identity), or a master a server we hold names (`server_names_master`: owner, member, or author of an op it keeps, one lookup in the log's author index `ServerState::has_op_author`; parked and held ops join with track C). A first sight on any other terms stores nothing, though every statement verifies and the deliverer is a member. Per master, not per room: the frame's room never reaches `handle_incoming_request_inner`, and a master any held server names is already bounded by our servers. The other first-sight arms carry `device_list` and keep their own rules: FriendRequest, ServerJoinRequest and FriendAccept/Reject (HOL-SEC-192), ProfileCard and ProfileUpdate (§58, HOL-SEC-193). Units `a_strangers_roster_notice_in_a_server_room_stores_nothing`, `a_roster_notice_from_a_master_we_know_nothing_of_stores_nothing`, `the_owners_roster_notice_in_its_room_is_kept`, `a_co_members_and_an_op_authors_notice_is_kept`, `a_pending_friends_and_a_held_masters_notice_is_kept`, source scan `the_roster_notice_arm_goes_through_the_gate` |
| What a join box may carry to place its asker (built by track C, 8.7) | `Roster::placing_subset` | The current recovery, the root's own admission and the vouch chain to the device with the consents along it, only for a device `authority()` counts; each statement verifies alone and a member merges it with what it holds, so a removal it omits still counts there. Unit `the_placing_subset_places_the_device_and_fits_the_box` |

## 57. What a stranger's friend request or join ask may leave behind (HOL-SEC-192, track t14-flood, 0.14)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A new incoming friend request: the requester's roster, carried bundle (`in_bundle_key` setting), card and pending row | swarm `FriendRequest` arm -> `social::incoming_request_has_room`, BEFORE the roster ingest | Room in the pending incoming requests: `MAX_INCOMING_FRIEND_REQUESTS` (64) in all, `MAX_STRANGER_FRIEND_REQUESTS` (32) for requesters sharing no server with us (`shares_a_server`: member of a server we are in, unbanned, never a meeting); a full share refuses the newcomer (silence; the relay mailbox hands it back on our next inbox join), never by age. A requester we hold a row for passes (our own request crossing, a re-request, a friend whose removal by us still waits to be delivered: the `removed` row), except a newer request past our decline. A removal deletes the row on both sides once delivered (`handle_remove_friend`, the `FriendRemove` arm), so a re-add after one is a new request that needs room like any other. The claimed master is only a lookup: a false one fails the ingest after. Harness `review_ab_incoming_friend_requests_from_minted_identities_stay_bounded`, `t14f_a_co_members_request_lands_past_a_stranger_flood`; unit `t14f_incoming_requests_hold_a_total_and_a_stranger_share` |
| The roster a `FriendAccept` or `FriendReject` carries | swarm arms -> `social::answer_meets_our_row`, BEFORE the ingest | Only an answer meeting our pending outgoing request or a friendship; any other keeps nothing. The row is looked up under the claimed master, the sending device, and each device whose OWN consent to that master the roster carries (a request sent while our resolver was cold is filed under the device we asked); a lookup only, the ingest after judges the roster whole. Harness `t14f_a_strangers_accept_or_decline_keeps_no_roster`; unit `t14f_an_answer_from_another_device_meets_a_request_filed_under_the_first` |
| A join asker's roster and card | swarm `ServerJoinRequest` arm: attribution by `roster_book::carried_member` (no write), then `join_verdict::{verdict, may_keep}` | Kept only when the shared verdict (ban, private, full, Twitch, owner-verify, consent: the same function the coordinator's answer uses, so the two never part; our own identity's device judged as a sibling) would admit the ask, with the asker's own ask verifying unless it is a returning member (`may_keep`). One exception: a BANNED asker's own signed ask keeps, so the members see who was refused; the ban list (moderators, never the asker) bounds those. An ask still waiting for Twitch or adult-content consent keeps nothing (anyone holding the invite can send one). Any other refused ask from an identity we already hold refreshes nothing either. Only a member that ANSWERS writes (every member reading a parked ask; for a live ask the elected coordinator, the picked member for a hidden asker, the owner on owner-verify), or any member when the asker is already a member (bounded by membership). A member that steps aside holds the roster and card in RAM (`node/join_stash.rs`, per node loop: 32 KiB per ask, a card whose avatar overweighs it dropped with it; 128 asks and 512 KiB in all; the server the ask came through is the share, its oldest ask gives way; 10 minutes) and lands them on the MLS batch tick only once the asker is a member of that server's state here (`land_admitted_join_asks`, before held commits are re-judged), so a non-coordinator still places an admitted joiner's devices for its MLS add and an ask nobody admits leaves nothing. On a public server the member cap is the bound. Harness `t14f_review_live_asks_a_member_steps_aside_from_keep_nothing_until_admitted` (review probe), `a_join_ask_flood_keeps_no_roster_rows`, `t14f_a_public_server_keeps_rosters_only_for_the_joiners_it_admits`, `t14f_a_member_who_did_not_answer_still_places_the_joiner`, `join_lock_the_card_rides_inside_the_request` (banned), `a_joiner_shows_its_card_to_the_members_it_asks` (consent); units `join_stash::tests::t14f_*` |

## 58. The roster a profile card or update carries (HOL-SEC-193, track t14-prof, 0.14, embargoed)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| A roster first seen through a `ProfileCard` or `ProfileUpdate` (the global book and the resolver), and the card or profile riding with it | `swarm.rs` Carried `ProfileCard` and `ProfileUpdate` arms and the MLS `MessageEnvelope::ProfileUpdate` (`social::handle_envelope_profile_update`) -> `roster_book::admit_profile_roster` (a sync check over the borrowed roster: the swarm handler's debug frame sits within a few KB of a Windows worker's 2 MB stack, and an async wrapper overflowed it in the call tests) -> `notice_may_be_kept`, then the plain `ingest` | Judged BEFORE the ingest, only on `notice_may_be_kept`'s terms (our own, a held roster, a friend row by master or by the delivering device, a master a held server names). A device consents to any identity its owner mints and a first sight needs only the deliverer as a member, so membership proves nothing; nor does an MLS leaf's certified master (a server group's leaves are members, which the server term covers; a meeting's host seats any leaf). A refused roster drops the whole card or profile, its card and the MLS caller's `keep_card` included (keeping it would let a refused roster's message still write a row). The MLS `keep_card` binds to the leaf's certified master, never the resolver. The push fetch handles no profile. Units `a_profile_roster_for_a_minted_master_is_refused`, `a_strangers_own_profile_roster_is_refused`, `a_profile_roster_from_siblings_friends_and_co_members_is_kept`, source scan `the_profile_roster_arms_go_through_the_gate`; harness `probe193_a_co_members_cards_fill_the_roster_book_with_minted_identities`, `probe193_b_a_co_members_profile_updates_over_olm_keep_no_minted_identity`, `probe193_c_a_co_members_profile_updates_over_mls_keep_no_minted_identity`, `rev193_a_meeting_hosts_churned_strangers_fill_a_guests_roster_book` |
| A `ProfileUpdate` decrypted in a meeting group | `swarm.rs` MLS `MessageEnvelope::ProfileUpdate` arm -> `conference::refuses_profile_update(&server_id, ..)` (`is_conference_sid`) before the handler | Dropped whole (roster, profile, card, invisible status): honest clients broadcast it only to servers they hold, and a meeting shows only its card line. Source scan `the_profile_roster_arms_go_through_the_gate`; harness `t14p_a_meeting_carries_no_profile_update_even_from_a_friend` (a friend row passes the roster gate, so this layer alone holds) |
| A meeting card (signed card + `user_profiles` card row, avatar) | `conference::handle_inbound_chat` card line -> `meeting_card_has_room` -> `profile_card::store_card` | The card must be the sending leaf's certified master's and verify (unchanged). Per (our master, meeting) at most `MEETING_CARD_IDENTITIES` (256) identities, RAM, cleared on our own `ConferenceLeave`/`ConferenceEnd`; past it a new identity's card is refused, never another's evicted (every seat is the host's to give); a held identity still updates. RAM-only cards were set aside: every profile reader (FFI profile and avatar calls, overlay looks, Dart `profileProvider`) reads the store. Harness `rev193_b_a_meeting_hosts_churned_strangers_fill_a_guests_card_store` |

Residuals: the roster book and the card store are never pruned. A master a held server names passes, so owner or admin add-kick churn, or minted identities that join an open server, send one profile and leave, grow both with the op log (an op's author stays named), surviving checkpoints and the membership; the member cap bounds members at once, not the total over time (same family as R31-8 and HOL-SEC-192). A meeting host writes up to 256 cards per meeting a guest joins, kept after it. Follow-up (not built): roster and card GC, dropping rows for masters no held server, friend row or our own identity names.

## 59. Checkpoint coverage: a checkpoint replaces only what its owner folded (track t14-cover, 0.14)

Design: `reports/shipped/security/CHECKPOINT_COVERAGE_DESIGN.md` rev 2 plus the rev 2 review changes (H2, H3, M1-M5, L1, L2) and the code review rev 3 fix round (floor, charge trees, exact tail, AR-11 voids) and the focused review 4 round (refused removals void nothing, checkpoint size measured before signing). Device proof (design section 10) NOT built: AR-02 for server ops still open.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Which checkpoint the fold stands on (the base) | `crdt::fold::plan` (`base_of`, base order) | Only a well-formed checkpoint of the anchor owner; base = greatest by (generation, op clock, content hash) among those whose named parent (content hash) is held and whose generation is the parent's plus one (checked add; no parent = generation 0). An ineligible higher one waits in `held`; one between the parent and the base is a DORMANT row; at or below the parent it is dropped. Never `checkpoint_hlc` or the state JSON. Units `rival_owner_checkpoints_converge_without_loss`, `a_checkpoint_with_a_wrong_generation_or_a_missing_parent_is_no_base`, `probe_concurrent_owner_checkpoints_lose_an_op_today` |
| Any remote op against a base | `fold_remote` door (`stateless_check`: signed bytes at most `MAX_OP_BYTES` 1 MiB, a checkpoint `MAX_CHECKPOINT_BYTES` 16 MiB, else `TooLarge`, never held; unit `an_op_past_its_byte_bound_is_refused`) + `plan` -> `crdt::dominance::Judge::dominates` (one exhaustive match per payload) | Decided by the base (every element it writes stamped at or after its clock, outside its target's window, or the very member event in the base's record) = not folded: stored DORMANT when the parent does not decide it, else dropped. Everything else folds on top in HLC order judged by `op_allowed` against the state just before it (today's authority). A 0.13 (unstamped) base decides exactly what its `covers` did, and every descendant keeps deciding it through the FLOOR (`cp_floor`: set on a rebase onto an unstamped base, and to a legacy server's first checkpoint's `covers` = what its owner witnessed; a floor above `covers` makes no base), in dominance AND the apply arm.
