A field write to a channel or label that does not exist at its point is refused (`op_allowed`), so it parks and expires instead of staying undecided. A late visibility write that restricts on its own still closes a public channel though a newer write of its field wins, when no `public` write landed after it or the newest one came while it held its field (`late_restriction_closes`, the `set`/`set_vis`/`set_labels` stamps, apply AND dominance); an assignment older than its label's newest create or delete is void (apply AND dominance). Units `rev3_a_stamped_child_of_a_0_13_base_still_decides_what_covers_decided`, `a_stamped_child_of_a_0_13_base_keeps_what_it_made_again`, `authz_a_checkpoint_comes_only_from_the_anchor_owner` (legacy floor), `a_write_to_a_missing_channel_or_label_is_refused`, `rev3_every_op_the_owner_folded_is_decided_by_its_checkpoint`, `rev3_a_late_removal_older_than_the_add_keeps_the_channel_on_a_fresh_joiner`, `a_checkpoint_keeps_late_writes_to_every_stamped_element` (and U10 and `a_late_older_write_never_undoes_a_newer_one_on_any_element`: each also on a joiner holding only the founding op, the base and the late ops), `a_field_write_before_a_channel_was_made_again_drops_at_the_door`, `an_op_the_base_decides_changes_nothing_on_top_of_it` (every generated kind: a decided op folded on top anyway changes nothing), `a_checkpoint_never_drops_an_op_its_owner_did_not_fold`, `uncovered_ops_fold_the_same_in_every_order`, `a_stale_checkpoint_undoes_nothing_members_did`, `a_dominated_replay_is_dropped_without_a_rebuild`, `rebase_equals_a_clone_and_apply_oracle` (U10, every payload but founding/checkpoint/deletion/join lock, source-scanned), `a_0_13_log_and_checkpoint_load_and_seed_stamps` |
| Membership (`member_record`) | `crdt::members` events (`ServerState.member_events`) -> `derive` | Spans are DERIVED from every admission/leave/removal event in clock order: an admission opens only on an ask newer than every ask that opened a span before it (HOL-SEC-092) and than the member's own last leave before it (F9). `op_allowed` for `MemberAdded`: no admission dated before its own ask minus `MAX_DRIFT_MS`; inside history it may only repeat a span or move the next span back on that span's own ask (H2, `members::admission`). Every op's author must hold a span containing the op's own clock (member at its clock, skipped on a legacy anchor, an author with no record passes, F12). A register write dated before its target's current span is void. Units `probe2_a_late_admission_of_a_current_member_skips_ask_once`, `probe_an_ask_older_than_the_askers_own_leave_readmits_it`, `late_leave_rejoin_and_readmission_fold_by_clock`, `member_at_its_clock_spares_legacy_and_unrecorded_authors`, `crdt::members::tests::*` |
| Two different ops at one (author, clock) | door (`fold_remote`), `plan` dedup, `digest::id_hash`, `persist_op_delta` upsert | The lower SHA-256 of the master-signed bytes wins everywhere; the digest id hashes it, so replicas holding different ops at one key see a differing range. Unit `equivocation_keeps_the_lowest_hash_everywhere` |
| A once-admitted row the fold now refuses | `install` -> `RowKind::Parked(until)`, `crdt_ops.parked_until`; `cap_parked` | PARKED: stored, not folded, not in the digest, judged again at every rebuild, 30 days; at most 128 KiB per charge tree, oldest first (staff acts against the 64 MiB staff budget; our own admission always kept, the silent re-ask reads it), the rest deleted; a restart deletes rows past their time (`Restored.dropped`). A never-stored refused op waits in `held` (256, 10 min, heaviest author evicted, never index 0) and only from an author the set knows (a record, or an admission held: F8; at the tail and in `plan`). A live op that makes only a dormant row is persisted (`sync_handler`, `swarm` broadcast arm and page ingest, `crdt_resync` read-again). Units `held_admits_only_known_authors_and_evicts_the_heaviest`, `held_takes_only_authors_the_state_knows`, `junk_below_a_checkpoint_stays_bounded`, `a_group_keeps_its_parked_rows_within_their_bound`, `a_restart_deletes_the_rows_parked_past_their_time`, `a_dormant_row_made_at_the_door_is_in_the_delta` |
| Undecided ops past the budget (H3) | `ServerState::allocate` on the set before authority; `Quota::tail` at the tail | A pure function of the op set (no hysteresis), the same at the tail and in a rebuild. 128 MiB per server over charge TREES rooted in the anchor's members (the base, else the founding owner, so a server without a checkpoint is bounded too): max-min with a 128 KiB floor over the trees plus one 8 MiB pool for chains reaching no root, then max-min inside each tree between a member's own ops and each subtree it admitted (earliest undecided admission = parent). The anchor owner's ops are never charged. Staff (the anchor's moderators and above, as the owner's own role changes leave them) admissions, lock moves, kicks, bans, mutes and demotions first, oldest first, up to 64 MiB. Within an author the oldest first up to the first that does not fit, so an author over its share stays over: its tail op waits in `held` with no rebuild, never a row; any other tail op rebuilds only when the shares move below what is folded. Units `rev3_a_joiner_flood_never_parks_the_ban_of_the_flooder`, `rev3_the_same_ops_fold_the_same_at_the_quota_bound`, `rev3_a_members_in_order_flood_keeps_the_stored_rows_bounded`, `rev3_a_server_without_a_checkpoint_bounds_a_flood_too`, `at_the_bound_a_capped_flood_costs_no_rebuild`, `quota_shares_are_order_independent_and_park_never_delete`, `an_admin_answering_joins_while_the_owner_is_away_keeps_them_going_through_a_flood`, `three_months_of_an_honest_admin_fit_the_shipping_budget` (measured with 0.14 lock links, grants sealed per staff device: staff-first 26.2 MiB at 16 grants a move, 80.6 MiB at 64, over 90 days of 60 admissions and 60 moves a day: the staff budget lasts about 220 days, or 71 at 64 grants, without a checkpoint; accepted as the shipping number, 64 grants fit two months, no budget raise, Vitalik 2026-11-05) |
| The store rows of an ingest | `Ingested::delta` -> `CrdtStore::persist` -> `MessageStore::persist_op_delta` | One SAVEPOINT: upsert written rows (with parked mark), delete only the rows named; the founding op is never named (F11: no JSON `LIKE`). Unit `persist_is_one_transaction_and_keys_the_founding_op` |
| Restart | `swarm` start -> `load_op_rows` -> `ServerState::restore_op_log` | The base comes from the ROWS; when the rows classify into the set the saved `retained_print` names the saved state stands, else one refold and a save. Unit `a_restart_through_the_store_matches_a_live_replica` |
| Digest and whole-log pages | `digest::entries`, `crdt_resync::page(served_log)` | Compared and served: the folded log, the parent and dormant rows (never parked). `OpDigest.bref` names the asker's base by content hash; an answerer holding that checkpoint leaves out what it decides. Unit `digest_entries_agree_across_bases_and_include_the_dormant_set` |
| The join answer (M4) | `swarm` ServerJoinRequest -> `ServerState::join_answer` | The founding op, the base, its parent, the joiner's admission and every admission its admitters' spans rest on back to the base plus their role changes, then the newest ops within 256 KiB; the rest by digest catch-up. Legacy servers unchanged. |
| Owner checkpoint (writer rule, M1) | `sync_handler::author_due_checkpoints` -> `ServerState::checkpoint_witnessed`, `OpSync::witnesses` | Only after finished digest rounds since the socket came up with two other identities, one a moderator+, or two whose admissions root (earliest admitter of each span) in different members of the base other than the owner (an identity the owner admitted counts for nothing: it answers anyone's join); or with every other member (a small server); legacy anchors exempt. Its size is measured BEFORE signing (`author_due_checkpoint`, `checkpoint_fits`: state bytes + one per quote and backslash + 16 KiB under 16 MiB); a try not kept waits an hour (`cp_retry_at_ms`, RAM only) instead of re-signing every tick. Units `the_owner_checkpoints_only_after_two_witnesses_one_vouched`, `the_writer_rule_needs_two_roots_in_the_base`, `an_over_bound_checkpoint_is_tried_once_then_waits` |
| A kicked or banned member's backdated grants (AR-11, decision 2026-11-04) | `op_allowed` -> `ServerState::void_at_its_point` (`crdt::voids`, look-ahead `Ahead` built in `install`); tail `voids_folded`; authoring `author_checked` -> `with_seen` | A kick or ban carries `seen` (`SeenGrants`: the remover's base as (generation, hash), 16-byte content-hash prefixes of every power-granting op of the target it folded in the span it closes, at most 1,024 then `before`). A target op of a power-granting kind (admission, role change, permission or channel grant, label to someone else, lock move, unban) dated inside a span a later kick or ban ends is VOID at its own point unless named, before the cut, or decided by the named base; a ban after the target's leave closes that last span if no admission came between; an end only the record holds (its op decided and gone) voids too. Counts only from an author who could kick the target at the op's point, and only if the removal FOLDS at its own point: `install` reruns the fold without each voiding removal that was refused there (`Ahead::of(.., refused)`, `Ahead::voided_by`, at most `VOID_PASSES` 4), so the tail and a rebuild agree. A removal naming a base we lack that is not older than ours is held. A list past the cap or not hex is refused. A removal without `seen` (before 0.14) voids nothing while its op is retained. Legacy anchors exempt. Residuals (AR-11): demotions; a kicked member's backdated acts that grant nothing; a leave carries no list (left, re-admitted, kicked: grants backdated into the older span stand); a removal only the record holds voids late honest grants too; a removal dropped in one pass that folds in a later one voids nothing. Units `review4_a_kicked_admins_refused_removal_voids_honest_grants`, `review4_a_demoted_admins_refused_removal_voids_honest_grants`, `a_refused_removal_leaves_the_tail_and_a_rebuild_equal`, `a_removal_parked_by_a_late_kick_of_its_author_voids_nothing`, `rev3_cover_a_kicked_member_backdates_an_admission`, `a_ban_after_a_leave_voids_an_admission_backdated_into_that_span`, `a_kick_keeps_the_grants_its_author_saw_and_voids_the_rest`, `a_member_kicked_and_admitted_again_cannot_backdate_into_its_old_span`, `a_removal_naming_a_base_we_lack_waits_for_that_base`, `a_removal_list_is_bounded_and_well_formed`, `a_removal_from_before_0_14_voids_nothing`, `a_ban_closes_only_the_last_span`, `a_kick_its_author_could_not_make_voids_nothing`, `a_legacy_server_voids_nothing` |
| Silent re-ask (M2) | `swarm` MLS batch tick -> `ServerState::refused_own_admission` -> `NodeCommand::JoinServer` | Only when we hold our own admission PARKED, are no member, not banned, and no leave or removal of us is later than it; doubling from 30 s, six at most. Unit `a_refused_own_admission_triggers_the_silent_reask_and_a_removal_does_not`; harness `t14c_the_f1_cascade_heals_by_silent_re_asks` (owner away throughout, survives a restart) |

Residuals: a demoted admin can backdate a promotion to before its demotion (AR-11, demotions, plus the kick residuals in its row); a server past about 15,000 departed identities (or 40,000 bans) beside 512 members outgrows the 16 MiB checkpoint and stops compacting (departed records judge late ops and keep each ask admitting once, so they are not trimmed); a late removal that the base saw re-admitted on a newer ask leaves the inner gap of that span unrecorded (spans are exact otherwise); a restricted-after-public interleaving errs closed (a channel can stay not public where history would reopen it, also when two late visibility writes straddle a `public` write); a create of a label or channel id that already exists dates its existence stamp, so a late delete or assignment older than it is decided by the base (admin-only, clients mint fresh ids); late live ops out of clock order still rebuild once each (no 250 ms tick, U20); while the uncovered set is past the budget, a tail op of an author still under its share rebuilds when it moves another's share below what is folded; a late removal before the newest door does not move the join lock; no per-device ceiling across servers (any such cap would differ per replica); no device proof (section 10).

## 60. Deleted public posts stay with members (HOL-SEC-194, track t14-gp1, 0.14, embargoed)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| The member channel sync page (a READ: deleted rows keep their text on members as evidence) | `swarm.rs` `HavenMessage::ChannelSyncRequest` arm | `crypto_handler::sync_partner(state, requester, Some(cid))`: not revoked, a current member by resolved MASTER, and may see the channel; the same rung a node asks by. Anyone else, a public channel's stranger included, gets silence, as a non-member already got for a non-public channel; public reading rides `PublicChannelSyncRequest` only (`get_visible_channel_messages_before`, no deleted rows). A filtered page for non-members was tried and dropped: its size, `has_more` and the gap digest's re-served days located deleted rows, a run of them stalled it, and it moved an unplaced member's watermarks past rows it never got. Every sender of the ask holds the server and asks a `sync_partner`. Harness `a_non_member_channel_sync_ask_gets_no_deleted_text` (a stranger with an Olm session gets no page while its guest page from the same member arrives); units `a_public_channels_sync_page_goes_only_to_a_member`, `a_member_once_refused_gets_its_old_evidence_on_the_retry`; member side `synced_channel_deletion_hides_for_late_joiner`. |
| The file of a deleted public post | `swarm.rs` `HavenMessage::FileRequest` channel arm | After the entitlement check: a requester that is not a member gets silence when `get_channel_message_hidden_at(file_meta.message_id)` is set, though `public_ok` holds; members are served. Harness `a_stranger_gets_no_file_of_a_deleted_public_post` (the kept post's file still reaches the stranger). |

Residual: a member device the responder cannot place yet (new device, phrase-recovered install) gets no page until its roster arrives, and retries, as for every non-public channel. The never-filled `pending_sync_requests` queue, whose flush served the page with no requester gate, is deleted.

## 61. The relay's join lock judged by the removal walk (track t14-r2, 0.14, embargoed)

Design: `DEVICE_PROOF_DESIGN.md` section 12 items 9 to 11. Code: `relay-uws/src/roster_book.h` (`counted_authority`), `ws_handler.cpp` `handle_lock_put`, `breaker.h` `lock_put_w`, `node/relay_pace.rs`, `node/lock_keeper.rs` (`Authorities`, `missed`), `node/roster_book.rs` (`dropped_by_recovery`), `identity/roster.rs` (`counted_by_rank`).

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Which device writes a lock link (relay) | `handle_lock_put` -> `RosterBook::counted_authority(held).counts(device)` | `roster::Authority` of the held roster (the walk of DEVICE_PROOF section 4, no clock, no maturity), never ID-1R's inbox fold: after a mutual removal the device the walk takes first still writes, the other writes nothing; a matured backup nobody vouched writes nothing (decision 3). The inbox keeps the fold. Cache reset by any `show` that changes the record; each walk charged like a fold. C++ `test_roster` (mutual removal block, vectors replayed against the cache), live `test_lock_after_a_mutual_removal` (`RELAY_LIVE_ONLY=lock_walk`), harness `t14d_after_a_mutual_removal_the_owner_still_moves_the_lock` (MockRelay `lock_counts`). |
| A lock put's breaker price | `plan_text` -> `breaker::lock_put_w` + `breaker::lock_put_held_w` (the held record), client `relay_pace::text_cost` | Every signature check is charged as it runs (`crypto.cpp` verify hooks); the plan judges the frame first and now prices what it will check: `2 + 2 * SIG_W * links + DH_W * proofs`, plus the held base's two checks for a new base that differs from the held one, plus the fork scan's bound `SIG_W * (2 * held + 2)` when the first link is not the next lock (none for an extension of the newest lock). The client adds the two for any base it sends (it cannot see the record; over-pricing is safe) and leaves a fork's scan to the relay. Pinned vectors "a lock put of forty links into a small server", "a new base pays two signatures a link and the two of the base the relay may hold"; unit `test_plan_prices`, `test_breaker` (`lock_put_held_w`), Rust `every_price_matches_the_pinned_vectors`, live `test_breaker_a_lock_put_pays_for_the_held_chain` (judged at exactly what it is charged, `RELAY_LIVE_ONLY=breaker_enforce`). Must ship before breaker ENFORCE. |
| Who gets a change key (client) | `lock_keeper::Authorities::grant_devices` (via `lock_grants`) | The stored roster's `counted_by_rank()` (authority, closest to the phrase first), at most `GRANT_DEVICES`; resolver placement only for an identity with no stored roster. Cached per identity under its roster REVISION (`roster_book::revision`, bumped after each committed save or followed store change in `point_resolver`), never the resolver epoch, so the loop reads the store only after that roster changed; 1024 identities, past it the least recently used goes. Units `review_probe_authorities_past_the_cap` (300 identities, a second pass reads no store), `authorities_past_the_cap_let_only_the_least_recently_used_go`, `authorities_read_a_roster_again_once_it_is_saved`. Unit `grants_follow_server_authority_after_a_mutual_removal`, `grants_go_to_the_devices_closest_to_the_phrase`. |
| A relay tip "made by a removed device" (client) | `Authorities::uncounted` in `on_chain` (`removed_tip`, the base log) and `tick_server` (`base_unproved`) | Authority over the stored roster, resolver removals only without one. |
| A recovery that drops a device (lock move) | `roster_book::fold_in_store` -> `dropped_by_recovery` -> `lock_keeper::note_dropped_devices(master, ..)`; own recovery: `api/roster.rs` `changed()` -> `roster_book::lost_by_own_change` -> `NodeCommand::RosterChanged` | Only when the base changed: devices the stored roster counted (fold or authority) that the new one counts in neither, never this device; noted WITH the identity (no resolver revocation names it). Our own change notes the fold members it removed plus that set, so a device only authority still counted (the walk's first after a mutual removal) is dropped too. Unit `a_recovery_that_drops_a_device_moves_the_join_lock`, `our_own_recovery_drops_the_device_only_authority_still_counted`, harness `t14d_a_recovery_that_drops_a_device_moves_the_lock`. |
| A recovery another process stored (push fetch, iOS NSE) | `stored_ingest` -> `MessageStore::add_roster_dropped` (table `roster_dropped`, beside `roster_dirty`) -> the node's `reconcile_dirty` (`take_roster_dropped`, `Reconciled.dropped`) -> `follow_reconciled` -> `Reconciled::note_drops` | The fetch keeps the fold's dropped devices with the dirty mark, since the base difference is gone once the node re-reads; taken once, never this device. Unit `a_recovery_the_push_fetch_stored_moves_the_join_lock`. |
| A mod's move ahead of removing its own device | `LockKeeper::move_before_removal` (`NodeCommand::RevokeDevice`, `ResetDeviceLists`) | Wherever we hold the newest lock's change key, every removal sends the next lock first on the same socket, never skipped inside `REMOVAL_GAP` (N12b). Harness `t14d_a_second_removal_inside_the_gap_still_moves_the_lock_first`; `REMOVAL_GAP` still spaces the owner's due moves for a member's removals (`t14l_device_removals_move_the_lock_at_most_once_per_gap`). |
| Offers the relay never holds | `LockKeeper::missed` (refused, taken but answered with another lock, unanswered past `PUT_TIMEOUT`) | Quiet for `base * 2^misses`, at most `RETRY_MAX` (600 s); reset by our offer held exactly, a newer lock our state holds exactly, a new relay connection (`on_connected` clears the streaks and waits), or no miss for `RETRY_MAX` after the last wait ended. Units `a_new_relay_connection_forgets_the_misses`, `a_miss_streak_quiet_for_its_bound_is_forgotten`. A 0.13 relay drops `dev`, so its answer never equals our link. Unit `a_relay_that_drops_the_device_proof_hears_fewer_and_fewer_offers`, `a_relay_that_refuses_or_never_answers_hears_fewer_and_fewer_offers`, harness `t14d_an_owner_on_a_relay_that_drops_lock_proofs_backs_off`. |

N12b closed (Vitalik 2026-11-05, AR-38 no longer carries it): a mod removing a device of its own moves the lock ahead of every removal, a second one inside `REMOVAL_GAP` too; the gap holds back only the moves a removal's own due causes.

## 62. Our own screen share across a room or call change (HOL-SEC-195, t14-left, 0.14, embargoed)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Our share going live (capture, preview, `isScreenSharing`, the `screen_state` announce, a served `screen_watch`) after the capture picker returns | `voice_channel_provider.dart` `startScreenShare` -> `_openShare` (session, capture and preview held in locals until the last await) -> `_finishShareStart`; DM `call_provider.dart` `startScreenShare` | A leave, a kick or channel delete (both reach `_cleanupAllScreenShares`), a stop or a newer start that lands while the picker is up loses the session (`_shareStartLost`: session, in the room, not leaving); the start then closes what it opened (`ShareCapture.close`) and returns quietly. A stop during the picker drops the session; a second start meanwhile is refused (`_shareStarting`). Tests `test/vc_leave_teardown_test.dart` group "a share that starts while the room changes (HOL-SEC-195)", `test/dm_share_start_test.dart` |
| The Windows entire-screen voice redirect a start arms | `_armVoiceRedirect(session)` | Arms run one at a time (`_armInFlight`, native holds one redirect); an arm whose session ended takes its own redirect back, so a re-share never keeps an older share's redirect. Test "a re-share during an old arm leaves only its own redirect" |

Test "a join from outside any room never inherits a share" |

Residual: the DM call's per-watch redirect arm (`_sendDmScreenOffer`) is not serialized; a stop during it ends in its catch, which disarms.

## 63. Device proof, CRDT side: the proof, the verdict, the cut (track C1, 0.14, embargoed)

Design: `reports/shipped/security/DEVICE_PROOF_DESIGN.md` rev 3.5 + build notes, sections 4 to 7, 10, 11, 12 (R1 to R3). Shared seams with track C2: `crdt/authority.rs`, `node/authority_view.rs` (`.team/INTERFACE.md`). Built so far: the proof, the view, the verdict, the cut, parking with the H3 carve-out, eligibility rules 1 to 3 with the freeze, the lineage checks, `DeviceRetired` with its carry, the remover's flow and its sync round (M4), the per-author bound on retire rebuilds, and t14-lock's R1 to R3. Since builder 4 also: restore points, settle marks (`SettleSign`, `SettlePin`), the owner's settling after a lost-or-stolen removal, the member's lost retire, co-signing marks. Since builder 5: content-dated `seen_ms`, mark-first settlings sent before the removal, two settlings at once, `cp_seen` rotation, held retires surviving a restart. Own-op retention (step 4). NOT yet: the settling's own sync round on R (needs C2's `sync_ask_on_base`), the post gate and post proofs of section 13 (the `cp_posts_until` carry is built), witnesses, floor marks and the hold, the owner re-fold, channel post proofs (section 13).

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| An op's device proof (`CrdtOp.dev`) | `CrdtOp::sign_device` (`create_op` when the signer holds the device key: `install_op_signer` at the 3 node sites, `found_on`), `fold_remote` door (`strip_bad_proof`) | The device key over `hollow-crdt-dev1:` + the master-signed payload, under the key the device id derives, with `hlc.counter % LANES == lane_of(device)`; anything else is stripped at the door (reads as no proof, never worse). Outside the content id. Units `a_device_proof_verifies_only_under_its_key_and_in_its_lane`, `a_proof_survives_the_tolerant_read` |
| A better proof for an op we hold | `fold_remote` -> `take_better_proof` -> `Ingested.rows` (the core's upsert) | One stored copy per (author, clock, content id); a copy whose rank is higher (`copy_rank`: Counted > Uncounted = Missing > ProofLess, then the lower device) replaces the proof in RAM and in the row; a held or parked copy whose verdict rose is judged again (rebuild). Units `a_better_proof_replaces_the_held_copy_and_its_row`, `copies_rank_counted_then_uncounted_or_missing_then_proof_less`, `after_the_cut_a_proof_less_op_parks_until_a_proven_copy_arrives` |
| Two variants at one (author, clock) | door and `plan` dedup | By verdict first, then the lower content id (was: lowest id alone). Unit `equivocation_keeps_the_counted_variant_before_the_lowest_hash` |
| Who counts (the view) | `ServerState.authority` (RAM only) filled by `node::authority_view::install` (book + co-member rosters + `cp_roster`; never the resolver) at every node state creation (load, create, join skeleton, adopted snapshot); `fill_view` before every ingest, author and restore; refreshed when `roster_book` saves a roster (`note_saved` -> `ViewDirty::due` on the batch tick, coalesced rebuild 10 s per server; a save re-judges its master in EVERY held server whose view holds it, whichever server it came through; at most 4 rebuilds per tick, the rest stay marked). The view reads only authors that passed the stateless checks and are known (or admitted in the same batch by a known author): a stranger's op costs no store read and no entry. Every rebuild (`rebuild_now`) judges the whole authority set again from the source and forgets the rest; the view digest in `retained_print` covers only the folded set's authors. A rebuild that ends OUR membership (and our own admission is not waiting for a re-ask) tears the server down like a kick (`leave_evicted_server`) | `authority::judge`: the key by section 5 (a founding nonce that commits to the owner's key picks that key over any other held first; else the first key held, book first), statements merged under it, `Roster::authority()`. No statements: `Missing`. A store that cannot be read caches nothing. An UNSET view (pure crdt tests only) counts every op. The view digest joins `retained_print`. Units `the_view_counts_only_what_the_owners_statements_count`, `a_committing_nonce_picks_its_key_over_a_forged_one_seen_first`, `a_saved_roster_marks_every_server_whose_view_holds_its_master`, `one_tick_rebuilds_a_bounded_number_of_servers_and_keeps_the_rest_marked`, `review_a_rebuild_after_a_mark_judges_a_cached_missing_entry_again`, `review_unsigned_ops_from_fresh_authors_never_grow_the_view`; harness `w3c1_a_kick_a_view_rebuild_folds_tears_the_server_down` |
| The cut | `plan` (any held owner-signed stamped checkpoint, or a founding nonce committing to a key; the tail sets it on the founding op), `RowKind::CutMark` | Monotone fact of held rows: the lowest stamped owner checkpoint the plan stores nowhere (dropped by the base order or as disagreeing with its parent, or only held) stays as a CutMark row (compared, served, never folded). Units `a_stamped_owner_checkpoint_puts_the_cut_in_force`, `the_cut_mark_keeps_the_cut_once_its_checkpoint_drops_below_the_parent`, `review_the_cut_of_a_dropped_stamped_checkpoint_survives_a_restart` |
| Any op after the cut | `op_allowed` = `device_counts_here` && `op_allowed_by_rules` | Only a `Counted` op folds (the founding op and checkpoints are judged by their own rules and eligibility). A refusal by the device clause alone PARKS the op when the rules admit it and its author has a span (and wants its roster: `Ingested.wanted`), else it is DROPPED unstored (never held); a tail park is charged to its group's parked budget (`park_fits`, as `cap_parked`: the owner's quota exemption never lets a removed owner device grow the store), past it dropped. A rebuild (`fold_all`: a checkpoint landing, a view change) fills `wanted` the same way for every op it refuses as `Missing` or `Uncounted`, parked or dropped (`wants_roster`; never for a missing proof). H3 carve-out (lead decision 11-05): a device refusal whose RULES refusal a roster may still end is kept as the core keeps a rules-refused op (a row it was: parked; else held in RAM, `trim_held`), never dropped: the device reads `Missing`/`Uncounted`, the author is known, and either an op that could admit others (`enables_others`, a `JoinLock` too: the next link rests on it) was skipped before it for a roster (`skipped_for_a_roster`; at the tail any parked or held such op, `holds_skipped`) or it is OUR OWN admission (the silent re-ask reads it), at most one parked own admission per server (else held). A proof-less refusal stays dropped. Units `after_the_cut_a_refusal_resting_on_a_skipped_admission_waits_for_the_rosters`, `a_rebuild_parks_a_refusal_resting_on_a_skipped_admission`, `a_skipped_op_that_enables_nothing_leaves_a_refusal_final`, `a_proof_less_refusal_stays_dropped_whatever_was_skipped`, `our_own_admission_refused_by_a_missing_admitter_parks_for_the_silent_reask`, `our_own_admission_parks_once_per_server`; harness `t14c_the_f1_cascade_heals_by_silent_re_asks` (rosters delivered by hand after the rebuild until C2 merges). Units `after_the_cut_only_a_counted_device_writes_and_a_members_op_parks`, `after_the_cut_an_uncounted_op_the_rules_refuse_too_is_dropped_unstored`, `before_the_cut_a_device_the_view_does_not_count_writes_as_today`, `a_rebuild_that_refuses_a_device_wants_its_authors_roster`, `review_a_removed_owner_devices_stream_parks_within_its_group_bound`; harness `w3c1_a_removed_device_writes_no_server_history_after_the_cut` |
| Which checkpoint may be the base | `plan` -> `eligible_bases`, `agrees_with_parent` | The core's parent rule, and after the cut a writer device the owner's view counts (rule 1) or a place in an eligible checkpoint's `cp_lineage` (rule 3, least fixed point over held checkpoints, never the base). While the owner's statements count no device, the device rule takes nothing (the freeze, N2). While the view holds NO owner statements (`Missing`) the state stays where it folded: only the current base and its `cp_lineage` stay eligible, newer checkpoints are held, the owner's roster is wanted (`owner_missing` -> `Ingested.wanted`), and the roster's arrival rebuilds (C4). A stamped checkpoint whose held parent disagrees on `cp_lineage`, `cp_cut` or `cp_settled` is no base. `checkpoint_json` writes all three. Rule 2: a held owner retire that counts names it (through the verdict), or an ELIGIBLE checkpoint's `cp_endorsed` entry of the owner, signed by a device the view counts, names it with that proving device (inside the same fixed point). Units `a_deeper_checkpoint_chain_is_the_base_whatever_its_clock` (lineage), `after_the_cut_a_checkpoint_by_an_uncounted_owner_device_is_no_base` (Missing holds, N2 freezes), `review_a_missing_owner_view_keeps_the_last_base`, `an_owner_retire_makes_the_checkpoint_it_names_eligible`, `an_eligible_checkpoints_endorsement_makes_the_checkpoint_it_names_eligible` |
| A device retire (`CrdtPayload::DeviceRetired`, section 8.2) | `op_allowed_by_rules` -> `retire_allowed`; `verdict` -> `is_endorsed` (map `ServerState.endorsed`, built by `refresh_endorsed` before every plan from every held retire in any row kind); dominance arm (`Judge.parent_retired`) | A member names ONE device its statements here state (counted or removed; with no statements the device clause parks it), at most 256 refs strictly sorted by (ms, c, h), each a 64-hex content id, `posts_until_ms` >= 0, a restore point only on a member's retire. It folds nothing (`apply_payload` no-op, `ServerUpdated`). A retire whose own proof is DIRECTLY `Counted` makes each op it names (same author, clock and content id, proved by the retired device) read `Counted`; an endorsed retire endorses nothing. An owner retire naming a checkpoint makes it eligible through rule 1. A retire the base or its parent lists in `cp_retired` is decided at the door. A retire at the tail rebuilds before the quota is asked, ranks first in its author's quota group, and an author keeps its newest 64 undominated retires (older ones drop, `allocate`); our own retire skips the author-side share check. `checkpoint_json` writes `cp_retired` (the retires folded since the base) and `cp_endorsed` (`carried_retires`: inherit the base's entries, add every held retire whose own proof counts DIRECTLY and the base does not carry, drop entries whose signer the view does not count, drop refs the new checkpoint decides, newest 4,096 refs). Each `Endorsement` names its author identity too (build note: rule 3 and step 6 judge `counts(author, by)`). Section 10 step 6: once the base is chosen (`install`, the restore's fast path) its entries whose signer counts endorse like held retires (`endorse_from_base`). The remover (`NodeCommand::RevokeDevice` Retire -> `sync_handler::retire_device_ops`, before the lock move and the removal) authors chunks naming every op of ours that device proved and we folded (base and parent included, never parked or held: `retire_payloads`) in every server we are a member of, MLS first + the Olm twin. Units `a_checkpoint_carries_the_retires_it_folded_and_what_they_name_it_does_not_decide`, `a_checkpoint_inherits_its_bases_endorsements_by_counted_signers_only`, `a_bases_endorsement_by_a_counted_signer_seats_an_op_that_arrives_later`, `a_checkpoint_carries_at_most_the_newest_endorsed_refs`, `a_remover_names_every_folded_op_the_device_proved_in_sorted_chunks`, `a_retire_ranks_first_in_its_authors_quota_group`, `an_author_keeps_at_most_its_newest_undominated_retires`; harness `w3c1_a_removed_device_writes_no_server_history_after_the_cut` (the retired laptop's earlier op stays folded). Units `a_retire_keeps_exactly_the_ops_it_names_of_the_retired_device`, `a_retire_from_a_device_that_does_not_count_endorses_nothing`, `a_retire_is_judged_by_its_shape_and_the_authors_statements`, `an_owner_retire_makes_the_checkpoint_it_names_eligible`, `a_retire_the_base_lists_is_decided_at_the_door` |
| The remover's sync round before a retire (section 8.2 step 1, M4, H4) | `NodeCommand::RevokeDevice` Retire -> `sync_handler::ask_retire_round` -> `retire_round::RetireRounds` (held removal) -> MLS batch tick `due` -> the removal re-queued to the event loop (`take_released`) | Only our own command. Every server we hold past the cut gets digest asks to one device each of up to 3 member identities other than ours (and the retiring device itself, never counted); the removal, its retires, the lock move and the roster change wait until 2 of those identities (or every one asked, if fewer) ended a digest round that STARTED after the removal (`OpSync::ended_since`), or 10 s. An identity there is the RESOLVER's master of the answering device (`OpSync` records `resolver::resolve`), not the view's (review B finding 9; the restore choice's round in `restore_flow::tick` reads the same). On the timeout it proceeds, and for 10 min a second identity's later round mints again on that server, naming only ops no counted retire of ours already names (`retire_payloads`). A lost-or-stolen removal cancels a held retire. Removals held at once: 64; a full command queue re-queues (`requeue`), never drops. Units `node::retire_round::tests::*` (8), `a_remover_names_every_folded_op_the_device_proved_in_sorted_chunks` (minting again); harness `w3c1_a_retire_first_fetches_what_the_remover_never_folded` (RED with the round skipped: "the retire names the op the round fetched") |

Another author keeps its own budget. Units `a_flood_of_retires_from_one_author_rebuilds_a_bounded_number_of_times` (RED with no cap: 40 rebuilds for 40 retires), `a_held_retire_marks_its_server_for_the_coalesced_rebuild` |
| Restore points (section 7) | `plan` -> `restore_picks` over `ServerState.first_folds` (the `cp_seen` records the node loads before `restore_op_log`; `install` notes each new base) | Local only. Of the checkpoints this replica once folded as its base that the plan would drop, the newest and oldest first fold of each window (a day, 1 to 7 days, 7 to 30, older) stay as `RowKind::Restore` rows: never folded, never served (`RowKind::served`: not in `served_log` or the digest). A checkpoint never folded here as the base is dropped as before. Units `restore_points_are_the_newest_and_oldest_first_fold_of_each_window`, `a_base_the_replica_moved_past_stays_as_a_restore_point_it_never_serves` |
| A settling signature (`CrdtPayload::SettleSign`, section 7, RC32-4) | `plan` -> `settle_marks` (classified before dominance and `op_allowed`; a tail one rebuilds on the retire budget); authoring `author_settle_sign` | Kept as a `RowKind::SettleSign` row only when the author is the server's owner, the stamp is this server's and well formed (64-hex parent, 1 to 64 sorted devices, T >= 0), it carries exactly one signature, by the device the op's proof names, that verifies over the settling id (`SettleStamp::id`: SHA-256 of `hollow-settle-id1` + the fields as one JSON array), and its parent checkpoint is held; else dropped unstored. Per (settling id, device) the lower (ms, counter, content id). At most 8 signatures a mark (counted signers first) and 32 marks a server; past it marks with no counted signer go first, then each mark is charged to its lightest counted signer and the heaviest-charged device loses its smallest (generation, id); pinned parents count toward 32 MiB the same way. The parent of every kept mark is a `RowKind::SettlePin` row (served, in the digest). A mark COUNTS with a counted signer and a parent that verifies as a base one generation below; with one held, only the greatest counted mark's line stays eligible (`cp_settled` names it, or its parent, or the parent's `cp_lineage`), whether the settling itself is still held or not. `agrees_with_parent` lets a settling name its own stamp. A refusal while the view holds no roster of the member asks for that roster (the roster-round row below), and the channel is caught up once it lands, so a quiet member's posts are never refused for good. Push fetch keeps the proof but judges nothing (its snapshot has no view and no cut); the full app judges what it backfills. Residual (design 13): rules 2 and 3 compare a date the author picks, so a stolen device holding the master key can still insert posts or changes dated before its window or the cut through a stale replica (content at an old date, not authority) |

An answer to our own ask (`asked`) that serves no proof (a 0.13 holder) is judged by the proof WE keep for that file (`header_meta_judged(.., pulled)`): it counts only when it verifies over the answer's own card (the digest binds file id, type, size class, dimensions and thumbnail; the change binds server, channel, author, post and date), so it can only re-admit the card we already judged, through the same gate a 0.14 holder's answer passes; an answer carrying a proof is judged by that proof alone (a wrong one is refused, never swapped for ours), and an unasked header or an MLS push never borrows ours (unit `a_pulled_card_without_a_proof_is_judged_by_the_proof_we_keep`, harness `t14_a_proofless_answer_lands_by_the_proof_the_asker_keeps`, guard `file_header_gate_stays_wired`) | The device key over its own domain (`hollow-del-dev1`, `hollow-react-dev1`, `hollow-card-dev1`, `hollow-filemeta-dev1`) + SHA-256 of length-prefixed (sid, cid, signer master, mid, date, kind fields); judged by the post gate at the later of the held post's date and the change's own (a live card also at its seal time). A synced card lands only past the row's card or edit date by its proof's `at`; a proven card or file card is never replaced by a proof-less one (`meta_swap_refused`); cards are bounded on receipt (`card_within_bounds`). Push fetch stores only. DEVICE_PROOF_DESIGN sections 8.7 and 9. The fold core's rows (verdict, cut, settlings, floor, hold, re-fold) are section 63.

|---|---|---|
| A co-member's roster (`SyncResponse.x.rosters`, and the owner's own `asker_roster`) | swarm `SyncResponse` arm -> `sync_rosters::on_reply`, swarm `SyncRequest` arm (owner only) -> `server_rosters::keep` -> `MessageStore::save_server_roster` | H3: never the book, the resolver or DMs. Only from a member of our state, only for a master in that server's authority set (`crdt::authority::authority_set`), or a current MEMBER outside it when the delivering device is the one we asked after the post gate refused that member's post or change (`server_rosters::keep_asked` behind `RosterRound::post_asked_of`, lead decision 11-06), each statement verified alone (`Roster::verified`), merged with our row under the same recovery key, `MAX_ROSTER_BYTES` and `within_caps`. Key slots: the key the server's rule names (the founding nonce's commitment for a 0.14 owner, else the book's key) always gets one, evicting other keys down to one; any other key only while no other key holds a row, so keys a master-key holder mints never lock out the key the rule names. Where the rule is `FirstHeld` and the book holds no roster of that master, no key is named: the first key to arrive holds the slot (trust on first use), so a master-key holder reaching us first keeps the honest key out of the view until the book learns it (review B finding 9; the `FirstHeld` rule itself is track C part A's). 64 MiB in all, rows charged to the master they are about: past it the heaviest master loses its row needed longest ago. Aged out 30 days after no held server needs the master (hourly). Every write tells C1's views (`authority_view::note_saved`). ## 65. Catch-up when holders lack part of a channel: "held from X" (AR-39, track t14-ar39, 0.14)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Serving, not a write: a member's channel sync page from a device that lacks part of the channel (it cleared it, or its history begins later: a late join, a copy of a cleared device) | `sync_handler::build_channel_sync_batch` (the one responder, after `sync_partner`, which is unchanged) asks `MessageStore::channel_held_from`; the guest page (`PublicChannelSyncRequest`) is cut by `guest_page_bounded` | ONE rule for every partial holder. Its mark is the higher of where it cleared to (the floor, raised past any row the clear took by id within `MAX_DRIFT_MS` above it) and the top of its hole; X = its OLDEST ROW at or after the mark, `i64::MAX` with none. It serves only rows at or after X whatever the ask names (`channel_marks_from` names every sender it holds from X; the gap digest is clipped to X), and puts `held_from: X` on the page when the ask reached below X (`channel_ask_reaches_below`). An asker that does not set `partial_ok` (0.13, 0.14-pre) gets the old answer, no rows and more to come (`Page::Stuck`). X names a row the page carries or the asker already holds, chosen by the same rule from the same rows, so a cleared device and a late one holding the same rows from X answer byte for byte alike, and no ask finds the floor or the hole beneath X (the old `Stuck` answer let a binary search on `since` find the floor to the millisecond). What any page shows, that this device holds nothing older than X here, is inherent to serving rows; a gap between X and the member's public join time says "lacks history", never a clear or when. Units `ar39_a_cleared_holder_and_a_late_holder_answer_alike`, `ar39_a_partial_page_names_where_it_vouches_from`, `ar39_an_older_asker_still_moves_on_from_a_partial_holder`, `ar39_a_gap_digest_is_answered_only_from_where_the_holder_vouches`, `ar39_a_clear_vouches_past_what_it_took_by_id`, `ar39_until_ends_the_page`, `ar39_a_guest_page_is_cut_where_the_holder_vouches`; harness `w3ar39_a_cleared_holder_and_a_late_joiner_answer_alike` |
| `channel_sync_holes` (what a device whose history begins later still lacks: every sender from `all_from` up to `below`) | `MessageStore::note_held_page` inside the page's ingest transaction, from `sync_handler::land_channel_page` (both batch arms, Olm and MLS, source scan `channel_ingest_gates_stay_wired`) | Opened only by a page answering OUR ask (`ChannelAsks::page_held` returns the chain, `None` = unasked, ignored), from a `channel_backfill_allowed_from` member, after `backfill_filter`, for a channel our state knows (`sync_handler::channel_known`), and only when this device held NO row of the channel before the page and has no floor: a device holding anything judges what it lacks by its watermarks and gap digest and keeps vouching for all it holds, so no page can make a full holder stop serving its history (no contagion). The hole's top is the oldest row the page LANDED at or after its X (else X, clamped to our clock plus the drift), so only a real signed row places it. A page answering the hole's own ask narrows it only to a row it landed, and a page of the hole ask vouching for all it served raises `all_from` to where it ended once its rows commit (`raise_channel_hole`); a catch-up page with a hole open never moves it. A note or commit that fails rolls the page back whole. One row per channel. Deleted by a clear of the channel (the floor is its line) and with the server (`delete_server_state`, leave and delete). A copy of the database (`scrub_device_secrets`) turns each floor into a hole whose top is its oldest row past the floor and drops what the clear counted. Units `ar39_only_a_device_holding_nothing_opens_a_hole`, `ar39_an_unasked_forged_page_never_changes_what_a_holder_serves`, `ar39_only_rows_ingest_accepted_place_a_hole`, `ar39_a_made_up_channel_gets_no_hole`, `ar39_a_full_holder_catching_up_from_a_cleared_one_keeps_serving_its_history`, `ar39_a_late_joiner_lacks_what_lies_below_a_partial_page`, `ar39_an_answer_to_the_hole_narrows_it`, `ar39_pages_filling_a_hole_keep_their_progress`, `ar39_a_device_copy_keeps_what_it_lacks`, `ar39_holes_and_cleared_counts_go_with_the_server`, channel_asks `ar39_an_unasked_page_names_no_chain` |
| `channel_cleared_senders`, `channel_cleared_tail`, `channel_cleared_days`, `channel_floors.history_from` (what a clear took, as this device's asks still count it held) | `count_cleared_batch` + `settle_cleared_counts` inside each batch transaction of `finish_cleared_history` (the clear, and at start the rest of one cut short) | Written only by our own clear, never by a peer. Each sender's newest cleared stamp (at most `MAX_CLEARED_SENDERS` 4096 per channel, the newest kept), the gap-digest hash of each cleared id-carrying row within the lookback of the newest one (at most `MAX_CLEARED_TAIL` 4096, the rest fold into their day), and per UTC day the count and XOR of the rest, the same `(n, h)` the gap digest counts a day by; days no later ask can reach (before the 30-day window behind the newest cleared row's lookback, since a later ask's `since` is never below it) go. So the asks of a cleared device (`channel_ask_spans`, `channel_ask_since`, `get_per_sender_timestamps`, `channel_gap_anchor` + `add_cleared_counts`) are exactly the asks it would have sent without the clear: no value on the wire comes from the floor or shows a clear, a day it cleared compares to a full holder's day as equal (never served again), and the posts made after its clear that it missed while away, older than the first one it received after, show as missing and are served (decision 11-05 night). A cleared device's `since` equal to its newest held row (which is the floor when it holds nothing newer) is accepted: its asks are byte-equal to the same device never having cleared, so they show neither the clear nor its time, which is what "no floor named on the wire" means (lead, 11-06). Every 0.14 channel digest window starts at a UTC day (`channel_gap_window`, within the span a responder honours) so a counted day compares whole. A catch-up's next page that served a sender from below the oldest row the clear took goes on from that row (`channel_page_start`), as a hole device's goes on from X. Deleted with the server and by `scrub_device_secrets`; a floor without them (a rejoin after a leave, a channel cleared while it held nothing) asks as a device whose history begins later, from X, and with nothing past the floor asks from our clock (nothing it would refuse). Units `ar39_a_cleared_device_back_from_away_takes_the_posts_it_missed`, `ar39_a_cleared_device_asks_as_it_would_have_without_the_clear`, `ar39_a_cleared_device_opens_no_hole`, `w2c_a_cleared_channel_asks_as_before_its_clear`, `w2c_cleared_history_is_refused_when_it_comes_back`, `w2c_sync_asks_reach_a_slow_clocks_post_the_floor_accepts`, `ar39_cleared_counts_stay_bounded`, `ar39_holes_and_cleared_counts_go_with_the_server`; harness `w3ar39_a_cleared_member_back_from_away_takes_the_posts_it_missed`, `w2c_cleared_history_stays_cleared_and_stays_on_other_members` (the ask after the clear equals the one before it) |
| The asks that follow (asker side, no write): the one-holder ask, next pages, the hole ask | `channel_asks::ChannelAsks` (`Kind::Catch`/`Kind::Hole`, `page_held`, `hear`, `sweep_asks`), `sync_handler::channel_hole_request`, `channel_next_page_request(.., until)` | Every ask catches up FIRST, so new posts never wait behind a hole. A catch-up's last page with a hole open asks for the hole next at the same holder if it served whole and is not lately known short of the hole, else at the next holder; a holder that vouched from at or above the hole's top is remembered short of it (`SHORT_TTL` 30 min, at most `MAX_SHORT_HOLDERS` 64 per channel, `MAX_ASKS` channels; reset when the top moves) and skipped for the hole, also after a later catch-up page from it that did not reach its X (`is_short`). A catch-up page that vouched for part moves the ask to the next holder; nobody left for the hole ends the ask. The hole ask names every sender we hold from where the hole starts (a sender we never met, in full) up to `until` = its top, no gap digest; its next page continues from the page end. An older responder ignores `until` (serves past it, costing bytes) and `partial_ok` (answers `Stuck`, swept on as before). State per (channel, holder) lives in the existing bounded `ChannelAsks` tables. Units channel_asks `ar39_catch_up_of_new_posts_is_not_delayed_by_an_open_hole`, `ar39_holders_short_of_the_hole_are_skipped_on_later_rounds`, `ar39_nobody_left_for_the_hole_ends_the_ask`, `ar39_a_page_vouching_for_part_hands_the_hole_to_the_next_holder`, `ar39_a_partial_holder_hands_the_catch_up_on`; sync_handler `ar39_a_hole_closes_only_on_a_whole_answer`, `ar39_a_hole_page_keeps_its_progress`, `ar39_a_copys_hole_ask_names_a_row_not_the_floor`, storage `ar39_a_hole_is_asked_for_by_itself`; harness `w3ar39_a_return_while_every_holder_cleared_brings_the_posts_after_the_clears`, `w3ar39_a_joiner_takes_every_post_the_cleared_holders_still_hold`, `w3ar39_a_full_holder_back_later_fills_what_a_cleared_holder_lacked` |

Wire: `ChannelSyncRequest.partial_ok` (bool, default false, skipped when false) and `.until` (Option), `ChannelSyncBatch.held_from` (Option), all `#[serde(default)]`; lanes and `live_only` classes unchanged (the request stays Carried and live-only, the page an Olm or MLS envelope that is not). Gates unchanged: `sync_partner` (HOL-SEC-194), `channel_readable_by`, `channel_backfill_allowed_from`, `backfill_filter`, the w2-c floor on every insert. The empty state of a channel whose history here begins later says "Older messages aren't on this device yet." (`api::storage::channel_history_begins_later` = a hole and no floor, `channelHistoryBeginsLaterProvider`, refreshed on `MessageSyncCompleted`; widget `channel_start_after_clear_test.dart`). Residuals: a member that answers a hole ask whole while leaving rows out closes the hole for good (the existing "one holder can leave rows out" residual, B1 L2); an asked member can mark a device that held nothing as beginning later at a row of its choosing, which heals when any whole holder answers the hole; a post made after every clear but stamped by a slow clock below where its holders vouch is served by none of them; an asked member can, over time, tell a cleared device (asks counting history it no longer serves) from a late joiner (hole asks), never when it cleared; history a device never held below its floor (it was behind or joined late when it cleared) is served again at catch-ups inside the 30-day digest window and refused, as a day that cannot converge is today, and the per-sender 30-minute lookback re-serves a cleared sender's newest rows as it re-serves every device's (refused here, dropped by id elsewhere); a day holding both cleared rows and missed posts is served whole once; a channel cleared while this device held nothing, or rejoined after a leave dropped its counts, fetches only what is newer than its first row after the clear (live delivery and the relay rings carry the rest).

## 66. The operator denylist, relay side (track t14-deny, 0.14)

Design `.team/DENYLIST_DESIGN.md` + hostile review D1-D13 (t14-deny worktree). Relay side here; the client's `denied` set and its rows come from track t14-deny-client. Code: `relay-uws/src/deny_list.h` (pure), the deny section of `ws_handler.cpp`, `snapshot_codec.h` v12, `file_stamp.h`. Every match is an exact compare on an id the relay already sees (server ids and `conf:` meeting ids lower-cased, master peer ids, `ip_limit_key` keys); the relay logs counts only, never an entry or a refusal.

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| The list in force (`deny::State.list`) | `deny_start` (once, after the restart snapshot, before listening), `deny_tick` (every 2 s on a changed stamp: `st_mtim` ns, size, inode, device) through `deny::load` and `deny::take` | The operator's file only (systemd `/etc/hollow-relay/denylist.json` 0640 root:hollow-relay under `ProtectSystem=strict`, Docker `keys/denylist.json` 0640 root:999 in the read-only mount; the relay cannot write it). Read all or nothing (`deny::parse`, never throws): version 1, all three lists present, each entry a string or an object (`id`, `ip` for networks; `ref`/`added` read past, kept nowhere); a 32/40-hex server id or `conf:` + one; a peer-id shape; an address keyed exactly as a socket from it is (v4 canonical, v6 to its /64, a written `/64` with no host bits, v4-mapped unmapped, no zones, no other prefix); 8 MiB, 65,536 entries per list, depth 4. A file missing or unreadable keeps the list in force, at start too (the snapshot's list, review D8/D9), with a loud line; only a valid empty file clears. The list counts as applied and the file as judged only once the apply returns (D10). Units `test_deny_list`, fuzz `fuzz_deny_list`; live `deny_reload`; restart phases in `test_relay_restart` |
| Devices learned for a listed identity (`deny::Learned`) | `inbox_owner_by_roster` on every roster shown for a listed master; `apply_denials` on an identity added (from the held `roster_book` record); the snapshot | From the roster FOLD only (members and consented pending joins), never the roster as shown, so nobody can name an innocent device (D5). Sticky until the entry goes: a later roster that drops a device does not clear it, up to the cap: 512 per identity (its own least recently confirmed go, never one the latest fold names; so past 512 fresh devices shown by the identity's own keys, a dropped one is forgotten and logs in again, residual R8, not pinned because pinning removed devices needs a cap of its own with the same eviction) and 262,144 in all (the identity holding the most gives way); only that identity's keys can add one. Units `test_deny_list` (learned); live `deny_learns_no_unconsented_pending`, `deny_identity_foreign_show_looks_normal`; restart "a device learned before the restart stays refused" |
| What an added entry removes | `apply_denials` (uncharged, collects, closes after), `drop_denied_room`, `deny_servers`, `purge_denied_peers`, `deny_networks`; devices learned on a show: `deny_settle` (after the loop iteration) and `deny_scan` (next tick, one walk a tick however many devices) | Server or meeting: every member told a counted `room_denied` once (a session in grace in its ring), the room erased whole (sessions' rooms and filters, sockets' fetch rooms, filters and withheld presence, door grace), its rings incl. `~join`, its buffered copies and channel-push throttles; the join lock record stays frozen (lock reads and writes answer `room_denied`). Identity (decision 3): its sockets closed 1008 with `account_denied`, sessions in grace ended, push token, prefs, opt-in and registration dropped, its own queue and its mailbox dropped, every frame it put in any buffer or ring dropped; the same for a device learned after the add (D11). Network: sockets closed with `network_denied`, sessions in grace from it ended. Kill orders aimed at denied devices stay parked. Session rings keep frames already ringed (bounded by the grace; a resume replays them), and their hand-off at grace end refuses a listed sender (live `deny_identity_frames_in_a_held_ring_stay_gone`). Live `deny_add_clears_the_room`, `deny_identity_learned_by_inbox_roster`, `deny_identity_add_purges`, `deny_network` |
| Refusals | `.open` (network, before `admit_socket`: no slot, no rate budget), `handle_auth` (listed or learned, after the signature, before the license), `handle_join` (a denied room first: `room_denied`, no slot, no fold, no door), an inbox show whose OWN device the fold puts on the list: verdict `Denied`, closed and returned before any room is touched (D1, D4: any other shower is answered as before), `handle_lock_get` / `handle_lock_put` (nothing taken), the top of `handle_binary_direct_msg` / `handle_direct` / `handle_binary_channel_direct` before any deposit or push wake (D3), `buffer_offline_msg` (a denied room, target or SENDER: layer two, so a ring's hand-off never parks it, nor brings back a listed device's frames from another device's ring, R3) | Live `deny_join_refused_for_member_guest_fetch`, `deny_direct_layers` (layer one: no wake, layer two: a grace ring's frame for the room is not handed off; a listed identity's mailbox), `deny_identity_bare_master`, `deny_identity_stranger_inbox_join_looks_normal`, `deny_network` |
| Restart snapshot (codec v12) | `snapshot.cpp` `capture` / `carry_denylist`; `deny_start` | The list in force and the learned map, memfd to memfd like every section; each entry read again by the file's own rules on the way back. A v11 snapshot decodes with no list (the file alone decides). A rollback to a v11 binary refuses the whole v12 snapshot: that one restart loses buffers, rings and push tokens (deploy note). Units `test_snapshot_codec` (byte-exact v12 fixture, v11 decode, every cut of the section refused) |
| keys.json on reload (HOL-SEC-203) | `license_pool.h` `parse_keys_file` / `reload_text`, `license.cpp`, `main.cpp` timer callbacks in try/catch | A field of the wrong type reads as nothing and keeps the previous keys; nothing a reload reads can throw into uSockets (which ended the process with no snapshot). Unit `test_license_pool` "a keys.json with enabled as a string keeps the previous keys"; live `deny_license_wrong_types` |

Wire: `{"type":"room_denied","room":"<id>"}` (a stream frame, counted: `session_vectors.json`, `test_session`) and `auth_failed` codes `account_denied` / `network_denied` (uncounted). No reason on the wire. `/server-stats` names entry counts per kind only with `PUBLIC_DENY_COUNTS=on` and the public stats on (default off). Operator tools: `hollow-relay --check-denylist <path>` (warns, by count only, of bare 32-hex server entries: an older server id has the shape of a DM room and `own_room`, so one blocks whatever room has that code, R7), `hollow-relay-setup --deny check|add|remove` (checked by the relay's reader before an atomic write that keeps owner and mode), `check-host.sh` (owner, mode, readable). What it does not stop, and the oracle any holder of a server id has (a join is answered `room_denied`): relay-uws/SELF_HOSTING.md, "Blocking a server, an identity or an address". Residuals: an unlearned device of a listed identity acts until its first inbox show, and a modified client that never shows its roster is never learned; a modified member can carry a denied server's traffic through DM, inbox, `recovery:` and other rooms the relay cannot map to it (honest clients stop, track t14-deny-client); a listed address refuses everyone behind it (carrier NAT); TURN credentials already issued live up to their hour.

## 67. The relay operator's denylist, client side (track t14-deny-client, 0.14)

The relay half (the file, `DenyState`, the learned map, the snapshot section) is the relay builder's; this half is what a client does with a refusal. A refusal deletes nothing on the device, persists nothing, and is per relay (RAM).

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| `Rooms.denied` (ws_client, the rooms nothing is written for) | `ws_client::Client::refuse_room`, on the node's `WsCommand::RefuseRoom` only | The relay's `room_denied` raises `WsEvent::RoomDenied` only for a server- or `conf:`-shaped room (`relay_denial::deniable_room`) we joined or whose lock we asked for (`deniable_ask`); the node confirms it only for a server it holds, a pending join, a guest room or a meeting (`relay_denial::ours_to_refuse`): a DM room has a legacy server id's shape, so a hostile relay cannot silence inbox, DM, own, fwd, link or share rooms (review D12). Filter in `render` for Command and Replay entries only, never an `Entry::Frame` resend (review D7). |
| `Denials` (node, the servers and meetings this relay refuses) | `relay_denial::Denials::deny` from the swarm `WsEvent::RoomDenied` arm; lifted by `lift` on the next `RoomMembers` of the room; probes every 6 h (jittered) and on the person's "Try again" (`NodeCommand::RetryRelayDenials`) | The sealing stage (`frame_auth::spawn_sealer`) passes every command through `Denials::pass` before AND after the Olm carry: nothing scoped to a refused server leaves, whatever room it rides (the room itself, a DM, inbox or heard room, a recovery pool aliased to it), judged by the room, the plaintext `HavenMessage::server_scope()` (exhaustive) and the Olm envelope's place (review D2). While probing, only the join and the lock read pass. |
| Our ops a refusal kept from everyone (`node/unsent_ops.rs`, t14-denyproof) | `UnsentOps::refused` from the swarm `WsEvent::RoomDenied` arm, for a server we hold as a member: a mark (the server's HLC ms at the refusal, minus 60 s), saved as setting `unsent_ops`, an earlier mark kept, so a restart during the refusal keeps it; `swarm::hand_unsent_ops` on every `RoomMembers` and `PeerJoined` of the room once it is no longer refused | The sealer drops everything scoped to a refused server, so an op of our identity made then sits in no ring and reached no one. Once the relay carries the server again, every op of our identity in `op_log` from the mark on goes to every device of ours (`carry_to_every_own_device`, once per process) and paced (`frame_budget::pace_carried`) to the member devices the room lists (a deleted server lists none: its tombstone goes to the online devices of every identity still a member when it was deleted, from `member_record`; harness `t14_deny_a_server_deleted_while_refused_is_deleted_for_members_back_first`); the mark ends only when a member device was handed them, or when no other identity is a member. No copies, no cap. The lift also asks 2 member devices in the room what they made (`op_sync.joined`), so a member's change made while refused still meets one back later; a sibling gets ours by the own-device carry and gives us its own by its return's re-announce (harness `t14_deny_a_members_change_reaches_a_device_whose_sibling_alone_holds_it`). No new ingest: receivers take the carried `CrdtOpBroadcast` through `ingest_remote`; only our own `op_log` feeds it; a lift needs our own probe. Tests: harness `t14_deny_a_change_made_while_refused_reaches_members_after_the_lift`, `..._reaches_members_who_came_back_first`, `..._reaches_a_member_back_after_its_author_left`, `rv_deny_a_change_made_while_refused_reaches_the_authors_sibling`, `rv_deny_every_op_made_while_refused_reaches_members_past_the_cap`, `rv_deny_a_change_made_before_a_restart_while_refused_reaches_members`, `t14_deny_a_change_made_while_refused_reaches_members_away_throughout`; units `unsent_ops::tests::*`. |
| Rooms we left while refused (t14-denyproof, review M2) | `Denials::forget` (the room, and every alias naming it) from a given-up join (`handle_discard_pending_join`), a durable teardown (`leave_evicted_server`), a meeting left or ended (`conference` leave/end) and a `LeaveServer` whose leave no longer holds the room; backstop `Denials::forget_unless(refusal_still_ours)` before every probe (Try again, the 6 h retry), with `RetryDenied { room }` to ws_client | A probe sends `JoinRoom`, so a refusal of a room we left would put this socket back into it. `refusal_still_ours` = a held server, a pending join, a guest view, a leave of ours still to hand on (`leave_book`, which keeps its room by design until the owner holds the leave), or a meeting we host, knock on or hold the group of. A pending leave hands nothing while refused (`LeaveBook::drive` skips a refused server, so no device counts as reached by a dropped frame) and lands on the lift, then lets the room go. A meeting's start or knock probes its own room only (`NodeCommand::RetryRelayDenial`, `Denials::probe_one`); for a meeting left while refused it probes that `conf:` room afresh (`Denials::probe_meeting_to_hold`, meeting rooms only), so the start hears a lift or the refusal again. Tests: harness `rv_deny_a_server_left_while_refused_is_not_rejoined_by_try_again`, `t14_deny_a_join_given_up_while_refused_is_not_rejoined_by_try_again`, `t14_deny_a_meeting_left_while_refused_is_not_rejoined_by_try_again`, `t14_deny_a_meeting_left_while_refused_is_asked_about_on_its_next_start`, `t14_deny_try_again_asks_only_about_rooms_still_ours` (the probe's own check, leaves forgetting nothing); units `relay_denial::tests::a_left_room_is_forgotten_and_never_probed`, `one_room_is_probed_alone`. |
| MLS groups of a refused server (`MlsManager.frozen`) | `MlsManager::set_frozen` on every deny and lift | `create_group`, `commit_membership_as` (every add, removal and admission), `stage_self_update` and `generate_key_package_for` refuse a frozen server's group and subgroups: no turn, no commit, no KeyPackage (review D6). |
| Serving a refused server's files | swarm `FileRequest` arm, `crypto_handler::is_vault_coordinator` | `relay_denial::denied(sid)` (the node loop's task-local set): silence, no stream, no vault repair. |
| Login refusal (`login_denied`) | `ws_client::on_login_denied` from `ConnectError::Denied` at the challenge (`network_denied`) and at sign-in (`account_denied`), the relay's exact codes only (`RelayDenial::from_code`) | No socket until the jittered 6 h retry or `WsCommand::RetryDenied { room: None }`; nudges never dial; `WsEvent::RelayDenied` once per refusal; the push fetch treats it as a quiet empty fetch. Never switches relays. |

Tests: harness `t14_deny_a_refused_server_sends_nothing_for_it_and_keeps_everything`, `t14_deny_a_pending_join_stops_asking_and_keeps_its_row`, `t14_deny_a_refused_server_s_file_is_served_to_nobody` (the FileRequest gate), `t14_deny_a_refused_server_s_recovery_pool_stays_shut` (both pool aliases); `crypto_handler::tests::no_vault_repair_runs_for_a_server_the_relay_refuses`; `relay_denial::tests::*`; `ws_client::tests::{a_room_denial_purges_the_room_and_blocks_what_follows, a_refusal_of_a_room_no_refusal_can_name_is_ignored, only_the_exact_denial_codes_refuse_a_login}`; wire `a_denied_join_queued_across_a_resume_keeps_both_counts`, `a_fresh_session_rejoins_no_refused_room_until_asked_again`, `a_refused_account_stops_until_asked_again`, `a_refused_network_is_a_denial_at_the_challenge`. Fix round (review R1-R6): a refused server's carry takes no carry-book place (`olm_lane::carry_json`) and one refused after queueing is released unencrypted (`OlmLane::deliver`; the sealer hands every Carry to the node); `Denials::lift` lifts only a room being probed, so an unasked `members` lifts nothing; a lift clears the room's `relay_catchup_done`; a pending leave (`leave_book.holds_room`) counts as ours; frozen groups keep their queued MLS changes until the lift (`crypto_handler::batch_commit_groups`). Tests: `crypto_handler::t14_deny_batch_tick::a_frozen_group_keeps_its_queued_key_package_until_the_lift`, `olm_lane::tests::{a_refused_carry_leaves_no_waiting_ticket, a_carry_refused_after_queueing_is_released_unencrypted}`, `relay_denial::tests::an_unasked_member_list_lifts_nothing`, harness `t14_deny_a_leave_after_a_relaunch_still_obeys_the_refusal` and the lift leg of `t14_deny_a_refused_server_sends_nothing_for_it_and_keeps_everything`. What it does not stop: a modified client can still carry the server's traffic over other rooms, end to end encrypted, and the relay cannot tell.

## 68. Age-restricted servers on phones (Apple 1.2, track t14-agegate, 0.14)

| Write | Where | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| The identity's age-restricted choice for its phones (`app_settings['age_gate_phones']`, `{allow, at}`) from a sibling | swarm `HavenMessage::AgeGateSync` arm -> `age_gate::take_sibling_at` -> `take_sibling` / `outranks`; `NetworkEvent::AgeGateChanged` | A roster-counted, unrevoked device of our OWN identity only (`resolver::is_own_unrevoked_device`; a bare master never reaches the arm, G1); stamp held to the frame (`at <= stamp_ceiling(frame_ts)`, else refused, never clamped: a clamped ON could outrank a real OFF); newest wins, OFF wins a tie; a stored row past `stored_stamp_limit` counts as planted and any genuine entry replaces it; no entry = closed. Lane `Carried` (Olm, own devices), `live_only = false`: the author deposits it to every own device through our inbox (`carry_to_every_own_device`), so an away phone learns an OFF with no computer online (A5), and every device re-shares the held entry with its original stamp when it verifies a sibling (`share_with_sibling`). Not a receive gate (accepted risk A6): "only a computer authors" is local (`age_gate::author` refuses a phone build); a modified sibling of ours could author ON. Unit `node::age_gate::tests::agegate_*`; harness `agegate_the_desktop_choice_reaches_every_own_device_and_its_lifting_too`, `agegate_an_off_sent_to_a_phone_that_just_dropped_still_reaches_it` (a stale presence view: the direct waits at the relay for the room the phone rejoins), `authz_a_friends_list_never_sets_our_age_gate`, `authz_a_revoked_device_never_sets_our_age_gate`. |
| What a link snapshot or backup may carry | `MessageStore::scrub_device_secrets` (export and landing) | Deletes `age_gate_phones`, `age_gate_local`, `age_gate_asked`, `age_gate_store_install`: a copied device learns the choice only from the sibling gate above. Unit `agegate_a_link_snapshot_or_backup_carries_no_gate`. |
| Settled marks (`age_settled:{server}`, `age_settled_init`): whether this device knows what a server is flagged as | `age_gate::note_settled_at` from a member's FINAL digest catch-up (`settle_age_gate` on `op_sync::Step::Done`/`Rosters`, member by resolved master) or a nonce'd final answer (`join_hold::sync_mark`), and at founding; `unsettle_at` at EVERY join start (`handle_join_server`, sibling announce, phrase re-entry) | Local: no remote party writes a mark directly; a member's answer only ends a catch-up we asked for. A phone with the gate closed hides a server that is flagged OR not settled and not holding the `is_nsfw` register (A4, fail closed); a database that never tracked marks (pre-0.14) counts its held servers settled once (`init_settled`). Harness `agegate_a_sibling_joined_server_settles_after_catch_up`, `agegate_a_non_members_catch_up_settles_nothing`; unit `agegate_hidden_servers_fail_closed_until_settled`. |
| Every surface on a phone with the gate closed (reads, not writes) | Rust: `get_joined_servers` leaves hidden servers out (A3, Archive reads it directly), `run_fetch` fetches nothing for a hidden server's wake (`wake_age_hidden`), the NSE shows a neutral banner with no ids in userInfo (`hollow_push_server_hidden`, A1), the node mutes hidden servers at the relay (`age_gate::Sent` overlays `nothing` on Dart's push prefs, phones only; nothing goes before Dart's own prefs, since the relay replaces the whole filter set, `~dm` mutes included; the relay's copy and Dart's copy of the hidden set are kept apart, so a prefs resend never swallows a move Dart has not heard), `handle_join_server` never carries 18+ consent while closed (`join_consent_at`). A hidden server's voice room is left (never a meeting), its share-backed media and recent files are not pulled (the post-link sweep takes no channel file at all since 0.14.1, §77), Settings > Storage and the Settings search name nothing of it. Dart (second layer): `ServerListNotifier` drops hidden and flagged servers and never inserts on an event before Rust lists it; the event arms that notify, toast or count (`ChannelMessageReceived`, `ChannelNotificationHint`, `SyncCompleted`, `MessageSyncCompleted`, `ServerJoined` via `applyServerJoined`, `AgeGateChanged`) ask `ageGateProvider` first; `UnreadNotifier`, `MentionPreviewNotifier`, `systemNotificationProvider.notifyChannel` and `_showChannelNotification` drop hidden servers; the push tap (`_openChannelFromPush`) asks Rust first; `nsfw_confirm:` shows the age-restricted message and discards the ask | Units `agegate_server_ffi_marks_hidden_only_where_closed`, `agegate_a_wake_for_a_hidden_server_fetches_nothing`, `agegate_a_closed_gate_never_sends_nsfw_consent`, `agegate_hidden_servers_are_muted_at_the_relay`, `agegate_a_move_seen_by_a_prefs_resend_still_reaches_dart`, `agegate_no_overlay_is_sent_before_darts_prefs`, `agegate_a_sideloaded_start_after_a_store_install_gets_its_toggle_back`, push_enrich `agegate_the_nse_hides_only_an_age_restricted_servers_wake`; harness `agegate_a_prefs_resend_between_a_flag_and_the_tick_still_tells_dart`, `agegate_a_phone_mutes_its_hidden_servers_again_on_reconnect`, `agegate_a_closed_phone_joins_with_no_18_plus_consent`; Dart `test/age_gate_test.dart`, `test/recent_files_sweep_test.dart`. |
| An age-restricted server's public channels (every place that decides a channel is public) | `ServerState::is_channel_public` and `public_channels()` (false/empty while `is_nsfw`), so the send branch, `public_frame_accepted` (receiver keep), `channel_readable_by` (FileRequest arm, replication), the `PublicChannelListRequest`/`PublicChannelSyncRequest` responders, `RequestPublicChannels` and `ChannelFfi.is_public` all follow; the author's `handle_set_channel_public` refuses ON, and the author of `is_nsfw = true` unpublishes every flagged channel and tells guests | Each member's own fold refuses guests; no guest-side flag is the gate (HOL-SEC-094 pattern). Unit `authz_an_age_restricted_servers_channels_are_never_public`; harness `authz_an_age_restricted_server_shows_guests_no_public_channel` (guest told it closed, a member's own list names none, the post goes out MLS only), `authz_a_member_holding_the_18_plus_flag_but_a_public_channel_lists_none_to_guests` (the list responder alone), `authz_an_owner_cannot_publish_a_channel_of_an_18_plus_server` (the author refusal alone). |

## 69. Relay presence batches, the short `members` answer, connection limits, breaker enforce (track t14-relay, 0.14)

Design `DESIGN_RELAY_014.md` (round 2, review F1-F15 folded). Relay tables a stranger fills here: per-room presence logs, `presence_subjects`, the login window, the connected book, the GCRA rate buckets.

| Path / table | Gate | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Presence to a viewer (`peer_joined`/`peer_left`, the new `presence` batch, relay `presence_batch.h`) | One writer, `announce_door_change` (source guard in `test_presence_batch`: no presence literal elsewhere, `drop_inbox_owners` included, F4). A batching socket is one whose FIRST join said `pv:2` (fixed per socket and carried on the session, F13); `fwd:` rooms stay per-peer. Flushes are uncharged relay work (`breaker::Uncharged`): the 25 ms timer, the leading edge, the 256-event cap, the 32 MiB total, the subject flush at the top of `.message` before the breaker plans the frame (keyed by peer id, F11), the viewer's slice before EVERY `members` (`send_members`, F1/F2) and before a `discover_peers` digest (F5). Inactive sessions are withheld as `send_presence` does. A room leaves `ws_rooms` ONLY through `erase_room`, which first drops what its log put in `presence_subjects` (else an entry outlives its room forever, review C3). A socket that took single frames until now (a session back from a snapshot, `presence_v` 0) and turns to `pv:2` is stamped in every room it holds, so no batch repeats a change it was already sent (C4). | Live `presence_subjects_go_with_their_room` (reads `/presence-test`), restart `t14_restart_after` (run_restart.sh: the second of two arrivals still waits in the log, checked by `/presence-test`, when the resumed socket's `pv:2` join lands, and each arrival reaches it once; kills the C4 stamp's removal), `presence_batch_coalesces`, `_subject_first`, `_fresh_session_survives_viewer_rejoin`, `_join_mid_window`, `_inactive_withheld`, `_split_256`, `_leading_edge_latency`, `_inbox_demotion_after_upgrade`, `_fwd_room_legacy`, `presence_digest_after_batch`, `presence_batch_flush_charges_nobody_else` (breaker_enforce); unit `test_presence_batch` (bounds, slices, own-id skip, guard). |
| The visibility rule of a join (`handle_join`) | Announce `peer_joined` iff the socket was invisible and the join makes it visible, `peer_left` iff the reverse (an inbox owner whose re-shown roster no longer counts, F12); a same-socket re-join after a lock move announces nothing. | Live `presence_inbox_visibility_both_ways`, `presence_rejoin_same_socket_is_quiet_after_lock_move`; harness `t14relay_a_kick_announces_no_rejoin` (RED with the mock's old noisy re-join: 12 arrivals). |
| The short `members` answer (`digest`, `count`) | Relay: only for `pv:2` + `have:true` + the slot already this socket's + visible before and after + not hidden, withheld or inactive; the viewer's slice is flushed first. Client (`ws_client.rs`): accepted only when `presence_audit::told_matching` equals digest AND count at READ time, else forget the room's record, `PresenceDrift`, and ws_client re-joins the room ITSELF without `have` for ANY room kind (DM, `inbox:`, `fwd:`, own room, server; `Rooms::ask_full`, drained by `rejoin_for_full_lists`), so a peer the relay dropped silently is purged even where nobody audits (review C1). `presence_audit::on_joined`/`on_left` touch only an existing record (a record comes only from a full `members`), and `asks_short` is false while a full re-join is owed (C2). A hostile relay could already forge presence; nothing here grants more (R3). | Live `presence_short_answer` (incl. inactive and wrong field types), `presence_short_answer_after_midflight_batch`; unit `members_short_replays_the_told_view_or_drifts`, `presence_batch_drops_own_id_and_a_bad_entry_drifts` (a bad entry voids the whole batch), `a_change_after_forget_brings_back_no_record`, `a_drifts_rejoin_asks_for_the_list`, `a_short_mismatch_rejoins_every_room_kind_for_the_list`; wire `a_short_answer_that_differs_is_joined_again_for_the_list`, `a_join_for_the_list_right_after_a_replay_is_not_its_echo` (never taken for a replay's echo), `a_drifts_rejoin_is_rendered_without_have`; harness `t14relay_g_a_short_answer_that_differs_purges_the_dropped_peer`, `t14relay_h_a_dm_refresh_purges_a_silently_dropped_friend` (MockRelay renders relay JSON through ws_client). |
| Connection limits (`conn_limits.h`, env, `--check-limits`) | Settings parsed once; garbage = default, out of range = nearer end + a loud start line; every limit refusal closes 1008 (0.13 clients back off on 1008 only, F8).
