GCRA per address (burst = the open cap); over-cap sockets come in pending up to `2 * cap` and settle at login only by the device's OWN older socket or grace session (R8). | Units `test_conn_limits`, `test_conn_rate`, `test_session_bounds`; live `limits_refuse_1008`, `cgnat_zombies_resume`, `cgnat_fresh_logins`; installer `t_conn_limits`, `t_adopt_carries_limits`. |
| Login window (`login_window.h`) | Every unauthenticated socket is an entry charged to its `socket_share`; past the per-block (soft) or total window the STALLED entry of the heaviest share closes first; never by age across addresses (F6/F7); a newcomer is never refused. Stalled = no NEW stage for 5 s (open, first `auth_hello`, `auth`): a repeated hello is no step (review C5). Each step may take up to 5 s, so a paced attacker stays fresh until the 10 s auth timeout (AR-36 counts it). `handle_auth` returns whether the socket still stands and nothing reads its user data past an `end()`; a window victim that is also the connected book's is closed once (C7). | Unit `test_login_window` (`stage_tests`); live `login_window_repeated_hello_still_stalls`, `login_window_stalled_first`, `login_window_slow_login_is_not_stalled` (2.5 s quiet keeps its place: pins STALL_MS), `login_window_total_heaviest_stalled`, `login_window_total_wave` (600 honest, 0 closed, exactly the 100 stalled give way), `cgnat_beside_silent_neighbours`. |
| Connected book (`conn_book.h`, `CONNECTED_LIMIT`) | Every open socket charged to its share; past the budget the heaviest share gives way, pending then loose (1008) then held (1013 + hint, the session resumes); the newcomer of the heaviest share is the one refused. | Unit `test_conn_book`; live `limits_connected_budget_sheds_heaviest`. |
| Breaker ENFORCE (`breaker::enforced()`, `BREAKER_MODE`) | Default enforce; `BREAKER_MODE=shadow` = count only; the fresh-start floor takes the operator's rate and burst of the block's family (`conn_limits::Limits::floor_rate`: v4, or the /64 numbers for a v6 /48 pool; review C6), never the compiled 120/512. | Live `start_floor_follows_the_operator_rate` (rate_exempt variant), `breaker_*` (enforce build), `breaker_mode_named`, `shadow_env` variant, `breaker_lost_hint_waits_at_login`; unit `test_breaker` (`start_pools_floor_follows_conn_rate`), `test_conn_limits` (a v6 /48 pool's floor takes the v6 rate and burst). |

## 70. The relay's roster fold as a second source (track t14-rostercheck, 0.14)

Design `.team/DESIGN_ROSTERCHECK.md` (v2, hostile design review folded). Closes t14-mlsjoin review finding 2 (a joiner's KeyPackage reaching a device its own identity removed, when every member it reaches holds the roster from before the removal) and the server-room half of AR-15's first-contact case. The relay already folds every roster each identity's devices show it (ID-1R, §22); a member asks for those statements and verifies them itself.

| Path / table | Gate | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| The relay's answer to `roster_check` (`ws_handler.cpp::handle_roster_check`, rule `roster_check.h`) | Statements, never a verdict, and only when ALL hold: a full socket; master, device peer-id shaped; nonce 1..32 `[0-9a-z]`; the room 32 or 40 lowercase hex and this socket's slot in it; a locked room only to the NEWEST door (`open == PROVED`, never a grace); the device `presence::listed` to the asker there; the relay's own fold of that master PLACES the device (member, removed or pending; a consent alone, which any key signs, places nothing). One null shape for every refusal. Priced `2 + ROSTER_CHECK_W` (fold, lookup, 1 W per KiB of the 256 KiB ceiling), the dump cached per record version. Read-only: no table, no snapshot change. | Unit `test_roster_check` (`roster_check_vectors.json`, shared with the harness relay; a planted consent places nothing; the dump cache); live `rc_unlocked_room`, `rc_locked_room`, `rc_resume_says_so` (`RELAY_LIVE_ONLY=rostercheck`); `test_breaker` price, `session_vectors` rows. |
| Whether the client asks (`auth_ok` / `resumed` `roster_check:true`) | Read from EVERY login and resume (`AuthReply::answers_roster_check` -> `WsEvent::RelayRosterCheck` -> `roster_check::set_relay`), so a resume onto a relay a restart replaced without it stops asking. A relay without it: today's rule, one `[HOLLOW-SECURITY]` line. | Unit `relay_session::the_roster_check_is_told_on_every_login_and_resume`; harness `w4rc_a_relay_without_the_check_seats_as_before`. |
| An answer (`roster_check::take_answer`) | Asked-set hard drop: only for an ask of ours with that nonce, master, room and device; the copy must name the master, fit the ceilings, and every statement verifies alone (`verified`). A copy serves every ask for the master; a NULL answer judges only the ask it answers: it marks the device it named for 60 s (no new KeyPackage to it, a walk naming only such devices passes on), lands only a staged roster that device delivered, and sets no placement, wipes no other ask and lands nothing else (so a name nobody counts stalls nobody). RAM only, 1,024 entries per table, our own asks only, one per master per 5 s. A node loop starts with an empty book (`reset_for_loop`). | Unit `roster_check::tests::an_answer_counts_only_for_an_ask_of_ours`, `a_null_answer_judges_only_its_own_ask`, `the_loop_starts_with_an_empty_book_and_a_delayed_reshow`. |
| The judgement (`authority_view::relay_placement` / `placement_from`) | Every copy merged under OUR key: the relay's copy is a CARRIED candidate of `judge_held`, so it never picks the key, and the swarm arm judges it BEFORE it merges the copy into a held roster; clock-free authority; Ask iff counted and not removed; a copy under another key than ours = Conflict (Skip/Hold, one security line per master, never a refusal); ours legacy and the copy keyed counts only what both count; no copy or no roster of ours places nothing. | Unit `authority_view::tests::the_relays_copy_is_judged_under_our_key`, `roster_check::tests::a_placement_reads_removal_before_count_and_conflict_before_both`. |
| Every KeyPackage to a foreign device (`crypto_handler::carry_key_package`, `key_package_devices`, so every caller, the Legacy re-bootstrap on `PeerJoined`/`RoomMembers` included: `carry_key_package_to_identity`, minted only when a device may take one) | Only a device a fresh (60 s) placement says Ask (`roster_check::gate`). A device not judged yet: the frame is HELD in RAM (`roster_check::hold`: 16 per (server, package owner), ours or a forwarded asker's, and a full book of 1,024 gives way from its heaviest owner) and its master asked about; held frames are looked at only when an answer or placement comes in or one falls due (never every loop turn), released once a placement says Ask, kept while not judged, not counted or in conflict (asked again every 5 s), dropped only when the merged copies REMOVE the device or after 60 s (`take_released`), so a once-only forward to a committer we never asked about is not lost. A device judged out = "not reached". Own identity and relays without the check exempt. The walk confirms its target BEFORE `walk_may_restart` and any mint; Wait parks (a join-time package is kept, `LeadBook::keep_walk_package`), Skip notes the target asked without a package (`skip_walk_target`, `walk_package` ignores an empty package) and tries the next within one pass. Harness `w4rc_the_walk_waits_for_the_answer_about_its_target` (no package before a held answer, the target asked the moment it lands), `w4rc_a_target_the_relay_rules_out_passes_the_walk_on`. | Harness `w4rc_a_joiner_never_hands_its_key_package_to_a_device_its_owner_removed`, `w4rc_a_silent_relay_costs_no_key_package_storm`, `g13w2f_the_walk_reaches_the_committer_past_a_leafless_and_a_silent_target` (the held forward); units `roster_check::tests::the_gate_passes_only_what_a_fresh_placement_asks`, `a_held_key_package_is_dropped_only_on_a_removal`, `held_key_packages_give_way_per_owner_and_wait_for_their_due_time`, `crypto_handler::standing_golden_tests::no_key_package_reaches_a_foreign_device_the_relay_has_not_confirmed`, source scan `rv_every_key_package_send_passes_the_relay_gate` (no KeyPackage paced around the gate); harness `w4rc_a_legacy_lone_joiner_is_seated_with_the_check` (an unlocked Legacy room; the mock judges `locked` from its lock map). An answer re-runs only a walk the batch tick would run now (`swarm::leaf_ask_due`): an ask in flight keeps its target (harness `g13w2f_a_ground_low_id_never_becomes_the_stand_in_committer`). |
| Every server Welcome (`mls_authority::judge_welcome` -> `relay_welcome_verdict`, server group and subgroups) | After our own rules: any bound leaf the merged authority removes = Refuse (the sender is handed the copy as a `RosterNotice`); a sender not confirmed (no answer, Skip, Conflict) = Hold and asked about; any other leaf in Conflict = Hold. | Unit `mls_authority::tests::a_welcome_is_judged_by_the_relays_copy_of_each_identitys_roster`. |
| A first-sight roster only a server names (`roster_book::ingest_notice`, `admit_profile_roster`, `held_for_relay` at `land_admitted_join_asks`) | Held (`roster_check::stage`, RAM) only when its own verified fold counts its deliverer (the rule the landing applies; any other goes on to the ingest, which drops it); a later one for the same master MERGES into it (never replaces it), its deliverer kept beside the first (4 at most). Asked about naming the devices that roster counts, the deliverer first (an ask no shown device can carry yet, presence lagging a join, is retried at each drain for 5 s). It lands merged with the relay's copy (`land_staged`: the staged roster's key pins, a keyless one takes no key from the copy, a copy under another key lands nothing, delivered by the first deliverer the merged result still counts), alone after 5 s without an answer or on a null answer about its own deliverer; a roster that landed alone is asked about again the moment a device of its identity shows (`rechecks`, polled each second, up to 10 min, past the background throttle). What rode in lands after it (`roster_check::Rider`): a profile frame (`ProfileUpdate`, `ProfileCard`, an MLS profile envelope as the direct frame) is handled again by its own arm once the roster is HELD (`take_replays` at the loop head), never fetched again; profiles held this way stay within 32 MiB in all, past it one is kept without its blobs (its hashes pull them); a profile whose roster did not land is dropped; a join ask's card is kept and stored once the landed roster places its carrier (`land_held_card`); revocations the landing finds are enforced. A held foreign master is checked in the background at most every 10 min (a landing counts as a check). | Harness `w4rc_a_stale_roster_notice_places_nobody_before_the_relays_copy`, `w4rc_a_first_sight_profile_lands_with_its_roster_without_a_second_fetch`, `w4rc_a_roster_landed_alone_is_checked_once_its_deliverer_shows`, `t14f_review_live_asks_a_member_steps_aside_from_keep_nothing_until_admitted` (the card), `lead_c_a_quiet_members_post_lands_after_the_roster_trade`; units `a_staged_roster_lands_with_a_fresh_answer_or_alone_after_the_wait`, `a_want_no_shown_device_can_carry_yet_is_asked_once_one_shows`, `a_later_staged_roster_merges_into_the_earlier_one`, `a_profile_past_the_byte_budget_is_kept_light`, `rv_an_ask_never_names_a_deliverer_the_staged_roster_does_not_count`, `roster_book::tests::rv_a_non_members_notice_never_displaces_a_staged_first_roster`. |
| The book from a relay copy (`roster_book::ingest_relay`, `Source::Relay`) | Only into a roster we hold (never a first sight), never our own master, no AR-29 freshness stamp; into a roster we hold keyless the copy comes without its recovery key and phrase statements (`roster_book::keyless`), so it never picks the key; `newly_revoked` enforced as for any ingest. | Unit `roster_book::tests::the_relays_copy_lands_only_on_a_held_roster_and_confirms_nobody` (a 0.11 device list alone is no held roster), `rv_the_relays_copy_never_picks_the_key_of_a_roster_we_hold_keyless`. |
| Our roster at the relay | Shown again every 30 min (jittered, `MissedTickBehavior::Delay`, so a stalled loop never bursts) besides every connect and change, so a record evicted while we stay connected comes back. | Unit `the_loop_starts_with_an_empty_book_and_a_delayed_reshow` (source scan). |

Residuals (AR-15): a hostile relay can withhold a removal or every answer (Skip/Hold, never a wrong admission); a relay that lost the record (reboot, eviction) and was re-shown the stale roster first agrees with it; a removal made by a device not online since; a deliverer the room hides from us cannot be named, so its staged roster lands alone after 5 s and is checked once a device of its identity shows (a hidden device is no fan-out target, and the KeyPackage and Welcome gates still need a visible device); a placement stands 60 s, so a removal shown inside that window is seen at the next ask; the join-answer path (the answering member ingesting a joiner's own roster) is not staged; first contact outside server-shaped rooms (friend requests, meetings) unchanged. The target of a PENDING outgoing friend request sits in the pair's DM room with the requester, which the relay cannot tell from a Legacy server room (both 32 hex, by design), so it can ask for the requester's current roster while the request stands, where it was handed only a snapshot; it learns what the requester's accepted friends hear on every change, about an identity that chose to contact it, and nothing once the request is withdrawn. No cheap fix: the requester must stay listed in that room to take the accept live (and the relay replays a buffered accept only on a DM-room join); labelling DM rooms to the relay would tell it which rooms are pairs, and moving the accept to the requester's inbox changes friending at both ends.
## 71. Push wakes of age-restricted servers, the fetch's relay, 18+ consent per device (track t14-iospush, 0.14)

| Write / surface | Where | Gate | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| Which relay a push process dials (the NSE, a cold Android fetch process, neither ever heard Dart's `set_relay_url`) | `fetch::fetch_relay_domain` inside `run_fetch`: the relay given, else this install's own `relay_domain` setting; only a database that reads and holds no choice means the official relay, any read error (unopenable, busy, locked) refuses the fetch; the NSE passes "" (the hints never carried a relay) | A self-hosted install's wakes never dial the official relay with its device key and room ids (HOL-SEC-204: an Android UnifiedPush wake with the app killed did, and fetched nothing; the NSE too where a custom relay has push credentials) | Units `fetch::tests::w4_iospush_a_fetch_with_no_relay_given_dials_the_installs_own_relay`, `w4_iospush_an_unreadable_relay_choice_refuses_the_fetch` |
| What a channel wake may name on Android | `api::network::push_channel_meta_in` (FFI `get_push_channel_meta`): a server this phone hides is "not held" (None), so the background handler names, fetches and nudges nothing; `age_restricted` = flagged or not yet known not to be | A wake whose posts were not read shows NO banner for an 18+ server (`_handleChannelWake`), a normal server keeps its fallback; Rust not ready or the lookup failing counts as 18+ (`kChannelWakeUnknownMeta`), the NSE's trade | Unit `api::network::tests_w4_iospush::w4_iospush_a_push_names_no_server_this_phone_hides`; Dart `test/age_gate_test.dart` "a channel wake of an 18+ server with nothing read shows no banner" (`channelWakeShowsFallback`), `test/age_gate_hold_test.dart` "a channel wake whose server cannot be read shows no banner" |
| What a channel wake may name on iOS | NSE `handleChannelWake`: `hollow_push_server_hidden` before the fetch (neutral banner, no ids); after a fetch that read nothing, `hollow_push_server_age_restricted` (flagged, unsettled, not held, unreadable = 1) gives the same neutral banner instead of the generic channel one | The extension cannot drop a push (no filtering entitlement): neutral names nothing and leads nowhere | Unit `push_enrich::tests::agegate_the_nse_hides_only_an_age_restricted_servers_wake` (nextest only) |
| A joiner's 18+ consent (`age_consent:{server}`, device-local) | Written by FFI `join_server` when the person confirmed (`age_gate::note_consent`, never on a closed phone); read by `handle_join_server` (`join_consent_at`: the answer or the held yes, and nothing while the gate is closed); the two automatic joins carry only a held yes: a rebuild's re-ask (`readmission_ask`, false, the held yes added downstream) and a sibling's announce (`sibling_announced_join` via `own_consent_at`), so a non-sibling member asks this device; scrubbed from link snapshots and backups (`scrub_device_secrets`) | Members ask the 18+ question of a returning member too (`join_verdict::verdict`; only our own devices skip it), so a device that lost the server asks again | Units `join_verdict::tests::w4_iospush_a_returning_member_is_asked_the_18_plus_question_again`, `age_gate::tests::w4_iospush_a_join_carries_only_the_consent_this_device_holds`, `agegate_a_closed_gate_never_sends_nsfw_consent`, `swarm::tests::w4_iospush_a_rebuild_reask_carries_no_18_plus_yes_of_its_own`, `swarm::tests::w4_iospush_a_sibling_announced_join_carries_only_this_devices_yes`, `messages::tests::agegate_a_link_snapshot_or_backup_carries_no_gate` |
| A sibling's push mute (relay `push_prefs[P]["~sib"]`, the first relay write by one socket into ANOTHER device's registration) | Relay `handle_mute_siblings` (`sib_mute.h`): a full, non-guest, non-fetch socket that owns `inbox:{M}` on this socket AND the fold it computes counts now (`sib::writer_counted`: a fold can drop a device by time alone, a matured backup's removal, with no show to take the inbox); written only into the fold's other devices that hold a push token; order (`at` the choice, `set_at` the flags view), a newer pair replaces, EQUAL stamps only add (a flag read late leaves the view's stamp unmoved; never lifts, capped at 256), stamps past relay now + 10 min or more than 256 or non-server ids refuse the frame whole; marks carry their writer (`~by`) and go when a roster change stops counting it (`purge_sibling_marks`) or the roster book evicts the identity's record (`forget_evicted_marks`); the device's own `set_push_prefs` keeps them (`sib::carry`), only its own full socket sets `sib_local` (fetch sockets send no prefs), and its `sib_view` (`at`, `set_at`) at or past the marks lifts them and keeps those stamps. Client: `set_at` = newest clock among the held servers' `is_nsfw` registers and our own admission to each (`age_gate::view_stamps`, data only, never a wall clock), clamped to relay now + the CRDT drift and sent only while `at` fits the relay's clock (`relay_clock`, `stamps_for_relay`); a frame the relay would refuse is not recorded, so the tick retries | A phone asleep through its computer's OFF (or a server flagged meanwhile) is not woken by an 18+ server; a computer with an older view never outranks a fresher one; nobody else silences anyone's wakes; a removed computer keeps no say; a phone that caught up mutes itself. WHAT THE RELAY MAY SEE: it can infer which servers are 18+ and which identity holds them (accepted, Vitalik 11-12) | Relay unit `test_sib_mute.cpp` (order, add, view, writer, forget), `test_roster.cpp` (eviction list); live `test_sib_mute` (`live_sib_mute.inc`: OFF silences a mention too, stranger/unproved/fetch refused, order, a late flag at equal stamps, stale phone prefs, a phone's view, local yes, removed writer purge); breaker/roster/session vectors; Rust `age_gate::tests::w4_iospush_a_computer_mutes_only_held_flagged_servers_and_stamps_each_move`, `rv_iospush_a_computer_with_an_older_view_never_outranks_a_fresher_one`, `rv_iospush_a_slow_clock_computer_still_carries_a_new_flag`, `w4_iospush_stamps_fit_the_relays_clock_or_wait`, `swarm::tests::w4_iospush_a_slow_computer_stamps_its_mutes_by_the_relays_clock`, harness `w4_iospush_an_off_while_the_phone_sleeps_mutes_its_flagged_servers_wakes`, `w4_iospush_a_phone_caught_up_lifts_its_siblings_marks` |
| The phone's server list right after away or start (Dart, a read) | `AgeGateNotifier.hold` on `paused` and at start on phones (never for a sideloaded phone whose own toggle is on); `ServerListNotifier` drops flagged servers from the list at once (`refilter`) but keeps them as held (`heldServersProvider`; `keptServersProvider` = listed + held is what "still joined" reads: `LeaveWithServer`, first-run, the setup checklist's server step, notification rows, call bar; the phone chats tab keeps its filters and compact checklist while a held server waits). Ends on Rust's `AgeGateChanged`, which a phone emits after `WsEvent::Flushed`: a heartbeat written in queue order (`WsCommand::Flush`) after the inbox join's answer, a resume, or a return to the screen, answered by the relay only after everything it wrote before (the inbox and ring replays); a flush answered before a fresh session's inbox answer says nothing. Fallbacks when nothing can arrive: a refused login, the relay settled `disconnected`, or 10 s on screen (`holdCap`, never counted while away). One quiet row in the phone chats tab while held | A choice our computer made while the phone was away never shows its servers for a moment, and a hold never closes a page or reads as leaving | Dart `test/age_gate_test.dart` "an open phone back from away lists no flagged server until the relay is back", `test/age_gate_hold_test.dart`, `test/age_gate_hold_ui_test.dart`; Rust wire `ws_client::wire_tests::w4_iospush_a_flush_ends_only_on_the_relays_answer_in_queue_order`, harness `w4_iospush_a_returning_phone_hears_when_its_relay_has_delivered` |

## 72. The shop item dialog: artist links, face, words and the right to cancel (track t14-shopdlg, 0.14)

The catalog comes from our shop, but artists type the words, links and pictures in it, so every artist field is hostile input. Nothing here is a store write; it is what reaches Dart and what a tap may open.

| Surface | Where | Gate | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| An artist's links (`artist.links[{label,url}]`) to Dart, opened on a tap | `api/shop::sanitize_link` <- `sanitize_listing`; Dart `_ArtistCard` -> `openUntrustedUrl` | https only; a typed URL with a control, space, bidi control or backslash, or over 512 bytes, is refused; everything else reqwest's parser normalises (it repairs `https:example.com`, an empty userinfo, a zero-width space in a host), and the URL Dart sees is ONLY that parsed form; no credentials, no port, a named host (two or more lowercase labels, a letter in the last one, so no IPv4/IPv6 literal or `0x7f.1` disguise) whose last label is not special-use (`localhost`, `localdomain`, `local`, `internal`, `arpa` incl. `home.arpa`, `test`, `invalid`, `example`, `onion`, `lan`, `home`: a tap must not reach this machine or its LAN; wildcard DNS such as nip.io still reaches any address, so this is depth, not a wall); label cleaned to one line of 32 characters, judged empty AFTER cleaning (an invisible label = the host minus `www.`); at most 8 links, in the artist's order; on a store build no link is shown. The tooltip names the host, the label is the artist's word | Units `artist_links_are_sane_https_only`, `artist_links_refuse_special_use_names`, `an_old_catalog_artist_has_no_face_and_no_links`; widget `the artist card shows the face, bio and links`, `a store build shows no price, no Buy and no way out` |
| An artist's face (`artist.avatar_hash`, else `header_hash`) to Dart | `api/shop` keeps it only as a 64-hex hash (`valid_emote_hash`); Dart `ShopArtistFace` -> `shopArtProvider` -> `fetch_shop_art` | The same path as listing art (§55b): the bytes must hash to the address and pass `catalog::shown_or_held` (the image check) before Dart sees them; RAM only | Units `artist_face_is_kept_only_as_a_hash`, `shop_art_reaches_dart_only_after_the_image_check`; `image_file_decode_guard_test` counts the new site |
| Listing title, artist name, bio, description, licence | `api/shop::clean_line` (title 200, name 80) / `clean_block` (bio 1000, description 4000, licence 1000) | Controls, bidi embedding/override/isolate controls, direction marks and every other invisible format character (Unicode Cf: U+200B, U+FEFF, U+2060-2064, U+00AD, tag characters...) dropped, the zero-width joiner kept (emoji need it); emptiness judged after cleaning (a title of nothing visible drops the listing); a name is one line; a block keeps line breaks, a line is blank when nothing visible is left on it, never two blank lines in a row, no trailing blank lines, and a line bound (description 60, bio 20, licence 12) besides the character caps | Units `artist_and_listing_text_is_plain_and_capped`, `artist_text_blank_lines_hold_with_whitespace_only_lines`, `artist_text_drops_invisible_format_characters` |
| Buy on Ko-fi (the right to cancel) | Dart `shop_item_dialog.dart` | Disabled until the buyer ticks "I want my code right away, and I understand that I give up my 14-day right to cancel." (EU: the right to cancel a digital item ends only on that agreement, given before paying; the app's Buy goes straight to the artist's Ko-fi, so this box is the only place an app buyer gives it). Unticked on every open, per piece; Terms and Refunds open `{catalog origin}/terms` and `/refunds`; while unticked Buy carries the screen-reader hint "Tick the box above to buy". A listing with no Ko-fi item (`buy_url` empty, incl. one `kofi_buy_url` refused) shows NO Buy and no box, only "Not on sale right now.", like the site | Widget `Buy on Ko-fi waits for the right-to-cancel box`, `the box is asked again for every piece`, `Terms and Refunds open the shop pages`, `a piece with no Ko-fi item shows no Buy`, `the disabled Buy names the box it waits for` |

## 73. What a zip from someone else may unpack to, and what a shards bundle may write (HOL-SEC-205, HOL-SEC-206, track t14-zipbomb, 0.14, embargoed)

Extends the HOL-SEC-129 rows of §28: every zip reader counts the bytes an entry really inflates to (`Read::take`, `zip_budget::UnpackBudget`), never the size its header claims, with a cap per entry AND one budget for the whole zip; a refused entry still pays for what it inflated. A pack card previews every RECEIVED `.hollow-pack` before anyone taps Add, so an uncapped manifest was a crash for every viewer of the conversation, on every open.

| Path | Site | Gate | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sticker pack preview + import (a peer's file in a DM or channel; the chat card previews it unasked) | `api/stickers.rs::open_pack` (both FFI calls) + the `import_sticker_pack` blob loop | Pack file <= budget + 1 MiB (`at_rest::plaintext_len`, before it is read); its zip index at most 128 entries, walked from the file's tail by `zip_budget::index_refused` before the zip reader parses it (which allocates a record per declared entry); manifest <= 128 KiB; each blob <= 512 KiB (the rail's sticker cap); manifest + blobs together <= 128 KiB + 120 x 512 KiB; past the budget the rest of the pack is reported rejected, what landed stays | `a_pack_whose_manifest_unpacks_past_its_cap_is_refused`, `a_blob_that_inflates_past_the_sticker_cap_costs_only_the_cap`, `a_pack_stops_unpacking_once_its_budget_is_spent`, `a_pack_file_past_its_size_is_refused_before_it_is_read`, `review_t14_a_pack_listing_far_more_entries_than_a_pack_holds_is_refused`, `a_pack_whose_index_lists_more_entries_than_a_pack_holds_is_refused`, `a_real_pack_still_imports` |
| The pack card (Dart) | `sticker_pack_card.dart::_previewOnce`, `_oneAtATime` | One pack read at a time; one preview per pack file per run (64 paths); a refusal of the file is remembered, any other failure is read again only when a card for it next opens; a failure is the calm "Can't open this pack" with no Add button, never a retry per rebuild | `sticker_pack_card_test.dart` "a pack that cannot be read shows a calm state and is read once", "a failure that is not the file is read again on the next open", "packs are read one at a time" |
| Shard bundle import (`.hollow-shards`, a user-picked file from anyone) | `api/archive.rs::check_shard_bundle`, before the content store opens | Index at most 65536 entries (`zip_budget::index_refused`); manifest <= 16 MiB; each shard <= the bundle's own size (ciphertext never inflates); everything <= max(64 MiB, 4 x size); any overrun refuses the WHOLE bundle before anything is stored, the import then reads each shard to its measured size | `a_bundle_whose_manifest_unpacks_past_its_cap_is_refused`, `a_shard_that_unpacks_past_its_bundle_refuses_the_bundle`, `shards_together_stop_at_the_bundle_budget`, `a_bundle_listing_more_entries_than_a_bundle_holds_is_refused`, `a_real_bundle_from_our_own_export_still_imports` |
| `.hollowpack` (shop art, user-picked) | `hollowpack.rs::read_manifest_from` / `verify_pack` (unchanged) | 20 MB zip, 64 KB manifest, 8 files of 4 MB, 16 MB total; header size and `take()` both checked | `a_manifest_that_unpacks_past_its_cap_is_refused`, `a_file_that_inflates_past_its_cap_is_refused_before_it_is_hashed`, `an_oversize_file_is_refused_before_it_is_decoded` |

| What a shards bundle may WRITE (HOL-SEC-206) | `api/archive.rs::import_server_shards`, `bundle_manifest_refused`, `ContentStore::fill_manifest`, then `vault_ops::shard_write_refused` + `shard_bytes_refused(.., pulling = true)` | A bundle names no sender, so it is judged against what we hold. A server we hold and are a member of: a manifest only FILLS (`INSERT OR IGNORE`, so one that lands first is never replaced), its creator a current member who sent the file card we hold for its message (no card held, no manifest), its channel one of the server's that we may read and never restricted (restricted files are never vaulted), its content id well formed; it relinks no file card. A server we do NOT hold (the ex-member fallback): no manifest at all, shards only as below. A shard lands only under the server its HELD manifest names, a member's through the one shard-write gate (pledge, never over a held shard), and only as the bytes that manifest pins (an unpinned legacy erasure shard is dropped); k/m/size come from the held manifest, never the bundle | `a_bundle_for_a_server_we_do_not_hold_stores_nothing`, `an_ex_members_bundle_restores_only_the_shards_of_manifests_it_holds`, `a_bundle_never_replaces_a_held_vault_manifest`, `a_fill_never_replaces_a_manifest_that_landed_first`, `a_bundle_manifest_is_judged_like_a_live_one`, `review_t14_a_bundle_manifest_cannot_squat_another_creators_content`, `a_bundle_shard_that_does_not_hash_to_its_manifest_is_not_stored`, `a_bundle_shard_its_manifest_does_not_pin_is_not_stored`, `a_bundle_shard_lands_only_under_the_server_its_manifest_names`, `a_bundle_stays_inside_our_storage_pledge`, `a_real_bundle_from_our_own_export_still_imports` |

Sweep 2026-11-14 (every `ZipArchive::new` in hollow_core and hollow_art): snapshots (`check_snapshot`, §28) and the archive viewer (`collect_entry_bytes`, §28) already held one total budget, streamed or retained; the updater (`extract_zip_to`) unpacks only a zip whose SHA-256 the signed manifest names, streaming to disk; hollow_art only writes zips and checks them with the bounded `hollowpack::verify_pack`. The zip crate (9.x) itself errors on an entry that inflates past its declared size, a second bound we do not rely on. Residuals: the archive viewer's floor lets a small archive take 256 MiB of memory, and a shards bundle is read whole from disk; both are files the person picks. `.hollowpack`, the snapshot and the archive viewer have no index bound (files the person picks; their budgets hold). An ex-member has no pledge for a server it no longer holds, so the unpack budget alone bounds that disk use.

## 74. A person's own name colour (#140, track t17-name-colours, 0.14.1)

A compatible patch: the field sits OUTSIDE `hollow-profile2`, so every 0.14.0 profile signature still verifies and 0.14.0 ignores the field (serde drops unknown keys; neither build denies them). Proven both ways against the real 0.14.0 tree (`.team/compat` in the track's worktree) and in-tree by `name_color_profiles_cross_0_14_0_both_ways`.

| Write | Site | Gate | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `user_profiles.name_color` + `name_color_sig`, `name_color_signed`, `name_color_for` (a peer's chosen name colour, `""` = the hashed colour; the owner's signature, the exact value it covers, the profile signature it was made for) | `name_color::gated`, the ONE gate, from (a) `social::save_incoming_profile` (Olm `HavenMessage::ProfileUpdate` arm in `swarm.rs` and the MLS `MessageEnvelope::ProfileUpdate` via `handle_envelope_profile_update`; a ProfileRequest answer arrives as a `ProfileUpdate` and takes the same path) and (b) `social::handle_profile_relay` (`HavenMessage::ProfileRelay`, the ProfileRequestFor answer a member sends while the owner is offline; gated against the RELAYED `updated_at`, `profile_sig` and verified `profile_pk`). Written by `MessageStore::set_name_color` only onto a row holding EXACTLY the profile the colour was signed for: in SQL, `updated_at = ?` AND `profile_sig IS ?` (the profile's own row write is not consulted; for a relayed copy of the very profile we hold, the colour alone settles) | Its OWN master Ed25519 signature `name_color_sig` over `hollow-name-color1:` + hex SHA-256 of (updated_at LE, length-prefixed master, length-prefixed `profile_sig` of the profile it rides with, length-prefixed value) (`name_color::sig_payload`, pinned), verified with the profile's verified `profile_pk`, which must derive the master. So a colour belongs to exactly ONE profile: a relayer cannot move the other equal-stamp profile's colour onto the one we keep. REQUIRED for every value including `""`: unsigned, signed by another key, signed over another value, stamp or profile = the field is ABSENT and what we hold is kept (logged once per master), never a clear. A SIGNED value is stored as `name_color::stored_form`: canonical version 1 (`1:` + one to three lowercase `rrggbb` stops) exactly, ANYTHING else (a later version, any shape, any size) as `""`, so the owner's move away from a colour never leaves it stale and nothing but those two shapes is SHOWN. The exact signed value is kept in `name_color_signed` while it is at most `name_color::RELAY_MAX` (256) bytes, never read by Dart, so a relayer can carry a later version on. Equal stamp: the profile rule picks the profile; of two signed colours for that one profile (two devices, one ms, identical fields) the higher `name_color_sig` stays, in SQL, so replicas converge whatever the arrival order. A stored colour signature counts in that tie only while `name_color_for` names the row's `profile_sig`: one left from another profile (a 0.14.0 build's upsert moves the stamp and `profile_sig` but never the colour columns) counts as none, so NO profile write has to clear it (`save_profile` does not touch them). An older announce never matches the row's stamp, so it never lands its colour; absent on the wire (a 0.14.0 sender or relayer) preserves. A relayer (`profile_relay_frame`, the ProfileRequestFor answer) forwards `name_color_signed` and its signature only when they verify for the row's stamp and `profile_sig` (`relayable_name_color`), else neither key. Our own: FFI `update_profile` refuses anything but `""` or canonical version 1 (`valid_own`), the node writes it onto the row stamped `now`, then signs the stored value for the announce's own profile signature at every announce site (`handle_update_profile`'s two frames, `own_profile_update`). Audience = the full profile (`profile_audience`); the card (`hollow-card1`) does not carry it, and a card that replaces a row clears `name_color_sig` | Unit `name_color_v1_grammar_is_strict_and_canonical`, `name_color_signed_values_are_stored_as_v1_or_a_clear`, `name_color_signature_binds_master_stamp_profile_and_value`, `name_color_signature_payload_is_pinned`, `name_color_gate_refuses_unsigned_and_forged_and_clears_on_any_signed_other`, `name_color_lands_only_under_its_own_signature_and_absent_preserves`, `name_color_signed_clear_clears_and_an_older_announce_never_lands`, `name_color_a_signed_later_version_never_leaves_the_old_colour_showing`, `name_color_a_later_version_clears_a_relay_only_receiver`, `name_color_follows_the_profile_kept_at_an_equal_stamp`, `name_color_belongs_to_one_profile_of_its_stamp`, `name_color_equal_stamp_equal_profile_converges`, `name_color_a_signature_left_from_another_profile_never_wins_the_tie`, `name_color_a_newer_profiles_colour_lands_whatever_its_signature_sorts`, `name_color_rides_a_relayed_profile_and_a_cleared_colour_clears`, `name_color_a_relayer_forwards_only_a_colour_signed_for_the_row`, storage `name_color_lands_only_on_the_row_of_its_own_stamp`, `name_color_binds_to_its_profile_and_a_stale_signature_counts_as_none`, compat `name_color_profiles_cross_0_14_0_both_ways`, `name_color_frames_parse_on_a_0_14_0_shaped_copy_both_ways`; harness `t17_name_color_reaches_a_friend_and_a_forged_field_is_refused`, `t17_name_color_rides_a_profile_relay` |
| What reaches Dart and the screen | `UserProfile.name_color` (FFI) -> `shownNameColorProvider` (empty while the viewer's "Show custom name colours" is off, a per-device `app_settings` key, loaded BEFORE the profiles at startup) -> `namePaintFor` / `NameText` | Dart only READS the stored, gated value; `nameColorStops` parses version 1 and falls back to the hashed colour on anything else. ACCENT: for anyone but the viewer (`self`, from `ownNameMasterProvider`), a stop whose hue is within `kNameAccentBand` (35 degrees) of the viewer's accent turns to the band's nearest edge, saturation and lightness kept, and where the RGB blend between two neighbouring stops would pass through the band (opposite sides, the short way round crossing it) the gradient blends each stop up to its own edge and steps across at their midpoint (`_skipAccentBand`; the long way round would paint hues nobody picked), so nobody can wear the colour that means "you". Then every stop's lightness moves until it clears 7:1 on every dark surface, 5:1 on every light one (the hashed floor). A blocked person's chosen colour is not shown on the profile card or the DM profile panel | Dart `test/name_colors_test.dart` (chosen colours group: `someone else in the accent band turns to its edge`, `a gradient turns only the stops in the accent band`, `someone else's gradient never blends through the accent, as drawn` (renders the shader's pixels), `no pair of stops blends through the accent` (samples every 15-degree pair), `your own chosen colour keeps its hue`), `test/widget/name_color_test.dart` (`only your own name keeps a colour in the accent band`) |

Later formats: motion or anything richer belongs in a SEPARATE field with its own signature, with `name_color` version 1 kept as the static fallback every 0.14.1 build draws. A signed non-v1 `name_color` is stored as `""` here, so changing this field's format would show 0.14.1 viewers the hashed colour, never the old one. Residual: a signed value longer than `RELAY_MAX` (256 bytes) is shown as `""` by whoever hears it directly but is never relayed, so a receiver who hears that profile ONLY through a relayer keeps the colour it holds until the owner's own announce reaches it.

## 75. Linux desktop toasts: what a peer's words become on the notification server (HOL-SEC-208, track t18-linux-notif, 0.14.1, embargoed)

| Write | Site | Gate | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| A peer's message preview and display name as a Linux toast (`org.freedesktop.Notifications.Notify` on the session bus) | `DesktopNotificationService._postNative` / `showTest` -> `LinuxNotifier.show` -> `linuxNotifyArgs` | The server's capabilities are asked once per connection (`LinuxNotifier.capabilities`, reset only when the connection fails or the ask fails). `body-markup` listed: the body is escaped (`&`, `<`, `>`, `linuxEscapeMarkup`) so it shows as typed, never as a link, image or formatting; not listed: raw. The summary is plain text by spec, never escaped. EVERY string argument passes `linuxDbusText` (drops U+0000: a NUL in an `s` argument closes our bus connection and GNOME takes back every toast from it). An error reply or a timeout keeps the connection; only a transport failure replaces it, and only the connection that failed. No server answering = `isReady` false, nothing posted | `test/linux_notifier_test.dart` ("a markup server gets peer markup as literal text", "a plain-text server gets the body as typed", "no string reaches the bus with a U+0000 in it", "capabilities are asked once, and again after a failure", "is ready only when a notification server answers"); real-bus probe in the track's `.team/probe` ("closing an expired toast keeps the connection", "a NUL in a peer message posts at once on the same connection") |

## 76. The push default for servers a phone never set (`~default`, track t18-push-default, 0.14.1)

A compatible patch: a phone's `set_push_prefs` gains the reserved entry `~default` (`{level}`), the level of every server it never set (Vitalik 11-19: mentions only). A relay older than it skips that one key as an invalid room code and keeps the rest of the set (its rule `server != "~dm" && !is_valid_room_code(server)` is `continue`, never a refusal), so such a relay wakes a never-set server on every post as before. A 0.14.0 client sends no `~default`, so a new relay wakes it as before. The snapshot carries the entry as one more server pref of the existing record: no codec VERSION change.

| Write | Site | Gate | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| Relay `push_prefs[P]["~default"]` (one level, no channels) | `handle_set_push_prefs` -> `push_pref::parse` (`push_pref.h`): only the device's own full socket writes (guests and fetch sockets refused, as before); the set is still replaced wholesale and `~sib` still carried by `sib::carry`; a key neither a room code nor `~dm`/`~default` is skipped alone; `~dm` and `~default` are read past the 256-server cap (each at most once, so a device in many servers keeps them); the default's `channels` are dropped (a channel id means something only inside its server); a level that is not one of all/mentions/nothing, or not a string, reads as "all" and never throws. The entry is charged to the registration ledger like any pref (`charge_registrations`, `sib::pref_weight`). Read ONLY by `push_pref::level_for`: a sibling's mark first (`sib::muted`), then the server's own entry (its channel's override beating its level), then `~default`, then "all"; `dm_push_muted` reads only `~dm`, so DM wakes never meet the default | A server the phone never set wakes it only at the default level; anything it set wins; a muted-by-sibling server stays silent whatever the default; DMs unchanged. WHAT THE RELAY MAY SEE: one level per phone and, as before, only the servers and channels set to something else. The client names only the servers the person set (even to the default) and channels that differ from their server (in a server never set, every channel choice), never a server left alone (`pushPrefsForRelay`, HOL-SEC-160, C-RP-06) | Relay unit `test_push_pref.cpp` (default, explicit and channel win, older client, shape, reserved entries past the cap, unknown key skipped alone, sibling marks); `test_snapshot_codec` "the default, muted DMs and siblings' marks come back as written"; live `test_push_default` (`live_push_default.inc`: mentions default silences a post and wakes a mention, explicit All wins, channel override wins, default nothing silences mentions, older client woken, DM still wakes) and its restart part in `hs_restart` (`pd_restart_after`); Dart `test/push_prefs_relay_test.dart`; Rust `age_gate::tests::t18_the_default_entry_rides_the_hidden_overlay` |
| Where the relay posts wakes (the push sidecar's loopback port) | `push_port::parse(HOLLOW_PUSH_PORT)` in `parse_args`: unset or empty = 3001 (production unchanged), anything not a whole number 1 to 65535 refuses the start (exit 2); `deliver_push` reads it from `setup_ws_handler`. `run_live.sh` and `fdstore_restart.py` give every test relay a free loopback port and the client holds it (`RELAY_LIVE_PUSH_PORT`); a client that cannot hold it FAILS, never skips | A test run on the relay box never posts fake wakes to the production sidecar, and a sidecar restart during a test never sends real wakes (tokens, ids, the `X-Push-Token` secret) to a test listener; the live push cases always run | `test_push_pref` "push port"; `run_live.sh` "a HOLLOW_PUSH_PORT that is no port refuses the start" and the startup-log check "[config] Push sidecar port: N" on every variant; every live push case (`hold_push_port`) |

## 77. A channel not on screen keeps no pushed bytes (track t18-archive, 0.14.1)

0.14.1 dropped the relay topic filter: every member device now hears every channel's posts live (the Archive lists channels never opened). The filter had also kept unseen channels' files off disk by accident (their headers never came; the bytes, which `replicate_channel_file_full` streams to every online member device anyway, parked as keyless ciphertext in an early-stream temp and only completed if the person opened the channel and its ring replayed the header). Vitalik's rule since: nothing a peer posts in a server channel lands on disk by itself unless that channel is on screen on this device. Cards, text and the header's thumbnail still land.

| Write | Site | Gate | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| A pushed channel file's pending stream (the decrypt key) and so its bytes on disk | MLS arm `file_handler::handle_envelope_file_header`, Olm arm `swarm.rs` `MessageEnvelope::FileHeader` (channel headers: leafless members, explicit answers) | `file_handler::channel_push_allows` = `on_screen::channel_on_screen(sid, cid)` (`node/on_screen.rs`) AND `auto_download_allows(server:{sid})`; the voice-note exemption therefore holds only in the channel on screen. Declined exactly like #41: card + metadata kept, `declined_file_ids` + `FileFailed{auto_download_off}`, early bytes and late stream bytes deleted at the completion intercepts. `explicitly_requested` (RequestFile receipts) and an already-registered stream still bypass; DMs unchanged. The on-screen set is process-global (FFI `set_channels_on_screen`, `#[frb(sync)]` so updates apply in call order, safe before `start_node`, survives node restarts), default EMPTY in builds (fail closed), `Any` only under `cfg(test)` so older harness tests keep their pushes | harness `t18_a_channel_push_lands_by_itself_only_in_the_channel_on_screen` (MLS arm: other channel on screen, nothing on screen, voice note, on screen, explicit pull), `channel_file_header_reaches_a_member_without_a_leaf` (Olm arm declines); mutants M1-M4 killed (`.team/mut_m*.log` in the track worktree) |
| What Dart says is on screen | `channelsOnScreenProvider` (`lib/src/core/providers/channel_on_screen_provider.dart`) -> `pushChannelsOnScreen` from `HollowShell` (`listenManual`, fireImmediately) | Desktop: the selected server channel plus a split pane's channel, none while the window is hidden or minimized, server settings cover it, ANY centre tab is open (`anyShellTabOpenProvider`: Settings keeps the selection underneath it, and a tab covers the split pane too), or the server is a meeting (`conf:`). Phone shell (`mobileShellActiveProvider`): a `MobileChatRoute` showing a server channel registers (`mobileChannelRoutesProvider`, token per route) only while its PAGE is the top one (`ChannelOnScreenRoute`, a `RouteAware` on `pageRouteObserver` in `app.dart`): a DM, profile, media viewer or any other page pushed over it takes the channel off screen until it pops; dialogs and sheets do not. None while backgrounded (phones only feed the lifecycle) or locked. The unread-inclusive subscribe set is NOT this signal | `test/channel_on_screen_test.dart` ("Settings opened over a channel...", "a centre tab covers the split pane too", "a DM page pushed over a channel...", "each update reaches the node at once, in order") |
| A cut pushed stream asked for again | `file_handler::reask_dead_file_streams` (sender back, stream stalled) | The re-ask is an explicit pull (receipt bypass), so a pushed stream nobody asked for is re-asked only while its push would still be let in: `pushed_stream_may_reask` (a channel's only while that channel is on screen; DMs as before) | harness `t18_a_cut_channel_push_is_asked_again_only_on_screen`; unit `a_cut_channel_push_is_asked_again_only_on_screen` |
| The decline pins (`declined_file_ids`) | `file_handler::DeclinedFiles`, written by both header arms | Every member's post can add one, so the set is capped at 1024; past the cap the sender identity holding the most entries gives up its oldest (an evicted pin only means its late bytes park as a bounded keyless early stream instead of being deleted) | unit `declined_files_stay_bounded_and_a_flood_evicts_its_own` |
| A device link's catch-up pull | Dart `recent_files_sweep.selectRecentFilesToFetch` (first launches after a link) | DM files only: never a server channel's file, voice notes included (Vitalik 11-19); a channel's files come with the viewport sweep once it is on screen | `test/recent_files_sweep_test.dart` "no channel file is swept, voice notes included" |
| A pull from the guest view | `guest_chat_pane.dart` viewport sweep | `guestSweepTakes`: images only, only while the app is on screen (`appOnScreenProvider`: window visible and not minimized, foreground, not locked), within the server limit (`sweepTakes`); re-runs when the app comes back on screen | `test/channel_on_screen_test.dart` "guest view sweep" |
| A share-backed (>34 MB) channel file auto-started from its header | Dart `event_provider` `FileHeaderReceived` | `shareAutoDownloadScope(channelOnScreen:)`: null (manual Download) unless the channel is on screen | `test/share_auto_download_scope_test.dart` |
| A pull of declined files when the channel comes on screen | `channel_chat_provider.requestVisibleFiles` from the desktop pane AND (new) the phone route; re-run when the channel turns on screen (`channelsOnScreenProvider.select`) | Runs only for a channel on screen; pulls only within the server limit (`sweepTakes`: off = voice notes only, over the limit = a tap), clears the decline pin first. A pull is an explicit request (receipt bypass) | `test/channel_on_screen_test.dart` sweep group |
| Channel file bytes in the push process | `fetch.rs::handle_file_header` | A header naming a server (`sid`) is dropped whole: nothing is on screen there; an MLS FileHeader in a channel wake falls to `_ => None` | `fetch_refuses_a_dm_header_over_the_send_limit` (channel case, mutant killed) |

Left as they were (reported, not new with this change): the header's thumbnail (image blur <=32 px, video poster up to 400 px / 24 KB) is stored with every card, live and from sync, for any channel; vault shards of 6+ member servers; avatar and banner fetches. The SENDER still streams a channel file to every online member device (`replicate_channel_file_full`); the receiver drops it: skipping the send would need a wire change that tells every poster what each member is looking at. See the track report for the poster follow-up design.
